# Open work, in order

**Assembled 2026-10-22, after the day that closed the last hardware-blocked
row.** This is the single ordered list the repository did not have: `READINESS`
carries the release order and the engineering queue, `README` §9 carries the
detail, and the research questions were in neither. Three stale pointers were
found on 2026-10-22 alone — `READINESS`'s step 3, `README`'s *scale beyond four
nodes* cell, and the dangling item 9 → item 22 reference — each because a
dependency closed and the thing waiting on it was not re-read. **This file
exists to be the thing that gets re-read.**

**Every line carries what it is blocked on.** That is the only property that
makes rot detectable: a line whose blocker has gone is a line to act on, and a
line whose blocker cannot be stated was never a task.

**Who can do what**, used as a column below rather than repeated in prose:
**[M]** model, bounded; **[H]** needs the human's hands or machines;
**[D]** a decision only the human can take; **[X]** outward-facing or
third-party.

## §8 GAINS A FOURTEENTH ROW, AND IT SHARPENS 8.6's AUDIT RATHER THAN ADDING TO IT

**ROW 8.14, from a later turn of the same external inquiry — on epigenetics and
transcription this time.** The `LIN28`/`let-7` loop is modelled as **bistable
with hysteresis**, and the claim made of it is that ***"a bistable switch is
both. It stores the state and it is the thing that acts"*** — the
register/governor split *"resolved by collapsing it."*

**§8.6's AUDIT PRESUPPOSES THE TWO ARE SEPARABLE AND ASKS WHAT COMPARES THEM.
THIS NAMES A THIRD CONFIGURATION: no gap to compare across, because the stored
state IS the actor.** And **this protocol ships a clean instance** — the very
field §8.4 measured as the best-governed in the zome. `SettlementStatus.state`
is not a declaration checked against reality; validation recomputes it from
`inputs` and `policy` and rejects any other value, so **it has no independent
existence to drift into.** *The reason it cannot go stale is the absence of
anything to compare, not a good comparison.*

> **SO THE BORROWABLE ITEM IS A BETTER QUESTION, NOT A MECHANISM: for each
> reported field, is the right fix a COMPARISON, or REMOVING THE FIELD'S
> INDEPENDENT EXISTENCE?** Free, and sharper than what 8.6 currently generates.

**THE OVERCLAIM IS NAMED IN THE ROW, because this source has a measured history
of exactly it.** The answer says the split is *resolved* and **its own
assignment table then keeps a separate recorder row** — *"Odometer — PMD
methylation loss — still a recorder, still gating nothing."* **Both cannot be
the headline.** It found a mechanism class where the two coincide; the split
survives in its own answer, for the odometer it had just named. §8.3's caveat
records the same shape once already — §5.2's *"single most frequent error"* was
unsupported by its own appendix — and **every honest flag in the source answer
is on the biology, none on the conceptual claim, which is the part being
borrowed.**

**NOT CHASED, DELIBERATELY: hysteresis proper.** This protocol is one-way **by
substrate** (append-only — a `Retraction` adds rather than removes), but **no
object has two thresholds forward and back**, and none was found looking. **And
the one piece worth an instrument is UNMEASURED and no instance is claimed:**
the test signature *intervene before the flip and the effect is large; after it
is nothing at all, not diminished* separates a switch from a decay **by the
shape of the failure**, and which shape this protocol's windows have has never
been measured.

**§8 IS NOW 15 ROWS; 14 CARRY A LIVE `[D]`** — counted, not asserted (and the
first count run here was itself wrong, matching the `D` in `DONE`). 8.14's
blocker is **8.6's own `[D]`**, which now carries a second question.

## THE INDEPENDENT-CONVERGENCE ARGUMENT WAS AUDITED IN TWO MORE PLACES, AND IT SPLITS

**The source uses the *"independently derived, therefore trustworthy"* move at least three times. One was already found false (§8.15). The other two were audited 2026-11-24 ON REQUEST and they do NOT fail the same way — one is worse and one is better.**

**CLAIM A — `"Four sections derived independently converge here, which is the reason to trust it"` — FAILS, AND THE DOCUMENT REFUTES IT ONE PARAGRAPH LATER.** The four are its §2.4c, conclusion 9, §2.4b-ii and §11.1a, supporting *"the far field can only hand the organism a number."*

> **THE NEXT PARAGRAPH BEGINS `"Chained:"` AND THEN CHAINS THEM** — *"outer domains can supply only scalars → scalars can specify only magnitude → magnitude is Problem B → and the mechanism by which a scalar reaches magnitude is rate or threshold modification."* **A CHAIN IS ONE ARGUMENT THROUGH FOUR LINKS; CONVERGENCE IS FOUR PATHS TO ONE NODE. Both cannot describe the same four items**, and the document asserts both within two paragraphs.

**AND THE COUNT IS INFLATED INDEPENDENTLY OF THAT.** Conclusion 9 (*"magnitude is dose-gated, pattern is position-gated"*) and §2.4b-ii (*"a scalar is dimensionally the right type for a magnitude reference in a way it can never be for a proportion or a pattern"*) **are the same proposition in two vocabularies** — dose is the scalar. **All four are internal sections of one document by one author**, so not even the weak different-literatures defence §8.15 had to weigh is available here. **The verdict: one argument's own links presented as four witnesses, with *"which is the reason to trust it"* making the overclaim load-bearing.**

**CLAIM B — `"This converges with three results reached independently elsewhere"` — THE COUNT IS WRONG AND THE UNDERLYING SUPPORT IS STRONGER THAN THE FRAMING.** The three are conclusion 9, §2.4b-ii and §2.4b-iii, and they are **one proposition at three levels of specificity**: §2.4b-iii is §2.4b-ii plus a domain premise, and conclusion 9 is §2.4b-ii's headline form. **So "three results" is about one.**

> **BUT THE CONVERGING ITEM IS NOT A DERIVATION — IT IS EXTERNAL EMPIRICAL DATA**, the dog body-size GWAS: six to seven loci across a ~17-fold mass range, **four endocrine scalars, two growth transcription factors, and no positional-identity gene at all.** **That is theory meeting independent data with the prediction prior, which is a genuinely stronger form of support than derivation meeting derivation** — and a different class from §8.15's case entirely. **The honest version therefore IMPROVES the argument: one theoretical claim, corroborated once, by outside evidence.** The *"three independent results"* framing **inflates a single real confirmation into three and makes a good argument look like a weak one.**

**WHAT THIS AUDIT CHANGES FOR THIS PROTOCOL, AND IT IS A SHARPENING OF 8.11 RATHER THAN A NEW ROW.** 8.11's rule is *counts cannot separate, types might.* **These two claims are both "N independent results agree" and they are opposite in value**, which means the discriminating type is not *how many* agreed but **whether any of them is outside the author's own reasoning.** Claim B has an external node; claim A has none. **So the type that matters is `internal derivation` versus `external evidence`, and a system counting agreements cannot see it** — exactly `docs/noesis-and-the-worth-blind-graph.md`'s 0% separation, with the discriminating property named.

> **AND ONE THING I CHECKED AND DID NOT FIND, recorded so it is not re-derived: §2.4b-ii's attribution is CORRECT.** Its heading is about the domain assumption, which made the scalar-typing citation look misattributed; **the claim is genuinely in the section body** (*"A scalar is dimensionally the right type for a magnitude reference"*). **Checked rather than asserted, and the suspicion was wrong.**

## §8.15 — THE SAME MOVE POINTED OUTWARD, AND IT GIVES 6.28's `[D]` A SHAPE

**THE REFRAMING THAT EARNS THE ROW:** of four routes around a hysteretic
switch, *"only route 4 ignores the switch. It stays thrown. You supply its
outputs directly"* — and ***"the barrier was never that the reverse transition
is impossible. It is that the reverse transition is the one evolution guarded
hardest, and the guard is cancer risk rather than mechanism."***

**THAT IS THIS PROTOCOL'S INVARIANTS EXACTLY. Ranking claims is trivial, not
hard** — Invariant 1 refuses it because it is **the dangerous direction**, the
one that mints a leaderboard. **The refusals here are GUARDS, NOT
IMPOSSIBILITIES**, which is precisely why one is being argued with right now.

**AND 6.28's PARTICIPANT IS ASKING FOR THE OUTPUT, NOT THE MECHANISM** — *"I
don't think it has to mean a popularity ranking. It shows the areas people feel
strongly about."* **He does not want the ranking; he wants what it would have
told him.** So the decision is not yes-or-no on a boolean: **it is whether that
consequence can be supplied without the ordering that normally carries it.**
Route 4's shape, and **8.14's collapse question pointed outward instead of at a
struct field.** The qualification travels with it: **a map of attention is
adjacent to a map of worth, so this NARROWS Invariant 1's exposure rather than
clearing it** — the argument 6.28 has to win, not a way round it.

> **AND THE SOURCE'S OWN STRONGEST-SOUNDING CLAIM IS ITS WEAKEST, WITH THIS
> REPOSITORY HOLDING THE INSTRUMENT THAT SAYS SO.** It calls route 4 and its own
> §11.6d route 1 *"the same proposal reached from opposite ends … that
> convergence is worth more than either argument alone."* **Two lines of ONE
> author's reasoning in ONE thread agreeing is a CONSISTENCY CHECK, not
> corroboration** — and `docs/noesis-and-the-worth-blind-graph.md` measured the
> exact failure: **0% graph internal separation**, because *"genuine layered
> work and a wash ring are locally isomorphic."* A self-reinforcing convergence
> is **locally isomorphic to independent support.** 8.11's rule is the fix —
> **counts cannot separate, types might** — and these two nodes share author,
> thread and session, so they are **one type, not two.**

**A LIMIT ON 8.7 FOLLOWS FROM THE SAME MATERIAL AND IS FOLDED INTO IT: A BUDGET
BUYS MAGNITUDE, NOT PATTERN.** *"Reopening gives execution, not
specification"* — extended nephrogenesis gave **more nephrons, not
differently-shaped kidneys.** So 8.7's answer to *"refusing the score refuses
determinacy with it"* is **partial by construction: it answers how much, never
what shape.** A narrowing, not a refutation.

**ROW 6.29 OPENED AND BUILT THE SAME DAY — their first point, and they
PREDICTED the outcome rather than hitting it.** *"If someone can't find a
domain, say 'Mental Health Reform' spelt differently, they'll just create a new
one. You'll end up with a dozen domains with slightly different spellings, plus
frustrated people."* **This is 6.27 for the third time**: the near-miss help was
wired to the two screens where a domain is READ and not to the one where a
duplicate is MADE. **And no layer underneath catches it** — measured, not
assumed: `validate_membrane` checks creator-matches-author and a non-empty
domain, `create_membrane` checks the constitution and the promises, and
**nothing checks uniqueness, exact or near.** Uniqueness is global and DHT
validation resolves deterministic dependencies locally, so two agents founding
one name at once would both validate; it cannot be repaired below the screen.
**The `[D]` left open is the SCOPE of the match** — `nearFoundedDomain` folds
case and whitespace only, so the reported CASE is covered and the reported
WORDING (*"spelt differently"* — a plural, a hyphen, a real typo) is not, and
widening it is irreversible because this protocol has no rename and no merge.

## THE THING TO READ TWICE: A PARTICIPANT IS DISPUTING AN INVARIANT-1 REFUSAL

**WE OFFERED HIM THE WEAKER OBJECT AND HE ARGUED PAST IT FOR THE STRONGER
ONE.** The 2026-11-19 message told him a critique count appears only on his own
claims because a count across everybody's *"would quietly sort a domain into
'much argued about' and 'not argued about' — and people read that as a ranking
of which claims are better"*, and offered a presence boolean instead. His
reply, point 3:

> I understand where you're coming from, but I actually see the benefit of
> seeing what others struggle with. I don't think it has to mean a popularity
> ranking. It shows the areas people feel strongly about, and that's probably
> where people learn the most from a debate.

**HIS COUNTER-ARGUMENT IS NOT THE ONE 6.28 ANTICIPATED.** The row assumed a
reader would misread disputedness as MERIT. He is saying they would read it as
*where the learning is* — a different claim, and one Invariant 1 does not
obviously answer, because Invariant 1 forbids a canonical comparative measure
of WORTH and says nothing about a map of attention. **6.28's `[D]` now has
evidence on both sides and it is still the human's.** And point 8 is the same
question for the THIRD time: *"I can see the claims, but it doesn't show whether
anyone has critiqued them. I really think it should say so, or at least be
clearer."*

**AND 6.28's ROW TEXT IS INCOMPLETE IN A WAY WORTH FIXING.** It says the count
*"appears only on the reader's OWN claims"* for Invariant 1 reasons. Measured
2026-11-23: `renderClaimCard` is shared by both tabs (`main.ts:2247` By Author,
`:2414` Browse), the count needs `myCritiqueCounts.has(key)`, and
`loadMyCritiqueCounts` is called from exactly ONE place — `:4870`, guarded to
the By Author tab viewing yourself. **So on Browse > domain > claims the line
renders for nobody, the reader's own claims included**, and the stated reason is
not the operative one there; the loader simply never runs on that path.
**THAT IS NOT A FREE WIRING FIX, and an earlier reading in this session that
said it was is withdrawn.** The Invariant 1 note at `:2124` turns on the count
having *"no second number beside it to compare it with"* — true of By Author,
which shows one agent, and FALSE of Browse, where the reader's claims are
interleaved with other people's, so a count on some cards and nothing on others
is an asymmetry a reader can read as *uncritiqued*. **It is part of the same
`[D]`, not a bypass around it.**

## 6.23's FOUR FOLLOW-UP QUESTIONS — THREE ANSWERABLE, ONE AMBIGUOUS

> The nickname is much better. Why can't people choose their own, and why do we
> need the numbers behind it? ... Also, a double nickname because of the
> character limit will bite back. I wouldn't keep that feature.

- **"Why can't people choose their own"** — answered by 6.23's own decision and
  it has been said to him once already: a chosen name *"would look like proof of
  who they are while being worth less than the key it sits next to."* **He is
  asking for it as an OPTION alongside the derived one**, which is a narrower
  request than the one refused, and a `[D]`.
- **"Why do we need the numbers behind it"** — the twelve base64 characters are
  the key, which is what a critique is actually held to; the mnemonic is a
  16-bit rendering of the same bytes. `agentLabel` (`:1199`) orders the byline
  by who vouches for it, weakest first.
- **"A double nickname because of the character limit"** — **AMBIGUOUS, AND THE
  TWO READINGS LEAD TO DIFFERENT WORK, SO ASK RATHER THAN GUESS.** Either (a)
  the hyphenated two-word form itself (`brisk-lantern`), or (b) a host name AND
  a mnemonic side by side, which is the three-part byline `agentLabel` builds
  under Moss and which the pilot never sees. **MEASURED: there is no character
  limit anywhere in `key-mnemonic.ts`**, so neither reading is literally what
  the code does. If (a): two words are 16 bits and collide about 1 in 65,536,
  where one 256-word list is 8 bits and collides about 1 in 256
  (`key-mnemonic.ts:65`). **A one-word mnemonic over a pilot of a dozen people
  would collide at a rate a reader would actually meet.**

**THREE RULES THIS SESSION PRODUCED, ALL FROM GETTING THEM WRONG FIRST:**

1. **A SENT RECORD IS EVIDENCE, NOT PROSE.** I swept a correction across four
   `docs/funding/` letters before noticing row 7.21 records them **SENT**. The
   remedy for an error already sent is a note, or a correction to the recipient
   — **never an edit to the file that records it.** All four reverted. Row 7.39.
2. **A SKIPPED JOB IS NOT A PASSING JOB.** `network.yml` skips
   `partition-rejoin` on docs-only diffs, and a rate that divided by all
   completed runs said 11% where the answer is **5 of 27 = 19%**. **A
   denominator you cannot see is the defect.** Row 7.40.
3. **READ THE HARNESS HEADER AND `READINESS` BEFORE OPENING A CI ROW.** Row 7.40
   re-discovered analysis already in `partition-rejoin.mjs`'s
   `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` note and `READINESS` §3 — **four of its five
   proposals were already done or already refused** — and is withdrawn to the
   one part that survives. **1.35's stale-blocker defect pointed the other way.**

**TWO NEW HAND-RUN TOOLS, NEITHER OF WHICH MAY EVER GO IN CI:**

- **`scripts/check-outbound-claims.mjs`** — checks ONE draft you are about to
  send, against what this repo measures. Its inputs are gitignored, so a CI job
  would grep an absent directory and go green. Exits **2 / CANNOT VERIFY**,
  never 0, when it cannot see them. `--list` inventories `docs/funding/` with
  **no extension filter**. Usage:
  `node scripts/check-outbound-claims.mjs <file> [--section N]`.
  **Never run it on a section marked SENT.**
- **`scripts/baseline-history.mjs`** — reads `partition-rejoin`'s Phase 0
  baseline out of every run in a window, prints **ran-vs-skipped above the
  rate**, and **refuses to be a gate** (always exit 0). Takes minutes; it
  downloads each run's log.

**AND A CI FACT WORTH NOT RE-LEARNING: `conductor` and `ui` ARE required checks;
`partition-rejoin` is NOT, nor are the heavy jobs on a docs-only diff.** #549
went `BLOCKED` on only those two, where #545 merged with `partition-rejoin` still
pending. **Stacked PRs need `--delete-branch` on the parent** so children
retarget, and **this repo refuses a merge from a branch behind `main`** — #547
was refused for exactly that, caught only because the merge was verified rather
than assumed.

range and two outcomes so that **no single figure is available to be lifted
out.** The first three instances were claims outrunning their evidence inside
this repo; this one is **the same defect committed by a reader on a correction
written to prevent it.**

**(a) DONE 2026-11-20 — the release-tag trap is now ROW 7.33**, out of this
header and out of reach of the next rewrite. **Measuring it in order to write it
down found that this header's stated REASON for the rule was wrong**: a

stands; its reason did not. **A third instance of the day's lesson.** **(b) DONE 2026-11-20 — THE LINUX APPIMAGE
AUTO-UPDATES, MEASURED END TO END**: a shipped v0.3.6 AppImage found v0.3.7,
took a **2.7 MB block-level delta of a 189 MB artifact (1%)**, installed it and
relaunched itself as 0.3.7, with `INSTALL.md` now saying so as a measurement.
**The install — the step that is impossible on macOS — completed.** Full
account in **row 6.21**, which also carries the two things the run found:
**the consent dialog overstates the download by about seventy times** (it
quotes the whole artifact's size while the updater fetches a delta), **~~a new
`[M]` in the desktop repo~~, taken the same day as (c) below**; and **row 6.10's defect being committed again, in
this very measurement, an hour after it was recorded** — a negative claim built
on an unrun mechanism, caught only because the item said to finish the test.
**(c) DONE 2026-11-20 — THE DIALOG NOW QUOTES A CEILING AND BOTH OUTCOMES,
AND FIXING IT FOUND A SECOND DEFECT OF THE SAME SHAPE. Desktop PR #21.**
`resolveUpdateArtifact` asks the question the updater asks — the arm64 filter
and `findFile` of `AppImageUpdater`/`NsisUpdater`/`MacUpdater` — because
**`files[0]` is not necessarily the file this platform fetches**: measured
against the published v0.3.7 feeds, `latest-mac.yml` lists the x64 zip first,
so **an arm64 Mac was quoted 162MB for the 165MB file it would download**, and
on Linux the AppImage is first only by the order electron-builder happens to
write. **And the real transfer cannot be known before consent at all** — the
delta is resolved inside `DifferentialDownloader` once the download starts — so
the artifact size is a **CEILING**, and the dialog now says *"The whole file is
194MB, and the updater fetches only the parts of it that changed: the transfer
may be a small fraction of that, or all of it if the parts cannot be
compared"*. **THAT IS ROW 7.34's REMEDY APPLIED TO A STRING THE APP ITSELF
SAYS**, on the same day it was learnt from a reader: a ceiling and two
outcomes, with **no single figure available to be lifted out** — because
quoting the measured 2.7MB as what to expect would be the same defect in the
other direction, true of exactly one version pair on one machine. The figure
the dialog could not have is **logged where it first exists**: the first
`download-progress` event carries the delta's grand total during a
differential download and the whole file during a full one, so one line says
which outcome happened and at what size. **`scripts/verify-update-download.mjs`
drives it against the three PUBLISHED v0.3.7 feeds, committed as fixtures and
parsed with the updater's own `js-yaml`** — restating the sizes inline would
agree with a wrong resolver as happily as a right one — **37 checks, in CI,
watched failing three ways.** Returning `files[0]` on darwin is five red **and
the x64 section stays GREEN**: the old code was right on one of the two Macs,
so no measurement taken on x64 could ever have shown it. Inverting the resolved
percentage leaves the full-download case unmoved, **because at 100% the
inversion is the identity** — a case where the two quantities are equal cannot
see which way the division goes. The `--live` leg is deliberately **not** in
CI: a GitHub rate limit there is indistinguishable from a real mismatch. The
item as opened read: **make the dialog quote what the updater actually
resolves** rather than `updateInfo.files[0].size`. The old item read:
**TEST THE LINUX APPIMAGE** — `INSTALL.md:401` claims *"Windows
and Linux auto-update normally"*; the macOS half of that sentence was measured
FALSE on the shipped artifact and **the Linux half has never been tested at
all**. No participant is recorded on Linux, and Jarrett on Omarchy would be the
first. **This machine is Arch x86_64 with `fuse2` installed, so the exact path
he would take can be measured here.** The release ships an AppImage (x86-64)
and a `.deb` (amd64, useless on Arch) and **no Linux arm64 artifact.**

**THE SESSION'S REUSABLE RESULT IS ABOUT FIXTURES. `applet-view.mjs`'s fake
Moss host had `on: () => () => {}`** — a no-op returning a no-op — so **no
signal could ever reach a page under it**, and that was invisible to all 42
earlier checks because nothing had ever asked the host for one. **A FIXTURE
THAT CANNOT DELIVER AN EVENT PROVES THE ABSENCE OF EVERY FEATURE BUILT ON ONE.**
Two more bridge defects followed, both silent: pushing with `page.evaluate`
does not carry a `Uint8Array`, and the shim took `on(cb)` where `holochain.ts`
subscribes `client.on('signal', cb)` — **two arguments** — so it stored the
string `'signal'` as its handler, which is READINESS item 21's defect one
argument over. **What made the third findable was the harness reporting its OWN
socket's signal count**, because from the screen a conductor that never emitted
and a bridge that never delivered are the same empty page. **And separately:
strengthening a check resets its coverage to ZERO** — coverage is keyed by
label, correctly, so the rewritten check in `applet-view` had inherited nothing
and `ledger verify` had been saying so.

**OPERATIONAL TRAPS PAID FOR, so they are not paid for twice.** **One clean
conductor per harness is not advice:** several runs were lost to overlapping
local runs fighting over `vite preview --strictPort`, and to a dirty conductor
carrying an **open signal channel** between runs — `open_signal_channel` writes
a grant that PERSISTS, so an opt-in control passes once and then fails for ever.
CI gets this right by construction (`ui.yml` cleans per step). **`critique_mode`
is an ENUM** — Experiential, Methodological, Logical, Evidential,
Phenomenological — and anything else is a `Deserialize` error, not a default.
**Read a CI log by its STEP-NAME column, not by content**: a grep for
`applet-view` matched a neighbouring step's label and over-counted by one.
**Read a release's assets only AFTER the run completes** — read mid-build they
showed no Intel mac artifacts and looked exactly like a regression.

  DNA exactly). **Three readings:** a node that has restarted and never seen a
  peer declares `null` and stays there; **the arc goes FULL within 5 seconds of
  a peer appearing** (`peers=1 arc=null` at t+0s → `peers=2
  arc=[0,4294967295]` at t+5s); and it **stays full after the peer leaves**.
  **So `null` is not "currently alone", it is "freshly restarted and nobody has
  arrived yet"** — a window in which nothing can be published anyway, because
  publishing requires being on the network, which is the event that closes it.
  **The disclosure sent to participants does not need retracting**, but
  *"continuously"* overstates the mechanism: the arc is established on contact,
  not held in advance. **Still unmeasured:** whether a claim published inside
  those five seconds is captured — that needs a real publish, and the probe
  deliberately made no zome call (zero app entries, invisible in the app).
  **And 5s is the sampling resolution, not a latency.**
- **THAT NARROWER QUESTION IS NOW ANSWERED TOO, AND IT FOUND A REAL EXPOSURE.**
  Measured on a **throwaway seed, deliberately not the pilot DHT** — a Claim is
  permanent, so a test claim on the live network would be junk no participant
  could remove. Two nodes, verdicts always taken with the publisher's process
  **dead**, so a read proves the archive STORED it; a control confirms that
  distinction is real. **Three arms:** publisher stayed ~57s → **CAPTURED**,
  including a claim committed when both nodes read `peers=1, arc=null`;
  publisher left **64ms** after committing → **NOT captured**; publisher stayed
  **10 seconds** → **STILL NOT captured**, and the archive never made contact at
  all. **So a null arc at publication does NOT lose a claim — the publisher's
  DWELL TIME does.** Discovery is the slow, variable step: ~2.5s in one run, not
  yet happened after 10s in another, so **the threshold is unbounded on this
  evidence (>10s, ≤57s) and the variability is the finding, not the number.**
  **THIS IS 7.29's ORIGINAL PROBLEM, ONLY PARTLY SOLVED:** the row exists

costs nothing. **The moment it is sent, its *"in the next build"* line is a

rather than a comment. That is half the reason the release hold was lifted the

**ROW 7.30 IS BUILT (2026-11-18): THE CHECK THAT COULD NOT FAIL NOW CAN.** The
seed carries the desktop's MINOR version, so a `0.4.0` release strands an
always-on node on an abandoned DHT **in perfect health**. The instrument meant
to catch that compared two pastes — `SEED` hand-written and `RELEASED_DNA`
hand-copied, against a `dna` read off the already-installed cell — **both sides
frozen at install time, so once it had printed YES it could not print anything
else.** Now one side is **derived from the live release** (unauthenticated
`fetch` of `releases/latest` → `tag_name` → a port of the desktop's own
`breakingVersion` → the seed string) and the other is **read off the running
conductor**: `admin.getDnaDefinition(cellId)` returns `modifiers.network_seed`,
so the seed the node is actually installed under can simply be read back — the
row expected to have to record it at install time or reinstall into a throwaway
conductor, and neither was needed. **The expected side moves the day a release
lands; the installed side cannot move without a reinstall. That asymmetry is
the whole check.** **Measured against the live `v0.3.6`: YES — the same word as
before, now for a reason — and WATCHED FAILING in a harness kept beside it,
9 modes, 9 as expected.** `v0.4.0`/`v1.0.0`/`v0.4.0-alpha.2` → **NO — WRONG
NETWORK**, exit 2; an unreadable tag, a `tag_name`-less 200, a 404 and a dead
network → **CANNOT VERIFY**, exit 1, **with no pasted fallback by design**; and
**two controls stay YES — the live tag, and `v0.3.9`, because a PATCH must keep
the seed.** The port was **differentially tested** against the real
`breakingVersion` over 27 strings (two divergences, both semver being more
tolerant; one closed, one left divergent because the stricter side fails loud).
**One thing that looks derivable and is NOT: the product name** —
electron-builder sanitises the space out of every release asset while the seed
keeps it, so a filename-derived name would be confidently wrong. **The sub-`[D]`
is unchanged and now has a second limb: the harness proving the check can fail
is outside this repository too**, so what cannot be sent to a second operator is
the check AND its evidence.

**AND THE macOS UPDATE IS MEASURED (2026-11-18): IT IS NOT FIXABLE BY US, AND
THE RELEASE HOLD'S OWN UNBLOCKING CONDITION IS THEREFORE INDEFINITE.** This was
asked because the block below holds 6.26 and 6.27 back on the grounds that *a
release carrying the macOS update fix would be the first that can be taken
automatically*. **There is no such fix to carry.** Measured on the shipped
artifact rather than read off `macOSCodeSigning: false` — by HTTP range requests
against `Epistemic-Resonance-0.3.6-arm64-mac.zip`, no mac needed — the bundle's
712 entries contain **zero `_CodeSignature/CodeResources` and zero provisioning
profile**, and the main executable's embedded signature is **ad-hoc, linker-
signed, identifier `'Electron'`, flags `0x20002`**: upstream Electron's own
linker signature, never re-signed for this app. **Squirrel.Mac accepts an update
only if the new bundle satisfies the RUNNING app's designated requirement, and
an ad-hoc signature's requirement is a bare `cdhash` — the hash of that exact
binary.** No successor build can satisfy it, by construction. **Two routes
exist: `[H]` an Apple Developer ID — the workflow's signing branch and its six
secrets are already written, so it is a one-line flip plus an account that costs
money yearly — or `[M]` a macOS updater that does not use Squirrel.Mac, which is
a project and not a patch.** **So "wait for the macOS fix" is an indefinite hold,
and that re-prices holding two built UI fixes behind it. The release call stays
the human's.** **AND THE CHEAP THING NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR:** v0.3.5 wired
`autoUpdater.logger` to the profile log that *Export logs* ships, so **the next
offered update writes the Squirrel.Mac error string into a file the reporter can
send with one button** — the *refusal* measurement 6.21 has been missing, one
ask away rather than one build away. **AND A CAUTION THE SAME WORK TURNED UP:
v0.3.5's download counts show FIVE unrelated assets each one above the floor**
(arm64 `.dmg`, x64 `.dmg`, x64 `mac.zip`, `setup.exe`, `AppImage`) — **a person
takes one artifact for one platform; one-of-each across five is a scraper.** The
counts cannot say which version a given person was running, and #496's
within-one-release reading survives only because it never claimed that.

| PR | row | what it was |
|---|---|---|
| **#478** `eee93ca` | **1.21** | the ledger resolved a label by reading ONE quoted literal; this suite writes any label longer than a line as literals joined by `+` — **60 call sites truncated, each in the denominator under a label it does not have** |
| **#479** `8c904bd`, `379d00f` | **6.18 CLOSED** | the critique TREE labelled nodes by mode alone — a byline on every node, the parent carried so the tree can say what the flat list cannot, **and the fixture was the real work** |

| **#482** `6edbad8`, `7a3a55d` | **the `partition-rejoin` rate** | *"roughly a quarter"* was borrowed from README's 48-trial gossip census: **measured 13.8%** over the job's complete history, and an assertion failure rather than a setup one in 56 of 63 — **then a bimodal distribution I had drafted on top was PULLED**, because the harness forbids inferring one from 5s lattice samples |

**EVERY FINDING THIS SESSION IS THE SAME SPECIES: A TOOL OR A CHECK THAT WAS
RIGHT BY COINCIDENCE.**

1. **A label is an EXPRESSION, not a literal.** Taken whole now, to the comma
   found by bracket depth. All 15 of the ledger's unresolved reds resolved:
   **51 → 63 distinct call sites**, `reading-a-thread` 7 → 19. The store
   changed by **0 labels and 15 kinds** — a label is the evidence, a resolution
   is derived from current source, which is why line numbers were already
   repaired there. And `verify` had only ever re-resolved reds ALREADY counted
   as coverage, so a record could never move TOWARDS coverage however much the
   resolver improved.
2. **`\bcheck\(` MATCHES PLAYWRIGHT'S `.check()`** — a word boundary matches
   after a dot, so ticking a checkbox counted as an assertion. The suite's
   figure is **1,761, not 1,763**, and the arithmetic closes exactly.
3. **AN INJECTION PASSED FIRST TIME AND THE CHECK WAS THE PROBLEM.** Restoring
   `\bcheck\(` reddened nothing, because `labelExpr` rejects a no-argument
   `.check()` before the lookbehind is ever consulted. Rewritten around a
   DOTTED call with label-shaped arguments — what the lookbehind actually does
   — it reds 2. **A guard whose removal costs nothing is the same as no guard.**
4. **THE DETERMINISTIC GATE EARNED ITSELF ON THE SPOT, MEASURED.** `verify`
   can only exercise the label shapes some injection happened to redden, which
   is a coincidence of history. Removing the computed-label guard reds two
   `selftest` cases and leaves **`verify` entirely green**, because the suite's
   one computed label sits in a harness with no records.
5. **THEN THE TOOL FOUND ITS OWN HOLE BY BEING USED: the first injection
   recorded after the fix resolved ZERO of its five reds.** `graph-ui`'s new
   labels spell their dashes as `—`, and `unescape` decoded only
   `\' \" \` \\ \n \t`, so the pattern demanded six characters where the run
   printed one. **9 of 1,773 sites carry such an escape and all but two are in
   that one harness** — a hole as old as the file, invisible until the tool was
   pointed at new data. Full JS-string decoder now, four `selftest` cases
   watched failing. **The harness was NOT restyled to suit the tool**, so the
   path stays exercised by real records.
6. **AND THE COUNTS #478 WROTE INTO ITS OWN HEADER WENT STALE INSIDE THE SAME
   BRANCH**, because `graph-ui` gained 12 checks three commits later. The
   second-place-a-number-is-written defect, committed inside the session that
   names it — re-measured, and **nothing downstream is keyed to those figures
   deliberately**: `selftest` asserts the SHAPES and `verify` re-resolves
   against the source as it is.

**6.18 — THE FIXTURE WAS THE WORK, AND THE ROW HAD ALREADY MEASURED WHY.**
`graph-ui.mjs` wrote every entry as `author: me`, so the impostor defect the
row names — a byline from `myAgentPubKey`, who is LOOKING, rather than
`entry.author`, who WROTE — was **indistinguishable from correct behaviour**:
every byline right by coincidence, exactly as in the list view, where the row
records the same injection turning *only 2 red*. With a second agent installed
(setup refusal if both slots resolve to one key; cross-author readiness waits
that refuse rather than seed a wrong-shaped tree) **the same injection turns
5**. `flattenPreOrder` carries each node's parent, so the tree marks *the
critique's author* — a relation that cannot exist at depth 1, the only depth
the flat list reaches. The byline is in the SVG `aria-label` as well as the
text peer, the accessible label being the whole reason this view is SVG.

**AND A SECOND FINDING IN THE HARNESS: `graph-ui`'s §7b.2 DECLARING-WAIT PROBE
COULD NEVER HAVE RUN.** The diagnostic that turns a bare timeout into an answer
called a `call` declared `const` INSIDE `seed()`, from `main()`, **after `main`
had closed the only socket it could have used** — a `ReferenceError` in place
of the explanation, on the one path where it runs. Invisible on every green
run; the same species as 1.29's harnesses that could only report a failure.
Keeping the socket open is safe for 1.29's own measured reason: this file ends
on `process.exit(failures === 0 ? 0 : 1)`.

**THE LEDGER NOW READS 71 of 1,773 call sites (4.0%), four harnesses, 19 runs**
— 92 resolved reds reaching 71 distinct sites, so a sum would overstate by 30%.
Coverage grows only as injections are run and recorded, which is the answer
1.21 asked for rather than a shortcut around it.

**WHAT IS LEFT, AND IT IS ONE DECISION AND THREE THINGS WAITING ON OTHER
PEOPLE.**

- **6.15's `[D]` — HELD 2026-11-14 by the human. AND ITS STATED REASON TURNED
  OUT TO BE FALSE, WHICH IS THIS SESSION'S LAST AND WORST FINDING.** Every
  version of this row said push is *expensive in the protocol* because
  `send_peer_signal` writes a permanent CapGrant per call on the SENDER's
  chain. **That sentence is `peer-signal.mjs`'s NEGATIVE EVIDENCE entry `CH` —
  a defect injected on 2026-11-01 and watched going 2 red — quoted as though it
  were the measurement.** The harness asserts the opposite (*the SENDER's chain
  did not grow across 5 signals*), and the source agrees: `send_peer_signal`
  writes nothing, `open_signal_channel` is idempotent. **One entry per agent
  once, zero per signal for ever. Push is not the expensive branch; nothing
  is.** It propagated into six places in this file, five memory checkpoints and
  one harness comment, and **`scripts/live-verify/README.md` had it right the
  whole time** — the correct reading was one file away. What survives is the
  Invariant 1 question alone, plus two real constraints: delivery can never be
  confirmed (`delivery_confirmed: false` by construction), and push reaches
  only an agent that has opened a channel. **And a smaller option nobody
  priced:** `create_critique` emits NOTHING while `create_claim` emits
  `NewClaim`.

**6.25 — WHAT THE AUDIT ACTUALLY FOUND WAS NOT CACHING.** Four per-claim
stores were simply missing from a clear block that explains itself, and each is
now cleared **with its open-set**, because an open panel whose store has gone
renders its absent state — and after a deliberate clear all four of those are a
lie. The family behind it was different: **four surfaces where a read that
failed rendered as something else** (resonance wrote `[]`, which renders as a
legitimate empty answer with an instruction that cannot help; a failed
constitution read said *"Loading…"* for ever; `loadCritiqueTree` had **no catch
at all** and is `await`ed from an `onclick`; and the period sample, the only one
that already said it had failed, was the only one whose retry its own guard
blocked). Its second finding is about the suite rather than the app: **three
harnesses were waiting only for the good outcome**, so a conductor that would
not answer made them stop reporting instead of report.

**1.29 — THE TWO THINGS WORTH CARRYING FORWARD.** First, the row's own count
was wrong, and instructively: `transitive-gossip`'s reason for being out was
real, measured and written down — **in `network.yml`'s header, which is not
where a reader of the index looks.** Two of the ten were already explained;
what they lacked was the explanation in the row the index points at. Second,
**`index-lens` and `superior-egg` called `process.exit` ONLY in their failure
branch**, and an open `@holochain/client` websocket is a ref'd handle nothing
in this suite closes — so each printed ALL CHECKS PASSED and then hung until a
`timeout` killed it. **A harness that can only report a failure reads in CI as
a job timeout rather than a red check**, which is much of why they were never
switched on: the defect is visible the moment somebody runs one, and nobody
had. `registry-install` now runs **weekly on a schedule and is never a required
context** — the first alert here that will not correspond to any diff, so a red
there means something a stranger can install today is broken while this tree is
fine, and the fix is publishing.

**6.23 — THE DECISION, AND WHY IT IS REVERSIBLE RATHER THAN SETTLED.** Hold:
nothing is minted from the key yet. The same-author marker answers the question
*within* a panel by a sentence; the reporter has the question; and a colour or
identicon **needs a text alternative** for a screen reader and a colour-blind
reader — a standard this repository already holds, since the critique graph is
SVG precisely so every node carries an accessible label — **so the alternative
would be doing the distinguishing work and the visual would be decoration on a
mnemonic rather than a substitute for one.** A two-word mnemonic is the option
to take if the hold is reversed. And the mechanical half found while deciding:
**every agent key's base64 begins `hCAk`**, measured on eight real keys, so
`slice(0, 12)` spent a third of every byline on a constant **at the front,
where the eye lands** — two different people's bylines were identical for four
characters before they differed.

**THE STANDING LESSONS THIS SESSION ADDED, all of the same species: a check
that passes for a reason nobody chose.**

1. **A 1-in-64 assertion was available and was refused.** *"Two bylines differ
   in their first character"* is true of 6.23's fix and also true of the old
   code one time in sixty-four. Both shipped assertions hold for every possible
   pair of keys instead. The session before this one spent real time on a
   1-in-64 regex defect; the lesson took.
2. **Making a wait tolerant can create a vacuous green on the spot.** Three
   harnesses were changed to admit either the answer or a failure surface, and
   a negative check immediately started passing against an empty string. Anchor
   every negative to the text existing.
3. **A shared FUNCTION couples like a shared class.** `short()` has 17 call
   sites; changing it needed the same sweep `.critique-item` needed, and found
   exactly one real coupling among a dozen look-alikes.
4. **The second place a number is written is the one that rots.** The suite
   table in `scripts/live-verify/README.md` was ungated because the gate
   matches `README.md`'s phrasing — ``​`<path>`, N checks`` ``, **path
   first** — and the table writes `**N checks**`. `reading-a-thread`'s own row
   said **30 while it ran 64**, through four sessions that each ran that
   harness and read that row. Now gated. **And of the six disagreements, four
   were the document being wrong and three were the CALL-SITE MEASURE being
   wrong** — loop-driven harnesses whose runs were confirmed by counting PASS
   lines rather than believed.
5. **Stacking a PR works and costs a second CI cycle.** #475 was opened with
   `--base` the lower branch, so its diff was one row, and GitHub retargeted it
   to `main` by itself when #474 merged — then branch protection required
   `main` merged INTO it, which was content-neutral and restarted all five
   workflows.

### 6.17 IS WORTH READING FOR HOW IT WAS MISSED, NOT FOR WHAT IT WAS

**THE HARNESS CERTIFIED THE DEFECT FOR AS LONG AS IT EXISTED.**
`worldline-ui.mjs` §4 asserted ``sample_period opens the window's real
records`` with the predicate ``/\d+ record/.test(sampleText)`` — a label
naming the missing feature, over a test that only required a digit beside the
word *record*. It was green on a runner throughout. **That is the
mislabelled-check class in its worst costume, and it is why this reached a
participant instead of CI.** When a check's label describes a capability,
assert the capability.

**A SECOND DEFECT CAME FROM READING THE OUTPUT AFTER IT EXITED 0.** A
`CapGrant` sits on every chain that has authorized signing credentials, and its
`entry_type` is the plain STRING `"CapGrant"` rather than an `{App:…}` object.
The first build assumed the object, so the first row of the first window told
the agent their own history could not be read — and the new check passed it,
because *"Entry type (unreadable)"* is a non-empty string. Machinery is now
named in plain words and still shown: Invariant 1 keeps raw history open, and
hiding rows from an agent's view of their own chain would be this UI deciding
what their history contains.

**NEW GATE: `scripts/check-entry-index-map.mjs`.** The client turns an action's
`entry_index` into a word a reader sees, and that index is POSITION-INDEXED
into the integrity zome's `EntryTypes` — the hazard `LinkTypes`' own comment
forbids, which nothing enforced for entry types and nothing connected to the
TypeScript map. Watched failing: one variant inserted mid-enum mislabels **ten**
indices, exit 1.

**AND THE COUNTER READS COMMENTS AS CODE.** `check-harness-counts.mjs` matches
a labelled call by regex, so a commented-out example counts as a call site —
then the comment written to explain *that* contained one and was counted
itself. Stated count, call sites and executed checks now all agree at 25. Do
not put the literal call shape in a comment.

### SIX DECISIONS WAITING ON THE AUTHOR, AND NONE OF THEM IS WORK

- **1.29** — per harness: register it, or declare it manual with the reason.
- **6.14's validation gap.** `validate_retraction` **permits a retraction
  naming itself as its own replacement** — it checks only that a named
  `replacement_claim` exists, not that it differs from the target. **MEASURED,
  not inferred:** removing the client guard turned 2 checks red and the second
  was *"nothing permanent was written"*, so the conductor accepted the loop
  onto a real chain. Closing it is an integrity change and a `protocol_version`
  bump.
- **3.2's asymmetry.** `get_standing` takes the strict reading (`None => true`,
  nothing dies); `get_effective_conductance` applies **thirty days regardless**.
  SPEC §7's own note already admits that half survives. Either conductance
  takes the strict reading — changing what every existing conductance read
  returns, explicitly not taken on 2026-10-19 — or the asymmetry is recorded
  as deliberate.
- **6.17's `[D]` — should `sample_period` honour `domain_tag`?** Measured: it
  filters the chain on TIME ALONE, so two boundaries sharing a window return
  byte-identical results matching neither's `entry_count`. That is arguably
  the point — the chain's raw answer is what makes it a CHECK on the trace
  rather than a repeat of it — so the coordinator was left alone and the
  window explains the difference on screen. Changing it needs no DNA bump,
  which makes it cheap enough to be worth deciding deliberately rather than
  by default.
- **6.19's `[D]` — where does a reply to a critique appear?** The write is
  three fields and the read already exists (`get_critiques_for` takes any
  hash; `loadCritiqueTree` already walks it to depth 3). What needs deciding
  is the flat list's shape: it is in the order the DHT returned it and a
  nested reply has no place in that order, so indenting it is a client
  deciding a thread's structure. **6.14's `[D]` on entry order is the same
  question one row over**, and this one is owed rather than merely open — a
  reply sent 2026-11-08 told the pilot's first publisher that an author can
  defend by critiquing a critique, which is true of the protocol and not of
  the app.
- **7.18 / 7.19** — both written; publishing is a decision.

### THREE OPERATIONAL TRAPS THIS SESSION PAID FOR

- **`scripts/check-*.mjs` IS NOT THE GATE SET, and it cost a red CI run.** The
  validation corpus stores the **line number** of every `Invalid` site, so a
  twelve-line comment in the integrity zome shifted **97** of them and
  reddened `no-conductor` for no behavioural reason. **Any edit to
  `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs` — comments included — needs
  `node scripts/validation-corpus/enumerate.mjs` re-run.** The full
  no-conductor node list is in the session memory note; it is fourteen scripts,
  not the `check-*` glob.
- **`partition-rejoin` fails in CI 13.8% of the time — MEASURED 2026-11-14,
  and both halves of what stood here were wrong.** ~~It fails roughly a quarter
  of the time and presents as a SETUP failure rather than an assertion
  failure; README already records non-crossings at 27%.~~ **THE RATE WAS
  BORROWED FROM A DIFFERENT MEASUREMENT.** README's 27% is a four-batch census
  of 48 gossip trials with 13 non-crossings; it was never a figure about this
  job, and the sentence that cited it said so in its own next clause. **MEASURED
  OVER THE JOB'S COMPLETE LIFETIME** — every `partition-rejoin` attempt since
  `7be3eeb` created it on 2026-10-13, through 2026-11-14: **592 job rows, of
  which 95 were skipped (docs-only diffs never run it) and 39 cancelled by
  concurrency. Of the 458 attempts that actually ran the harness, 63 failed =
  13.8%**, about half the figure that was written down. **AND IT IS AN
  ASSERTION FAILURE, NOT A SETUP FAILURE, IN 56 OF THE 63:** the precondition
  `the baseline is fast enough to measure divergence inside the job budget
  (<= 60s)` goes red and the harness **reports normally**. Only **7** are the
  setup shape — the job dies with no `FAIL:` line and no baseline printed,
  ending in orphan-process termination, which is the same cause at its
  extreme. **THE MAGNITUDE IS THE REAL FINDING AND IT ANSWERS `network.yml`'s
  OWN OPEN QUESTION.** That file says the harness *"does NOT tell you whether a
  slow baseline is intermittent or routine"* and holds the retry-the-baseline
  change until somebody measures it. Measured: **86% of attempts cross
  inside 60s by definition of passing**, so propagation is **intermittently
  slow, not routinely minutes-slow** — which is the condition that file names
  as making a retry worth its job time rather than wasted. **THE MAGNITUDE, STATED AS THE INSTRUMENT PERMITS AND NO FURTHER.** `partition-rejoin.mjs:605-617` forbids inferring a distribution from these figures: the baseline is a **5s-lattice sample**, so two runs agreeing to 0.1s means two crossings landed in one window — and that comment exists because **READINESS §3 already made this error**, reading a cluster *"inside ±5s of ~420s"* as a finding about gossip when it is the sampler's own step size. So no median and no percentile is quoted here, and the 28-of-56 pile-up in the 420-479s bucket is **named as the documented artifact rather than a cluster**. **WHAT THE SAMPLES DO SHOW, as observed extremes rather than a shape: across all 458 attempts no baseline has ever been sampled between 35.1s and 75.2s.** The slowest passing sample is 35.1s (395 runs); the fastest failing one is 75.2s (56 runs). Quantised to the 5s step that is a true separation of roughly 30s to 75s. **The practical consequence is the only one claimed: the 60s bound has never come near a passing run** — it sits about 25s above the slowest pass ever sampled and 15s below the fastest failure, in a range nothing has occupied. It is not a tight threshold catching marginal runs. Confirm the diff cannot reach
  `partition-rejoin.mjs` or `network.sh`, re-run once, and treat a second
  failure as signal — **though two consecutive reds were observed on #481,
  whose diff was documentation plus one comment, so a second red is not by
  itself proof the diff is implicated.** Note
  `gh pr checks` reports a check's **last concluded** status, so it keeps
  showing `fail` while a re-run is in flight — trust
  `gh run view <id> --json status,conclusion`. The *never re-run `--failed`*
  rule is **release-scoped**, not general.
- **`pkill -f "holochain --"` KILLS THE ALWAYS-ON PILOT NODE.** Done on
  2026-11-10 while clearing an orphaned sandbox holding port 8889; the sandbox
  conductor and the pilot node are the same binary. systemd restarted it
  (`NRestarts=1`) and it was verified healthy, but the orphan only existed
  because `sandbox.sh clean` and `start` were fired in overlapping background
  calls. Run them sequentially, wait for *are up*, and kill by the PID holding
  the port — never by process name.

### TWO MORE TRAPS, BOTH OF WHICH PRODUCED A GREEN THAT MEANT NOTHING

- **`npm run build` IS `tsc && vite build`, AND `noUnusedLocals` IS ON.** An
  injection that leaves a local unused fails the typecheck, never reaches
  `vite build`, and leaves the PREVIOUS bundle in `dist/` for the harness to
  drive. Injection G of `reading-a-thread.mjs` reported ALL CHECKS PASSED
  **twice** that way — once on `cap`, then again on `PROSE_BOX_CAP` after
  `cap` was deleted — and the only tell was a 200-line draft reporting the
  cap's exact value. **Check for `built in` before trusting a run**, and
  prefer an injection that neutralises a value over one that removes it.
- **A FIXTURE THAT SPENDS CRITIQUES HAS A RUNS-PER-HOUR CEILING.** The
  integrity zome allows 20 critiques per agent per 3600s;
  `reading-a-thread.mjs` spends 2 of the stranger's per run, so about **ten
  runs against one sandbox**. Two injections first reported 0 red because the
  conductor refused the SETUP — the harness printed the zome's own refusal
  and exited non-zero, which is the right failure, and both were re-run
  against a clean sandbox for new agent keys.

### THE STANDING LESSON HELD, AND THREE FINDINGS CAME FROM BEING WRONG

For any check of the form *X is NOT shown*, ask what makes it capable of
failing and put the control that answers it FIRST. This session: a harness
caught **6.13's own defect in brand-new code** (an unasked read rendered as
*"nothing came back"*, visible only to a MutationObserver — every check that
READ the panel was green); a control **overturned a written conclusion about
`get_standing`** before it became a result; and a self-review found **a check
of mine whose cheaper disjunct could not fail** (`|| back.length >= 2`). An
injection that PASSES is a finding about the assertion, not a gap — #455's
cross-group injection stayed green because the refusal has two independent
layers.

**HOW TO READ A SOURCE CHAIN WITHOUT WRITING TO IT, because the obvious route
is a write.** `get_agent_constitution` is a zome call and needs signing
credentials, and authorizing those **commits a capability grant** to a chain
where nothing can ever be deleted. Use `admin.dumpFullState` instead — purely
read-only. Admin ws is `127.0.0.1:33135` origin `kangaroo`, app ws `39001`
origin `webhapp://webhappwindow`; the client is in
`mobile-ui/node_modules/@holochain/client`. Entries are msgpack; Constitution
is `entry_index` 6, Membrane 5, Claim 1.

**THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY VERIFYING THE RELEASE, NONE OF WHICH ANY GREEN JOB
WOULD HAVE SHOWN** — the pattern this file keeps recording, holding again:

- **`latest-mac.yml` advertised ONE architecture on every release since
  `v0.1.0`.** `scripts/merge-mac-yamls.mjs` had `holochain/launcher`
  hardcoded, inherited from the template, so it asked the Launcher whether it
  had a `v0.3.1`, logged `No release found. Skipping merge` and exited 0.
  Harmless only because every prior release was a MINOR, which
  electron-updater refuses to offer across — **`v0.3.1` is the first
  auto-update-eligible release this app has cut**, so it would have handed an
  Apple Silicon install the Intel build. Fixed, with a stale-snapshot race in
  the same script that meant the merge only ever worked because the two mac
  jobs happened to run minutes apart.
- **Re-releasing an existing tag no longer leaves assets stale — on macOS it
  FAILS.** electron-builder now overwrites, but `productName` contains a SPACE
  and GitHub stores the blockmap with a DOT, so its delete misses and the
  upload 422s. Fix: delete the two `*.zip.blockmap` assets and re-run the two
  mac jobs. **And every re-push replaces every artifact — the builds are not
  reproducible, so any verification done before a re-push is void.**
- **`INSTALL.md` and the invitation §1 still said `v0.3.0` — the THIRD
  occurrence of a failure that file already documented twice.** It mattered:
  §1 asks every invitee for a published `Constitution`, and on `v0.3.0` that
  ask is **unexecutable**, because before 6.7 the only route to
  `publish_constitution` was founding your own domain. **Cutting a release is
  not finished until those two files name the new version.**

`docs/funding/holo-ventures-approach.md` — an unsent approach to a named
funder — was swept into a commit by a blanket `git add -A` meant for two other
files, and reached public `main` for about thirty minutes before #381 removed

force-push of a public repository. **The rule: never `git add -A` in a
repository holding untracked private drafts**, which this one now does.
