done. **The run of five is the thing to notice**: 7.18 opened 7.19, 7.17
opened 6.6, 6.6 fixed 1.16 and opened 1.17, and 1.17's fix added the ninth
checker. Bounded work here is found by finishing something, not by scanning this
column. `[M]` rows that exist but are blocked:
4.3 (on 3.4), 5.2 (on a decision), 5.3/5.4/7.5 (on the bump), 4.5 (held).
**Do not go hunting for bounded work — but do read paragraph 2 before
concluding there is none.**

**2. "`[M]` IS EMPTY" WAS SAID THIS MORNING AND WAS WRONG, THREE TIMES OVER —
and the third was found by acting on this very paragraph.** Item **1.11**
(2026-10-25, after the evening checkpoint): four counted claims about link
types, wrong in BOTH directions at once. SPEC §5.19 overstated how much it
covered (*"eighteen ... every link type anything writes"*, when 27 are written),
and the corpus README understated what the protocol validates, listing
`ClaimToBridgeRecord` as having no validation branch when it has seven rules
and a case. **The second was written by the sweep that was fixing the first
kind of rot, the same day** — it read the inventory's denominator as a fact
about the zome. Now gated as a SET EQUALITY rather than a number.
**So paragraph 4's instruction has a corollary: read counts from the commands,
and check WHICH command — a tool's denominator is a claim about the tool.**

**It is the sentence this file is most likely to be wrong about.** A closed row
is not a closed claim. What it missed on 2026-10-25: four documents still
telling readers the published build was `v0.2.0` (#347); `check-packages.mjs`
wired to no workflow at all (#352, item 1.9); and ten validation rules the
corpus demanded nothing of (#353/#354, item 1.10). **None of those was a row.**
So after anything ships, grep for the fact it falsified before deciding the
list is empty.

**3. TWO ROWS UNDERSTATED THEIR OWN SURFACE IN ONE DAY, and both times
BUILDING the thing is what found out.** 4.5 was costed at one entry type and
one validation rule; discovery needs a link type too, so it is nine validation
sites. 1.9 said wiring a checker in "is the whole change"; it also needs both
packages' dependency trees. **The standing instruction for whoever assembles
the bump: re-price 4.1, 4.2 and 4.3 on the question that caught 4.5 — *what
indexes it?***

**4. COUNTED CLAIMS ROTTED FASTER THAN THEY WERE WRITTEN.** The drift-checker
count moved three times on 2026-10-25 (six of seven wired, then seven, then
eight) and each move falsified prose written an hour earlier. The corpus
README's live figure was **72 checks behind**. The zome's link-type count was
written as 32 and is 31. **Read counts from the commands, not from prose —
`ls scripts/check-*.mjs`, `grep -c 'run: node scripts/check-'
.github/workflows/live-verify.yml`, and the conductor job's own output for
anything the corpus prints.**

**WHAT CHANGED STRUCTURALLY, so it is not re-derived.** A docs-only pull
request now skips `conductor`, `network`, `ui` and `zomes` and costs about two
minutes instead of fifteen — `scripts/ci-scope.mjs`, gated by
`check-ci-scope.mjs`, with `live-verify` deliberately ungated because every
checker that reads a document runs there. **Nine drift checkers, all nine in
CI** — the ninth, `check-time-units.mjs`, added 2026-10-26 night for 1.17, and
the first that gates a CONVENTION rather than a count, a document or a rule.** Corpus coverage is now demanded rather than hoped for:
`check-corpus-coverage.mjs` fails on any type with assertable rules that is
neither covered nor declared. Corpus totals: **332 on `main`, 346 on #350**.

~~**START HERE: `[M]` IS EMPTY.**~~ **IT WASN'T, AND THE WAY IT WASN'T IS THE
POINT.** Every *row* the model could take unblocked was indeed struck — 1.7,
1.8, 5.1, 7.9, 7.10, 7.11, 7.12 — and that is still true. But **the release
itself created bounded work that no row described**: four documents were left
telling readers that the published build was `v0.2.0` at `protocol_version: 9`,
which stopped being true the moment `v0.3.0` shipped. `INSTALL.md`'s top notice
— the first thing an invitee reads, and the file #344 was already sent to fix
once — was pointing at the superseded release; `README`'s installing section
said the published release matched this tree *"as of 2026-10-12"*;
`docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §4 still listed the version gap as *"a live
inconsistency to resolve"*; and `docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md` told Luck Light
that a trial today runs two forks behind. Swept 2026-10-25.

**So the standing instruction is stronger than "check the list".** A closed
row is not a closed claim: the list tracks work, and the prose that quotes the
work's result rots silently because nothing gates it. After anything ships,
grep for the fact it falsified — here, `v0.2.0`, `protocol_version: 9` and
*"two forks behind"* — before concluding there is nothing bounded to do.
`READINESS.md` was swept on the day and is the model for what that looks like.

**THE ONE THING THAT MOVED FURTHEST: `v0.3.0` SHIPPED AT `protocol_version:
11`** (2026-10-24, tag pinned to `9372f558`, 15 assets, Latest). Until then an
invitee would have joined a network two forks behind the protocol this
repository describes, which made release step 4 fiction whatever else was
decided. **So 7.4 is now blocked on 1.4 alone** — its release half closed with
the release, its decision half with 7.1, and 7.3 never gated it. One Setup
click per profile and a second machine, both `[H]`.

**AND 7.1 IS DECIDED: vouch for honesty, not for safety.** The commitment is
that these documents say accurately what the protocol does and does not do —
explicitly not that it is adversary-resistant, which `SECURITY.md` contradicts
in writing. It obliges keeping them true, and #344 was the first payment:
`INSTALL.md` was telling the ten people about to be invited that cross-network
discovery was untested, three days after it was measured.

~~**THE DECISION THAT GATES THE MOST, and it is not on any row above:** whether
the integrity bump goes **before** inviting ten people, **after**, or
**never**~~ — **TAKEN 2026-10-25: INVITE FIRST, BUMP SECOND.** The ten are
invited onto `protocol_version: 11` and told before they write that what they
publish does not survive the next integrity change.

**`v0.3.0` is what decided it.** While the published build was two forks
behind, step 4 was blocked whatever anybody decided, so the bump could ride a
delay that already existed — that was the only cheap argument for *bump first*,
and the release closed it. *Bump first* now means the invitation waits on three
answers nobody here controls: Andras's three for 4.1, pricing 3.5 for 4.2,
settling 3.4 for 4.3.

**THE OBLIGATION IS THE DECISION, not a courtesy attached to it.** Under
Invariant 6 nothing can be deleted afterwards, so knowing in advance is a
tester's only protection, and 7.1's commitment is to honesty rather than to
safety. `INSTALL.md` now says a further break is **planned rather than
hypothetical** — that sentence is the decision being kept.

**AND 4.5 IS WRITTEN AND HELD (PR #350, draft).** It was the one candidate
ready, and writing it found that `docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2 had
**understated its own integrity surface**: discovery needs a link type too,
because the tagged-`Claim` witness it replaces was findable for free by riding
the domain index and a typed entry cannot. Nine validation sites, not one. The
other three candidates should be re-priced on the same question — *what
indexes it?*

**Research opened rather than closed:** 3.7 states the sybil question once,
with its boundary written down — *the cheapest promise structure that makes
sybil cost high enough without creating a centre* — and fences off the version
that cannot be answered, since validation can no more observe a person than it
can read a clock. Candidate (1), attestation capacity, is already shipped.

**THE COLD-READ INSTRUCTION BELOW EARNED ITS KEEP ON THE FIRST TRY.** #333 was
found open because a required check had gone red — `ui`, `taxonomy-ui` §5 —
not because the queue had stalled. Check that first, every time. And note what
the red turned out to be worth: chasing it produced item 1.7 and overturned a
documented cause (see the contention entry below).

**Each of the five was branched before the one ahead of it landed**, so
`strict: true` left each `BEHIND` in turn and each was updated from `main`
rather than moving the rule. Five sequential ~20-minute cycles for five
documentation changes is the concrete cost of that rule, and it is the evidence
the `[D]` below is asking for.

Before those two this section said **nothing**, and had listed five items on
2026-10-22; all five merged, and so did everything they were waiting on. The
heading is kept rather than deleted because an empty in-flight list is itself
the status.

`main` has branch protection with **`strict: true`**, and on 2026-10-23 that
cost was paid in full: fourteen pull requests landed one at a time, each merge
putting the rest behind and forcing a fresh ~20-minute cycle. It was relaxed
for the nine documentation PRs — verified safe first by building the combined
tree locally and running all eight drift checkers against it — and **restored
immediately after**. `enforce_admins` is true, so there is no lighter
alternative: `--admin` does not bypass it, the rule itself has to move.

~~**Whether `strict` earns its keep on a repository where most pull requests
touch only Markdown remains a decision [D]**~~ — **DECIDED 2026-10-25:
`strict: true` STAYS, AND THE COST WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE.** The bill was read
as the price of the rule, and it is not. It is the price of **what the rule is
bound to**: five required contexts, none of them path-filtered, four of which
cannot be affected by a Markdown-only diff and all of which ran anyway. #347
is the receipt — a five-document change, nothing else, and it spent `conductor`
11m59s and `ui` 12m37s proving that sentences about `v0.2.0` had not broken a
real DHT.

**`strict` itself earns its keep, and there is a demonstrated catch rather than
an argument.** Two pull requests each inserting into
`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs` regenerate the validation inventory
independently, are green alone, and red `main` when the second lands carrying
the first's line numbers. That is exactly the semantic conflict `strict` exists
to prevent, and it went red in CI on 2026-10-24 for that reason. Dropping the
rule would trade a proven catch for throughput.

**So the binding moved instead.** `conductor`, `network`, `ui` and `zomes` now
carry a `scope` job that skips them when the diff is documentation and nothing
else; `live-verify` is deliberately ungated, because every checker that reads a
document runs there. A docs-only pull request satisfies all five contexts in
about ninety seconds, and `strict: true` keeps its teeth on everything that
touches `dna/` or `scripts/`. **It is a conditional and not a `paths:`
filter**, which is not a detail: a workflow skipped by `paths:` creates no
check run, and a required context with no check run hangs the pull request on
*"Expected — waiting for status"* forever, while a job skipped by a conditional
reports success. The documented remedy — a stub workflow sharing the job name —
is unsound here, because `paths` and `paths-ignore` are not complements and a
mixed diff would have both jobs reporting under one name.

**The new risk is a green that means nothing**, which is worse than the red
that means nothing this repository already refuses: a classifier that calls a
code diff documentation would have four required checks pass having run
nothing. Three things answer it rather than one — the classifier is
**default-deny** (anything not root-level `*.md` or `docs/**.md` is code, so a
mistake is a slow pull request and never an unverified merge), the gate
expression carries `!cancelled()` (without it a failed `scope` job would skip
its dependents into success), and `check-ci-scope.mjs` asserts all of it from
inside the one workflow the gate cannot touch — including that no harness any
gated job runs reads a document. See `scripts/ci-scope.mjs`.

**AND THE CONTENTION EXPLANATION IS WRONG — the mechanism cannot exist on this
CI.** The 2026-10-23 correlation stands as an observation: six branches at
once, four `ui` failures, every one `taxonomy-ui` §5 — *"proposing a species
from the UI"* — timing out on `waitForSelector`, against 4-for-4 green when the
same branches ran solo. But every workflow is `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`
(`.github/workflows/ui.yml:160` and its six siblings), so **each job gets its
own VM**: jobs share no CPU and no ports, since `sandbox.sh` binds 8887-8889 on
`127.0.0.1` inside the runner it is given. And on 2026-10-24 #333's `ui` failed
on the same selector at 04:17:38, **by which time every other job in both
batches had finished except its own `conductor`** — one sibling, on another
machine — then passed on re-run with the tree unchanged.

~~So two things are now established and one is not. **Established**: §5 has an
intermittent race that does not need a loaded queue, and a re-run clears it.
**Not established**: why six branches correlated with four failures at all.~~

**ANSWERED THE SAME DAY BY #340, AND BRANCH COUNT PLAYED NO PART.**
`taxonomy-ui` §5 was missing the 3-second settle its own section 3 already
took. `render()` rebuilds `app.innerHTML` wholesale, so a re-render landing
between filling the species name and clicking **Propose** destroyed the input;
the form submitted an empty name; validation correctly refused it
(*"CritiqueSpecies name cannot be empty"*). Nothing was written, so nothing was
readable, so the wait timed out.

**The race is per-run and decided by the runner's speed.** A development machine
wins it every time — which is why this harness passed locally all day, twice
against the unfixed code — and a runner carrying vite, Playwright, lair and a
conductor on four cores does not. Six branches meant six samples of a coin a
slow runner loses; four reds in six against none in four solo is **sampling, not
causation**.

Two sentences above are therefore struck rather than edited. *"A re-run clears
it"* was also wrong: the re-run on #339 failed identically, twice in a row,
which is what ended the flake theory. And **"push one branch at a time" is dead
as a remedy** — it addresses a variable with no causal role. The thing that
actually settled it was making the read declare its own mode (§7b.2, item 1.7).

**THREE ATTRIBUTIONS WERE WRONG IN SEQUENCE, and that is the lesson worth
keeping.** Inter-job contention — mechanically impossible, every job holds its
own VM. Then the render path — which *this very section* named as the suspect.
Then commit-to-readable latency — the instrumentation's own first conclusion,
which conflated a refused write with a slow one. Each was retired by a
measurement rather than by an argument, and that is the only reason to trust the
fourth.

---

## 1. Immediate engineering — bounded, and the model can do all of it

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | ~~**Why `send_remote_signal` does not deliver.**~~ Stage 1's receiving half, cost accounting, idempotence and control all verify; remote delivery does not. Every failure path in the host's implementation is `tracing::info!` then `return` under *"timeouts and errors are ignored"*, so the default log level hides it. Diagnostic: both nodes at `RUST_LOG=info` **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#318). It delivers and always did** — the harness unwrapper missed `value.payload.PeerSignal` by one level, so *"nothing arrived"* and *"something arrived I cannot read"* were the same observation. `RUST_LOG` could not have found it: `network.sh:592` exports `CUSTOM_FILTER` at base `warn`, which overrides it. `NETAUDIT=trace` did. **Six silent paths, not the four the HDK documents.** | — | done |
| 1.2 | ~~**The scale run above twelve.**~~ `scale-dht.mjs` exists, `EPI_SCALE_NODES` parameterises it, twelve all claimed a FULL arc. The figure wanted is **the N at which an arc first narrows**. ~95-98 MB and ~8% of a core per conductor, so 48 is ~4.7 GB and ~4 cores against 11 GB and 12 cores free **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#323). Measured at 48 members: 48 of 48 arcs still FULL**, 5,983 MB across 49 conductors, load 3.1-5.6 of 12 cores. **There is no N** — `kitsune2_gossip`'s narrowing path sits behind a `sharding` cargo feature that is OFF, and holochain declares `unstable-sharding = []`, an empty feature that does not forward to it. A feature-flag question, not a scale question. | — | done |
| 1.3 | ~~**The `ui` flake, which is probably not flake.**~~ Four failures on 2026-10-22 across `taxonomy-ui` and `graph-ui`, every one a `waitForSelector` timeout of the shape *write something, then wait for it to render*, every one green on re-run. The two-house run established that transit is sub-second and **local integration under contention** is the cost, which is exactly what a loaded runner has. Wanted: a counted record beside the harnesses the way the `network` pass rate is kept, and the wait declaring its own mode per §7b.2 **COUNTED 2026-10-23 (#316). CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-10-24 — see §0 and 1.7:** the 4-for-4-solo against 4-for-4-batch correlation stands as an observation, but *inter-job* contention cannot be its mechanism — every workflow is `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`, so no two jobs share a machine. What looked like it survived was `scripts/live-verify/README.md`'s *intra*-job hypothesis — one runner contending with itself — and **that was wrong too, settled by #340 the same day**: §5 was missing the 3-second settle §3 already took, so a re-render wiped the form and validation refused an empty name. Runner speed decides the race, but it was never about op integration. Re-running does **not** reliably clear it either — #339's re-run failed identically. See §0 and 1.7. **A THIRD HARNESS JOINED THE SHAPE ON 2026-11-01, WHICH IS WHAT THIS ROW ASKED TO BE COUNTED: `foresight-ui`.** On PR #403 — a comment-only edit to `index-lens.mjs` and one documentation row, so nothing it could plausibly have caused — `ui` failed with `FAIL: the commitment appears in the pending list on screen`, `FAIL: and the commitment reached the conductor, question bound`, then `HARNESS ERROR: locator.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded`. Same *write something, then wait for it to render* shape as the `taxonomy-ui` and `graph-ui` failures above, on a harness this row had never named. **It CLEARED on re-run (13m50s, green, unchanged tree), which is the half that does not match the record:** #339's re-run failed identically, and this one did not — so *re-running does not reliably clear it* is still true as written and is not true of every instance. The §5 settle that explained `taxonomy-ui` is a `taxonomy-ui` fix; whether `foresight-ui` has its own missing settle is **not established here** and nothing in this entry claims it. **A SECOND `foresight-ui` OBSERVATION, PR #405, 2026-11-01 — AND IT IS THE SAME THREE LINES.** `FAIL: the commitment appears in the pending list on screen`, `FAIL: and the commitment reached the conductor, question bound`, `HARNESS ERROR: locator.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded` at `foresight-ui.mjs:409` — byte-for-byte the #403 failure, §7 then §8 cascading off it. **The diff again could not have caused it:** #405 changed three `scripts/*.mjs` endpoint callers and two docs, and **nothing under `scripts/live-verify/` or `.github/workflows/ui.yml` references any of the three** (the only mention anywhere is a comment in `index-lens.mjs`), so there is no code path from the change into the `ui` job. **It cleared on re-run again** — all seven checks green, with every other job passing on its FIRST attempt including `partition-rejoin`. So `foresight-ui` was at that point **twice observed and twice cleared by one re-run**, which looked like it sharpened the split this row recorded: the harnesses differing in whether a re-run clears them, with `foresight-ui` always on the clearing side where #339 was always on the other. **THAT READING IS WITHDRAWN — see the 2026-11-01 entry below, where it failed, cleared on re-run, and then failed AGAIN on the same pull request.** The clearing-side claim was built on two instances and did not survive the third and fourth. **Two instances is still not a mechanism** — and the detail that resists the settle-timing explanation is that §7's CHAIN-READ check failed alongside the render check, so the write did not land, not merely that it did not paint. Counted, not explained. Recorded as the counted observation this row wanted rather than as a cause. **A THIRD AND FOURTH OBSERVATION, 2026-11-01, on PRs #407 and #413** — again `foresight-ui`, again byte-for-byte the same three lines at `foresight-ui.mjs:409`, and again on diffs that **cannot reach the `ui` job**: both are documentation-only negative-evidence records (comments in `scripts/live-verify/*.mjs` plus one README table row). **So `foresight-ui` stands at four observations in two days, every one on a diff incapable of causing it.** That is no longer an intermittency noticed in passing — it is the most reproducible failure in this suite, and the §7 chain-read check failing beside the render check in every instance still says the WRITE does not land rather than the paint arriving late. **AND A CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CLAIM, MADE THE SAME DAY IT WAS WRITTEN.** The earlier entries above said `foresight-ui` had been *twice cleared by one re-run* and was therefore *always on the clearing side* where #339 was always on the other. **#413 falsifies that on a single pull request:** its `ui` job failed, was re-run and went GREEN, and then failed AGAIN with the identical three lines after the branch was updated to current `main` — a content-identical rebase, no source change. So a re-run clearing `foresight-ui` does not predict the next run, and the clean split between it and #339 does not exist. **What survives is the weaker and more useful statement: a re-run SOMETIMES clears it, with no known predictor.** The claim was built on two instances, and the third and fourth broke it — the same two-observations-is-not-a-mechanism caution this row already prints, applied to itself. **THAT HYPOTHESIS WAS TESTED 2026-11-01 AND IT IS CONFIRMED, BY SOURCE READING RATHER THAN BY ANOTHER RUN. `foresight-ui` §7 HAS `taxonomy-ui` §5's DEFECT.** The chain: `main.ts`'s `render()` is `app.innerHTML = ''` plus a full rebuild (`main.ts:588`), and the commit button's handler reads `.value` off the input elements **its own closure captured**. So a render landing between the harness's `fill()` and its `click()` replaces both — the click hits a NEW button whose closure reads NEW EMPTY inputs, the handler's own guard refuses with *"A question, a prediction and a salt are all required"*, and **nothing is committed**. Observable: no pending row AND nothing on the chain, which is exactly the pair of reds, with §8 cascading off the absent pending item. **The renders are neither hypothetical nor the harness's doing:** `main.ts` has **twenty-one fire-and-forget `void load*()` calls and twelve of its fourteen loaders end in `render()`**, and §7 reaches the form FROM A CLAIM CARD, which fires `loadResonance`, `loadGroundingChain`, `loadClaimEpistemicState` and `loadFrictionStatus`. Any of those resolving mid-fill wipes the form. **This also explains both things the counting never could**: the intermittency is a race whose window is the gap between two Playwright calls, so a loaded runner loses it more often (the ~17% job rate, and §7b's contention finding), and a re-run clears it or not depending on timing alone — which is why the *clears on re-run* predictor had to be withdrawn. **§7 was the one interactive point in the file with NO settle**: §3 waits 6s, the preview 3s, §8 2s and 3s. **FIXED, and made self-diagnosing rather than merely fixed**: a 3s settle before the fill, the filled values READ BACK and asserted before the submit so a wiped form reports itself instead of masquerading as a commit that never happened, and the UI's own error box printed when the pending list fails to render. 25 checks become 26. **If it recurs now, the new check says which of the two it was** — and four observations produced no diagnosis precisely because nothing distinguished them. | — | done |
| 1.4 | ~~**Two desktop installs talking to each other.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-26 — IT CROSSED.** A `Claim` published on machine 1 into domain `xnet-1026-a` appeared on machine 2 **on its first `Load claims`**, between two packaged `v0.3.0` installs on two machines. **This is the first time two installed copies of this app have exchanged anything**; every prior crossing used `hc sandbox` conductors, which declare no network seed and so cannot see desktop peers at all, which is exactly why the packaging was the one layer no measurement had reached. Verified from the CELL rather than from release notes: machine 1's running DNA hashes to `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o`, the documented `0.3.x` hash, read live through the admin interface; both installs reached `Conductor ready.` on dynamic admin ports (42253, 33239); and `INSTALL.md`'s two origins are confirmed — `kangaroo` on admin, `webhapp://webhappwindow` on the app interface. **Release step 3's *"against that build"* clause is closed, so 7.4's last blocker is gone.** **THE RUN FOUND TWO DOCUMENTATION DEFECTS, AND ONE WAS A CORRECTION THAT WAS ITSELF WRONG** — `READINESS.md` §1 and `INSTALL.md` both said `v0.3.0` logs `Connected to admin websocket.` *instead of* `Conductor ready.` and said *"do not grep for it"*; measured across all three installed profiles, `Conductor ready.` is in every one and the two replacement strings are in **none**, so the instruction would have reported a working conductor as dead. Also established: **founding a domain is not a prerequisite for publishing into one** (`validate_claim` requires only a non-empty `domain`), so the advice to found one first would have written a permanent `Constitution` for nothing. **AND THE REVERSE CROSSED TOO**, measured separately rather than assumed: a claim published on machine 2 into a fresh domain `xnet-1026-b` — which machine 1 had loaded and seen EMPTY first — appeared on machine 1. **Both directions between two packaged installs.** That matters because a one-way result is consistent with a receiver that can only receive, and the ten invitees will all be publishers. **It does not soften §3b**: both machines were up with the receiver attached before each write, which is the favourable case, so the five-to-twelve-minute returning-node catch-up is still the number to quote. Closes the clause still open in release step 3 — *"against that build"* — since both ends of every 2026-10-22 crossing were `hc sandbox` conductors. The relay path is measured; the packaging is not **NARROWED 2026-10-23 (#325): the protocol half is DONE and the blocker is smaller than this row said.** Verified against a running `v0.2.0` — the cell hashes to the seeded desktop hash recorded here, the zome reports `declared_version: 9`, a Claim written through the app's own conductor rendered in its UI, and a Retraction showed it retracted by its author while the claim stayed readable by hash. The conductor is reachable **without the GUI** (admin origin `kangaroo`, app interface `webhapp://webhappwindow`), so the crossing test is scriptable. **What remains: one Setup click per profile** — `passwordMode` is `password-optional` and the conductor does not launch until somebody clicks — **and a second machine.** ~~Note `v0.2.0` is `protocol_version: 9` against main's 11, so testing it validates packaging two forks behind; whether that is what *"against that build"* means is a decision.~~ **NOT A DECISION ANY MORE: `v0.3.0` shipped at 11, so run the test against `v0.3.0` and the clause means the documented build.** Three things do not carry over from the `v0.2.0` record — the seed is `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x`, the running hash is `uhC0kozxl…`, and the launch log says `Connected to admin websocket.` rather than `Conductor ready.` See `INSTALL.md`. | H | **done 2026-10-26** |

| 1.6 | ~~**The parked gate.**~~ Branch `chore/gate-probabilistic-setup`, no commits, to gate probabilistic setup searches in `ordering-policy.mjs` (three sites, lines 244/266/310) **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#317), then corrected again in #326.** The gate was right; its arithmetic was not. §2 compares 32 hashes against ONE pivot, so a miss is *"is the pivot the maximum of 33"* = **1/33 ≈ 3%**, not 2^-32 — and CI drew it while the gate printed the false figure one line above the red. | 1.9 | ~~**`check-packages.mjs` is gated by nothing, and the reason is a reflex rather than a decision.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-25.** It is now a `live-verify.yml` step, and the sentence that hid it is fixed. **THE ROW UNDERSTATED ITS OWN SURFACE, which is the second time in one day** — it said *"wiring it into `live-verify.yml` is the whole change — it needs no conductor, no DHT and no browser"*, all true and not the whole change: `check-packages.mjs` runs `npm run build` itself, so it needs both packages' dependency trees, and `mcp-server` must resolve the SDK from THIS checkout rather than the registry copy, which is broken on npm. So `conductor.yml`'s two build steps are now a marked shared block in both files, gated byte-for-byte by `check-workflow-setup-drift.mjs` — a drifted copy would make the publish check green about a package nobody ran. 4.5 did the same thing with a link type (#350). **Both were found by building the thing, not by re-reading the row.** It asks whether `npm publish` would produce a *working* package by reading what `npm pack --dry-run` actually puts in the tarball — `files` missing so `src/` ships, `dist` listed but never built, a `file:../thing` dependency that resolves here and nowhere else, a licence declared in metadata and absent from the tarball. None of those is visible until somebody installs the published package, by which point the version number is spent. `scripts/live-verify/README.md` records it as *"not a live-verify harness, but run by the same reflex"*, which was the accurate description when there was no CI at all. **There is CI now, and that passage is ALREADY STALE for its other example**: `check-harness-counts.mjs` is introduced the same way, two paragraphs down, and has been a step in `live-verify.yml` for weeks. **1.8 does not cover this**: `published-packages.mjs` drives the tarball the REGISTRY is serving, from `conductor.yml`; this one asks whether the local tree would publish something installable, which is the question a release asks and the one `v0.3.0` asked four times. Wiring it into `live-verify.yml` is the whole change — it needs no conductor, no DHT and no browser. **One thing it is NOT, recorded because it was discovered by trying**: the fix to that stale passage cannot ride a documentation-only pull request, because `scripts/live-verify/README.md` classifies as *code* under the scope gate — `check-harness-counts.mjs` reads it. That is the classifier being right rather than coarse. | [M] | **done 2026-10-25** |

The five rules of each type are a CHAIN — ActionHash, resolves, right type, base matches the entry's own target, author is the owner — so each owner case moves the base as well as the target, to agent 2's own target, or the base rule fires one line above the rule being probed and the case passes for the wrong reason. The original text follows. **Ten validation rules the corpus demands nothing of.** `TargetToAssessment` and `TargetToSettlementStatus` carry **five assertable rules each** and are absent from `replay.mjs`'s `COVERED_LINKS`, so the per-type coverage loop never looks at them: the corpus reports *"0 failed, 0 skipped"* and an `N of N` line for every type it is asked about, and it is not asked about these. Shipped that way at `protocol_version: 11` — settlement's three ENTRY types went into `COVERED` and its two LINK types did not go into `COVERED_LINKS` — and it stayed green for four days. **Found 2026-10-25 while measuring the corpus README's rotted figures, and the same omission was made again on the 4.5 branch the same day**, caught there only because its cases happened to name rules and tripped the orphan check from the other side. **The structural half is now closed**: `check-corpus-coverage.mjs` fails on any type with assertable rules that is neither covered nor declared, and both of these are declared with their reason pointing here, so the number stays visible. Measured: **31 declared, 27 written, 27 with a `validate_create_link` branch, 23 bound, 4 unconditional.** **The error ran the other way too, and that one understated the protocol.** `scripts/validation-corpus/README.md` — swept the same day — listed `ClaimToBridgeRecord` as having *"no `validate_create_link` branch at all"*, telling a reader a validated link type was unvalidated. It has a branch, **seven** rules and a case. **The cause is worth more than the fix: the sweep read the INVENTORY's 26 as a fact about the zome.** `ClaimToBridgeRecord` shares one branch with `MewToBridgeRecord` (`else if A || B`) and `enumerate.mjs` files a branch under the first `LinkTypes::` its regex finds — so all seven rules sit under `MewToBridgeRecord`, which IS in `COVERED_LINKS`, so the per-type loop demands all seven and `replay.mjs` asserts all seven. **Nothing was untested; one type is counted under its sibling's name, and `replay.mjs` said so at its bridge spec the whole time.** A denominator borrowed from a tool is a claim about the tool. SPEC §5.17, which says four, was right and was not consulted. **WHAT IS NOW GATED is the set equality, not the integers**: `written === has-a-branch`, both directions, so a link type the coordinator writes with no rule fails CI — §5.17's *"each acquires a rule with its first writer"* enforced rather than hoped for. Watched failing twice: a stale SPEC count (red, `says 26, the zome says 27`) and a retargeted branch (red four ways, naming both types). Added to `check-spec-drift.mjs` rather than as a ninth checker, so the gate count stays **8**. | [M] | **done 2026-10-25** |
| 1.12 | ~~**Three sources disagree about whether `min_attestations` is enforced, and the read PUBLISHES the lie.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-25 — THE DOCS WERE THE LIE, AND THERE WERE FIVE OF THEM, NOT THREE.** Decided: `is_agent_attested`'s `return Ok(true)` is CORRECT and unchanged. **What settled it was evidence the row had not looked at** — `mobile-ui`'s lens builder already REFUSES an empty root set, with the reasoning *"a lens that looks applied and filters nothing is worse than no lens at all"*, and `trust-lenses.mjs` §2 already asserts that refusal. So the ambiguous shape was already unrepresentable at the only layer a human can express it, and the zome's behaviour was deliberate rather than missing. **The count is over a FREE quantity** (N minted keys buy N attesters — `docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md` §2.1), so enforcing it would bound nothing while reading as a verdict. **Fixed: the field doc comment, `describe_method` (now renders `attestation lens: INERT — no roots named, nothing was filtered`), and — found by sweeping for the string rather than trusting the row's count of three — `README.md`'s AttestationPolicy explainer, which carried the same false claim in the most-read place, plus `federation/README.md`'s quoted run output.** That last one is left AS PRINTED and annotated: it is a record, and it proves the method description reaches an entry Invariant 6 forbids editing — **which is sharper now that the string it carried is known to have overstated the read.** Two tests repaired: a unit test asserted depth wording under a policy whose depth is never read, and `microbiome.mjs` asserted the false string *including its arrival in the permanent entry*. New unit test watched failing (the inert arm removed → red, 76/1; restored → 77/0). **No DNA hash change.** Driving a REAL attestation lens in `microbiome.mjs` — actual roots, actually filtering — is deliberately not in this change: it alters which claims import and needs its own conductor run. *Original row follows.* **Three sources disagree about whether `min_attestations` is enforced, and the read PUBLISHES the lie.** `is_agent_attested` returns `true` immediately when `require_attestation_from` is `None` — `min_attestations` is never read. But `require_attestation_from`'s own doc comment says *"this still requires `min_attestations` distinct attesters, just from anyone"*, and `describe_method` renders *"attestation lens: N distinct attester(s) required, …, roots unrestricted"* into two published reads (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs` 5467, 8487). **So `{require_attestation_from: None, min_attestations: 5}` filters NOTHING and reports that it required five.** This is 7.12's own §7b.1 discipline inverted: that row insisted a read returning three of a hundred must say so, and this read returns all hundred while claiming a lens. **The fix is not obvious and that IS the finding** — enforcing the count is implementable and still worthless as a sybil defence, because the quantity it counts is free (`docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md` §2.1), so returning `true` may be the right behaviour and the DOCS may be the defect. Decide which of the three is the lie, make the other two agree. Coordinator-only: no entry type, no link type, no validation rule, **no DNA hash change**. | D to decide which is the lie; M to apply | **done 2026-10-25** |
| 1.13 | ~~**`direct_attesters_of`'s two sources are each documented and their UNION is not, and the union is the security property.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-26.** Written down where the costs are claimed rather than where the union happens — the `ATTESTATION GRANT` section header spent twenty lines on tenure and budget and then noted the union in a single trailing clause, so a reader met both costs and never met the sentence that nullifies them. It now says that **a set union costs what its CHEAPEST member costs**, names the free member, gives the K-minted-keys arithmetic, records that 3.7 asserted the opposite and that 7.12 had the premise as an aside about test setup, and — the part that is not merely a correction — states **what the two costs are still for**: they make conferring attestation deliberate for an agent who HAS tenure, which is what makes a grant worth more than a critique as a signal. | 1.14 | ~~**`SECURITY.md`'s free-identity position overstates what the attestation machinery provides.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-26, and it understated as well as overstated.** `SECURITY.md` was already honest that identity is free and containment is topological — what it had wrong was *"the right shape of a fix **is understood to be** a read-layer lens"*, written before 7.12 built it. So it was telling readers a protection was hypothetical while it shipped. It now states the lens exists and states its limit as the one sentence that matters: **a reader who names a root set is unaffected by a sybil flood; a reader who does not is unprotected** — nothing in between, nothing automatic. That passage gains the third thing: a **per-reader** scarce resource, scarce because no transaction adds you to somebody else's root set, and per-reader rather than global is exactly why it needs no centre. | M | done | The honest statement is not *sybils are expensive* but **a reader who names a root set is unaffected, and a reader who does not is unprotected** — because `require_attestation_from` is the only input a minted key cannot obtain for itself, there being no transaction that adds you to somebody's root set. 7.1 committed to vouching for honesty rather than safety, and this is a live gap between what the documents imply and what the code does. | 1.15 | ~~**SPEC §2.4 said `past_cone` has four validation rules and then listed five.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-26, found while verifying a cross-reference for the §6 brief — which is the only reason it surfaced.** The sentence read *"Validation MUST enforce **four** rules and MUST NOT claim a fifth"* and immediately enumerated (1)–(5). `validate_critique` enforces all five, as **six** refusals (rule 3 fails separately for *does not resolve* and *is not a `Critique`*). **The miscount was not cosmetic: it made rule 5 — transitive closure, the one the same paragraph calls load-bearing, and the only rule that makes claimed IGNORANCE non-selective — read as the thing being DISCLAIMED.** A reader counting four enforced rules would take the fifth listed item as the unenforceable one, which is exactly backwards; the genuinely unenforceable thing is that the author actually saw them, which the paragraph goes on to say. Now *five rules, and MUST NOT claim the sixth thing a reader will assume.* | M | **done 2026-10-26** |
| 1.16 | ~~**`check-ci-scope.mjs` read a YAML COMMENT as an invocation, and died instead of failing.**~~ *Numbered 1.16 because 1.15 was taken — the collision was made on 2026-10-26 night and corrected the same night.* **FIXED 2026-10-26 NIGHT, in the same pass that found it.** Rule 3 of that gate scans each workflow for `node scripts/...` to find the scripts it runs, then refuses any that read a document. The scan did not skip comment lines — so when `conductor.yml`'s header was rewritten to document the command that counts its own harness steps (`grep -cE '^ +node .*live-verify/...'`), the quoted shape was read as an invocation, resolved to the **directory** `scripts/live-verify`, and the checker died with a raw `EISDIR` stack trace. **The failure mode is the one this repository cares about more than the bug**: a gate that crashes says nothing about the thing it gates, and an unanchored count claim in a comment is exactly the drift the same header was being rewritten to prevent. Fixed by dropping comment lines before extracting invocations — a commented-out invocation does not run either, so no coverage is lost, and the per-script scan below it already applied that same rule to its own input. Found by building 6.6; the header's count claim is now a re-checkable command rather than a number, and is anchored so it does not match itself. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | nothing |
| 1.17 | **App-level timestamps have no declared unit, and this repository interprets them in TWO — seconds in one read, microseconds in another.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night, found while building 6.6 (`docs/the-joining-promise.md` is what made a `Constitution`'s expiry worth looking at).* **MEASURED, FUNCTION BY FUNCTION.** Two reads actually CONSULT a caller-supplied time field, and they disagree: `get_agent_constitution` drops an expired `Constitution` by comparing `expires_at` against `sys_time().as_seconds_and_nanos().0` — **seconds** (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5255`) — while `get_foresight_record` compares `reveal_deadline` against `sys_time().as_micros()` — **microseconds** (`:7360`), which is the right one THERE because `validate_pre_registration` compares the same field against the action's own timestamp in micros (`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:1392`) and that comparison is DHT-enforced. So the unit is per-field, correct in each place for its own reasons, and **declared nowhere**. **AND THE SAME FIELD IS WRITTEN IN BOTH UNITS BY THE SAME ZOME**: `Claim.timestamp` gets seconds from `assert_expertise` (`:3375`) and `promote_mew_to_claim` (`:5106`), and micros from `publish_discourse_reading` (`:5561`) and `import_foreign_critique` (`:8635`). **WHAT IS AND IS NOT AT RISK.** Nothing consults `Claim.timestamp` — §5.22's refusal is explicit in the ordering code (*"timestamps may be DISPLAYED; they are never consulted here"*), and SPEC §678 already records `PreRegistration.timestamp` and `Revelation.timestamp` as never read — so today this is a DISPLAY and EXPORT inconsistency: a screen or an N4L consumer rendering both kinds of `Claim.timestamp` shows one of them in 1970 and the other in the year 56000. **THE TRAP IS THE EXPIRY FIELD, AND 7.17 WALKS TOWARD IT.** A caller who follows the most common harness idiom (`nowMicros()`, which `domain-index.mjs` and `attachment-links.mjs` both use for `published_at`) and then sets a real `expires_at` in the same unit gets a constitution that **can never expire**, because a micros value is ~10^5 times larger than the seconds `now` it is compared against. **Validation cannot catch it**: `validate_constitution` only requires `expires_at > published_at`, which is satisfied in either unit, so the inconsistency is invisible to the one layer that refuses things. If invitees are asked for a joining promise (7.17) with any expiry at all, this is the first thing that bites. **THE FIX IS THREE SMALL PARTS AND NONE FORKS**: declare the unit per field in SPEC (micros wherever validation compares against an action timestamp, seconds elsewhere), make the coordinator's own writes consistent per field, and state it in the client types — the third part is **already done**, in `agent-sdk/src/types.ts`'s `Constitution` doc comment and `promise-compliance.mjs`'s `nowSecs` note, which is what makes this a row rather than a surprise. **A fourth part is a judgement call**: changing `Claim.timestamp`'s unit in two coordinator functions makes existing entries' values mean something slightly different, and since nothing reads them the cost is zero today and rises the moment anything does. **AND A FIFTH PART EXISTED AND WAS NOT SEEN UNTIL IT BIT: 2026-10-27, THE DAY AFTER THIS ROW CLOSED, `docs/the-joining-promise.md`'s PAYLOADS — the ones an invitee copies verbatim — specified `published_at` and `created_at` in MICROS.** The code was right, the example was wrong, and the gate could not see it because it scanned code only. **`check-time-units.mjs` now also reads fenced code blocks in every root and `docs/` Markdown file** (prose excluded on purpose — the text around these fields legitimately *describes* the error, so a prose scan would flag SPEC §5.23 itself and this row), with its own vacuity floor, and **watched failing on the documentation half: both payloads reverted → 2 reds naming file, line and field.** It found a second instance on its first run: `docs/metabolic-biosignalling-currency-brief.md` documented `MutualCreditTransfer.timestamp` as *"Unix seconds"* — which matters because 3.4 leaves open whether that layer gets rebuilt from the brief, so the example is a future implementation's unit. **The lesson is narrower than "gate everything": a convention is violated first wherever it is COPIED rather than wherever it is computed.** **FIXED 2026-10-26 NIGHT — BEFORE 7.17 IS ADOPTED, which is the order the row asked for.** **(1) DECLARED: SPEC §5.23**, a table covering all 21 app-level time fields, with the reason each family is what it is rather than the other — `timestamp` family **micros** (104 of ~118 caller sites already were, and it matches Holochain's own `Timestamp`), `created_at`/`published_at`/`expires_at`/`start_time`/`end_time` **seconds** (three reads already assume it, and `mobile-ui` renders `published_at * 1000` as a date), `reveal_deadline` **micros and the only one with a DHT-side consequence**. **(2) MADE TRUE RATHER THAN ASSERTED OVER CODE THAT DISAGREED**: `assert_expertise` and `promote_mew_to_claim` now write `Claim.timestamp` in micros, joining the two functions that already did, and **30 call sites** across the harnesses, `mobile-ui` and `agent-sdk` were corrected — 0 mismatches remain of 186 minted time values. `nowSecs` was added to `mobile-ui`'s types (with the trap in its doc comment) and REMOVED from the SDK, where it became dead code once every write there was a `timestamp` field. **(3) GATED, because a convention nothing checks is the thing this list keeps finding rotted: `scripts/check-time-units.mjs`**, the **ninth** drift checker and the first that gates a convention rather than a count, a document or a rule. It reads both sides of the wire — the coordinator zome and every client — refuses to pass on an unrecognised expression rather than skipping quietly, requires SPEC §5.23 to still exist and still name every field it enforces, and fails if it finds fewer than 50 minted values, since a vacuous scan is how the original drift survived. **Watched failing on both sides**: `promote_mew_to_claim` reverted to seconds → 1 red naming file, line, unit found and unit declared; `promise-compliance`'s `published_at` switched to micros → 1 red with the consequence in the message. **(4) VERIFIED LIVE, not just compiled**: coordinator rebuilt, hApp repacked, and five harnesses run on a clean conductor each — `mew-lifecycle` and `expertise-ui` cover the two changed functions, `read-scope`, `domain-index` and `promise-compliance` cover the edited call sites. **All five green.** `cargo check` clean for wasm; nine checkers green; UI and SDK typecheck clean. **NO FORK**: the DNA hash derives from the integrity zome and nothing in it changed, and the one DHT-enforced comparison (`reveal_deadline`) was already micros and is untouched. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | nothing |
| 1.18 | ~~**`get_agent_constitution` returned the OLDER constitution when two were published in the same second.**~~ **FOUND AND FIXED 2026-10-27 by `promises-ui.mjs` §3**, which publishes twice in a row and asserted the second had superseded the first. It had not: the read ordered candidates by `published_at` **only**, and `published_at` is **seconds** (SPEC §5.23), so two publishes inside one second compare EQUAL and the winner was whatever order `get_links` happened to return. **This is 1.17's mirror image rather than a new kind of problem**: 1.17 was a unit stated nowhere, this is that unit's RESOLUTION being too coarse for the one read that depends on ordering. **THE FIX USES THE ONLY CLOCK §5.22 ALLOWS ANYTHING TO CONSULT**: ties break on the host-stamped **action** timestamp, which is micros and which no author can set — author-supplied `published_at` still leads, because it is what the author claims and a reader can see it, and the action timestamp only separates claims that are otherwise identical. **Coordinator-only, so no fork**: the `Record` was already in hand at that line, the entry type is untouched, and the DNA hash derives from the integrity zome. **Why it mattered beyond the harness**: 6.7's new surface tells a user that publishing again is what readers see, and that sentence was false for anyone who corrected a typo immediately — which is exactly when somebody republishes. | ~~M~~ **DONE** | — |
| 1.19 | ~~**The agent SDK is missing the protocol's central mechanism, and six other functions that already exist in the zome.**~~ **BUILT 2026-10-28 in both clients, verified against a live conductor and injected five times.** *Opened 2026-10-27 while scoping AI integration, and found by listing the SDK's surface against the coordinator's rather than by reading either one.* **MEASURED:** `grep -rn 'assess\|publish_discourse\|past_cone\|pre_register' agent-sdk/src` returns **nothing**. The zome functions exist, their payload types are specified, and `SPEC.md` is the authority. **DO NOT LET IT BECOME AN ABSTRACTION** — `agent-sdk/README.md` is explicit that the library *"is not an abstraction over the protocol and should not become one"*, and each addition should be typed payloads plus the protocol's norms, nothing more. **`mcp-server/` must follow in the same pass**, since it is a thin wrapper where every tool maps to one SDK call; a function added to the SDK and not to MCP is discoverable by nobody. **BUILT, IN BOTH, AND THE MCP HALF WAS NOT OPTIONAL** — stopping at the SDK would have been this row's own defect one layer up. `agent-sdk` gained all eight plus `witnessedCritiquesOf`, `MAX_PAST_CONE` and `randomSalt`; `mcp-server` gained **ten tools, 9 to 19**, and `agent-sdk.mjs` went **34 checks to 67**, `mcp-server.mjs` **21 to 52**, all green on a live conductor. **THE PAST CONE WAS THE ONLY DECISION, and it is 6.5 narrowed rather than 6.5 answered:** 6.5 refuses to let a *client* populate `past_cone` because it would assert what its *user* read, and an over-claiming client makes its user look better informed than they were. For an agent under its own key that victim does not exist — the agent **is** the reader — so `witnessedCritiquesOf` reads the critiques and returns the cone of exactly the hashes it fetched, which makes the convenient path the accurate one without making the claim unfalsifiable. And the two constitutions are published MORE THAN A SECOND APART on purpose: `published_at` is seconds, so a same-second tie falls through to the host action timestamp and the ordering becomes untestable. **THE RAW `call` ESCAPE HATCH IS GONE FROM BOTH HARNESSES** — the discourse reading and the domain-name tools used to be seeded through it with hand-written snake_case payloads, and are now seeded by the typed calls and the tools under test, so the hatch's disappearance IS the verification: founding breaking now takes four and seven later checks red with it rather than none. **THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY BUILDING IT, and one of them by writing the harness rather than by running it:** (1) `create_membrane`'s *"Referenced entry is not a Constitution."* was UNREACHABLE for a Claim's ActionHash — `to_app_option` failed before the arm carrying it and the founder got `Guest("Deserialize(\"missing field `agent`\")")` — **the same defect shape as `get_my_sealed_prediction` hours earlier, found the same way, in a second function**; now on both arms. (2) **`found_domain`'s handler had always read `a.constitution_hash` and its SCHEMA NEVER DECLARED IT.** The tool list is the whole of what an agent knows about a tool, so the parameter was unreachable by any agent — while `publish_my_constitution`'s own note told the caller to KEEP THIS HASH because *passing it explicitly is the only way to be certain which constitution the domain references*: advice that could not be followed through the surface that gave it. The harness could send the undeclared argument, which is exactly why the handler checks could not catch it and discoverability needed its own check against the schema. (3) **the century ceiling `publishConstitution` was documented as having did not exist** — the `expires_at > published_at` rule is TRUE IN EITHER UNIT, so a micros expiry passes the coordinator, passes validation and round-trips intact, and the constitution never expires while its author believes it does; the comment described the silent case while accepting it. **AND A WRONG-HASH GUARD ADDED, with the prefixes MEASURED rather than remembered** (ActionHash `132,41,36`, EntryHash `132,33,36`, AgentPubKey `132,32,36`, all 39 bytes): handing a typed struct an EntryHash where it declares an ActionHash fails while deserialising the WHOLE PAYLOAD, before the function body, and the caller sees `Deserialize([135, 166, ...])` — the msgpack bytes of their own arguments as a decimal array, naming nothing. | 1.23 | **`render()` rebuilds the whole DOM, and the architecture was never a decision — it was a default nobody had argued for.** *Opened and DECIDED 2026-11-02, after two bugs had already been paid for.* **MEASURED:** `render()` is `app.innerHTML = ''` plus a full rebuild (`main.ts:669`), reached from **29 call sites**, fired by **23 fire-and-forget `void load*()` calls** across **20 async loaders**. #418 and #419 fixed two symptoms of it — a harness flake and real typing loss — by ADDING capture machinery rather than by changing anything. **THE FACT THAT REFRAMES IT, and it was sitting in the file the whole time: there are already TWELVE module-level `Set`/`Map` state holders whose only job is to survive a rebuild** (`expandedClaims`, `graphOpenClaims`, `chainOpen`, `resonanceOpen`, `unseenOpen`, `lensBuilderOpen`, `openPeriods`, and the rest). So the architecture is **already** *UI state lives outside the DOM; the DOM is a pure function of it* — the same discipline a declarative framework enforces, hand-rolled and nowhere written down. **The rebuild is not wrong. It is correct given an invariant nobody had stated**, and every bug it has produced was a case where the invariant did not hold because the state belonged to the BROWSER rather than to the app. **DECIDED: KEEP IT, NAME THE INVARIANT, GATE IT.** The alternative considered and rejected was per-region rendering — each loader re-rendering only its own subtree. That removes this bug class and **trades it for a worse one**: a global rebuild that drops browser state fails LOUDLY and in a BOUNDED list (caret, selection, field value, scroll, media playback — three of five already handled), while a stale subtree or a missed invalidation fails SILENTLY and in an unbounded one. It would also rewrite 29 sites and 20 loaders for a defect nobody is currently reporting. **A framework was not seriously considered**: this file's own preamble records minimal dependencies over convenience as a settled preference, and adopting one now would be re-deciding that in passing. **SO THE WORK IS THREE THINGS, none of them a rewrite:** write the invariant into `main.ts` where the next person will read it before adding state; add a gate that fails when component-local state is held in the DOM instead of lifted; and **MEASURE what a rebuild actually destroys before extending the capture helpers** — page scroll is the obvious candidate and is UNVERIFIED, because `innerHTML = ''` and the appends happen in one synchronous block and the browser may never reflow in between. **Fixing a bug I have not watched happen is the thing this repository keeps recording as the expensive mistake**, so the measurement comes first and the harness is what decides whether `captureScroll` exists. **BUILT 2026-11-02, AND THE MEASUREMENT CHANGED WHAT GOT BUILT.** `scripts/live-verify/render-invariant.mjs`, 21 checks, in `ui.yml`. **PAGE SCROLL NEEDS NO FIX AND `captureScroll` DOES NOT EXIST:** scroll survives a rebuild mid-page AND at the very bottom, because `innerHTML = ''` and the appends are one synchronous block and the browser never reflows in between — measured at maximum scroll with the content growing, so not a clamp either. **THE ONE REAL GAP WAS SOMETHING ELSE ENTIRELY: focus on a BUTTON or link did not survive**, falling to `BODY`, so a keyboard user's next Tab restarted from the top of the document. **AND `captureFocus`'s OWN COMMENT ASSERTED THE OPPOSITE** — *"a render caused by pressing a button leaves the button focused"* — which is precisely what justified the function handling text boxes ONLY. The claim was false and the scope it justified was wrong with it: 1.20's bug class in the docstring explaining why the code is shaped as it is. Fixed with `controlKey`/`controlsWithKey` and a `preventScroll` restore, where **`preventScroll` is load-bearing** because a bare `.focus()` scrolls its target into view and would have introduced the jump §1 had just proved does not happen. **AND THE FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS AN ARTIFACT THAT AGREED WITH MY HYPOTHESIS, which is the dangerous kind.** `locator.click()` scrolls its target into view BEFORE clicking, so a run that scrolled to the bottom and clicked a toggle near the TOP reported scroll as MOVED by four thousand pixels — Playwright's scroll read as the app's — while the mid-page case, where the target happened to be visible, reported PRESERVED and matched what I expected. Both numbers were about the instrument. Every trigger is now a DOM-level `el.click()` inside `page.evaluate`, and §2 asserts the clicked element was OFF SCREEN and the page still did not move, which is the control that makes any of it about `render()`. **THE GATE IS BEHAVIOURAL RATHER THAN STATIC, which is the other thing the build settled.** There is no mechanical way to check *no component holds state in the DOM*; what there is, is a harness that fails when a rebuild drops something — so the invariant is enforced by consequence. Injections **CX** (`captureFieldValues` no-op) 1 red with the focus half green, **CY** (control restore no-op) 2 red, **CZ** (restores moved out of `finally`) **GREEN — a recorded LIMIT**: the early returns `finally` exists for are the connect and notes-without-conductor screens, which this harness never drives. **Next free injection ID: DA.** | ~~**M**~~ **DONE 2026-11-02** | nothing — decided, built, gated |
| 1.24 | **The commitment index is a convention, not a rule — a client can commit and stay out of its own denominator.** *Opened 2026-11-02, found by fact-checking 7.24's outline rather than by any gate.* **MEASURED:** the denominator is a `get_links` over `AgentToPreRegistration` (`coordinator:7619`); the link is created by the coordinator's `pre_register` (`coordinator:7456`); and **`validate_pre_registration` contains ZERO references to that link type.** What validation enforces is that such a link, IF PRESENT, targets a real `PreRegistration` — it never requires one to exist. **`attempt_raw_pre_registration` (`coordinator:2392`) writes the entry with no link at all**, and that prober already ships. So an author running a modified client can hold a sealed commitment that no reader's count includes. **THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE SHAPE AND THAT IS THE REAL FINDING:** `required_evidence` declared and unread (7.25, still one declaration and zero readers, re-verified 2026-11-02), `min_attestations` claimed as enforced by a read that published the lie (1.12), and now an index described outward as validation-enforced when it is coordinator convention. **Each was caught by somebody writing an outward-facing sentence, never by a gate** — which is 1.20's argument arriving a third time, and an argument for a gate that pairs each *enforced* claim with the validator that enforces it. **WHY IT IS NOT MERELY A PROSE FIX:** the denominator is the one property this protocol's outward-facing material leads with. A reader who can skip the index can also decline to be counted, which is precisely the invisible-denominator problem the mechanism exists to remove. **THE FIX IS PROBABLY A READ AND THEREFORE CHEAP, AND IT IS STILL A `[D]`.** Counting from the author's chain via `must_get_agent_activity` — already proven in this codebase, it is how the friction check scans (`integrity:2316`) — cannot be skipped, because writing the entry puts it on the chain whatever the client does. That would be **coordinator-only, so no DNA hash change and no fork**. The decision it carries is what the denominator IS: an index a writer opts into, or an enumeration of a chain that an observer must be able to fetch. The second is stronger and changes the answer for any observer whose arc cannot reach that chain, which is not nothing on a partition-tolerant DHT. **DO NOT PUBLISH THE STRONG CLAIM UNTIL THIS IS BUILT** — 7.24's outline now carries the honest version in §3b and the gap in §5. **DECIDED 2026-11-02: THE DENOMINATOR IS THE CHAIN ENUMERATION. The index stays as a fast path, and the report names which source answered.** **WHAT KILLED THE OBJECTION, and it was the only real one.** The case for keeping the index was availability: a chain read depends on reaching the agent-activity neighbourhood, and a link read supposedly does not. **It does.** `AgentToPreRegistration` hangs off `agent_anchor_hash(&agent)`, so counting it is a `get_links` against that anchor's neighbourhood — **both routes are DHT neighbourhood reads that can fail**, and the index's only distinguishing property turns out to be that *a writer can decline to appear in it*. There is no availability advantage to buy the hole with. **THE MECHANISM, CHECKED AGAINST THE HDK RATHER THAN ASSUMED** (`hdk-0.7.0/src/chain.rs:29`). `get_agent_activity(agent, query, ActivityRequest::Full)` returns **action sequences and hashes** for actions matching a `ChainQueryFilter`, filtered **at the source** before transmission, and the activity is held by the neighbourhood of the agent's public key. Hashes are enough — a denominator needs a COUNT, and the commitments are sealed anyway, so nothing here reads content it should not. `must_get_agent_activity` is already trusted in the integrity zome for the friction scan (`integrity:2316`), so this is a proven dependency rather than a new one. **AND THE REPLY CARRIES ITS OWN STATUS, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THE HONEST VERSION POSSIBLE.** `get_agent_activity` reports whether the chain is valid, contains an invalid item, is **forked**, or is empty, plus any warrants. So *I could not see this chain* is distinguishable from *there are none* — the distinction the rest of this protocol is built on (`cone_recorded` gating `unseen`, `total_known` as one observer's count, `horizon_exhausted` on `get_standing`). **A count that could not reach the chain must report that and not a smaller number**, because a silently-smaller denominator is the exact defect being fixed. A forked chain is information about the author rather than an error. **WHY NOT DROP THE INDEX.** It is a cheap read for the author's own screen and it is already written and validated; removing it is a change with no benefit. It becomes a **hint**, never the answer, and the report says which one it used — the same division `renderUnseenState` keeps between the client's figures and the protocol's note. **COST ACCEPTED:** a chain read per agent is dearer than a link read, and the figure now depends on a neighbourhood the observer may not reach — which was always true of the index too, and is now *reported* instead of silently absorbed. **NOT DECIDED HERE:** whether validation should additionally REFUSE a `PreRegistration` whose link is missing. It should not be needed once the count no longer depends on the link, and it would mean an integrity change and a fork for a property the read already has. **BUILT 2026-11-02 — AND THE MEASUREMENT SAYS THE CHAIN ROUTE DOES NOT WORK ON THIS SUBSTRATE, WHICH IS THE FINDING.** `get_agent_activity` returns `ChainStatus::Empty` with no activity for an agent whose index demonstrably holds commitments — **for their own chain AND for another agent's**, at 0, 5, 10, 20 and 30 seconds, with `GetOptions::default()` already being `network()` (checked, not assumed). Two agents, one conductor, holochain 0.7.0. So the decision above is right and **currently unavailable**, which is a fact about the substrate rather than about the design. **WHAT SHIPPED IS THE HONEST DEGRADATION, AND IT IS A REAL IMPROVEMENT ON ITS OWN.** `get_foresight_record` now carries `counted_from`, `chain_status`, `unindexed` and `note`. When the chain cannot be read it falls back to the index **and says the number is a FLOOR rather than a denominator** — where before it silently presented the index AS the denominator. The UI renders that caveat from the coordinator's own `note` whenever `counted_from != "chain"`, which on this substrate is always. SPEC's §10 row now states both sources and their different standing. **MY FIRST IMPLEMENTATION REINTRODUCED THE DEFECT IT WAS FIXING, AND THE PROBE CAUGHT IT.** It treated `ChainStatus::Empty` as a successful count of ZERO — a silently smaller denominator, inside the fix for silently smaller denominators. An empty chain beside a non-empty index is **unreadable, not zero**, and the two can only disagree in that direction because the chain is a superset of the index by construction. **Reasoning did not catch this; running it did.** **AND AN EXISTING GUARD EARNED ITS KEEP:** `pre-registration.mjs` asserted `ForesightRecord` has EXACTLY three keys, and went red the moment provenance was added. That is what an exact key set is for — a new field on this read is a decision, because what it guards is that no SCORE ever appears. Updated to the new exact set rather than loosened, with a control asserting the one number it carries is about the INDEX's completeness and not about an agent. `pre-registration.mjs` 27 to 36 checks. ~~**WHAT IS LEFT, AND IT IS NOT A `[D]`:** find out whether `get_agent_activity` serves a chain on a real multi-conductor network~~ **MEASURED 2026-11-08, AND THE ARM IS NOT DEAD CODE.** On a settled multi-member DHT (`network.sh` A/B plus `start-node nodeD`) `get_agent_activity` returns `ChainStatus::Valid` and `counted_from` is `"chain"` — read by the author, and by **two peer observers for whom the activity authority cannot be self**, at 0, 15 and 30 seconds. So the `ChainStatus::Empty` recorded above was a property of the one-conductor sandbox and not of the code, and the decision taken here was available all along. **7.24's §3b no longer needs the unavailability caveat — it needs a different one**, because the same measurement found the chain route under-counts briefly after a write: **that is row 1.25**, and §3b must carry its honest version instead. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-02; the chain route MEASURED WORKING 2026-11-08 — see 1.25** | nothing — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.25 | **The chain-derived count under-counts for ~5 seconds after a write, and `unindexed` is structurally unable to say so.** *Opened 2026-11-08, found by the multi-node measurement 1.24 asked for — run on the author's word AFTER #436 merged, which is the wrong order and is why this row exists.* **WHAT WAS MEASURED.** Writing a commitment and polling the author's OWN record on nodeA, three members on the shared DHT: at **+0s** `counted_from=chain chain_status=valid pending=6 unindexed=0` and the commitment just written is **absent**; at **+5s** `pending=7` and present. `get_pre_registrations_for_question` returned it **throughout**, including while the record denied it. Transient and converging, not structural. **IT FALSIFIES A LOAD-BEARING COMMENT IN THE CODE THAT SHIPPED** (`coordinator:7705`): *"the chain is a superset of the index by construction, because the index is written from the same call that writes the entry."* That holds for a LOCAL index against a LOCAL chain. It is false the moment the chain is served by a **remote activity authority**, whose view lags the write — and the remote authority is the only reason `counted_from: "chain"` is reachable at all, so the branch that makes the fix available is the branch that breaks the invariant the fix was reasoned from. **AND THE FIELD THAT EXISTS TO REPORT DISAGREEMENT CANNOT REPORT THIS ONE:** `unindexed` counts, by construction, only the chain hashes MISSING FROM THE INDEX — and never the index hashes missing from the chain, which is the direction the lag actually runs. It read `0` at every sample, including the samples where the index held one more commitment than the chain did. A reader is told the two sources agree, at the one moment they do not. **WHY IT IS NOT COSMETIC, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT:** the moment a denominator is read is the moment somebody has just committed — the screen is open BECAUSE they acted. So the ~5-second window is not a rare race, it is the common path, and what it produces is a **silently smaller denominator**: the exact defect 1.24 exists to remove, reintroduced by 1.24's own fix, pointing the other way. 1.24's first implementation did this too (empty chain read as zero) and the probe caught it; this one the probe could not catch, for the reason below. **THE GATE IS GREEN BECAUSE ITS SUBSTRATE NEVER TAKES THE BRANCH.** `pre-registration.mjs` against nodeA with the chain arm live: **7 checks FAIL** — `sits in PENDING`, `the sealed prediction is NOT readable`, `moves to REVEALED`, and the question-binding pair. On one conductor the index arm is taken and every check passes, and `conductor` and `network` install extra agents on a SINGLE conductor rather than forming a multi-member DHT. **Main was green on all five gates at `e13084b` while carrying this** — a real green, silent on the arm that shipped. **That is the fourth instance of the shape 1.24 named as its own real finding** (after `required_evidence` unread, `min_attestations` claimed-and-unenforced, and the index described as validation-enforced), and the first where the unexercised code is code THIS row's predecessor added. **TWO THINGS THE MEASUREMENT DID NOT ESTABLISH, STATED SO THEY ARE NOT READ AS SETTLED.** (a) The first run, on a freshly started TWO-member network, still said `counted_from=index chain_status=unreadable`; between it and the positive result **nodeD was started AND time passed**, so *needs a third member* and *needs time to settle* were never tested against each other. (b) Of the 7 failures, the membership ones follow from the 5-second lag, but **`CONTROL: the one number it does carry is about the INDEX, not the agent` looks like an assertion written FOR the index arm** rather than a product defect — those two causes were not separated, and the fix for each is different. **THE DECISION.** Whether the read should **prefer the local index when it is a strict superset of what the authority served** — which is sound in exactly the direction the lag runs, costs one set comparison, stays coordinator-only, and would make `unindexed` honest by giving it a counterpart; or whether a count that disagrees with the chain must report the disagreement and let the reader hold a floor. **WHAT MUST NOT HAPPEN, because it was already tried once on row 7.28:** giving `pre-registration.mjs` a settle-wait so the chain arm goes green is **printing a clean PASS on a weaker assertion** unless the under-count is fixed first — the probe would then assert that the count is right five seconds after a write, which is not the property anyone needs. The wait is worth adding so the arm can be asserted AT ALL; it is not a fix, and ordering matters. **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08: PREFER THE LOCAL INDEX WHEN IT IS A STRICT SUPERSET.** Both directions of disagreement are now computed — `missing` is chain-not-in-index, which is 1.24's finding, and `unserved` is index-not-in-chain, which is the authority behind the write. When `missing == 0 && unserved > 0` the count is the index and `counted_from` is a THIRD value, **`"index-superset"`**, which is deliberately not a weaker `"index"`: the chain WAS read, nothing on it was unindexed, and the index merely held more. Only `"index"` is the floor, and `"index-superset"` must never be rendered as one — a reader told to treat it as a floor would discount the commitment they had just made, which inverts the screen's job. **THE MIXED CASE IS REPORTED AND NOT COUNTED, deliberately:** when the two disagree in BOTH directions the count stays the chain, because that is the source a writer cannot skip, and the note states how many it is short by. **Counting the union would be a different decision than the one taken and was not taken here.** **MEASURED ON BOTH SUBSTRATES, because the whole point of this row is that one substrate cannot see the defect.** Three-member DHT (`network.sh` plus `start-node nodeD`): `counted_from=index-superset chain_status=valid unindexed=0`, **37 checks green, the 7 failures gone**. Single conductor: `counted_from=index chain_status=unreadable`, **36 checks green, the index arm untouched**. `hc dna hash` returns `uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh`, byte-identical to shipped v0.3.1, so coordinator-only holds and no fork. **AND THE ONE FAILURE THIS ROW COULD NOT ATTRIBUTE WAS THE PROBE'S, WHICH IS NOW SEPARATED.** §7's `CONTROL: the one number it does carry is about the INDEX` required the word *index* in the note. Every substrate it had ever run on took either the index arm or a chain arm with `unindexed > 0`, so the word was always present and the requirement looked free; on a settled DHT the chain is read, both sources agree, and there is nothing true to say about the index. **The check was asserting where the harness was running rather than what the field means** — it now asserts that the note names its SOURCE and leaves which source to the substrate. **STILL UNRESOLVED, and it is the same confound as before:** whether the chain becomes readable because of a THIRD member or merely because time passed was never tested, since nodeD was started and time passed together. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08** | nothing — coordinator-only read, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.26 | **A claim cannot cite another claim, so the protocol's own advice produces claims whose dependency it cannot express.** *Opened 2026-11-08, found by the FIRST PILOT PARTICIPANT on their first claim — and found because of advice given here, which is the part worth keeping.* **WHAT HAPPENED.** They brought a bundled claim: an empirical half (in a system with waiting lists, every place has a cost — someone else is waiting) and a normative half (care should come with criteria and end when no attempt is made). The advice was to SPLIT it, and the reason was structural: a `Critique` targets a WHOLE claim and carries exactly ONE `CritiqueMode`, so a critic who accepts the cost argument and rejects the discharge rule has nowhere to put that — the most interesting position in the room is unsayable, and a critique that lands leaves no record of which half fell. **They split it. The split is right. And now nothing can say that the second claim rests on the first.** `Claim` carries `content`, `domain`, `author`, `timestamp`, `evidence_hashes`, `confidence`, `semantic_tags` and `source_mew` — there is no claim-to-claim relation of any kind. **SO THE GAP IS GENERATED BY THE FIX, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A ROW AND NOT A NICETY:** splitting is the correct response to one-mode-per-critique, and splitting is exactly what creates a dependency the graph cannot hold. A critic who refutes claim 1 does not visibly touch claim 2, though claim 2 is worthless without it, and the only thing joining them is a shared `domain` and the wording. **THREE PATHS, AND THEIR COSTS ARE NOT CLOSE.** **(a) `evidence_hashes`, AND IT ALREADY VALIDATES TODAY.** `validate_claim` requires each hash to resolve to SOME entry and DELIBERATELY NOT to `Evidence` (`integrity:1337`, and the note beside it says so in as many words), so claim 1's EntryHash in claim 2's `evidence_hashes` is accepted right now with no change anywhere. **But nothing reads it as support**, and the field's name says *evidence* to every reader — SPEC, the SDK and the UI all present it that way. Overloading it silently would be meaning with no reader, which is the half-surfaced shape this list keeps recording, and 7.25 already has `required_evidence` declared-and-unread on the same field family. **(b) A claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork.** `create_synaptic_link` (`coordinator:792`) takes `impl Into<AnyLinkableHash>` for base and target, so claim-to-claim is expressible with the types that already ship. **AND IT HAS A TRAP THAT MAKES THIS A `[D]` RATHER THAN AN `[M]`:** `direct_attesters` treats a `SynapticLink` pointing at an agent's Claim as a DIRECT ATTESTATION of that agent and collects `link.author` **with no self-exclusion** (`coordinator:4508` and `:4520`, verified). So an author citing their own claim would enter their OWN attester list, minting self-attestation out of a citation. Whether that is reachable as an exploit depends on root-set membership and was NOT traced here — **TRACED 2026-11-08 and it is REACHABLE: see row 1.27.** A candidate who is themselves in the reader's root set counts as one of their own attesters, so this option would add a second free route to a hole that is already live via self-critique — but a citation that contaminates the trust lens is not a cosmetic concern. Taking this path means either adding a self-exclusion to `direct_attesters`, which is cheap but CHANGES AN EXISTING LENS'S ANSWERS and so is its own decision, or accepting the contamination, which is not acceptable. The conductance-and-atrophy semantics are a second question on this path: a support relation that decays like a critique connection may or may not be wanted, and nobody has said. **(c) A distinct typed link.** Clean semantics, and an INTEGRITY change — new `LinkTypes` variant, new DNA hash, a fork.
