That puts it behind §4's one-fork rule and it should accumulate there rather than travel alone. **WHAT THE DECISION ACTUALLY IS**, stated so it is not mistaken for a plumbing choice: whether *rests on* is a relation this protocol holds at all. Today the graph has one edge between epistemic objects — a critique against its target, which is DISAGREEMENT. Adding support means the graph carries agreement too, and every read that walks critique edges (standing, discourse health, the unseen cone, attestation) would have a second edge type in scope and would have to say whether it follows it. **THAT IS THE WHOLE COST, and it is larger than any of the three implementations.** **NOT BLOCKED ON THE PILOT, AND THE PILOT SHOULD NOT WAIT FOR IT:** the participant was told plainly that the dependency will not show on screen, that it is our gap and not their wording, and nothing about publishing two claims in one domain is broken. **AND THE ADVICE STANDS REGARDLESS** — split, don't bundle. A dependency the graph cannot express is a smaller defect than a critique that cannot say which half it refuted. | **D** | (a) nothing; (b) nothing — coordinator-only; (c) §4's integrity fork |
| 1.27 | **An agent in the reader's own root set counts as one of their own attesters, so `min_attestations` overstates independent vouching by exactly one.** *Opened 2026-11-08 while tracing the question 1.26 recorded as NOT TRACED — and the answer is that 1.26's cheap option would not CREATE this, it would widen it.* **THE TRACE, read rather than reasoned.** `count_attestations_pure` (`coordinator:4539`) inserts the candidate into `visited` and then, for each attester, checks `roots.contains(attester)` **before any self-exclusion, of which there is none**. `direct_attesters_of` (`coordinator:4496`) collects `link.author` for every `SynapticLink` pointing at the candidate's claims and never compares that author to the candidate. **So the two cases diverge:** a candidate NOT in the root set is protected, because the `else if max_depth > 0` branch recurses and the cycle guard returns 0 — which is precisely what that guard's comment claims it does. A candidate who **IS** in the root set is not protected at all: their own link satisfies `roots.contains` directly, and counts. **CONSEQUENCE, stated narrowly because the narrow version is the true one:** for a reader with `require_attestation_from: [A, B]` and `min_attestations: 2`, candidate A needs two root-set attesters and can supply one of them themselves — **one genuine voucher plus one self-link clears a two-voucher bar.** It cannot manufacture trust from nothing: an agent outside the root set gains exactly zero, so this never promotes a stranger. What it does is **overstate independent vouching by one for every agent the reader has already named**, which is the population where `min_attestations` is the only thing still doing work. **THE SELF-LINK IS FREE AND ALREADY REACHABLE — THIS IS LIVE IN SHIPPED CODE, NOT A CONSEQUENCE OF 1.26.** `create_critique` writes a `SynapticLink` to the target claim's hash, and `validate_critique`'s only author rule is that `critique.author` matches the action author — anti-spoofing, never anti-self (`integrity:1691`). So an agent may critique their own claim, and that is all it takes. 1.26's option (b) — a claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` for citation — would add a SECOND free route to the same place, which is why that option's cost is now known rather than suspected. **IT IS A REFINEMENT OF 3.7 RATHER THAN A NEW SHAPE.** 3.7 established that `direct_attesters_of` unions a tenure-gated source with a free global one and that *a union costs what its cheapest member costs*; `docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md` carries the argument. **This row names the cheapest member yet found: the candidate.** Minting K keys costs K critiques; self-attesting costs one, from a key the reader has already chosen to trust. **NO UNIT TEST COVERS IT, AND THE NEAREST ONE LOOKS LIKE IT DOES.** Seven tests exercise the walk — direct, non-root at depth zero, transitive allowed, transitive blocked, **a two-agent A-attests-B-attests-A cycle that must terminate**, multiple distinct attesters, and the empty graph (`coordinator:6799-6889`). The two-cycle test is the one a reader would assume covers this and does not: **A to B to A is not A to A**, and only the second reaches the `roots.contains` branch before the guard. **THE DECISION, AND IT IS WHY THIS IS A `[D]`.** Excluding self is two lines in either function, but it **CHANGES AN EXISTING LENS'S ANSWERS** — every historical `is_agent_attested` verdict involving a root-set self-link flips, and under Invariant 6 nothing already written changes, so readers would get a different answer tomorrow about the same permanent records. That is a real decision rather than a fix to apply quietly. The alternative is to leave it and DOCUMENT that `min_attestations` counts self for named roots, which is honest and makes the threshold mean something different from what a reader would assume. **DO NOT ADD A UNIT TEST FIRST:** a test written against today's behaviour would enshrine the hole as intended, which is the one outcome worse than the hole. The test follows the decision. **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08: SELF IS EXCLUDED.** Excluded at BOTH layers, because they fail for different reasons — in `direct_attesters_of` the returned list would otherwise be wrong for every consumer, and in `count_attestations_pure` the guarantee belongs to the walk rather than to one of its callers, since that function takes an arbitrary closure. §2.6 is defended at two layers for the same kind of reason. **In the pure walk the check must come BEFORE the roots check**, which is the whole defect restated as an ordering constraint. The `AttestationGrant` source is excluded too: the integrity zome has no reason to refuse a self-grant, since its checks are tenure, budget and authorship and a self-grant violates none of them. **THE TESTS WERE WATCHED FAILING BEFORE THEY WERE BELIEVED, and the injection proved three things at once.** With the pure-walk exclusion removed: both new tests go RED (`...self_attestation_by_a_root_member_does_not_count`, `...self_link_cannot_pad_a_two_attester_threshold`), the new CONTROL stays green — correctly, since a genuine attester counts with or without the fix, and an assertion that only ever goes down is indistinguishable from a function returning zero — and **all seven original tests stay green, which demonstrates rather than asserts that they never covered this.** 80 tests pass restored; the attestation group is 10, from 7. **DNA HASH VERIFIED UNCHANGED** — `uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh`, byte-identical, so coordinator-only holds and nothing forks. **TWO PROSE CLAIMS WERE INVALIDATED BY THE FIX AND ARE CORRECTED IN THE SAME PASS, which is 1.20's bug class caught on the way past:** `README` §197 said *"Seven unit tests cover the walk"* (now ten, with the near-miss recorded — `A→B→A` is not `A→A`) and defined *"A directly attests B"* without requiring them to differ; and `docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md`, the document 3.7 cites, costed the attack at K keys when **the candidate themselves was cheaper at zero** — its figures were an upper bound and now say so. **WHAT THIS UNBLOCKS:** 1.26's option (b) was priced by this row, and with self excluded a claim-to-claim `SynapticLink` no longer widens an attestation hole — so that option is now genuinely coordinator-only with no fork, which is the cheapest route 1.26 has. | ~~**D**~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-08** | nothing — coordinator-only, no DNA change, no fork (hash verified identical) |
| 1.28 | ~~**The applet view — Moss detects this UI as a Tool and gets a message saying it is not implemented.**~~ **BUILT 2026-11-10, and the implementation corrected three names this row took from documentation and found two defects nothing was watching for.** *Opened 2026-11-08 out of #450, which took the dependency and wired the branch.* **WHAT SHIPPED.** `AppletView`'s three arms each render a surface: `main` is `renderTabs()` — the whole app, unchanged — `asset` is one claim read with `get_claim`, and `creatable` is the same New Claim form with the tab bar removed and both halves of the host's dialog contract honoured. `RenderInfo` is consumed rather than detected: the host's group name is on screen, and the first `WeaveClient.connect()` is made. `HolochainConnection.fromAppClient` is a third connection shape (four lines, no socket, no token) which is what keeps this one client rather than two. **THE ROW'S OWN FOUR CONSTRAINTS ALL HELD, and two of them changed shape when the source was read.** (1) The standalone app is unaffected, measured in the same run rather than argued. (2) The gate rule is enforceable rather than remembered — `hostMayGate` names `publish`, `critique`, `retract` and `join` as REFUSED explicitly, because an empty allow-list would also pass a test and would say nothing about intent. (3) HRLs persisted, WAL urls refused — **and the guard had a hole**: it was anchored (`/^weave-\d+\.\d+:\/\//`) and `weaveUrlFromWal(wal, webPrefix)` returns `https://theweave.social/wal?weave-0.12://hrl/…`, so the host's own default shareable link passed straight through into a permanent entry. Un-anchoring it turns 3 checks red and the other 10 in that section stayed green throughout, which is how it survived. (4) **No WAL parser was needed at all**: a WAL is `{ hrl: [DnaHash, ActionHash|EntryHash], context? }` — already-decoded hashes, not a string — and the url direction is `weaveUrlToWAL`, the host's own function. **THREE NAMES IN THIS ROW WERE DOC-DERIVED AND WRONG, which is #450's lesson arriving on schedule.** `MossPrivilege`/`MossRole` exist but are not what a Tool is handed (`myAccountabilitiesPerGroup()` returns `MossAccountability[]` per group, async and plural); `pickAsset` is `client.assets.userSelectAsset(from?)` returning `WAL | undefined`; and `notifyCreated` did not exist at all — it was invented from a sentence, and declaring it hid that `view.resolve(wal)` is MANDATORY inside a creatable render while `notifyFrame` is optional. **TWO MEASURED DEFECTS, both in code nothing was watching.** First, **`WeaveClient.connect()` CAN NEVER RESOLVE**: with no `window.__WEAVE_RENDER_INFO__` it awaits an `applet-iframe-ready` event with no timeout and an unused `_reject` (`api.js:189`), and `isWeaveContext()` is true on the `applet:` protocol ALONE — so an unguarded await there is a permanently blank page with nothing in the console, strictly worse than the notice this row replaced. Hence `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` and a stated `applet-unavailable`. Second, **the cross-group arm carries no `appletClient` and its `view.type` is also the string `'main'`**, so switching on `view.type` alone renders the single-group view and then reads a client that is not there. Declined in words. **AND THE HARNESS FOUND A THIRD, IN THIS SESSION'S OWN NEW CODE:** the asset panel said *"Nothing came back for that address"* on its first frame, about a claim that was there, because `bootApplet` renders once before starting the read — **6.13's finding, unprompted, written by someone who had just read 6.13**. Caught only by the MutationObserver; all three checks that READ the panel were green. Fixed with a named `unasked` state, and `unreadable` split from `absent` on the same principle. **GATED BY TWO HARNESSES, and one of them was running nowhere at all before this:** `applet-prototype-check.mjs` (48 pure checks, registered in `ui.yml` for the first time) and `scripts/live-verify/applet-view.mjs` (42 browser checks against a FAKE Moss host whose `appletClient` bridges to a real conductor, so *a Tool render reaches real DHT data without touching the Admin API* is measured, the same way `launcher-packaging.mjs` measures the Launcher). **STILL UNPROVEN: a real Moss install.** None exists here, and the fake host is faithful only in the respect that matters — the client comes from outside the page and the page has no admin access. **COST, re-measured:** the view added 8,556 bytes to the bundle (679,703 → 688,259, +1.26%) on top of the 2,726 the dependency already cost. | ~~M~~ **BUILT 2026-11-10** | ~~nothing~~ **nothing — closed, and gated by `applet-prototype-check.mjs` and `applet-view.mjs` in `ui.yml`** |
| 1.29 | **Nine harnesses carrying 210 assertions are run by no workflow, and not one of them says so.** *Opened 2026-11-10, found while closing 1.28 — which fixed this exact defect in a tenth file and then asked whether there were others.* `mobile-ui/applet-prototype-check.mjs` shipped with #450, carried 14 checks, and appeared in no workflow at all for two days; it is registered now. **Sweeping for the same shape found nine more**, every one of which `process.exit(1)`s on a failure and is therefore a gate rather than an experiment: `network-partition` (26), `superior-egg` (33), `registry-install` (6), `index-lens` (15), `microbiome` (19), `proprioception` (34), `peer-signal` (19), `secretion` (24), `standing` (34). **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A DEFECT RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE.** This directory already has deliberately-unrun files and they ALL declare it in their own README row — `scale-dht`, `dht-isolation-probe`, `stall-bisect`, `stall-threshold`, `strategy-compare` and `clock-skew-reveal` each say **"an experiment, not a gate and not in CI"** and give the reason (twelve conductors would measure the runner; `libfaketime` moves a clock and does not belong on a shared machine). **None of the nine above says anything of the kind**, so a reader of `live-verify/README.md` cannot tell a gate that runs from a gate that does not, and the suite's own index reports 69 harnesses while CI invokes 25. **AND `check-harness-counts.mjs` CANNOT CATCH IT, which is why it needs a row rather than a fix.** That gate asserts the README's four index claims against what EXISTS — table rows, `CHROMIUM` importers, the suite count, and `ui.yml`'s own header against `ui.yml`'s own invocations. Every one of those is currently green. Nothing compares the set of harnesses to the set that any workflow runs, so a harness can be perfectly documented, perfectly counted, and never executed. **A gate that does not run is indistinguishable from one that was never written, except that it reads like coverage** — and this list exists to catch exactly that shape. **THE WORK IS A DECISION FIRST AND WIRING SECOND, and the decision is per harness, not global.** Some of the nine plainly cannot go in `ui.yml`: `network-partition` needs `scripts/netns.sh` and a real partition, and `registry-install` installs from a registry. Others look like ordinary single-conductor harnesses that were simply never wired. So the unit of work is: for each, either **register it** in the workflow whose setup it already needs, or **declare it manual in its README row with the reason**, in the words the six existing experiments use. The cheap, mechanical half worth doing either way is a **gate for the gate** — assert that every `check()`-bearing harness is either invoked by a workflow or carries a manual declaration, which is the only thing that stops this recurring. **BUILT AND CLOSED 2026-11-13, and the row's own count was the first thing the sweep corrected: TEN harnesses, not nine, and 232 assertions rather than 210.** The tenth is **`transitive-gossip`**, and it was missed for an instructive reason — its reason for being out is real, measured and written down, **in `network.yml`'s header**, which is not where a reader of the index looks. **Two of the ten were already explained; what they lacked was the explanation in the row the index points at.** **THE GATE, which the row called the cheap mechanical half and which turned out to be the part that found everything else.** `check-harness-counts.mjs` gained a fifth assertion: a harness with at least one `check()` call site is either invoked by a workflow STEP or says in its own `scripts/live-verify/README.md` row that it is *"an experiment, not a gate and not in CI"*. **That phrasing was not invented for the gate** — six harnesses wrote it before the gate existed, each with its real reason attached, and gating it turns a convention they happened to share into something a seventh cannot quietly skip. **A MENTION IS NOT AN INVOCATION**, so it reads lines and skips commented ones: `network.yml`'s header discusses two harnesses at length precisely because they do NOT run. That also means a step commented out reads as a harness that stopped running — and **the injection for exactly that found a hole in one of the four checks ALREADY in the file**: `ui.yml`'s header count matched commented-out invocations, so commenting a harness out left it reading 27 of 27 while the job ran 26. Fixed in the same commit. **A harness with no `check()` sites is out of scope and listed as such rather than silently skipped** — it asserts nothing, so running it would gate nothing (`dht-isolation-probe`, `stall-bisect`, `stall-threshold`, `strategy-compare`, `peering-rate`). **THEN THE DECISION, PER HARNESS, AND MEASURED FIRST RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT.** Every candidate was run locally on a freshly cleaned sandbox before anything was wired: **`index-lens` 5s, `microbiome` 4s, `proprioception` 8s, `secretion` 6s, `standing` 5s, `superior-egg` 8s — 159 assertions for 36 seconds of harness time**, all registered in `conductor.yml`, which already provides everything they need. **`peer-signal` 18s on a two-node network**, registered in `network.yml`. **`registry-install` got a workflow of its own** — weekly plus manual dispatch, never a required context — because the harness's own header had asked for exactly that from the day it shipped: *"RUN IT ON A SCHEDULE, NOT PER-PUSH ... That is also why it is NOT added to any required context."* **The decision had been taken and written down; only the wiring was missing, and nothing could tell that apart from a decision never made.** `network-partition` and `transitive-gossip` stay out and now SAY so in their rows, carrying the measured reasons out of `network.yml`'s header: twenty-five minutes and iptables in a throwaway namespace for the first, and for the second a 3-in-25 failure rate whose cause is still unknown after four falsified hypotheses. **AND THE FINDING WORTH READING IS NOT THE WIRING.** **`index-lens` and `superior-egg` called `process.exit` ONLY in their failure branch**, and an open `@holochain/client` websocket is a **ref'd handle that nothing in this suite closes** — so on the happy path each printed ALL CHECKS PASSED and then hung for ever. Watched: `index-lens` killed by a 240-second `timeout` with every check green, `superior-egg` by a 300-second one. **Measured rather than inferred** — a four-line probe opening one admin and one app socket and doing nothing else never exits either, while the control `past-cone` exits in 6 seconds because it ends on `process.exit(failures === 0 ? 0 : 1)`, which is the form every harness already in CI uses. **So two of these were STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE OF REPORTING SUCCESS: in CI they would have read as a job timeout rather than a red check.** That is a large part of why they were never switched on — the defect is visible the moment somebody runs one, and nobody had, which is this row's whole thesis arriving as evidence for itself. **Result: 62 harnesses invoked by a workflow, 5 declaring themselves manual, 67 that assert anything — and the gate now refuses any other state.** | ~~**M** for the sweep and the new gate; **D** for whether a given harness belongs in CI at all~~ **M BUILT AND D TAKEN 2026-11-13 — every one of the ten resolved, eight registered and two declared** | ~~nothing~~ **nothing — closed, and the new assertion is itself gated by `check-harness-counts.mjs` in `live-verify.yml`** |
| 1.30 | **Moss can raise a notification in its own frame, which reaches somebody whose app is CLOSED — and that, narrowly, is the whole of this row.** **ITS ORIGINAL PREMISE WAS FALSE AND IS WITHDRAWN, 2026-11-14:** it read ~~*"Moss delivers notifications for free, and 6.15's push half is open because the PROTOCOL's way of doing it is expensive — so inside Moss the cost that blocks that decision does not exist"*~~. The protocol's way is NOT expensive, so there was never a cost inside Moss to sidestep — see the correction below. **The row survives only as the app-closed difference, which is real and much smaller than what it was filed as.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by auditing what 1.28 did NOT take.* **THE BLOCKER IT SIDESTEPS, restated so the two are not confused.** 6.15's `[D]` is push-versus-pull. ~~Its cost is concrete: `send_peer_signal` writes a permanent CapGrant **per call on the SENDER's chain**, so notifying on every critique would grow a critic's own chain in proportion to how much they disagree — a permanent per-act cost on exactly the behaviour this protocol wants more of.~~ **THE COST CLAIM THAT STOOD HERE WAS FALSE, AND IT WAS A MISREAD INJECTION RECORD — CORRECTED 2026-11-14.** It said `send_peer_signal` writes a permanent CapGrant **per call on the SENDER's chain**. That sentence is `scripts/live-verify/peer-signal.mjs`'s own **NEGATIVE EVIDENCE** entry `CH` — a defect somebody *injected* on 2026-11-01 and watched go **2 red** — quoted as though it were the measurement. What the harness actually asserts, on separate conductors and gated in `network.yml`, is *the SENDER's chain did not grow across 5 signals* and *the RECEIVER's chain did not grow either*. The source agrees: `send_peer_signal` (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:8181`) calls `send_remote_signal` and writes nothing, and `open_signal_channel` (`:8116`) creates its grant **only if one is not already there**. **THE REAL COST IS ONE ENTRY PER AGENT ONCE, AND ZERO PER SIGNAL FOR EVER** — which `scripts/live-verify/README.md` has stated correctly all along. **Push is not the expensive branch; nothing is.** **`WeaveClient.notifyFrame(notifications: Array<FrameNotification>)`** (`@theweave/api/dist/api.d.ts:267`) writes to no chain at all: Moss raises the notification in its own frame. `FrameNotification` is `{ title, body, notification_type, icon_src }` (`types.d.ts:43`), which is enough for *somebody critiqued your claim* and carries no ranking, no count and no ordering. **IT IS MENTIONED IN THIS CODEBASE AND NEVER CALLED** — `mobile-ui/src/applet.ts:340` names it in a comment describing the host surface, which is the same shape as `profilesClient` in 1.31: received, documented, dropped. **AND IT DOES NOT ANSWER 6.15, WHICH IS THE POINT OF FILING IT SEPARATELY RATHER THAN INSIDE IT.** This reaches a reader only when the app is running AS A MOSS TOOL. **Every pilot participant runs the standalone desktop build, so a notification built here reaches none of them**, and 6.15's `[D]` stands untouched for the only people currently using this protocol. What it does is make the Moss arm the place where push is cheap, ~~which is worth knowing before anybody pays the CapGrant price to have it everywhere.~~ **There is no CapGrant price to pay — see the correction above. The protocol arm is cheap too, and needs no host.** ~~**THE HONEST SHAPE OF THE WORK:** one call at the point `create_critique` succeeds on somebody else's claim, guarded on the applet arm being live~~ **THAT CALL SITE WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED, 2026-11-15 — IT WOULD HAVE NOTIFIED THE WRONG PERSON.** `notifyFrame` raises a notice in the LOCAL user's Moss frame, so calling it where `create_critique` succeeds would tell the CRITIC about their own act and tell the author nothing. The description predates 6.15's receiver by two days: when this row was opened on 2026-11-13 this client handled no signals at all, so there was no inbound event to hang it on and the outbound one looked like the only seam. **The right seam exists now** — `wireSignals()`'s `onSignal` handler (`mobile-ui/src/main.ts:677`), the point at which THIS user learns somebody critiqued THEIR claim. **AND THE `[D]` IS TAKEN 2026-11-15, DELEGATED: YES, NOTIFY — and the question had already been half-answered by the thing it was waiting on.** The blocker column said to settle 6.15's `[D]` first *because building one arm may quietly answer the other by precedent*, and it did. 6.15 decided the protocol DOES tell the author, as **a plain line with no badge, count, dot or ordering**, opt-in by a gesture. So *whether a Tool should notify when the protocol deliberately does not* is a question about a premise that no longer holds: **the protocol does.** `FrameNotification` is `{ title, body, notification_type, icon_src }` (`types.d.ts:43`, read rather than recalled) and carries no count and no ranking field, so the Moss arm can match 6.15's shape exactly rather than approximate it. **THE OPT-IN NEEDS NO SECOND GESTURE, which is the property that makes this cheap:** the notice is driven by the 6.15 signal, and that signal only arrives for an agent who opened the channel — so 6.15's gesture gates the Moss notification too, and nothing is raised for somebody who never opted in. Moss's own per-Tool notification settings are the second layer, and `applet.ts`'s interface comment already records `notifyFrame` as *optional and user-configurable*. **WHAT IT STILL DOES NOT DO:** reach a pilot participant. Every one of them runs the standalone desktop build, so this is correct work with no reader today, and that is a sequencing fact rather than an argument against it. | ~~M for the call, D for whether notifying is this client's business~~ **`[D]` TAKEN 2026-11-15 (yes, notify, matching 6.15's shape) and the call is BUILT** | nothing technical — but it is worth settling 6.15's `[D]` first, since building one arm may quietly answer the other by precedent. ~~the cheap arm / the expensive one~~ **neither arm is expensive; that framing came from the cost claim corrected 2026-11-14** |
| 1.31 | **The app is handed real agent profiles by Moss and drops them — which is a better answer to 6.23 than anything this client could mint, and reaches nobody in the pilot.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by the same audit.* **WHAT IS BEING DROPPED, read out of the installed types rather than the docs.** `RenderInfo` for an applet view carries **`profilesClient: ProfilesClient`** (`@theweave/api/dist/types.d.ts:107` and `:180`), and `WeaveClient` exposes **`appletParticipants(): Promise<AgentPubKey[]>`** (`api.d.ts:295`). `mobile-ui/src/applet.ts:243-244` names `profilesClient` **in a comment** documenting the shape and nothing consumes it. **THE CONTRAST THAT MAKES THIS A REAL GAP RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP:** the sibling field `groupProfiles` IS consumed — `applet-host.ts:215` reads `appletView?.groupProfiles?.[0]?.name` for the group label — so the GROUP has a name on screen and the PEOPLE in it do not. **WHY IT BEARS ON 6.23's `[D]`.** That row asks whether to mint a key-derived identity — a colour, a mnemonic, an identicon — because twelve base64 characters are not something a reader can compare, and it rules out a display NAME on the grounds that nothing in the protocol binds one to a key. **Moss binds one.** Inside a Moss group the nickname is the group's own, vouched for by the host rather than asserted by this client, so the Moss arm does not need to invent anything: it asks. **That is strictly better than an identicon where it is available, and available nowhere else.** **AND IT MUST NOT BE READ AS ANSWERING 6.23**, which is why this is its own row: the standalone desktop build has no profiles client, no group and no host to ask, and **that is the build every pilot participant runs**. A reader on the desktop app would still be comparing key prefixes. 6.23's `[M]` — *same author as an earlier critique* — already shipped and is the answer that reaches them; 6.23's `[D]` remains exactly as open as it was. **THE TRAP WORTH NAMING NOW:** a nickname is a thing people choose and can change, so a byline that says a name is making a weaker claim than a byline that says a key, and both on screen at once needs a rule about which one a critique is held to. That is not a styling question and it is not answered here. **THE `[D]` WAS TAKEN 2026-11-16 — A HOST-SUPPLIED NAME MAY BE SHOWN, AND THE KEY STILL BINDS — AND IT IS BUILT.** The decision went to the middle option on the ground the row itself named: a nickname is chosen and changeable, so a byline showing ONLY a name makes a weaker claim than one showing a key while looking to a reader like a stronger one. So both are on screen, the name first because that is the half a reader scans, and **the rule about which one a critique is held to is: the key.** `agentLabel()` in `main.ts` is that rule in one function, and it replaced `short()` at the fourteen call sites that render an AGENT while leaving the hash sites alone — a hash has no name and routing one through a name lookup is a read that can only ever miss. **`WeaveHost.participantName` is the whole surface**, `profilesClient.getAgentProfile(agent, true)` behind it, and three things are deliberately indistinguishable to its caller: no profile, no host, and a local-only read that missed. Telling them apart would mean making a claim about a person out of a cache miss. **`getAgentProfile` RETURNS AN `EntryRecord<Profile>` AND THE NICKNAME IS AT `.entry.nickname`** — reading `.nickname` off the record yields `undefined`, which renders as a missing name rather than as an error, so the fixture is shaped like the real return precisely so it cannot certify that bug. **AND IT WAS NOT BUILT ALONE**, which this row required: 1.32 shipped in the same change, so neither is a surface built and left unreachable. **GATED BY `applet-view.mjs` PASS 9, five checks**, two of them controls — an agent the host has no name for still renders bare on the same screen, and no profile read appears on the bridge, so a name being shown is a name that was fetched rather than a constant being painted. **The cached names are cleared on DISCONNECT and not on a domain read**, unlike every other agent-keyed cache in that module, because this is the only one whose authority is the host rather than the DHT. | ~~M for the read, D for whether a host-supplied name may stand where a key stands~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-16** | **nothing. 6.23's `[D]` is untouched** — the standalone build every pilot participant runs still has nobody to ask, which is why this was always its own row |
| 1.32 | **6.24 left a sub-decision open — a panel LEFT open goes stale — and Moss already carries the push side that would settle it without polling.** *Opened 2026-11-13 by the same audit.* **WHAT 6.24 SETTLED AND WHAT IT DID NOT.** Opening a critique panel now re-reads it, which covers the gesture a reader makes. What it does not cover is a panel somebody is already looking at: a critique published while they read it is invisible, and the two options the row named were **polling** — a continuous read nobody asked for — and **saying nothing**. **WHAT MOSS OFFERS INSTEAD**, read out of the installed types: `WeaveClient.sendRemoteSignal(payload: Uint8Array, toAgents?: AgentPubKey[])` (`api.d.ts:305`), `onRemoteSignal(callback)` (`:313`), and `onPeerStatusUpdate(callback)` (`:198`), with `peerStatusStore: ReadonlyPeerStatusStore` on `RenderInfo` itself (`types.d.ts:181`). **None of the four is referenced anywhere in `mobile-ui/src/`.** A remote signal is ephemeral and writes to no chain — it is not `send_peer_signal`. ~~and carries none of that extern's per-call CapGrant cost (see 1.30)~~ **THAT CLAUSE WAS STALE AND IS STRUCK, 2026-11-15: `send_peer_signal` HAS NO PER-CALL CapGrant COST.** The sentence cited 1.30 as its authority, and 1.30 is precisely where that cost claim was **corrected as false** on 2026-11-14 — it was `peer-signal.mjs`'s NEGATIVE EVIDENCE entry `CH`, an injected defect, quoted as a measurement. So this row's parenthetical pointed at its own refutation, which is the same error §0 logs six instances of and is why a correction has to be chased into every citing row rather than filed where it was found. **The contrast this clause was drawing is void** — both mechanisms are cheap, the protocol's costs one entry per agent once and zero per signal for ever, and so Moss offers no cost advantage here. What it still offers is the thing below: an event instead of a timer. **WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT SHAPE AND POLLING IS NOT:** a signal arrives exactly when something happened, so a panel refreshes on an event rather than on a timer, and a quiet claim costs nothing. Polling pays continuously to discover that nothing changed, which is the trade 6.24 declined to make blind. **AND THE PROTOCOL'S OWN SIGNAL IS NO HELP HERE, which 6.24 already established and is worth not re-deriving:** `SignalPayload::NewCritique` is declared at `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5234` and **emitted nowhere**, and `emit_signal` reaches only the LOCAL conductor's websocket clients — so a critique published by a remote agent emits on their node and never arrives. Moss's remote signal is a different mechanism that actually crosses agents. **THE SAME CEILING AS 1.30 AND 1.31, AND IT IS THE REASON ALL THREE ARE SEPARATE ROWS RATHER THAN ONE: this reaches only the Moss arm, and every pilot participant runs the standalone desktop build.** 6.24's sub-`[D]` is therefore **not** answered by this and must not be closed by it — what changes is that the Moss arm has a third option that is neither polling nor silence. **A CAUTION ON SCOPE:** a live-updating panel is a surface changing under a reader while they decide what to say about it, which is the one thing §4.4 cares about most. Arriving critiques appearing silently mid-read is a different defect from stale ones, not an improvement on them — so the useful version of this probably marks that something arrived rather than re-rendering underneath them. **THE `[D]` WAS TAKEN 2026-11-16 — A PANEL MAY NOT CHANGE WHILE IT IS BEING READ — AND IT IS BUILT.** The decision is the one this row's own CAUTION ON SCOPE pointed at, taken rather than left implied: an announcement **marks** that something arrived and the reader chooses when to take the re-read. That is the same shape 1.30 settled on a day earlier — reach somebody without taking an interruption a disagreement has not earned — and it keeps §4.4's concern intact, because a surface rewriting itself while somebody decides what to say about it is a DIFFERENT defect from a stale one rather than a fix for it. **`sendRemoteSignal` / `onRemoteSignal` are both wired**, through `WeaveHost.announceCritique` and `onCritiqueAnnounced`, with the payload codec kept PURE in `applet.ts` so it is exercisable with no host: UTF-8 JSON carrying a claim reference and a namespaced tag, **and nothing else** — no critique, no author, and no count, a count being the tally Invariant #1 refuses arriving by the back door. **THE CHANNEL IS GROUP-WIDE AND NOT APPLET-SCOPED**, so another Tool's traffic is the ordinary case and is dropped in silence. **A SIGNAL FOR A PANEL NOBODY HAS OPEN IS DROPPED TOO**, which is what keeps this from becoming a notification channel by accident and routing around 6.15's opt-in. **GATED BY `applet-view.mjs` PASS 10, eleven checks, three of them controls** — another claim's announcement, another Tool's signal, and a malformed payload must each mark nothing. **AND THE PASS MEASURES THE DECISION ITSELF**: a real critique is filed before the real announcement, so the item count can move; it does not move when the marker appears (3 before, 3 after) and does move when the reader takes the re-read (3 → 4). **Both halves were probed by arming the defect** and recorded as injections H and I. | ~~M for the wiring, D for whether a panel may change while it is being read~~ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-11-16** | **nothing. 6.24's sub-`[D]` is NOT closed by this** and must not be recorded as closed by it — the Moss arm now has a third option that is neither polling nor silence, and the standalone arm has the same two it had |
| 1.33 | **This Tool answers Moss's own search box with `[]`, and the reason written beside that stub EXPIRED THE DAY BEFORE 1.31 AND 1.32 WERE BUILT IN THE SAME FILE.** *Opened 2026-11-16, by auditing the whole `@theweave/api` surface against what `mobile-ui/src/` consumes rather than against what the rows remembered.* **THE STUB AND ITS STALE JUSTIFICATION.** `services.search` is left as the library's default, which is `async () => []` (`api.js:113`). The comment above it in `applet-host.ts` gives the reason: searching would need a domain enumeration, and *"whether this protocol should offer one AT ALL is an open decision — open-work 6.12's `[D]`"*. **6.12 WAS DECIDED ON 2026-11-15 AND ITS DECISION NAMES THE INDEX: *"The index is `get_membranes`"*.** Read off the source rather than the row: `get_membranes()` (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:2815`) resolves a **network-wide registry anchor** and returns every registered membrane via `GetStrategy::Network` — deliberately link-only with no chain-query fallback, so it is an index of what the NETWORK has, not of what this device happens to hold. **The enumeration the stub says would have to be invented both exists and ships.** **AND THE STUB'S SECOND OBJECTION IS ANSWERED BY THE SAME FACT.** It warned that a result *"scraped from whatever this device happens to have loaded"* would be the looks-wired-and-is-not shape. A read off the registry anchor is not that. **That objection stays as a CONSTRAINT on any implementation rather than as a reason not to have one: a partial search is worse than none, and the honest failure is an error, never a short list.** **WHY THIS IS A CONTRADICTION AND NOT MERELY A GAP, which is what makes it worth a row.** 6.12 decided that **founding is the price of being findable**, and #490 built three surfaces telling a user exactly that as they type a domain name. **Meanwhile the Tool declines to answer the host's own find-things box.** The app promises findability in-app and delivers nothing at the one surface in Moss where a group member goes looking. **HALF OF IT ALREADY SHIPS, which changes the size of this.** `search` returns `Array<WAL>` (`api.d.ts:54`) and Moss renders each result by calling **`getAssetInfo`, which this Tool already implements** — so the rendering, the truncation rule and the bad-WAL behaviour are all done. What is missing is only the part that produces the hashes, and building a WAL from a claim is a shape `finishCreatable` already handles. **THE `[D]`, AND IT IS A COST DECISION RATHER THAN A DESIGN ONE.** The naive implementation is one `get_membranes` plus one `get_claims_by_domain` PER DOMAIN, per query — an N+1 network read on every keystroke in a search box belonging to another program, with no debounce this Tool controls. **That is the thing to settle before any code**, and the options are not obvious: cap the domains read, search only domains the reader has visited (partial, and therefore refused by the constraint above), require a minimum filter length, or add a real search extern to the DNA — which is a protocol change and a different row. **NOT STARTED, AND DELIBERATELY SO:** 1.31's rule applies — a surface that looks wired and is not is worse than one that is honestly empty, and today's empty result is at least honest. | M for the wiring once the cost is settled, **D for the cost** | **the `[D]`. 6.12's `[D]`, which this stub names, is GONE — decided 2026-11-15** |
| 1.34 | **A reader's root set names PEOPLE, and nothing in this project says how a person becomes a key — Flowsta answered that in detail on 2026-11-09 and the answer was never recorded.** *Opened 2026-11-17 from Eric Doriean's reply, a week after it arrived.* **WHAT HE ANSWERED, and it is his account of his own system rather than anything measured here.** **(1) NO CENTRE FOR KEYS.** A Flowsta DID *is* the identity's agent key — `did:flowsta:u` followed by the key in base64url — so a client turns a DID into an `AgentPubKey` **locally, with no lookup**. **(2) A CENTRE FOR NAMES, at the moment of naming**: *"who is @name"* is answered by their servers and nothing else. **(3) THE FLOWSTA KEY IS NOT THE KEY THAT SIGNS ON THIS DHT**, which is the part most likely to be assumed wrong. The bridge is `flowsta-agent-linking` (**v0.4.0 for Holochain 0.7.1**, v0.3.0 for 0.6.x, docs at `docs.flowsta.com/holochain/agent-linking`): the person's Vault and this app's conductor **both sign the sorted key pair**, the resulting `IsSamePersonEntry` **lives on OUR DHT**, and `get_linked_agents` turns a root-set member's Flowsta key into their app keys there. **(4) DOES IT SURVIVE A NEW KEY — mostly yes.** The identity key derives from the person's 24-word recovery phrase, so it is the same on every device and after a reinstall; a new app key here is just another link to the same identity; **one app key links to exactly one identity, one identity links many app keys**, and old links stand until one of the two named keys revokes them. **So a root set naming people by Flowsta key keeps meaning the same people.** **(5) TWO EXCEPTIONS HE VOLUNTEERED:** accounts predating their Vault got a new key when they moved into it — the DID resolver lists the earlier one under `alsoKnownAs`, but **links already made under the old key stay under it** — and **there is NO KEY ROTATION TODAY**, so a lost or compromised phrase has no path that carries the identity to a new key. **(6) THE ESCAPE FROM THE CENTRE, and he proposed it himself rather than being pushed to it:** resolve a name **once**, when the reader adds somebody; show them the profile it resolved to so they can confirm it is who they meant; **store the key or DID in the root set and never ask again.** After that *"we can't change what your records mean, and an outage on our side doesn't touch the lens."* **(7) WHERE HE PUSHED BACK, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** A wrong answer at naming time is the residual risk — from them or from anything in between — mitigated by showing the resolved profile **and by accepting a key or DID handed over directly** (profile link, QR code). And the sharper one: ***"opt-in per reader holds only if Flowsta isn't the only way in. If your client makes us the default resolver, most readers will use it, and you have a centre in practice if not in the protocol."*** **That is a constraint on THIS client, not on his service, and it is the reason this row exists rather than a TODO in a branch.** Also: an app's **first** link needs internet, because the Vault checks the app's registration with Flowsta; later links use the cached check and a finished link verifies without them. **(8) AND THE SYBIL ANSWER IS A REFUSAL, WHICH IS WHY IT IS CREDIBLE:** a Flowsta identity tells you **which** identity somebody is, **not** that they are not one of several held by the same person. *"So the root set still gets its strength from readers naming people they actually know."* His proof-of-humanity plan is explicitly **not one credential from one issuer** but signed attestations a person collects from sources they choose, **each service deciding which it accepts** — which he notes *"follows the same rule you've written into your ordering code: no single point of calibration."* **It is in planning and there is nothing to build on.** **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO: it does not make 1.27 or 3.1 easier**, and it is not a sybil defence. A root set still means what it meant. What it would buy is that **naming somebody stops being a base64 paste**. **THE `[D]`: whether to integrate at all, and if so under constraint (7) — that Flowsta must not be the default or only resolver**, since a default makes it a centre in practice whatever the protocol says. **Not started.** | **D** — whether to integrate, bound by the not-the-default constraint | **the `[D]`. Nothing blocks the reading of it; the integration artifact exists (`flowsta-agent-linking` v0.4.0) and is third-party** |
| 1.35 | **This file's central promise is unenforced: it says a line whose blocker has gone is a line to act on, and on 2026-11-16/17 FOUR records claimed to be blocked on something already answered.** *Opened 2026-11-17, after the session that found all four.* **THE FOUR, because the mix is what sizes the gate.** (1) **7.29** — a participant said YES to a second always-on node on 2026-11-10 and the row still asked the question six days later. (2) **1.33** — `applet-host.ts` justified a stubbed `search` by citing **6.12's `[D]` as an open decision**, and 6.12 had been DECIDED the day before, with its decision naming the index; the stale comment survived while 1.31 and 1.32 were built **one function below it.** (3) **Eric Doriean's depth sweep** — accepted 2026-11-09, while the memory went on saying *"nothing is outstanding on this side, the ball is his"* for a week. (4) **7.29's `[M]` half** — *"stores everything continuously"*, never re-measured since the day it was built, and false as written. **All four were found by a human reading the thing NEXT TO the work instead of the summary of it, which is not a method that scales.** **ONLY ONE OF THE FOUR IS MECHANICALLY CATCHABLE, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT OF THIS ROW.** Cases (1) and (3) turn on a message arriving in somebody's inbox — **evidence that does not exist in this repository**, so no gate can see it. Case (4) is a measurement going stale, which is not a textual fact at all. **Case (2) is a CROSS-REFERENCE between two things both in the tree**, and that one is string work: a citation of the form `<row>'s [D]` on one side, and on the other a row whose owner column already says `~~**D**~~` or *the `[D]` is TAKEN*. **A row promising to catch all four would be the overclaim this file exists to prevent.** **PROTOTYPED 2026-11-17 RATHER THAN PROPOSED, and the numbers are why this is a REPORT and not yet a gate.** Against `applet-host.ts` as it stood before #502 it fires **exactly once — the real defect, 1/1 recall.** Against the current tree it fires **once more, and that hit is a FALSE POSITIVE of a single well-defined class**: text that **quotes or strikes a stale claim in order to describe or correct it** (`~~It is the only open decision~~ — FALSE`, or a wrong summary line quoted as an example of a defect). Unfiltered the same scan returns **25 hits across 12 rows with settled `[D]`s**, nearly all legitimate references to what a decision SAID. **SO THE SHAPE IS SETTLED BY EVIDENCE: it ships as a REPORT THAT EXITS ZERO**, exactly as `check-prose-claims.mjs` already does for the same reason — that script's own output records **63 false reds on correct prose** when claims were compared naively, and tuning three successive regexes against ONE positive example is how a gate gets over-fitted to its own test case. **It may be promoted to failing only after its false-positive rate is measured on a real corpus, not on the one example that motivated it.** **TWO IMPLEMENTATION FACTS THE PROTOTYPE PAID FOR.** (a) **Normalise newlines and ` * ` comment prefixes before any phrase match.** This codebase wraps prose at about 76 columns, so *"is an open decision"* arrives as `is an\n *  open decision` and **every multi-word phrase silently fails depending on where the wrap falls** — one prototype run reported a clean 0/0 that was really a broken matcher, which is [[an-exit-code-is-not-a-measurement]] in a regex. (b) The settled-side test is cheap and already reliable: `check-row-status.mjs` parses these rows with `split(' | ')` today and is the precedent for the whole shape — it catches *"the disagreement between a row's two halves, which is a mechanical fact"* and explicitly refuses the human judgement. **This row is that same trick applied between two rows, or between a row and a comment.** **WHAT IT WOULD NOT DO, listed so nobody reads this as rot-proofing:** it cannot know a person replied, cannot know a measurement is stale, cannot read a memory file, and cannot judge whether a citation is historical — only whether it sits beside words asserting openness. **The other three cases need a human re-read, and the only honest systemic answer to those is to re-read the thing beside the work, which is what found them.** **BUILT 2026-11-17 as `scripts/check-stale-blockers.mjs`, AND THE FIRST THING IT DID WAS FIND THREE DEFECTS NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR.** Three live citations of **6.15's `[D]` — decided, built and gated on 2026-11-15** — still say it is open: `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:1189` (*"is 6.15's `[D]` and is deliberately not here"*, **four lines above the built push half it says is absent**), `scripts/live-verify/bridge-signal.mjs:318` (*"that is `send_remote_signal`, 6.15's `[D]`, and it is not built"*), and `scripts/live-verify/own-critiques.mjs:20` (*"Push is 6.15's `[D]` and stays undecided"*). **So the row's own estimate understated the yield in the direction that matters: one catchable CASE in four, yes — but that one case had FOUR INSTANCES, not one**, and the three new ones date from the days around 6.15's close, exactly as 1.33's did. **RECALL IS CONFIRMED AGAINST THE TREE AS IT STOOD BEFORE #502**, run in a throwaway worktree at `4d159b6^`: `applet-host.ts` fires there and fires **UNANNOTATED**. On today's tree the same file fires **annotated as the known false-positive class**. **That before/after is a CONTROL for the annotation rather than a claim about it** — one file, one fix, two buckets. **THREE MATCHER FACTS THIS BUILD PAID FOR, each found by the scan being wrong rather than by review.** (a) **The first vocabulary carried only the "has it been DECIDED" family and missed two of the three live defects**, because both assert the decision's status by saying its WORK has not happened (*"it is not built"*, *"deliberately not here"*); the has-it-been-BUILT family is in the vocabulary now. (b) **The correction-class annotation, scoped over the whole 220-character window, mislabelled a REAL defect as a false positive** — `own-critiques.mjs` says *"stays undecided"* and then, three sentences later and about something else entirely, says *"THE REASON GIVEN HERE WAS FALSE"*. It is scoped to the citation's own sentence and its two neighbours now, which is where a correction actually sits. (c) **A row id contains a dot, so a naive sentence split cuts every citation in half** and the neighbours become fragments of the citation itself — masked before splitting. **THE LEDGER IS REPORTED SEPARATELY, AND THAT TOO IS A MEASUREMENT.** A citation inside `open-work.md` sits three columns from the owner column that settles it, so it cannot be the defect this report is named for — which is a citation OUT OF SIGHT of the row, the shape all four real ones had. **And this file is where the noise lives: it narrates every decision's history and quotes the stale wording it corrected, so the split on 2026-11-17 was 4 elsewhere against 11 here** — and **this row's own entry, quoting the three defects below, took the ledger side from 4 hits to 11 while it was being written.** Folding them into one list is how a report stops being read; they are printed as a labelled, non-actionable tail rather than hidden. **AND TWO THINGS WERE REMOVED BY MEASUREMENT:** `blocked on` and `waiting on` left the vocabulary because across this corpus they matched only unrelated prose, and **this script excludes ITSELF by name, printed in its own output** — it quotes the defects it hunts for, so it matched itself four times, which is an artifact of carrying a corpus rather than a fact about the tree. **EXTENDED 2026-11-18 AFTER IT MISSED THE WORST DEFECT IT HAS FOUND, and the miss is the reusable part.** `mobile-ui/src/main.ts:621` justified a design choice with *"Push is the row's `[D]` half and stays undecided"* — **beside a cost claim this repository had measured FALSE on 2026-11-14**, in the one place where a design's stated reason is read — and the scan could not see it, because it was looking for a ROW ID and the citation says *"the row's"*. **There is nothing to resolve: which row "the row" means lives in a doc comment above, or in the reader's head.** So bare citations are now reported as a THIRD, **UNADJUDICATED** section — the report says where to look and a human says which row. **THE PATTERN WAS NARROWED BY MEASUREMENT, in the direction that costs recall.** A first version also matched a plain `` the `[D]` `` and returned **five further hits of which NONE was a defect**: inside a file whose header names its row that is ordinary self-reference, and the rows it pointed at (6.23, 6.14) are genuinely open. **The possessive form is the one worth flagging, because it points at an external referent while giving no way to find it** — the shape that looks resolvable and is not — and **both real defects are in that form.** With the same openness filter applied, the unadjudicated list was **exactly the two live defects** when measured against the tree before #510, and `reading-a-thread.mjs`'s *"the row's `[D]` WAS about where"* was correctly excluded by tense alone. **Two for two is NOT a measured false-positive rate and is not claimed as one; three examples is an anecdote.** **AND THE LIST IS EMPTY ON THE CURRENT TREE, BECAUSE #510 FIXED BOTH** — stated in the past tense deliberately, since a row about staleness that described its own findings in the present tense would be the first thing here to rot. **An empty unadjudicated section is the expected steady state and is NOT evidence the scan works**, which is why the figure above names the commit it was measured against. **AND A THIRD UNCATCHABLE FORM IS NOW KNOWN AND NOT FIXED:** `main.ts:3174` writes *"the `[D]` half of 6.14"*, which neither scan matches — the row id is present but not possessive. **6.14 is open, so it is not a defect today**, and inventing a fourth pattern against an example that is currently correct is how the 63-false-reds script was almost written twice. **It is in NO workflow and never exits non-zero**, exactly as `check-prose-claims.mjs` is and for the same measured reason; **declared here so it is not a 1.29 case.** | ~~**M** — a report, ~60 lines, `scripts/`~~ **BUILT 2026-11-17** | ~~nothing. Unblocked, and the prototype already runs~~ **done 2026-11-17 — and it opened three stale citations of 6.15's `[D]`, which are a FINDING and not this row's work** |
| 1.36 | **Nobody knows whether a participant will fill in a FORM, and the whole typed-observation design rests on it.** *Opened 2026-11-18, out of a design conversation about people-as-sensors and structured input.* **THE DESIGN HALF IS ALREADY DONE AND IS ROW 5.3** — a typed class on `Evidence`, whose recorded reasoning is the right one (*a type, not a score, and the symmetry is what stops it being a politics*) — and **5.3 is behind §4's integrity fork**, because a typed field is an integrity change and **a bump is a RELAUNCH, not an upgrade**. So the expensive half is scheduled behind a collision (`docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §4: a bump after *invite ten people* strands their data permanently under Invariant 6). **WHAT THIS ROW IS: the cheap half that needs NO BUMP, and it tests the one thing no amount of design settles.** A client-side form — Node Health (which node, symptom from a fixed list, app version, recurred yes/no) and Usability (which screen, what you tried, what you expected, what happened) — writing a CONVENTIONAL structure into the existing `Claim` fields, with a free-text note alongside. **WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT BUY, stated so the row cannot be read as 5.3 arriving early: validation cannot check a convention.** A number in a text field is a number only by agreement, so nothing is enforced and no read may claim it was. That is the entire difference between this and 5.3, and it is why this is a behavioural instrument rather than a feature. **WHY IT IS WORTH BUILDING ANYWAY:** if people use it, the fork is worth spending on 5.3; if they go back to prose, an integrity change has been saved on a feature nobody wanted — **and that is cheaper to learn before the bump than after, because Invariant 6 means a typed entry nobody fills in cannot be removed.** **THE FREE-TEXT NOTE IS NOT A FALLBACK, IT IS THE SENSOR.** Invariant 4 forbids flattening, and a fixed list squeezes out exactly the report that would have opened a new row — Kupukupu's seven points would not have survived a dropdown. **When the same thing keeps appearing in notes, that is the signal to add an option**, which makes the note field the thing that improves the form. **AND THE RISK IS NOT TECHNICAL.** The pilot is two people, one of whom has written every row in §6 in prose and in his own register. **Asking him to switch to a form may cost more goodwill than the data is worth**, and that is the `[D]` — not whether to build it, but whether to put it in front of a participant at all. The honest middle is to offer it ALONGSIDE prose and never require it. **If this is ever read by anything automated rather than by a person, [`docs/fixed-core.md`](fixed-core.md) rule 7 binds: the reject count is the condition, not the volume.** | **M** to build, **D** on whether to ASK a participant to use it | **nothing for the build.** The `[D]` is whether a two-person pilot's attention is spent on adopting a form, and that is the human's — see the goodwill note |

## 2. The autopoiesis staging — in dependency order, not preference order

**COMPLETE as of 2026-10-24.** All five stages are on `main`: the medium (#318), proprioception (#319), standing (#324), secretion (#320) and the microbiome (#321). Every one is coordinator-only — no entry type, no link type, no validation rule — so the DNA hash is untouched and nothing here forked the network.

The ordering is forced, not chosen. Stage 1 exists to make Stage 2 affordable;
Stage 2 is the only item that decides whether **operational closure** is true
rather than aspirational; Stage 3 is the first *consequence*; 4 and 5 are
downstream of both.

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | ~~**Stage 1 — the medium.**~~ Typed ephemeral peer signals. One entry to open a channel, zero per signal. Coordinator-only and **proven not to fork**: the DNA hash was byte-identical before and after **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#318).** One entry to open a channel, zero per signal, four typed variants round-tripping, 22 checks. | — | done |
| 2.2 | ~~**Stage 2 — proprioception.**~~ `get_local_tension`: one read bundling closure deficit, ordering ambiguity, friction headroom, conductance decay, arc width. Then dispositions respond to it. **This is the stage that matters.** Luhmann's criterion for an autopoietic system of communications is that a communication be produced by prior communications rather than by the environment; right now every claim is produced by a human or an externally-triggered agent, and this is the single change that makes the graph's own state the proximate cause of the next write **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#319).** `get_local_tension`, caller-scoped **by construction** — no agent parameter, so it cannot become the per-agent scalar Invariant 1 bars. 34 checks, four injections. **Four signals, not five: arc width is unreachable from a zome**, and the gap is reported as `signals_bundled: 4 / signals_intended: 5` rather than hidden. | — | done |
| 2.3 | ~~**Stage 3 — standing / apoptosis.**~~ Standing as a function of causal distance, computed at read time, writing nothing. Primitives exist (`order_records`, `past_cone`, `get_grounding_path`). Needs a harness with injections, since a standing rule that cannot go red is this repository's recurring failure **CLOSED 2026-10-24 (#324) WITHOUT waiting on 3.1**, by making the threshold the caller's promise. Apoptosis not necrosis: reported, still fetchable by hash, never deleted. Counted in causal **edges**, never ordinal position, since `tie_broken` means ordinal distance is part hash. 26 checks, five injections. **3.1 still decides whether a membrane may DECLARE N** — an addition to this read, not a replacement. | — | done |
| 2.4 | ~~**Stage 4 — secretion.**~~ Dispositions propose `CritiqueSpecies`; `get_species_adoption` read a second time **at a causal distance from the first**, which is the only way exploration becomes distinguishable from degradation. The swarm currently passes `species: null` (`scripts/swarm/run-swarm.mjs:250`) **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#320).** Uptake told from a private vocabulary by **causal distance** from `past_cone`, not by polling twice — two reads are separated by a clock and §5.22 rules that out. A live 3-agent run produced both classes: 1 downstream adopter at depth 1 on `unevidenced-claim`, all-proposer citations on `method-unstated`. | — | done |
| 2.5 | ~~**Stage 5 — microbiome.**~~ Membranes read each other's published policies and import foreign critiques. `federation/` currently contains **zero** references to `ConductancePolicy` or `AttestationPolicy` **CLOSED 2026-10-23 (#321).** A foreign critique is a **witness** under the importer's own key — §5.2 binds author to the action author, so writing somebody else's objection would forge provenance. Excluded from `get_discourse_health` and counted as `imported_claims`, or importing fifty would make a domain's health a reading of another network. Verified across two genuinely separate DNA hashes. **`ForeignCritique` as a real entry type remains §4's bump.** | — | done |

## 3. Research questions — no code until these have answers

| # | question | who | why it is open |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | ~~**What sets the atrophy threshold?**~~ Quantitative Promise Theory: a threshold turning a signal into a decision **is itself a promise and needs an agent behind it** — the argument that retired `CONDUCTANCE_HALF_LIFE_SECS` as a universal constant with no promiser. **DECIDED 2026-10-24: THE READER IS THE PROMISER, and the answer was already shipped.** `StandingPolicy::max_causal_distance` (Stage 3, #324) makes the threshold the CALLER's promise. The row offered two candidates — per-membrane **declared** or **derived** — and a third had appeared since it was written. The deciding property is that the caller-supplied form has **a neutral off position**: `None` means no atrophy, not a default, so Invariant #1's "constant with no promiser" objection is answered in full rather than half-answered as it had to be for a half-life, where time passes whether or not anybody promised a rate. **Per-reader is enough; no membrane-wide answer is wanted.** Consequences: **4.4 leaves the §4 bump** (a declared-N `Constitution` field is not being built), and **3.2 loses its driver** — see its row. | — | done |
| 3.2 | **Is there a derived measure that does not punish exploration?** Project 89's dead-head criterion has exactly the right *form* — `τ = 0.96/√d`, local to the component, zero parameters, validated against ablation ground truth at 95-100% across six models. But it equates low alignment with the receiver's state with death, and in a discourse graph the orthogonal contribution is sometimes the only valuable one. **The swarm deliberately runs an exploring minority that this criterion would classify as dead on sight.** Candidates to replace alignment: grounding density, citation closure, assessment reachability over causal distance **NO LONGER BLOCKS ANYTHING, 2026-10-24.** Its blocker read *"3.1's second branch depends on it"*, and 3.1 closed per-reader — so the derived branch is not being pursued and nothing waits on this. **Kept as an open research question rather than closed**, because the thing it actually asks is still unanswered and still interesting: whether a measure can be derived that does not classify the exploring minority as dead on sight. **AND IT NOW HAS A WORKED EXAMPLE IN SOMEBODY ELSE'S DOMAIN, which is what it was missing — `docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md` §13.7**, written because Luck Light asked for one: a membrane arguing about milligrams of choline per egg (methodology, sample sizes, lab variance), and one buyer who says *your number is fine, the weak link is your feed supplier's certification chain.* **Aligned with nothing around it, so an alignment-keyed criterion scores it dead on arrival — and it is also the only critique in the thread that could cost the seller a contract.** The example is worth more than the abstraction: it names what the measure must NOT do in a case where the stakes are legible, and it is the version to hand to anybody designing the simulations 7.15 accepted. Reopen it if a membrane-wide or derived threshold is ever wanted. **MEASURED 2026-11-10, AND THE ANSWER IS NEGATIVE — `scripts/live-verify/exploration-measures.mjs`, 12 checks, which builds §13.7 on a real conductor instead of reasoning about it.** An experiment rather than a gate and declared as one, because §3 records which way the protocol behaves rather than requiring it. **(1) GROUNDING DENSITY, THIS ROW'S OWN FIRST CANDIDATE, FAILS FOR THE SAME REASON ALIGNMENT DOES.** Fixture: the choline claim with a lab report behind it, an ALIGNED methodology critique citing a counter-study, the ORTHOGONAL *your feed supplier's certification chain* critique citing nothing, and a claim nobody engages. Measured: the orthogonal critique and the unengaged claim **both score zero**, so a grounding-keyed measure cannot tell them apart — while the claim everyone is arguing about is grounded, so the key is not simply broken. The buyer who spots a certification gap has no study to cite because the point is **structural rather than empirical**, and a measure reading *cites nothing* as *contributes nothing* kills exactly the contribution §13.7 was written to protect. **(2) AND A ZERO-PARAMETER GROUNDING CRITERION CANNOT EXIST.** Zero cited is `0/0`, not zero: substitute 0 and every structural critique is dead on arrival, substitute 1 and every unevidenced assertion is maximally alive. **The substitution IS the parameter**, and having none is the whole of `τ = 0.96/√d`'s claim to authority. **(3) THE THIRD CANDIDATE IS ALREADY BUILT AND DOES NOT MEAN WHAT THIS ROW ASSUMED — a control caught the mistake rather than prose.** The first draft of that section asserted that a tight `max_causal_distance` atrophies the unengaged claim and spares the argued one. It does **neither**. `engagement_distance` is the HEIGHT of the shallowest record referencing a claim, and with nothing referencing it the coordinator uses `heights[idx]` — the claim's own depth — with `own_depth_used: true` saying so. So *nobody engaged it* scores as **as fresh as its own position**. Swept over thresholds 0, 1, 2, 3 and 5: **nothing atrophies at any of them**, with `own_depth_used` (false for the argued claim, true for the lonely one) as the control proving the report does distinguish the two cases, just not through `has_standing`. **So reachability over causal distance is an ENGAGEMENT-DEPTH REPORT, not a dead-head criterion** — it needs causal depth to say anything, and §13.7's egg membrane is exactly the flat shape most disagreements have while they are still live. It does not punish exploration, and it does not punish anything else here either. **(4) SO THE QUESTION HAS NO POSITIVE ANSWER IN THIS PROTOCOL AS BUILT, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A LACK OF CLEVERNESS.** No measure here separates *orthogonal and valuable* from *nobody engaged* — and **Invariant 1 is why nobody has built one**: its own note says a hardcoded, always-applied version of a caller-supplied policy *"would itself be the kind of canonical signal this invariant rules out"*, so **any protocol-DEFAULT threshold is forbidden whatever it is keyed to.** Changing the key from alignment to grounding does not rescue it; it changes which minority it kills. The repository already hit this exact wall once — `CONDUCTANCE_HALF_LIFE_SECS = 30 days` was precisely this shape, one constant over every membrane converting a signal into a decision with no promiser — and the resolution was not a better constant but **moving the threshold to the reader**. The exploring minority is protected by there being no default at all. **(5) AND THE LIVE REMAINDER IS AN ASYMMETRY, NOT A MISSING MEASURE.** The two read-layer atrophy measures answer *what does a reader who promised nothing get* differently: `get_standing` takes the strict reading (`None => true`, nothing dies, and `standing.mjs` already gates it), while `get_effective_conductance` applies thirty days regardless. **SPEC §7's own note on Invariant 1 admits this half survives** — *"a reader who supplies no policy still gets thirty days, still with no promiser"* — so one existing default has the shape `τ = 0.96/√d` has and the shape the note rules out. **That is the actionable thing this row leaves behind, and it is a decision rather than research:** either conductance takes the strict reading too (no promiser, no decay — a change to what every existing conductance read returns, explicitly not taken on 2026-10-19) or the asymmetry is recorded as deliberate. **STAYS OPEN**, because (5) is undecided and because a measure keyed to something nobody has proposed yet is not ruled out by any of this — but it is open with three candidates retired and a reason rather than with an abstraction. | **M** — ~~research~~ **the measurement is DONE 2026-11-10; what remains in (5) is D** | nothing — research, not a blocker. **(5) needs the human's decision on whether conductance takes the strict reading, which is a change to every existing conductance read** |
| 3.3 | ~~**Why was rejoin round 1 slower than round 3, from a *shorter* outage?**~~ Round 3 self-recovered in 687-692s from 884s offline; round 1 was still dry at 905s from 861s offline, then crossed 65s after a third node joined. Recovery scales with absence in both clean runs, but round 1 is outside that. **The `Unsolicited Accept` hypothesis is dead** — it coincided to the second with round 2's recovery and failed to recur at round 3's **ANSWERED 2026-10-23 (#322)** by reading kitsune2's scheduler. Round 1 needs no cause of its own: recovery is gated by a URL-keyed unresponsive marker whose TTL is the peer's `agent_info.expires_at`, fixed **before** the outage. **Three recorded claims retired** — there is no 300s per-peer floor (`min_initiate_interval_ms` is read by no code), *"scales with absence"* is unsupported, and the first initiate is at ~120-130s not ~1s. The clean closed form is refuted by round 3; a fourth round needs the instrumentation listed in the runbook. | — | done |
| 3.4 | **Can a metabolic budget exist without being Invariant 1's leaderboard?** The mutual-credit ledger was built, verified and **removed** — `docs/metabolic-biosignalling-currency-brief.md`, 1,020 lines — and reason 3 was that `get_credit_balance` was a canonical per-agent scalar over an enumerable agent set. The escape route the brief itself names: `AttestationPolicy` survives because **its answers are caller-scoped and legitimately differ between observers**. So a budget can exist only **per-reader** — my budget for you, under my policy — and never as a number the protocol asserts about you. Without that constraint settled first, building it is a re-run of a deletion | D | the constraint is clear; whether to build on it is not |
| 3.5 | **Observer diversity is unverifiable, and it is one problem wearing two costumes.** Ten observer identities may be one actor; and if every observer runs one model, the exploring minority is one mind in different hats. **The swarm has an accidental defence**: dispositions are *mechanical*, every rule a property of auditable data, so the variation does not come from the model. **Observers-as-entries would throw that defence away**, since the thing being selected becomes a prompt **AND IT IS THE SAME PROBLEM AS THE SYBIL LIMIT, now stated once in 3.7** rather than half here and half in `SECURITY.md`. The mechanical-disposition defence is why ten observers genuinely differ even on one model: a disposition is a threshold and a mode preference published as a `Constitution` promise before the agent acts, every rule a property of data a reader can check — so divergence from a declared threshold is caught by comparison rather than by anybody's say-so. Prompts would make ten observers one mind in different hats. **THE DEFENCE WAS AN ASSERTION UNTIL 2026-11-08 AND IS NOW BOTH MEASURED AND ENFORCED — `scripts/swarm/check-dispositions-mechanical.mjs`, wired into `no-conductor`.** It is exhaustive rather than sampled, and that is cheap for a reason worth stating: **`assess` reads exactly four things** — the claim's confidence, whether `evidence_hashes` is empty, whether the content matches a literal method-word regex, and whether this disposition has already critiqued the target. The first three are finite and small, so **the swarm's ENTIRE behaviour is a 60-state table** (3 dispositions x 5 confidences x 2 evidence x 2 method), which the gate enumerates and evaluates **twice per state**. Result: acts in 15 of 60, zero non-determinism, no clock, no randomness, no network, no environment and no `await` anywhere in the module, every disposition carrying a numeric threshold, a mode and a promise, and the constitution published before any critique in `run-swarm.mjs`. **AND THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: the majority and the deliberate minority differ in exactly 4 of 20 states, and EVERY ONE sits between the two declared thresholds with zero evidence** — so the divergence IS the published number and nothing else, which is what *"the variation does not come from the model"* means when it is checked rather than said. **ALL FIVE FAILURE MODES WERE WATCHED GOING RED BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED**, one specific check each and no collateral: randomness added to the module; the minority given the majority's threshold (*a minority that never diverges is not a minority*); the minority made STRICTER than the majority, which puts the divergence outside the declared band; a disposition stripped of its promise; and the constitution moved after the first critique. **TWO DEFECTS IN THE GATE ITSELF, FOUND WHILE BUILDING IT:** its no-method fixture first read *"an assertion with no method words"*, which contains *"method"* — a fixture that fails its own predicate proves nothing, and an assertion caught it; and the ordering check first searched for the bare strings, so a mention of `create_critique` in a comment would have reported the order wrong, and worse, a mention of `publish_constitution` below the code would have reported it RIGHT while the code did the opposite. Both now match call sites. **WHAT THIS DOES FOR 4.2:** that row's cost is no longer asserted. Adopting observers-as-entries replaces a 60-state enumerable table, deterministic and attributable to a published number, with a prompt that is none of those things — **the gate above would have nothing left to check.** The decision stays open; its price is now measured. | D | a cost to price before adopting observers-as-entries, not a bug to fix; the question itself is now 3.7 |

(2) **7.16's residual risk**: the people most likely to be harmed never signed the joining promise (7.17), and a remedy promise is the only structure on this list that reaches them. (3) **The sharpest formulation the egg case produced**: §11.1 says a challengeable claim is one that says what would make it wrong; **a warranty adds who pays when it is.** **THE HONEST LIMITS.** Enforcement happens OUTSIDE the DHT, in a jurisdiction somebody chooses — this protocol can record that a promise was made and point at who made it, and it cannot compel payment, which is the half 7.16's advice and `README` §2.4 both already say is not here. An underwriter is also a concentration of trust by construction; what keeps it from being a CENTRE is that it is named per claim and chosen per reader, never a protocol default (Invariant 1's note). And pricing requires loss data nobody has. **Related**: 3.7 (the scarce resource), 3.4 (why a running balance is not available), 7.16 (the exposure), 7.17 (what a joining promise cannot reach), 4.6 (what a withdrawable claim does to a warranty — an unresolved interaction worth stating before either is built). | **D** to adopt any of it; M to cost it as research | nothing — and 7.17's adoption is the first place it would show up |

Raw correlation would therefore fire hardest exactly where it means least, so the measure needs a baseline — correlation conditioned on the spread of revealed outcomes, or restricted to questions where the outcomes actually diverged — and that baseline is the whole research question, not an implementation detail. (2) **Selection is a second channel, not noise.** Agents choose what to pre-register, so two agents correlate partly because they chose the same targets; target choice is itself observable and measurable, but it is a distinct signal and conflating it with content correlation would overstate both. (3) **THE DETECTOR'S POPULATION AND ITS SUBJECT ARE THE SAME POPULATION.** The idea's own argument is that only AI agents can generate pre-registrations in volume — which reintroduces precisely the condition being measured. **And 3.5 records that this repository has already bought the defence it would be testing for:** swarm dispositions are *mechanical*, every rule a property of auditable data, so the variation does not come from a model and ten observers genuinely differ on one model. **So the condition this row detects does not currently exist by design.** It becomes live exactly when 4.2 is adopted — observers-as-entries makes the selected thing a prompt and throws that defence away, which 3.5 already names — or when model-driven participants join a real membrane. Building the measure before either is costing a detector for a disease nobody has yet. | **D** on whether the baseline in (1) is answerable at all; then M to cost it | **4.2, or the arrival of model-driven participants.** Not blocked on 6.8 (commit path BUILT 2026-10-28) and not on §5.22, which it is specifically shaped to respect — blocked on there being correlated observers to find |
