## 4. Behind one integrity fork — accumulate, then bump once

Every edit to the integrity zome forks the network, additive changes included,
so these batch into a single `protocol_version` bump rather than four. **Stages
1 to 3 need no fork at all**, which is why they come first.

**The candidate set is six, and this section listed three until 2026-10-24,
four until 2026-10-26 night and five until 2026-11-15.** **AND THE PLAN'S OWN
TABLE WAS MISSING ONE OF THEM, which is worth recording because that file
declares itself the authority on the set:** `docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2
listed four and had never carried **4.6**, opened 2026-10-26 night — the same
night this header started saying four. Filing 4.7 is what found it. An index
that lists a candidate the authority does not is the authority being wrong, not
the index.
`docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2 is the authority on the set and says so in its
own heading: 4.1-4.3 below, plus **4.5**, which existed only in the plan, and
now **4.6**, added the night 7.16 got its advice. 4.4 —
a declared atrophy threshold `N` — was added and dropped on the same day, when
3.1 decided the reader is the promiser; it is not listed because it is dead,
not pending. **The gap is the lesson**: a candidate added in a plan document
does not reach the ordered list by itself.

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | **The foresight ordinal anchor.** `PreRegistration.reveal_deadline` changes type to a position in a declared order; `Revelation` gains an association; `Membrane` gains a declared notary key. Validation then checks shape only — the association resolves, its author is the declared notary, its counter precedes the deadline's. **Not adopted** — the 2026-10-19 decision stands, since no surface grants this timestamp authority — but the reopen order changed: repair 3 first because it is free, then **repair 4**, and countersigning only if an ordinal anchor is unavailable **THE QUESTIONS ARE ASKED (7.6, sent). This now waits on ANSWERS, not on anybody writing an email** — which is a different blocker and outside this repository's control. Nothing else in §4 depends on them, so the bump is not held up by 4.1 alone.  **RE-PRICED 2026-11-15 on the bump plan's *what indexes it?* question, and the estimate was understated: TWO validation rules, not one, and both are DELETIONS rather than edits.** `reveal_deadline` has 5 sites in the integrity zome and 7 in the coordinator; two of the integrity sites compare it as a TIME — `:1383` refuses a commitment born already expired, `:1443` refuses a late `Revelation` — and an ordinal cannot be compared against `action.timestamp()`, so both go. **That is the point of this row (it is the §5.22 clock-skew repair), but the plan said *validation checks shape only* without saying two guarantees are surrendered to get there.** | D | Andras's three answers |
| 4.2 | **Observers as entries**, if adopted — prompts, heuristics and thresholds as signed entries, so selection acts on the agents. Contrary to one claim on file, this **is** a new entry type and therefore does fork **AND ITS COST IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED, 2026-11-08.** 3.5's *accidental defence* — that dispositions are mechanical, so ten observers differ even on one model — is enumerated and enforced by `scripts/swarm/check-dispositions-mechanical.mjs`: **the swarm's whole behaviour is 60 states, deterministic, with the majority/minority divergence confined to exactly 4 of them, all inside the declared threshold band.** Replacing a threshold with a prompt discards every one of those properties, and the gate would have nothing left to check. **So this row's decision is now: is selection over prompts worth losing an enumerable, attributable swarm?** — a priced question rather than an open-ended one. See 3.5.  **RE-PRICED 2026-11-15, AND IT IS 4.5's DEFECT REPEATING — an entry type is never the cost, what INDEXES it is.** Measured: **16 of 19 entry types are resolved inside a `validate_create_link` branch**. Of the three that are not, `Evidence` is linked by two of the four link types that have **no validation branch at all**, `SealedPrediction` is embedded at its construction site, and `SettlementPolicy` is reachable only by a hash the caller already holds. **An un-indexed entry type exists here only where nobody needs to FIND it** — and this row's purpose is selection acting on agents, which is discovery. So **at least one link type and one validation branch** on top of the claimed entry type, 42-77 lines with 3 `Invalid` returns by the measured pattern. | D | 3.5's cost, priced |
| 4.3 | **Species provenance and metabolic policy fields**, if Stage 4 and a budget are adopted  **RE-PRICED 2026-11-15, AND ITS INDEX HALF IS THE ONLY ESTIMATE OF THE THREE THAT SURVIVED.** `CritiqueSpecies` is already indexed three ways (`TaxonomyToSpecies`, `CritiqueToSpecies`, `SpeciesToParent`), so fields need no new index and no new link type. **One thing missed:** a *provenance* field that points at anything is a **reference field**, and §5.3's gated rule is that those resolve their target in validation — currently 8 of 9, the exception being `Critique.evidence_hashes` — so provenance makes it 9 of 10 and adds a resolution site. **And *policy fields TBD* is not a measurement; it cannot become one until 3.4 settles.** | M | 2.4, 3.4 |

| 4.6 | **Per-field content mode: `public_permanent` or `withdrawable`, declared by the membrane.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night, out of 7.16's advice — `docs/erasure-and-the-content-split.md` §3.* Readable content moves off the shared network (or onto it encrypted, §3.2 there) while **the hash, the signature, the order and the links stay** — which is Invariant 6's operative reading (§5.22: *no agent may erase another's record of what was promised*) left intact, because that reading is about the RECORD and not the CONTENT. **IT IS NOT A NEW PATTERN HERE: `SealedPrediction` already is this**, and `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:798-810` writes down both halves — the Action publishes the hash so the author cannot quietly change what they committed to, and **validation cannot see a private entry's body at all** (hdi returns `None`, `Some(_)` is an error). **WHY IT FORKS**: new fields on `Claim` at minimum, plausibly a new entry type for the custodial-key variant, so it batches with 4.1-4.5 rather than going before the invitation. **THE COST IS INVENTORIED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED — four content-inspecting rules exist in the whole integrity zome** (`validate_claim`'s non-empty content `:1331`, `validate_mew`'s 560 chars `:1309`, `SettlementPolicy` name/statement `:1513`/`:1516`, `WorldlineTrace`'s 64KB `:2119`), and only the first two are personal content; both are shape rules that move author-side. That is §5.22's family — a goal no deterministic rule over reachable data can express — joined for the fourth time, not a new kind of problem. **THE PER-FIELD GRANULARITY IS THE WHOLE DESIGN**: *"batch 47: 410mg choline"* stays public and permanent in Luck Light's egg membrane, which **requires** that mode by promise, while a farmer's name and address stay withdrawable — so the objection this risks (`docs/cases/luck-light-egg.md:390`, *a track record with the bad quarters removed is not a track record*) does not apply to the case that raised it. **The custodial variant needs 4.1's ordinal anchor for a SECOND purpose** — *erased as of version N* has to be an ordered, agreed event, and Andras's work supplies the ordering while key destruction supplies the erasure; that reading is second-hand and wants confirming with him. Honest limits in §5 of the doc: nothing erases a copy someone kept, and encryption-as-erasure is legally debated. **RE-PRICED 2026-11-15 on the bump plan's *what indexes it?* question, and like 4.7 it is a FORK IN THE ROAD rather than a number.** The content-rule inventory above **verifies exactly** — all four line numbers still land on the rules it names — so that half was honest. **BUT *PER-FIELD* IS NOT EXPRESSIBLE BY THE MECHANISM THIS ROW CITES.** Holochain visibility is declared **per ENTRY TYPE** (`#[entry_type(visibility = "private")]`), and `SealedPrediction` — **the only private entry type in the whole zome** — is private wholesale, not per field. So **(a) SPLIT THE ENTRY** (public `Claim` + private companion): **1 new entry type, 1 new link type, 1 validation branch** — 42-77 lines, 3 `Invalid`, one `must_get_valid_record` by the measured pattern — **and the binding can only be STRUCTURAL, because validation cannot see a private body at all** (`:798-810`: hdi delivers `None`, and `Some(_)` is an outright error). Or **(b) ENCRYPT IN PLACE** (§3.2 there): no new entry type, but withdrawal becomes key destruction, **which makes this row DEPENDENT on 4.1's ordinal anchor** rather than merely adjacent to it. **AND THE PRECEDENT CUTS BOTH WAYS:** `SealedPrediction` is cheap because it is **both private AND one of the three un-indexed entry types** — embedded at its construction site, so nothing has to find it. A private companion to a `Claim` must be findable from the public half, so it **cannot inherit that cheapness**. | **D** to adopt; M to build once adopted | the bump batch, and the adoption decision — nothing legal, which is the change |
| 4.7 | **A domain index — enumerating the SET of domains, which is 6.12's `[D]` half.** *Filed 2026-11-15 by re-pricing 6.12 on this plan's own question — **what indexes it?** — which §3 says to apply to every candidate because 4.5, the one anybody built, is the one whose cost was wrong.* **THE RE-PRICE'S HEADLINE: 6.12's DECISION DECIDES WHETHER THIS IS A BUMP CANDIDATE AT ALL, and that is why it is filed here with a condition rather than a cost.** **IF FOUNDING BECOMES THE PRICE OF FINDABILITY, NOTHING GOES IN THE BUMP: a founded domain IS a `Membrane`, `MembraneRegistry` already indexes every Membrane against one global anchor, and `get_membranes` already reads it.** Zero integrity change, no fork, and it ships today — the enumeration 6.12 says is missing is already built, already gated and already in CI. **IF FOUNDING DOES NOT BECOME THE PRICE, THE CHEAP PATTERN IS UNAVAILABLE, and not for the reason first assumed.** Measured across the three index/registry link types: `TaxonomyToSpecies` 42 lines, `DomainToClaim` 59, `MembraneRegistry` 77 — **each with exactly 3 `Invalid` returns, each resolving a real record with `must_get_valid_record`, and NONE of the three reading its tag.** Every one binds the author through a field on a real entry: `Membrane.creator`, `CritiqueSpecies.proposer`, `Claim.domain`. **A DOMAIN IS NOT AN ENTRY** — a domain anchor resolves to no record whatever — so all three properties this codebase uses to make an index non-forgeable are simply not available, and the pattern cannot be copied. **THE WORKAROUND AND WHAT IT COSTS:** point the registry link at the CLAIM and carry the domain in the TAG, which validates cleanly (target is a real Claim, tag equals `claim.domain`). But it would be **the first tag-validating link type in this zome**, and `DomainToClaim`'s own SPEC §10.0 row declined exactly that — *"Tag deliberately empty: the domain is already the anchor, so repeating it in the tag would be a second copy validation must keep honest for no reader's benefit."* And it is **one link per CLAIM on a single global anchor**, so the index grows with claims rather than domains — which is the *"single global index over an unbounded, ever-growing set"* §10.0 weighed for a class of three and **sustained against** for `get_critiques_by_mode`, on precisely this ground. **AND THE PATH-HIERARCHY ROUTE IS DEAD, measured rather than assumed:** there is no `ensure()`, no `TypedPath` and no `children()` anywhere in the DNA, and every anchor here is hash-only — `path_entry_hash()` with no entry ever written. Enumerating path children is unbuilt machinery, not reuse. **THE DECISION IS THEREFORE NOT "is founding worth the step" IN THE ABSTRACT.** It is: accept a cost this project has already argued against once, or accept a friction a participant has already demonstrated they will not pay — 6.12 records them publishing a claim that the founding step is worth it and filing that claim into somebody else's domain, because that was the one place guaranteed to be read. **The row's own position is that the revealed preference is what a design should be built against, which points at the expensive branch.** **WITHDRAWN 2026-11-15, THE SAME DAY IT WAS FILED — 6.12's `[D]` WAS TAKEN AND THE ANSWER IS *REQUIRE FOUNDING*, SO THERE IS NOTHING HERE TO BUMP.** `get_membranes` already enumerates the founded domains — link-only, one global anchor, `GetStrategy::Network`, `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:2815-2836` — shipped, gated and in CI. **AND IT WAS WITHDRAWN AGAINST THE EXPENSIVE BRANCH THIS VERY ROW POINTED AT**, because the revealed preference it rested on was measured and found unpriced: nothing in the app connects founding to findability (`findable` / `be found` / `discoverable` have zero user-facing hits in `mobile-ui/src/main.ts`; the tab is plain `['membranes', 'Domains']` at `:1774`), and the *"one more click away"* the participant quoted is in fact a four-field form plus a permanent `Constitution` written under their key before `create_membrane` is reached (`:6408-6470`, `:6486-6500`). See 6.12 for the full reasoning and for what would reverse it. **THIS IS THE SECOND CANDIDATE A DECISION REMOVED RATHER THAN SCHEDULED** — 4.4 was the first, and §2's own paragraph had already named that as the property worth noticing about this list. | ~~**D** — 6.12's founding question~~ **ANSWERED**; **M** — but the `[M]` is 6.12's three honesty surfaces, which are UI-only and not a bump item at all | ~~**6.12's `[D]`, and nothing else**~~ **nothing — withdrawn.** It does not join the batch and inherits none of §4's blockers |

| 5.2 | **Apply 7b.1 to an ingestion path.** The discipline's point is that automated inflow becomes safe to switch on exactly when its rejects are counted. Nothing ingests yet, so the rule currently binds nothing | M | a decision to build ingestion at all [D] |

That is Invariant 2 and Invariant 10 implemented as a feature rather than a slogan, and the strongest evidence seen that *"creates conditions for truth to resonate"* is buildable | M | 5.3 |
| 5.5 | **If the system reviews its own changes through its own protocol, the infrastructure discourse has nowhere to live that is not the pilot's DHT.** *Opened 2026-11-18, out of the same conversation as 1.36 and 7.31.* **THE PROPOSAL, WHICH IS SOUND IN SHAPE:** a change to node policy or to this project's own code is published as a **claim** with test results as evidence, open to **critique**, adopted only after it passes and gets sign-off, **retracted on the record** when it turns out bad — an auditable history of how the system rewrote itself, using the settlement machinery that already exists (`recompute_settlement`). **THE QUESTION THE PROPOSAL DOES NOT ASK, AND IT IS THE BLOCKER:** *on which network?* **Putting node-policy claims on the pilot DHT would overturn a decision already taken the other way, silently, as a side effect of a logistics choice** — row 7.29 records refusing exactly that when the one-line fix would have put the declared automated agent into the pilot, because *an AI the author prompts is the author one layer removed* and **under Invariant 6 that record could never be tidied up afterwards.** Infrastructure chatter is the same shape: permanent, high-volume, and about us rather than about anything a participant came for. **So this needs its own seed, which means its own `breakingVersion` suffix and its own always-on node** — and [[pilot-node-always-on]]'s trap applies twice over, since the seed carries a MINOR version and a second network doubles the number of ways to be stranded in perfect health. **AND ONE MECHANISM FROM THE PROPOSAL MUST NOT BE BUILT AS DESCRIBED.** It reaches for decaying signal strength as the integrator — strong when reinforced, fading when stale. Per-link conductance does exist and decays (`get_effective_conductance`), but **`CONDUCTANCE_HALF_LIFE_SECS` WAS RETIRED as a universal constant** on exactly this argument: *a threshold turning a signal into a decision is itself a promise and needs an agent behind it* (3.1, decided 2026-10-24). What replaced it is `StandingPolicy::max_causal_distance`, **supplied by the CALLER, with `None` meaning no atrophy rather than a default**, so a reader who promises nothing has nothing imposed. **A system-wide fade re-introduces the retired constant** — time passes whether or not anybody promised a rate. If this layer wants staleness it must be per-reader with a neutral off position, which is 3.1's answer already. **AND 3.1 IS THE ANSWER TO THIS ROW RATHER THAN THE OBSTACLE TO IT, which is the reframe worth keeping.** Its rule is not *no thresholds* — it is **every threshold has an owner, and every owner can say `None`**. That transposes one layer down: in 3.1 the READER promises the threshold for how they read; here **the HOST promises the threshold for how its own machine behaves.** Same shape, same neutral off. **SO THE STACK SPLITS, AND MOST OF THE PROBLEM DISSOLVES.** (a) **Homeostasis needs no collective threshold at all** — a host converging its own machine is keeping a promise about its OWN substrate, nothing crosses the network and no bar is agreed by anybody. That is the bulk of self-maintenance and it is free of the promiser problem entirely. (b) **Self-production already has its mechanism and it is NOT aggregation** — settlement is explicit two-sided agreement with no dissent, and there is no threshold to own because there is no bar. *The parties agree and nobody objects* is a different kind of object from *signal strength exceeded X*. The unpromised-constant problem arises only when a LEVEL replaces AGREEMENT. **WHICH MEANS THE 'MISSING NERVOUS SYSTEM' IS A CENTRAL CONTROLLER — AND *THAT* IS WHAT AUTOPOIESIS HAS NONE OF. CORRECTED 2026-11-18, SAME DAY, BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS SENTENCE SAID *AUTOPOIESIS HAS NO NERVOUS SYSTEM* AND THAT IS FALSE** — a human has one and is autopoietic, which was pointed out and is right. **The accurate claim is narrower and more useful: in Maturana and Varela the nervous system is OPERATIONALLY CLOSED and NON-REPRESENTATIONAL**, defined as a system *without inputs and outputs towards the external world* — it does not pick up information from an environment, it maintains correlations between its own sensory and motor surfaces. **So the nervous system is not an exception to operational closure, it is another instance of it**, and Varela later generalised closure beyond cells to nervous, immune and social systems. **SO: A NERVOUS SYSTEM IS ALLOWED. A HOMUNCULUS IS NOT** — a centre that builds a representation of the world and issues commands from it. **The usable test:** *signals fade unless reinforced and at threshold X the system acts* is representation plus command, which is a controller; **a per-host threshold with a neutral off, each node coupling its own sensing to its own acting, is closed and is nervous-system-shaped.** **AND PLASTICITY IS ALLOWED AND ALREADY EXISTS:** structural coupling — structure changing with the history of interactions — is exactly per-link conductance changing with reinforcement. **What 3.1 retired was a UNIVERSAL RATE. Plasticity yes, universal clock no**, which is narrower than *no fading* and is the line to hold. This protocol's refusal to compute a single verdict is the same refusal one layer down, not a gap to be filled. Integration belongs at each node, in its own reading — which is what §4.4 already requires and what Invariant 9 already bounds. **AND THE DECAY IDEA KEEPS A LEGITIMATE HOME:** a host may promise *I act when X* in its own published policy, and conductance already decays per link. **What is forbidden is the PROTOCOL shipping X.** **ONE CONSTRAINT THAT RULES OUT THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION, and it is measured rather than argued: the always-on node PUBLISHES NOTHING, and `verify.mjs` asserts `publishes nothing: HOLDS` on every run.** So the archive cannot report its own health — that would break the invariant it exists to demonstrate. Node-health observations need a DIFFERENT key, which is 7.29's refusal again, which is why the network question above is the blocker and not a detail. **The limits this row's loop would run under are [`docs/fixed-core.md`](fixed-core.md) rule 3 (a proposal is never self-applied) and rule 7 (nothing writes at volume, because Invariant 6 means nothing can be cleaned up).** | **D** | **the network question above** — and 7.31, since there is no policy to review until something maintains policy. **AND BEFORE BUILDING ANY OF IT, READ MAPE-K**: the proposal has a canonical name and reference architecture (Monitor-Analyse-Plan-Execute over a shared Knowledge base, IBM's autonomic computing initiative), it is **the** standard model for self-adaptive systems, and **it is the controller shape by construction** — an external feedback loop with an *autonomic manager* distinct from the *managed element*. **Its canonical self-\* properties are specified as *without human intervention*, including self-protection as *the ability to defend itself against misuse without human intervention*** — which is `docs/fixed-core.md` rule 2's dangerous version written into the reference architecture as a GOAL. **So the standard answer to this row ships with the one property the fixed core forbids**, which is a better reason to depart from it than taste |

**Do not import the surrounding cosmology.** Project 89's broader framing
includes deriving the fine structure constant from a lattice learning rule at
1.5 ppb with zero free parameters — an extraordinary claim, treated here as
unvalidated. The disciplines above and the dead-head *form* stand on their own
and do not depend on it.

## 6. Interaction design — five unsurfaced reads, and one surface that was MISSING

Each is deliberately unsurfaced **with its reason recorded**, and each needs a
design decision rather than wiring. Verified 2026-10-22 against `mobile-ui/src`:
all four return zero hits, while `get_discourse_health` returns three — the
read is wired, the publish is not. **AND A SIXTH ROW IS NOT A READ AT ALL:
6.7, opened and BUILT 2026-10-27**, was a missing WRITE —
`publish_constitution` was reachable only by founding a domain, so a membrane
could demand promises a joiner had no way to make. That one was a gap rather
than a decision, which is why it was built the same day it was found. **A
FIFTH JOINED ON 2026-10-26 NIGHT AND IT
WAS A DIFFERENT ANIMAL: 6.6 was unsurfaced by oversight rather than by
decision**, needed no design question answered first, and **is now the only one
of the five that is BUILT** — `EpistemicAgent.promiseCompliance()` and
`scripts/live-verify/promise-compliance.mjs`, 19 checks on a real conductor.
So the four that remain are the four that were always a decision.
**AND THEN THERE WERE THREE, 2026-11-02: 6.1 IS BUILT.** It was always the
oddest of the four, because by 2026-10-28 its design question had been answered
and demonstrated — the honest rendering of an absent cone — leaving only the
application of that answer to `critiques_unseen_by`'s own output, which is
wiring. The three that remain (6.2, 6.4, 6.5) still hold real decisions.
**AND A TENTH ROW IS NEITHER A READ NOR A WRITE: 6.10, opened 2026-10-29.** It
is the compose-time moment itself — the only point at which a permanent entry is
still free to change — and it is the first row in this file that would put a
language model in the app. It is `[D]` for exactly the reason the other four
are: *what must be on screen before a user signs?* What it does **not** share
with them is a protocol dependency, because it writes nothing.

**AND TWO ROWS WERE OPENED BY A USER RATHER THAN BY A READING: 6.12 AND 6.13,
2026-11-09.** Every other row in this section was found by looking at the code
and asking what was unsurfaced. These two began with a question a pilot
participant asked out loud the day they published — *how do you search for what
others wrote?* — and the answer is that they cannot, unless somebody tells them
the exact domain string first. **6.12 is the finding and 6.13 is what the
attempt to verify it ran into.** 6.12 splits the way none of the others do: a
small `[M]` half plainly missing (a claim did not display its own domain) and
a `[D]` half the project's own security reasoning has already half-answered
against. 6.13 was not a decision at all — the Browse tab could not tell an
empty domain from an unloaded one or from a failed read, while By Author
distinguished all three a thousand lines earlier in the same file.

**BOTH OF THOSE HALVES WERE BUILT 2026-11-10, AND THE `[D]` IS EXACTLY WHERE IT
WAS.** 6.13 is closed and gated; 6.12's breadcrumb is on every claim card. What
neither touches is the question a reader who has arrived nowhere asks, which is
the half that needs a decision rather than a morning — so the useful reading of
this pair is that **the user's question split into two hours of work and one
open question, and nothing about doing the work moved the question.**

**AND THREE MORE CAME FROM ONE REPLY, 2026-11-11: 6.18, 6.19 AND 6.20.** The
same participant who opened 6.17 came back the following day with three
sentences about the screen they had just used — who is speaking, how big the
box is, and how the text is shaped — and all three were real. **The ratio in
this section is now the thing to notice.** Every row opened by reading the
code needed a design decision before anything could be built; of the six
opened by a user, five were plainly missing and four were built the same day
they arrived. A non-technical reader describing what they cannot do has turned
out to be the highest-yield source of work in this file, and the reason is not
that the code was carelessly written — it is that the person who wrote it
cannot see the screen for the first time twice.

**AND THE REPLY CLOSED A QUESTION OF OURS, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE THE RULE HERE
IS NEVER TO RE-ASK.** They wrote *"I think i misread what you wrote, it will
reach in next build ok"*, so the *"still not showing the record"* half of their
earlier report is 6.17 waiting on a release rather than a second defect. The
*"unable to see your full critique"* half reads, in the light of this message,
as 6.18 and 6.20 rather than as truncation — they describe the text as all
there and the problem as unreadable — but **that is an inference and is
recorded as one.** Nothing is asked again.

**AND A THIRD REPLY, 2026-11-12, ANSWERED A DECISION THAT WAS BEING HELD FOR
THE HUMAN — THE FIRST TIME THAT HAS HAPPENED IN THIS FILE.** 6.19's `[D]` was
*where a reply appears*: the critique list arrives in the order the DHT returned
it, a nested reply has no place in that order, and indenting one is a client
deciding a thread's shape. Unprompted, and without having been shown the row,
the participant described the shape they wanted: *"the reply comes under a
critique, in where others agreeing or disagreeing with that particular critique
to the claim can reply. A new critique gets its own section. It provides
organisation."* That is an answer to the question as the row framed it, given by
the person the row is owed to, and it is taken as one — see 6.19, where the
`[D]` is closed and the `[M]` is unblocked. **A decision this file was holding
because it was not ours to make turned out to be answerable by asking nobody:
they volunteered it.** The same message asked for one thing more, filed
separately because it is independently shippable: *"I'd include an option if
possible in where the list minimizes after 3-4 critiques but can be opened to
see all, to not clutter?"* — 6.22.

**AND ONE ROW IN THIS SECTION WAS NOT REPORTED BY ANYBODY — 6.24, FOUND BY A
HARNESS THAT COULD NOT SEE WHAT IT HAD JUST PUBLISHED.** Building 6.22's gate
meant publishing three more critiques and reading them back; collapsing the
panel and re-opening it showed the old three, and only a full page reload
showed six. **The critique panel is a snapshot taken the first time it was
opened.** Its cause is one guarded line whose own neighbouring comment says
the skip-on-re-open was understood — for the number beside the list, which is
refreshed, and not for the list. **It is §4.4's failure mode reached from a
direction §4.4 does not discuss:** that section blames per-user adaptation for
two readers seeing different artifacts, and a session cache produces exactly
that with no adaptation anywhere. **6.22's fold was measured against the rule
and cleared it; the cache beside it has been breaking it since the panel was
written.**

**AND A FOURTH REPLY THE SAME DAY CONFIRMED TWO FIXES AND REOPENED A THIRD
PROBLEM UNDER A NEW READING — 6.23.** The confirmations first, because they are
the only direct evidence this file has that anything shipped works for the
person it was shipped for: *"great to see the domains that a user has published
in. Makes things a lot easier"* is 6.16 landing, and *"thats a good one"* is the
reply layout. **The reopening is the one that matters.** On a build that
already carries 6.18's byline they wrote *"People writing critiques, still
unclear if it is one person or the same."* 6.18 read their first report as
*there is no byline*; the half it never answered was the next sentence — *"you
wouldnt know if you're speaking to the same or others"* — which is not about
presence but about whether twelve base64 characters are something a human can
compare. **A screen can pass every structural assertion in `reading-a-thread.mjs`
and still not answer the reader's question**, and the gap between *the data is
on screen* and *the screen answers the question* is where this pilot keeps
finding work. They also answered the one question we asked: **macOS**, which
settles 6.21's platform without a log — the log being the thing that did not
exist.

**AND THE SAME MESSAGE OPENED 6.21, WHICH IS THE WORST DEFECT THIS PILOT HAS
TURNED UP AND IS NOT IN THE HAPP AT ALL.** *"App shows a window stating new
version available. Install and restart. Clicking this - does nothing. No update
happens."* The release that was cut because this participant asked for it could
not be delivered by the mechanism built to deliver it, and the way it failed
left no record of itself on their machine or in this repository.

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | ~~`critiques_unseen_by` has no screen~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-02 — it has one, and the row's own remaining half is what closed it.** Its HONESTY RULE was built and checked on 2026-10-28, and the row was narrower than it was. The rule: a screen must render `cone_recorded` as prominently as `unseen`, or silence reads as ignorance. The 2026-10-26 reclassification was right: `cone_recorded` was false for every critique in existence because no client populated `past_cone`, so the screen could render **one state and only one**, and a one-state indicator asserts nothing. 6.5's agent half unblocked it — an SDK agent now records a cone that is true by construction — so both states occur and the rule is enforceable. **BUILT:** `renderConeState` draws the state on every critique and every position, at the SAME typographic weight in both cases (a fainter absent case would re-assert ignorance typographically after the wording had avoided it), and in a list where one entry says what it saw, an unmarked neighbour reads as the weaker claim by implication — so every row states which it is. **VERIFIED:** `positions-ui.mjs` §4, five checks, both states on one screen. **AND IT CAUGHT THE ROW'S OWN DEFECT BEING COMMITTED BY ITS OWN FIX:** the first version distinguished an *absent* `past_cone` from an explicit `[]` and rendered the latter as *"Recorded having seen none of this claim's other objections"* — **a positive claim of having seen nothing, which is exactly what this row exists to prevent.** It is also unrepresentable: `past_cone` is a `Vec<ActionHash>` with `#[serde(default)]`, so an omitted field and `[]` are the SAME STORED VALUE, and the coordinator's own `cone_recorded = !past_cone.is_empty()` makes both *not recorded*. **There are two states, not three**, and the harness surfaced it by failing against correct behaviour. **WHAT IS STILL OPEN:** `critiques_unseen_by` itself has no screen — the cone is rendered from the entry, not from that read, so the `unseen` set and its `total_known` caveat (another observer may know more) are still unsurfaced. That is a smaller row than this one was, and it is no longer blocked on 6.5 or on a design decision — the honest rendering is now demonstrated, so the remaining work is to apply it to that read's own output. **AND THAT IS WHAT WAS DONE, 2026-11-02 — `renderUnseenState` plus `loadUnseen`, `positions-ui.mjs` §5/§5b/§5c, 19 checks, 42 in the file, green on a live conductor and watched failing five ways.** **WHAT THE QUERY ADDS THAT THE ENTRY CANNOT, which is why this half was the half left:** §4's line reads the ENTRY, so it states a numerator with its denominator hidden — *"recorded having seen 1 other objection"* says nothing until a reader knows whether there was one other objection or forty, and **no entry can carry that figure**, because it is a count of what THIS conductor resolves over its own arc at read time. So every rendered figure is attributed to *this reader* rather than to the world, which is `total_known` said out loud. **THE DESIGN DECISION INSIDE THE WIRING, and it is an asymmetry that looks at first like a lapse from this row's own rule:** the unseen SET is offered only where a cone was actually recorded. The equal-weight rule governs the STATE — both branches state themselves, same class, same measured weight — but a SET is a different object. Where a cone exists, the omitted siblings are a checkable gap in a statement its author chose to make. Where none exists there is no statement to have a gap in, and the "set" is every objection already on screen above it, so a disclosure there would **manufacture a gap out of a missing feature** and caption the author's neighbours as what they failed to see. The not-recorded branch says why there is nothing to show instead. **AND THE CLIENT STATES THE FIGURES WHILE THE PROTOCOL STATES WHAT THEY SUPPORT:** `UnseenReport.note` is rendered VERBATIM rather than paraphrased, because a paraphrase is a second place for SPEC's own wording to rot — 1.20's bug class, avoided by not duplicating the claim. **THREE OF THE FIVE INJECTIONS WENT RED WHERE NO WORDING CHECK COULD, which is this row's lasting finding:** fading the not-recorded line to `opacity: 0.55` breaks the rule typographically after the words were careful not to, and is caught only by reading `getComputedStyle` back off the page; paraphrasing the coordinator's note turns three red while *"the note is rendered for every critique"* stays green, because **a count of elements cannot tell whose words are in them**; and moving the denial under the list it qualifies turns one red with both wording checks passing, since the denial is still present and merely too late. Full record: `scripts/live-verify/README.md`, injections CJ-CN. **Next free injection ID: CO.** ~~**WHAT THIS DOES NOT COVER:** `Assessment.past_cone` gets no denominator, because nothing can say how many POSITIONS an assessor's cone was recorded out of~~ **THAT CLAIM WAS WRONG IN TWO WAYS AND IS WITHDRAWN, 2026-11-02 — see 6.11, which built the thing it said could not exist.** **(1) IT IS NOT POSITIONS.** `Assessment.past_cone` holds **CRITIQUES** of the target: `validate_assessment` refuses any member that does not resolve to a `Critique` of the same target. So the denominator is the target's critique count, and *how many of the other assessments did this assessor read* is a question the protocol does not record at all — there is no sibling-assessment cone, by design. Had the claim stood, the obvious next build would have been a query counting the wrong set, and the figure it printed would have looked right. **(2) A CLIENT COULD ALREADY HAVE DERIVED IT** — `get_critiques_for` returns the denominator and the cone is on the entry — so *nothing anywhere can say* was false as stated. What the new query actually adds had to be argued rather than assumed: the gating rule travels with the data, the denominator is counted over the conductor's own arc at read time rather than over whatever a panel happened to fetch, and the protocol states what the figures support instead of leaving a client to invent it. **The pattern is this file's own:** a load-bearing prose claim, written in passing while closing a different row, caught by the next piece of work that touched it rather than by any gate. | ~~**M**~~ **DONE 2026-11-02** | nothing — the read has its screen |
| 6.2 | `publish_discourse_reading` | D | same |
| 6.3 | the settlement screen | D | same |
| 6.4 | the assessment screen — **the READ half shipped 2026-10-28 and this row is NOT closed by it.** Other agents' positions now render on the claim card: stance, their own confidence, their reasons, the assessor named, in the protocol's order, with **nothing counting them** — `positions-ui.mjs`, 23 checks, and the central one is that a deliberate 2-1 split is on screen and no tally appears, because a client-computed tally is a verdict nobody published and Invariant #1 through the back door. That half needed no decision: rendering somebody else's attributed entry is the critique panel's settled pattern. **The read was built because this project created the asymmetry** — `agent-sdk` and `mcp-server` gained assessment publishing the same day (1.19), so an agent could file a position on somebody's claim and the person whose claim it was had no surface that showed it, which is the half-surfaced defect pointed the other way. **WHAT REMAINS IS THE WRITE, and it is still a decision, for the reason README §9 already states:** a stance control commits a user to a permanent attributed position under their own key, and doing it honestly means showing what they are assessing ALONGSIDE the objections already raised — `past_cone` records what they had seen. A one-click Supports/Disputes without that is a vote, which is the one thing this entry type is deliberately not. | **D** for the write half | same — the write screen, not the read |

passed. | ~~**D** to choose the surface; **M** to build it~~ **DONE 2026-10-28** | nothing — decided, built, injected |

That case is reported in those words rather than as a failure, which is what the author check in the coordinator is for. **VERIFIED IN A BROWSER** — `foresight-ui.mjs` §8, three checks: Recover fills the prediction, fills the salt, and **a reveal driven only by the recovered values is accepted**. **Injected and watched failing:** a Recover that fills the prediction and drops the salt turns **two** red — the salt check and the decisive one — with the prediction check still green, correctly. **That is what establishes the decisive check is not vacuous**: it goes red on its own, from validation recomputing the commitment hash over a salt the read failed to return. It is not positions. `validate_assessment` refuses any cone member that does not resolve to a **`Critique` of the same target**, so the set is the claim's OBJECTIONS and **there is no sibling-assessment cone anywhere in this protocol** — *did this assessor read the other assessments* is unanswerable by construction and no surface may imply otherwise. Both claims are struck in place rather than edited away. **SO THE NAME WAS THE FIRST DECISION, and it is not the one that was asked for.** The instruction said `assessments_unseen_by`. That name says the unseen things are assessments, which is exactly the wrong reading, and an extern name is permanent — it would have shipped the error into the API surface and sent a future reader looking for positions. Built as **`critiques_unseen_by_assessment`**, with SPEC §10 stating the asymmetry normatively rather than leaving the name pairing to imply a symmetry that does not exist. **WHAT THE QUERY ADDS OVER A CLIENT SUBTRACTING TWO LISTS, because a client can:** the gating rule travels with the data; the denominator is counted over THIS conductor's arc at read time rather than over whatever a panel happened to fetch; and `note` is the protocol's own statement of what the figures support, which a client would otherwise invent.
