| 6.10 | **The only cheap moment to improve a permanent entry is BEFORE it exists, and nothing is in that moment.** *Opened 2026-10-29 while answering "is AI already an ambient inhabitant, or are the hooks just in place?" — and it is the one AI use this repository's own stated reason against autonomous writing does not reach.* **MEASURED 2026-10-29 — AND THE FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG, CORRECTED THE SAME DAY.** `grep -rni 'anthropic\|openai\|claude-\|llm\|inference\|completion'` over `agent-sdk/src`, `mcp-server/src`, `mobile-ui/src` and `dna` returns **two hits, both incidental** — `main.ts:4179` quoting README §4.4, and `coordinator:3718` on HRR query completion. That is true of **the happ**, and the first version of this row generalised it to *no model exists anywhere in this project*, **which is false.** `notes/src/assistant.ts:193` calls `claude-opus-5` through the official SDK — tool-use structured output (`suggest_promotion`), `betas: ['server-side-fallback-2026-06-01']` with a refusal reported to the asker rather than swallowed, and a **dynamic** import so the notes server sharing the package never loads it. `notes/package.json` carries `@anthropic-ai/sdk ^0.124.0`. **The error was the sweep, not the reading: `notes/` was never in the grep**, because the dirs came from an earlier session's list rather than from `ls`. It is the defect [[sweep-adjacent-claims-not-just-the-one-you-came-for]] names, committed inside a row about measurement. **WHAT THE PRIOR ART ACTUALLY CHANGES, which is more than the correction.** (a) There is a working pattern in this repository to copy: credential-gated (`chooseSuggester` at `assistant.ts:269` falls back to fixed keyword rules when neither `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` nor `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` is set, **and says so**), dynamically imported, refusal-handled. (b) The notes assistant is this row's NEAREST NEIGHBOUR rather than a distant cousin — it already answers *"What should I know before publishing this?"* on a note below the promotion gate. **The distinction is narrower than first written**: the notes assistant advises on a NOTE in the soft off-DHT layer, where the next action is promotion; this row advises on a CLAIM DRAFT in the happ's own compose form, where the next action is a permanent DHT write under Invariant 6. The gate is what separates them, and that is the whole reason this row is about the happ's form specifically. (c) **IT ALSO PUTS THIS ROW IN TENSION WITH A CHOICE ALREADY MADE:** `notes/` uses the HOSTED Anthropic API, so *where inference runs* was decided once in this repository in the opposite direction from what §(3) below argues for. That is a real counter-argument rather than an oversight — the notes layer is off-DHT and its content was never promised to be local — and the decision in this row has to say why the happ's compose form is different rather than assume it. And the compose flow has nothing between the draft and the permanent write: the claim form's `onsubmit` (`main.ts:4582`) records evidence, builds the `Claim`, and calls `create_claim` (`main.ts:4624`) with no step in between. **WHY THIS IS THE FIRST AI ROW RATHER THAN THE INHABITANT.** README:1743 records why the swarm's dispositions are mechanical rather than generated — *"the reason is Invariant 6 rather than caution about language models: the first permanent writes an autonomous population ever makes should have a basis anybody can audit line by line."* A compose-time adversary **writes nothing**: no entry, no link, no §6 friction budget spent, no `protocol_version` bump, no DNA change, and nothing attributed to anybody's key. So that reason is silent here — and, unlike the inhabitant, it needs **no evaluation harness gating its output**, because the output is a suggestion on the author's own screen and the author is still the one who signs. That asymmetry is the whole argument for doing this one first. **WHAT IT WOULD DO:** generate the objections a claim will attract, before the claim exists, so the author can answer them, attach the evidence that is missing, or lower `confidence` while all three are still free. Distinct from a mode classifier — it attacks the claim rather than labelling it. **THREE HONESTY PROBLEMS, AND THE THIRD IS THE DECISION.** **(1)** Machine objections rendered near real ones would fabricate discourse: 6.1's rule applies unchanged and bites harder, because here one side is not a weaker promise but **nobody's promise at all**, and a screen that lets them sit at the same typographic weight invents critics. **(2)** A claim pre-hardened against a model's objections reads as better-grounded than it was, with nothing on the record saying a machine supplied the anticipation — arguably fine, since authors consult colleagues and cite nobody, but it should be decided rather than inherited; the related trap is an author raising `confidence` because the draft survived an adversary that observed nothing and published nothing. **(3) WHERE INFERENCE RUNS IS THE LOAD-BEARING CHOICE, and a hosted API would invert a privacy property this project has had for free so far.** The text of every draft — **including the claims the author decides NOT to publish** — would leave the machine before the entry exists. That is a disclosure channel the protocol has never had and structurally cannot govern, because nothing was committed for any rule to bite on, and it is worse than the published case rather than better: an abandoned draft is the one thing a user is entitled to assume stayed local. 7.19 already records that this repository discloses network metadata nowhere; this would add a second undisclosed flow, at compose time, to a third party. **Local-only inference is therefore the only version consistent with the membrane posture**, and two routes exist today: `yourownai.net` (built by Flowsta, AGPL, shipped on macOS/Windows/Linux, Holochain-backed signed transcripts) serves **an OpenAI-compatible endpoint on `localhost`**, so the call is a URL change with no key and no egress; Ollama is the generic fallback. **That makes the integration an ecosystem collaboration rather than a vendor pick** — a named Holochain-ecosystem dependency of the kind 7.21's door asks about, with a maintainer who already follows this project. **The boundary, restated because it is adjacent and easy to cross: this row puts a model on CLAIM DRAFTS and on nothing else.** 7.25 is already a decision about a false claim in public after `Pangram 95%`, and 7.24's article and 7.4's invitations stay in the author's own voice. **A SPIKE RAN ON 2026-10-29 AND IT MOVED BOTH DECISIONS, WITHOUT TAKING EITHER.** `scripts/adversary-spike.mjs` — writing nothing: no conductor, no entry, no link, no budget. Driven by **Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct Q4_K_M** through `llama-server` (the Vulkan build, offloaded to a GTX 970), which is **the same engine Your Own AI wraps**, so the route is the local one §(3) argues for and needed no hosted call. **FINDING 1 — THE SHAPE MUST BE CONSTRAINED AT THE SERVER, AND THIS REPOSITORY ALREADY KNEW IT.** Asked for one JSON object, the 3B model replied with a bare ARRAY of objections **and then a separate object** carrying `evidence_gap` and `confidence`. Spanning first `{` to last `}` swallows both and parses as neither, so a perfectly readable answer rendered as raw text. Fixed with `response_format: json_schema, strict` — supported by `llama-server`, with a single unconstrained retry for servers that lack it, and the render saying **UNCONSTRAINED** when that path was taken. This is `notes/src/assistant.ts`'s tool-use structured output arrived at a second time from the local side; the lenient parser survives as the fallback's safety net and now scans every top-level JSON value rather than one span. **FINDING 2, THE SUBSTANTIVE ONE — THE OBJECTIONS ARE USEFUL AND THE MODE LABELS ARE NOT.** Measured across runs: *Experiential* attached to *"can you provide personal or experiential accounts…"* and *Phenomenological* to *"the draft does not provide evidence of the specific inflammatory markers"*. Both are wrong in the same direction — **the model reads the five modes as TOPIC categories rather than as the kind of objection being made**, where `integrity/src/lib.rs:526-530` defines Experiential as the objector's *own* practice report and Phenomenological as *their* subjective experience diverging. The objection TEXT was consistently worth having (3-4 per draft, naming unmeasured variables and absent controls); the label on it was not. **WHY THAT DOES NOT SINK THIS ROW, AND WHAT IT IS EVIDENCE FOR INSTEAD.** Nothing here is published, so a wrong label is a defect on the author's own screen rather than a corrupted public statistic — which is exactly the asymmetry that put this row before the inhabitant. But it is **direct measured support for the claim that the inhabitant needs an evaluation harness**: `agent-sdk/examples/critique-agent.mjs`'s `decide()` contract warns that a dishonestly-chosen mode *"would corrupt the mode distribution that `get_discourse_health` reports, which is a real cost borne by everyone reading that domain"* — and a 3B model gets it wrong routinely, unprompted, on easy cases. The harness was the expensive half of that estimate; this is why. **AND IT DECIDES ONE SUB-QUESTION OF THE SECOND DECISION: `mode` MUST NOT BE AN ENUM IN THE SCHEMA.** Constraining it is trivially available and was deliberately NOT done — an enum forces a well-formed label at the sampler and thereby **hides** the misunderstanding, leaving the author five plausible-looking modes and no sign the vocabulary was missed. Shape errors are noise worth removing; mode errors are the finding. A free-string `mode` keeps the *NOT A MODE THIS PROTOCOL HAS* surfacing live for the unconstrained path. **A THIRD, SMALLER THING THE SCHEMA CANNOT FIX:** one constrained reply satisfied `required` by setting `evidence_gap` to the literal string `"..."`. A schema cannot forbid a well-formed string, so the render now drops a field whose content has no alphanumeric character — showing it would present a placeholder as a model's judgement. **WHAT THE SPIKE DID NOT SETTLE:** where inference runs for the SHIPPED feature is still open, because a spike on the author's own machine does not answer it for every installer; and how a machine objection renders beside a real one is untouched — the spike prints to a terminal, which is not a screen beside a critique panel. **THE ENDPOINT HALF OF THAT WAS CORRECTED BY THE MAINTAINER ON 2026-10-31, AND IT NARROWS THE DECISION RATHER THAN REOPENING IT.** The spike drove `llama-server` directly, and this row described that as *the same engine Your Own AI wraps*, so the route was the local one — **true of the engine, wrong as an integration**. Eric Doriean's answer: **do not build on the `llama-server` port.** It is an implementation detail — it restarts whenever the app switches models, its port may change, and **calls to it skip the person's AIs altogether**. The supported endpoint for another app on the same machine is **`http://localhost:11435/v1`**, OpenAI-compatible and documented (`docs.yourownai.net/external-apps/`, `/external-apps/connect`), up only while Your Own AI is open, with a health check at `/health`. **Three consequences land directly on this row's §(3), which is the privacy argument the whole route exists to serve.** (a) Two headers exist for exactly the unpublished-draft case this row is about: **`X-Your-Own-AI-Record: off`** keeps the exchange out of the person's records and **`X-Your-Own-AI-Memory: off`** leaves their memories out of the call — so the draft is neither retained by the app nor mixed with the person's own history. (b) **A calling app cannot force on-device answering.** Each call is routed by the chosen AI's own mode, so an AI set to an online model would send the draft off the machine — **the exact flow §(3) says the protocol cannot govern**. The integration must therefore **ask the person to select an offline-only AI** and say why, which is a UI obligation in this row rather than a configuration detail. (c) It is a **documented public interface**, not a private port — so the ecosystem-collaboration framing above strengthens rather than weakens. **Verified end-to-end here 2026-10-31**: `/v1/models` returns each AI twice (`<name>` and `<name>:agent`), both headers are accepted, and routing resolves — the call returns `503 routing_failed, "No offline models downloaded"` on this machine only because no model has been pulled through the GUI yet. **RUN AGAINST THE SUPPORTED ENDPOINT 2026-10-31, AND THE MODE FINDING REPRODUCED ON A DIFFERENT MODEL.** Ministral-3-3B-Instruct-2512-Q4_K_M downloaded into the app at the catalog's own pinned revision (exact byte match, 2,146,497,824 bytes), `scripts/adversary-spike.mjs` pointed at `localhost:11435/v1` with both privacy headers and `--model reeves`, three drafts, ~10s each. **Three things are new.** **(a) The endpoint honours `response_format: json_schema`** — every run came back constrained, so the *UNCONSTRAINED* fallback never fired and finding 1's server-side-shape remedy holds on this route too. **(b) The call is routed through the AI's persona, which is the whole difference from the raw port:** a six-word user message billed **385 prompt tokens**, and the reply came back tagged with the GGUF — the person's AI answered, not a bare model. **(c) THE MODE LABELS ARE WRONG IN THE SAME DIRECTION AS THE QWEN SPIKE, AND THE SPLIT IS SHARPER THAN FIRST RECORDED.** `Logical`, `Evidential` and `Methodological` were used correctly across all three drafts. `Experiential` and `Phenomenological` were wrong every time they appeared: on the zazen draft `Experiential` was attached to *what is the evidence that 'unconscious goal' is the primary cause... if this is anecdotal* — a demand for evidence rather than the objector's own practice report — and `Phenomenological` to a conceptual objection citing Zen's historical emphasis rather than a diverging subjective experience; on the trunk-based-development draft `Phenomenological` was attached to a textbook confounding objection. **The three modes that describe the STRUCTURE of an argument survive; the two defined by WHOSE experience grounds the objection do not** — `integrity/src/lib.rs:526-530` makes Experiential *I tried this and...* and Phenomenological *My subjective experience differs because...*, and both models read them as TOPIC categories instead. **That it reproduces on Ministral-3B after Qwen2.5-3B, through a different serving path and with a persona in front of it, makes it a property of small models reading this vocabulary rather than a quirk of one model** — which strengthens the no-enum decision and strengthens the evidence that the INHABITANT needs an eval harness. The objection TEXT remained worth having in all three runs, naming confounds, absent controls and unoperationalised terms. **AND THE MODE-LABEL FAILURE REPRODUCED A FOURTH TIME** on the verification run of that fix (Ministral 3 3B, persona mode, draft *"Every claim in this repo is counted"*): `Experiential` went to *are there substantive assumptions about the value or purpose of these claims collectively* — a structural question with no practice report in it — and `Phenomenological` to *what are the practical, lived, or theoretical consequences*, a consequences question rather than a diverging subjective experience. The three structural modes were again correct. **Four runs, two models, one serving path changed in between: this is settled, and the no-enum decision is safe.** **THE ENDPOINT HALF OF THE FIRST DECISION IS NOW CONSTRAINED BY A MEASUREMENT, 2026-11-01.** Your Own AI's maintainer, unprompted, named the variant an integration should use: `<ai-name>:agent`, or `X-Your-Own-AI-Mode: agent`. Reading `docs.yourownai.net/external-apps/connect` to confirm it turned up **a defect in this repository's own code**: the page says *"persona mode always includes memory"*, so `X-Your-Own-AI-Memory: off` **is honoured in agent mode ONLY**. All three callers here — `adversary-spike.mjs`, `generate-injections.mjs`, `check-prose-claims.mjs` — sent that header while taking `data[0].id`, which is the PERSONA variant (verified against the running app: `reeves, reeves:agent, teresa, ...`). **The header was a no-op and the author's own memories were being mixed into every call about a draft they might never publish** — the exact property the comment above it claimed. Fixed by preferring `:agent` and sending the mode header. **BUT AGENT MODE CANNOT RUN ON THIS MACHINE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING FOR THE SHIPPED FEATURE.** It requires an *agentic* model: Ministral 3 3B is refused (*"No installed model can drive agent work"*), and Qwen2.5-Coder-7B — which the directory scan DOES classify as agentic — is refused `MODEL_TOO_LARGE` on the 4 GB card, because the app will not split layers across GPU and CPU the way a hand-run `llama-server` does with `-ngl 20`. **So the privacy-preserving mode and the hardware that is actually here are mutually exclusive today**, and the scripts now REFUSE rather than fall back to the leak; `YOAI_ALLOW_PERSONA=1` is the author's explicit consent. For the shipped feature this rules out one answer to *where inference runs*: routing through a person's own AI is the right shape ethically and **is not available on a low-VRAM machine**, so either the feature carries its own model, or it must degrade in a way that states the privacy cost out loud rather than silently sending memories. **ERIC ANSWERED ON 2026-11-03, AND ONE OF THIS ROW'S LOAD-BEARING CONSTRAINTS HAS AN EXPIRY DATE.** **(1) `X-Your-Own-AI-Memory: off` WILL WORK WITH THE PLAIN MODEL** in the next Your Own AI release — send the header and the AI answers as itself, without its memories, with no agent mode required. So *memory-off is agent-mode only*, which is **why the YOAI route was ruled refuse-or-leak on this card**, stops being true when that ships. **(2) SPLIT OFFLOAD FOR AGENT MODE IS A DELIBERATE LINE, with reasons sharper than the question assumed:** an agent session runs its model for many steps in a row and **the agent instructions alone are ~9,000 tokens**, so a model that *only just fits* is where they measured agent sessions turning into timeouts on small cards; the engine's automatic splitting also **crashed on hard card-and-CPU splits**, so the app leaves it off. A hand-run `-ngl 20` is fine for chat and is a much lighter load than a long prompt on every step. An opt-in split for dense models, marked slower, is something he is considering. **(3) AND A ROUTE THIS ROW DID NOT KNOW ABOUT: mixture-of-experts agentic models run with their EXPERTS IN MAIN MEMORY and the rest on the card**, badged *GPU + RAM* on the Offline Models page, gated on RAM and CPU rather than VRAM. **Granite 4.0 Tiny and LFM2.5** are the small ones. **So *agent mode cannot run on this machine* was true of DENSE models and is not true in general** — a correction to this row's own claim. **WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT DO TO THE DECISION. It does NOT reverse it.** Carrying a model was decided on four grounds — universal, fully local, independent of a second app, no routing UI obligation — and **availability was only one of them.** The other three stand, and **the load-bearing one is untouched: a calling app still cannot force on-device answering**, so an AI set to an online model would send the draft off the machine, which is exactly the flow §(3) says the protocol cannot govern. **WHAT IT OPENS is the option rejected partly on availability:** prefer the person's own AI when it is present and answering locally, fall back to the carried model. **Worth revisiting once that release lands and not before** — the feature works today and nothing is blocked on it. **AND (3) IS NOW MEASURED, 2026-11-03 — THE GPU + RAM ROUTE REALLY DOES CARRY AN AGENT LOOP ON THIS 4 GB CARD, BUT ONLY FOR ONE OF THE TWO MODELS HE NAMED.** Run through Eric's OWN shipped engine and headless matrix (`cargo test --lib -- --ignored live_matrix_* --nocapture` in `~/Your-Own-AI/src-tauri`, head of `main` `6935069`), which is the point of the exercise: the numbers come from his decision path, not an approximation of it. **LFM2.5-8B-A1B PASSES EVERY GATE:** auto-pick `--n-cpu-moe 18` of 24 layers, load 39.8 s, **123 prompt / 39.9 gen tok/s**, compact-cache arm within 5% — against `agent_caps("lfm2")` **6** >= 6, `agent_template_ok` true (`tools` + `tool_calls`, no `raise_exception`+`alternate`), `fit.is_fast()` **Split** (earned on real figures: `offload_wanted(5.78 > 3.56)`, RAM budget 11.3 GB, `offload_fits(4.80 <= 11.3, 5.78 <= 14.86)`, active fraction **0.125** — 4 of 32 experts, no shared expert — under the 0.30 ceiling), and `measured_tps` **39.9** >= `AGENT_MIN_TPS` 8.0. **GRANITE 4.0 TINY CANNOT, AND NOT FOR A HARDWARE REASON:** `agent_caps` returns **5** for `granite-4` against the `>= 6` filter at `router.rs:893`, with no runtime promotion path — the rule above `agent_caps` promotes a family only after a live folder test, i.e. a maintainer source change. So the model he named first is excluded by his own code on every machine, and this row must not report it as a 4 GB option. **THAT LAST SENTENCE WAS OVERTURNED BY ITS OWN SUBJECT ON 2026-11-20, AND THE REVERSAL IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THIS ROW.** Eric took the `agentic` tag off on this argument, then **measured Granite on a 4 GB card** and put it back: **8 of 8 correct tool choices on project tasks and 2 of 3 required calls — slightly AHEAD of LFM2.5-8B on required calls** — so it ships in the agentic list again in the **0.9.1 betas, this time on a measurement.** **What was right here was the epistemics and what was wrong was the verdict.** The filter reading was correct — `agent_caps` 5 against `>= 6` is what excluded it, and nothing about the hardware — and he says so: *"You were right that it shouldn't have carried the tag before one existed."* **But *cannot* was an inference from a gate, and the gate was the only thing stopping it.** The tag was unmeasured in both directions: his catalog asserted a capability with no test behind it, and **this row asserted the absence of one with no test behind it either**, then wrote *must never be reported* — a prohibition resting on a filter nobody had run against. **THE SHAPE TO CARRY: a conservative gate is not a measurement, and reading one as evidence of incapacity is the same defect as reading an optimistic tag as evidence of capacity.** This repository hunts claims that outrun their measurement; this is one of its own, and it was a NEGATIVE claim, which is why it read as caution rather than as overreach. **It also closes the abandoned download honestly:** *not worth finishing* was true of the question then being asked and is false of the model. **TWO MEASUREMENT TRAPS, both of which caught the first reading and are the reusable part:** `leg_fit_truth` called with `app: None` prints the **plain dense** grade and not `assess()`'s, so it says `claimed RED, actually RAN` for a model the app grades Split — **a headless Red is an artifact, not the app's verdict**, and reporting it as one would have inverted the finding; and the dense estimator is pessimistic by **2.8x** here (5.78 GB predicted, **2.05 GB** actual), which is what produces that Red. Separately `live_matrix_tune_bench` hardcodes `total_ram_gb = 31.0` into `arms_for` -> `choose_ctx`, so a small-machine run need not bench the app's own context choice — latent, and it did NOT change this run (both legs chose ctx 32768 regardless), so it is reported as a harness defect and not as a result. **STILL UNMEASURED: gen tok/s at ~9k depth**, the figure his timeout observation is really about — it cannot change the gate, because the app's own `measured_tps` IS the tune bench's near-empty-context `gen_tps`. **WHAT IT CHANGES HERE: the refuse-rather-than-leak scripts now have a local path that answers**, since `LFM2.5-8B-A1B-Q4_K_M.gguf` is in the models dir; Granite's download was interrupted at 2.36 of 4.2 GB and is not worth finishing.  **THE DECISIONS, TAKEN 2026-11-02, ALL FOUR, AND THE SHAPE THEY MAKE IS SIMPLER THAN THE DEBATE WAS.** **(1) WHERE INFERENCE RUNS: THE FEATURE CARRIES ITS OWN MODEL.** Not the person's Your Own AI, and not a hosted API. The YOAI route was the right SHAPE and lost on availability: `X-Your-Own-AI-Memory: off` is honoured in agent mode only, agent mode needs an agentic model that fits, and that excludes the hardware the invited cohort actually has — so that route is *refuse* or *leak* on a modest machine, and a feature most installers never see is not a feature. Carrying a model wins on four counts the debate had been treating as separate: it is **universal** (every installer gets it), **fully local** (no egress on any path, so §(3)'s disclosure channel never opens), **independent** (no second app need be running), and it **removes a UI obligation** — there is no *please select an offline-only AI, and here is why* screen, because there is no routing for the person to get wrong. The spike is what makes this affordable rather than a compromise: four runs across two models and two serving paths established that the objection TEXT is consistently worth having at the 3B class, and that the only thing a small model reliably gets wrong is the mode label — **which this feature does not use.** The quality objection to a carried model lands on the half being discarded. **WHAT THIS DECISION DOES NOT SETTLE, and it is an implementation question rather than a `[D]`:** whether the model is bundled into the desktop build or pulled on first use. Bundling makes the download large and the feature instant; pulling keeps the installer small and makes the first use a wait with a progress bar and a refusal path. Decide it against the release artifact, not here. **(2) HOW A MACHINE OBJECTION RENDERS BESIDE A REAL ONE: IT NEVER DOES.** Compose-time only. The objections exist in the draft form, before the claim exists, and are gone the moment it is published; the critique panel never shows one, and no surface co-locates them. **This dissolves the honesty problem rather than answering it** — §(1) worried that machine objections at the same typographic weight as attributed ones would invent critics, and 6.1 spent weeks establishing how to render two unequal claims honestly side by side. The lesson 6.1 actually earned is that **sometimes the honest answer to *how do we show this beside that* is *not beside it*.** There is no shared surface, so there is no weight to equalise, no caption to get right, and no injection that can make a machine look like a critic. **(3) NOTHING GOES ON THE RECORD.** No field, no link, no marker that a model reviewed the draft. Authors consult colleagues, reviewers and textbooks and cite none of them; a compose-time adversary writes nothing, is attributed to nobody, and the author is still the one who signs. A flag would make this one kind of consultation uniquely visible among all the kinds an author has, and under Invariant 6 it would be permanent and cost an integrity change and a fork. **§(2)'s trap is real and is answered on the SCREEN rather than in the record** — the form must say that surviving these objections is not evidence, because the model observed nothing and published nothing, so it cannot license a higher `confidence`. **BUILT 2026-11-02, AND THE SCOPE IS SPLIT ACROSS TWO REPOSITORIES, which this row did not say.** `mobile-ui/src/adversary.ts` (the endpoint client, the schema, the lenient parser, the placeholder drop) and the compose form's *Before you sign* section; `scripts/live-verify/compose-adversary.mjs`, **30 checks**, in `ui.yml`. **WHAT IS NOT DONE: pulling and serving the weights.** Decided 2026-11-02 to **pull on first use rather than bundle** — the desktop installers are **113-150MB** today and a 3B Q4_K_M is **~2.1GB**, so bundling would make each of six platform artifacts about fifteen times larger. **That half lives in `StateofIntent/epistemic-resonance-desktop`**, a separate repository holding electron-builder, auto-update and the `breakingVersion` mapping. ~~The interaction with `breakingVersion` is unexamined~~ **THAT WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN, checked 2026-11-02: it is examined thoroughly, in that repo's own `kangaroo.config.ts`**, which records *"A MINOR HERE WOULD HAVE FORKED THE NETWORK AND ABANDONED THE DATA ON BOTH MACHINES THAT RAN 0.3.0"* and proves the `v0.3.1` patch by hashing both webhapps' DNAs rather than reading the diff. **The rule is established; what the model work adds is a tension it does not anticipate.** **THE MECHANICS, READ RATHER THAN RECALLED.** `breakingVersion` maps `0.<minor>.<patch>` to the literal `0.<minor>.x`, and that string has THREE consumers, not two: `defaultAppNetworkSeed()` in `cli.ts` makes it the network-seed suffix (so a change forks the network); `KangarooFileSystem.connect` makes it a path segment for `userData`, `logs` and `sessionData` (so a change gives a fresh conductor — **new keystore and new agent key**, not merely abandoned data); and `index.ts:382` gates auto-update on `breakingVersion(new) === breakingVersion(current)`. **THAT THIRD CONSUMER CHANGES WHAT THE HAZARD IS.** A minor would not silently move anybody: electron-updater would REFUSE to offer it, so existing installs would sit on `0.3.x` and never be told a newer version exists. **Stranded rather than forked** — milder, still bad, and invisible. **SO THE MODEL MUST SHIP AS A PATCH (`0.3.2`), WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT CONVENTION SAYS.** Adding a feature is a minor everywhere else — it is what the npm packages did today going to `0.3.0` — and here a minor at `0.x` is a **network migration event**. **A patch is not a loophole for this change, because the model is app-side and the DNA hash is untouched**: `kangaroo.config.ts`'s own test, hash both webhapps and confirm identical, passes trivially when no zome changed. And `installHappIfNecessary`'s early return — the thing that kept 1.18 from reaching upgraded installs — does not bite here for the same reason: nothing in the happ changes. **THAT A WEBVIEW MAY CALL A LOCALHOST SERVICE WAS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — `notes.ts` already fetches `http://localhost:8790` in a feature that ships, so the pattern was copied. **THE ORIGIN CHECK IS IN THE CLIENT, NOT THE SCREEN:** `isLocalOrigin` refuses any non-localhost endpoint, because §(3)'s privacy property would otherwise be given away through a settings box, and a refusal in a screen can be bypassed where one in the client cannot. **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY BUILDING IT, BOTH THE SAME SHAPE — STATE CHANGED WITHOUT A RENDER, which is 1.23's invariant from the other side.** (1) The run button's `disabled` is computed when the form is built and typing deliberately does not rebuild, so an author typed a draft and the button stayed off until something unrelated re-rendered; the content field now toggles it directly. (2) `clearAfterPublish` nulled the verdict and **the objections stayed on screen**, because the publish path avoids `render()` on purpose — a rebuild there would let `captureFieldValues` restore the text the publish just cleared. The verdict's DOM now lives in one container that path empties. **The second defect was 6.10's own second decision broken by an invisible gap between state and DOM.** **INJECTIONS DA-DC, AND DA IS THE RESULT WORTH KEEPING: IT WENT GREEN.** §3 asserted only the COUNT of unreal-mode markers — one marker, fewer markers than objections — and **both stay true when the marking is exactly inverted**, so the section passed against a client that marked the real mode and spared the invented one. **A count cannot tell you which row it is on.** Fixed by reading the marker's own row. DB (placeholder rendered) 1 red; DC (verdict survives the publish) 2 red with the publish control green. **And the DC run was wrong the first time** because the injected file did not type-check, the build failed, and the harness ran the PREVIOUS injection's bundle with the build output piped to `/dev/null`. **Next free injection ID: DD.** **THE RUNTIME IS PINNED, 2026-11-02 — llama.cpp, MIT, Vulkan where it exists** (desktop #13). The AGPL alternative was Your Own AI's own build output, which is a licensing question before an engineering one. **The Vulkan archives carry the per-microarchitecture CPU backends beside `libggml-vulkan.so`, so a machine with no Vulkan driver falls back to CPU rather than failing to start** — read out of the archive rather than assumed. 11-36MB per target against a 113-150MB installer, which is why the RUNTIME is bundled and the WEIGHTS are pulled. **AND VERIFYING IT PRODUCED A FIFTH REPRODUCTION OF THE MODE FAILURE PLUS A NEW ONE THE SCHEMA CANNOT STOP.** Serving `qwen2.5-3b-instruct-q4_k_m` through the pinned build and sending the adversary's exact constrained request came back with **seven** objections, of which **two were not objections at all**: entries with `mode: "evidence_gap"` and `mode: "confidence"`, carrying prose that belonged in the top-level fields — **which the model ALSO filled**, putting `"High"` in `evidence_gap` where a description of the missing evidence was asked for. **SO A STRICT `json_schema` DOES NOT STOP A MODEL USING THE OBJECTIONS ARRAY AS A DUMPING GROUND FOR THE OTHER FIELDS.** `strict: true` and `additionalProperties: false` were both honoured — every entry had exactly `mode` and `text` — and the shape was still wrong in a way no schema can express, because the error is about WHICH THINGS ARE OBJECTIONS rather than about structure. **The client handles it correctly and by accident of a good decision:** `modeIsReal` marks both as *NOT A MODE THIS PROTOCOL HAS*, so they are surfaced rather than rendered as objections with odd labels — which is the free-string `mode` earning its keep a second time, for a failure it was not designed for. **WHAT IS NOT HANDLED, AND IS LEFT ALONE DELIBERATELY:** `evidence_gap: "High"` passes `meaningful()` and renders as *"What would answer the strongest of these: High"*, which is useless but is the model's own output. The client states figures and surfaces labels; it does not adjudicate content, and a filter that dropped answers it judged unhelpful would be the client deciding what the author may read. **(4) `mode` STAYS A FREE STRING** — decided earlier by measurement, restated here because the three decisions above are consistent with it: the feature shows objection text and never a mode, so the enum question is moot for the shipped form and the free string keeps *NOT A MODE THIS PROTOCOL HAS* visible in the spike. | ~~**D** twice~~ ~~**ONE AND A HALF LEFT**~~ ~~**ALL DECISIONS TAKEN**~~ **BUILT 2026-11-02 — the client half. The model's distribution is the desktop repo's half and is NOT done; see the build block.** | **nothing in the protocol** — no entry type, no link type, no validation rule, no `protocol_version` bump, no fork, and no dependency on 6.2 or on item 22's reopen condition. Blocked only on the two decisions in this row |
| 6.12 | **A reader who is told a domain name can read it; a reader who is not told has no way to find it. Nothing in the app or the protocol enumerates the domains that hold claims.** *Opened 2026-11-09 by a pilot participant's second message after publishing — "how do you search for what others wrote?"* The first time that question has been asked by somebody who was not told the answer in advance, and it has no good answer. **WHAT EXISTS: three discovery reads, and every one takes something the reader must already hold.** `get_claims_by_domain(domain)` hashes `Path::from(format!("domain_{}", domain))` (`dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5136`), so the lookup is a literal string match and `mental health reform` and `Mental Health Reform` are different domains that cannot find each other; `get_claims_by_agent` needs a base64 agent key; `get_membranes` needs nothing and returns only the domains somebody FOUNDED. **There is no `get_domains` and no `list_domains` in either zome** — checked against the full extern list, not inferred. **AND FOUNDING IS NOT REQUIRED TO PUBLISH** — 1.4 established that by measurement, `validate_claim` wants only a non-empty `domain` — so the one read that needs no prior knowledge is structurally incapable of listing the domains most likely to be missing from it. **THE BREADCRUMB WAS MISSING TOO, and that was the cheap half — BUILT 2026-11-10, see the end of this row.** As written, `renderClaimCard` printed content, confidence and author and **not the claim's own domain**; the only place `entry.domain` reached a screen anywhere in `mobile-ui/src/main.ts` was the notes room's target picker at line 549. **ONE NEAR-MISS, FOUND BY A PARTICIPANT RATHER THAN BY THIS ROW, AND IT SHARPENS THE POINT RATHER THAN SOFTENING IT:** the Worldline tab's period list DOES render a domain string, as `` `${start_time} · ${domain_tag}` `` at line 3753 — it is how Kupukupu quoted `2026-11-09 08:14 · Mental Health Reform` back on the day they published. But that is `WorldlineBoundary.domain_tag` on the reader's OWN trace, not `Claim.domain` on anybody's claim, **so the one screen in the app that shows you a domain shows you only the domains you already wrote in.** A reader can learn their own strings and never anyone else's, which is the row's thesis stated by the one counterexample to it. So a reader who arrives at one claim by any route cannot learn the string that would show them its neighbours, and the By Author byline — the one affordance built for exactly this move — carries them to an agent rather than to a field. **AND BROWSE CANNOT SAY A DOMAIN IS EMPTY — that is 6.13**, split out of this row on 2026-11-09 because it is a defect in its own right and bites every domain read rather than only the ones a reader could not find. It compounded this one: a reader who guessed the string wrongly and a reader who guessed it rightly into an empty domain were shown **the same sentence**, so the app could not tell them which of the two problems they had. **That is closed as of 2026-11-10, and the fix carries this row's reasoning rather than only its own** — the empty answer now names the typo as the other hypothesis, because this row is why a reader cannot rule it out. **WHY THE ENUMERATION HALF IS `[D]` AND NOT WIRING.** `SECURITY.md`'s honest half, quoted by 7.12 and by the coordinator above `get_membrane_claims`: **containment holds for what you traverse to and nothing for what you look up.** A critique-graph walk degrades under a flood; an anchor index returns the flood in full. The project already decided the fix is a read-layer lens, and anchored it to a MEMBRANE rather than a bare domain string precisely so that a caller cannot ask about a domain nobody founded and so the membrane-scoped `AttestationGrant` has something to be scoped by. **An index of every domain string anybody ever typed is the one shape that decision forecloses** — a bare-string enumeration of the whole network with nothing for a lens to attach to. So `get_membranes` may already BE the answer, and the real defect is that no screen says so: a reader is left to infer that the Domains tab is the index when it is a registry of the founded. **THE SPLIT, AND A PARTICIPANT CUT IT BETTER THAN THIS ROW DID — SEE 6.16, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT IS NOT BLOCKED ON ANYTHING HERE.** *2026-11-09, unprompted:* getting BACK to a domain is a different problem from finding one you have never seen, and **only the second carries this row's `[D]`**. That half was written into this row first and then split out on the same day, because an unblocked `[M]` living inside a row whose last column reads *blocked on a decision* is invisible to the scan this file tells a reader to run — the defect `check-row-status.mjs` exists to catch, in a different costume. The breadcrumb is `[M]` and small — render the domain on the claim card; the empty state is 6.13. The enumeration is `[D]`: either founding becomes the price of being findable (which makes the Domains tab honest and gives the lens its anchor, at the cost of a step before a first claim), or a second uncontained index gets built against the reasoning that removed the last one. **AND THE FIRST OUTSIDE EVIDENCE ON THAT `[D]` ARRIVED 2026-11-09, FROM THE PERSON WHO WOULD PAY THE COST.** Kupukupu — a self-described non-technical user, and the pilot's first publisher — was given the honest version of the lookup-versus-traversal argument and answered: *"requiring founding to be findable seems fair, as long as it's simple and the app explains why when you create a domain"*, with the risk named back — *"interesting topics in unfounded domains could exist but never be found by others"* — and a mechanism proposed: **prompt people to found a domain at the moment they create one**, so good topics are not lost by accident. **That is the evidence this row could not generate for itself**, because the objection to charging a step before a first claim is entirely about what it costs a newcomer, and the only people who can price it are newcomers. It is one person and should not be read as more; it is also the first time anyone outside this repository has been asked. **THE MECHANISM IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING EVEN IF THE `[D]` GOES THE OTHER WAY** — a prompt at creation time is not a gate, so it costs nothing in the branch where founding stays optional, and it converts the choice from one nobody knew they were making into one they declined. **AND THEN THEY DID THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THEY SAID, WHICH IS THE BETTER EVIDENCE.** Hours later they published the position as a claim — *"Asking people to found a domain before others can find it is worth the extra step. A topic nobody can find is worth less than one that's just one more click away"* — and filed it **into somebody else's existing domain rather than founding one of their own**, because that domain was the one place guaranteed to be read. **That is not hypocrisy, it is the rational move, and it is exactly what this row predicts**: offered a choice between topical precision and being found, a participant picks being found. | 6.20 | **Permanent prose was collapsed on the way in and composed three lines at a time: every line break in a published entry was thrown away by CSS, and the box you write one in was `min-height: 5rem`.** *Opened 2026-11-11 by two more sentences from the same report: "The window to write in could be made bigger, right now wanting to read back (as it is permanent) is difficult" and "formatting, right now reading such a blurb becomes unpleasant."* **MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS FIXED, AND THE SHAPE OF THE MISS IS THE INTERESTING PART.** `white-space` appeared NINE times in 1,857 lines of stylesheet, three of them `pre-wrap`, and not one of those three was on a surface that renders a published entry's free text: they were the 6.17 window added hours earlier, plus two notes-layer surfaces, which are device-local drafts rather than permanent records. `textContent` had always put the whole string on screen and CSS had always run it together. **BUILT 2026-11-11 AS A LIST OF SURFACES RATHER THAN THE TWO THAT WERE REPORTED** — claim content, critique content, the retraction banner, an antibody flag and an assessment's rationale — and the injection that justifies the list is the one applying `pre-wrap` to ONLY the two complained about: exactly 1 red, the assessment rationale. | 6.21 | **The update button did nothing, and every way an update could fail was built to fail silently — one of them by stopping the app from starting.** *Opened 2026-11-12 by the pilot's first publisher, in two sentences: "App shows a window stating new version available. No update happens."* **THE FIRST READING WAS WRONG, AND THE TRAP IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT WAS CONVINCING.** `gh release view v0.3.4` in the desktop repo listed assets named `org.holochain.kangaroo-electron-test-0.3.4-*` while all three `latest*.yml` manifests name `org.stateofintent.epistemic-resonance-0.3.4-*` — an exact manifest/asset mismatch, which would 404 every download on every platform and explain the report completely. **It is not real.** That repository has an `upstream` remote pointing at `holochain/kangaroo-electron`, the template it was made from, which publishes its own identically-numbered releases; with no default repo set, `gh` resolved the query there. Asked through `gh api repos/StateofIntent/...` the real assets match the manifests exactly, and a ranged GET on each manifest-named URL returns 206 while the kangaroo-named one returns 404. **A tooling default silently answered about a different project's release, and the answer was coherent.** `gh repo set-default` is the fix; reading `gh api` with an explicit path is the habit. **WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG, THREE DEFECTS, ALL OURS.** (1) **NOTHING WAS EVER LOGGED ANYWHERE A HUMAN COULD REACH.** `electron-updater` defaults its logger to `console` (`AppUpdater.js:142`) and the only `error` listener it registers is its own constructor's, whose entire body is `this._logger.error(...)`. `autoUpdater.logger` was never set, so in a packaged app every failure went to a stdout belonging to no terminal — **measured: the profile log file that "Export logs" ships contained zero lines matching `update`.** So the participant could not tell us what failed and we could not ask. (2) **NOTHING WAS EVER SHOWN TO THE USER AFTER THEY ACCEPTED.** A rejected `downloadUpdate()` was indistinguishable from a download still in progress, and both were indistinguishable from a button wired to nothing. (3) **AND THE UNGUARDED `await` ALSO ABORTED STARTUP.** `await autoUpdater.downloadUpdate()` sat bare inside the `app.whenReady()` callback, and the `launch()` that starts the conductor comes *after* it in the same callback — so a rejection there did not merely skip the update, it left the app on its splash screen forever. **The one case where somebody most needs a working app — they accepted an update that then failed — was the case where it would not start.** A fourth thing is not a defect but is half the report: the download is **140–200MB** and the dialog promised a restart without mentioning that anything had to be fetched first, so *"does nothing"* was also the honest reading of a download working normally. **THE PLATFORM IS ANSWERED: macOS. They replied "I am on a MAC" on 2026-11-12**, which promotes the suspicion below from a reading of the source to the right place to look — and settles it without anybody sending a log, which matters because the log did not exist. **SO THE LEADING CAUSE IS NOW THE ONE THIS ROW ALREADY NAMED:** `macOSCodeSigning: false`, `MacUpdater` hands the zip to Squirrel.Mac through a local proxy server, and Squirrel will not swap a bundle it cannot verify. **STILL NOT MEASURED, and the distinction is the whole discipline of this row:** nobody has yet watched that refusal happen, `nativeUpdater.on("error")` routed to the same invisible logger the fix replaces, and there is no Mac here to drive. What the fix buys is that the NEXT report arrives with the answer attached rather than requiring one. **WHAT WAS KNOWN BEFORE THEY ANSWERED, and is unchanged:** **Linux/AppImage was driven end to end on 2026-11-12 and updates correctly** — the shipped `v0.3.3` AppImage was run, offered 0.3.4, downloaded it, replaced its own file in `~/Downloads` and relaunched as 0.3.4. So the failure is macOS or Windows. **macOS is the suspect and only the suspect:** `macOSCodeSigning: false`, `MacUpdater` hands the zip to Squirrel.Mac via a local proxy, and Squirrel will not swap a bundle it cannot verify — but its `nativeUpdater.on("error")` routes to the same invisible `_logger`, so nobody has ever seen that error and suspecting is not measuring. **Windows signature verification is ruled OUT by reading the artifact rather than the docs:** `NsisUpdater.verifySignature` returns `null` early when `publisherName` is absent from `app-update.yml` (`NsisUpdater.js:84-99`), and the shipped `app-update.yml` has only `owner`, `repo`, `provider` and `updaterCacheDirName`. **THE FIX, BUILT 2026-11-12 in `epistemic-resonance-desktop`.** `logs.ts` hoists the rotating file transport out of `setupLogs` and exports `createUpdaterLogger`, so the updater writes `label: "UPDATER"` lines into the same file "Export logs" ships; `const.ts` exports `RELEASES_URL`, **read out of `app-update.yml` itself** so the download page a failure offers cannot drift from the repository the update was offered out of; and the whole flow moves into `offerUpdateIfAvailable()`, wrapped in `try/catch` at the call site so no update failure can stop the app starting. A failure now raises a dialog naming it, offering the download page, and saying that installing by hand keeps the data, the key and the network. The offer dialog says the size and that the app restarts itself. Progress goes on the taskbar/dock item via `setProgressBar` and into the log. **A check that cannot reach GitHub stays silent on purpose** — that is the ordinary offline start, and the `error` listener is therefore registered only *after* the user has asked for the update, since the updater emits `error` for a failed check too (`AppUpdater.js:230`). **MEASURED ON A PACKAGED BUILD, INCLUDING THE FAILURE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT WOULD OTHERWISE BE A VACUOUS GREEN.** An `electron-builder --linux dir` build was run with `APPIMAGE` set so `AppImageUpdater.isUpdaterActive()` passes, and its `resources/app-update.yml` repointed at a local `python3 -m http.server` serving a `latest-linux.yml` that advertises a 0.3.5 whose file does not exist. Driving the real dialog needed `hyprctl dispatch 'hl.dsp.send_shortcut{mods="", key="Tab", window="title:Update Available"}'` then the same with `key="space"` — **Return is useless for this, because GTK fires the DEFAULT button regardless of focus, which is `Deny`, and that mistake silently measured the decline path twice.** Results: the 404 arrived, **both** failure routes logged to the file (the rejected promise and the `error` event, two lines) while **one** dialog appeared — *"The update could not be installed"* — and after dismissing it **the app launched to its main window**, which is defect (3) shown fixed rather than argued fixed. The no-update path logs three `UPDATER` lines where there were none. **THERE IS NO CI GATE AND THIS ROW DOES NOT PRETEND OTHERWISE:** the desktop repo has no test script and no test file anywhere, and its two workflows only build. The harness above is written down here so it can be repeated by hand. **AND THE ROW IS NOT CLOSED BY SHIPPING IT, FOR A REASON THAT IS THE WHOLE SHAPE OF THE BUG: A FIX TO THE UPDATER CANNOT BE Log kept. **THE DIFFERENTIAL DOWNLOADER WORKS, AND THE CONSENT DIALOG OVERSTATED THE COST BY ABOUT SEVENTY TIMES — ~~A NEW `[M]` IN THE DESKTOP REPO~~ FIXED 2026-11-20.** Measured transfer: **`Full: 189,168.97 KB, To download: 2,688.79 KB (1%)`** — 2.7 MB, over in seconds. The dialog says **"This downloads about 194MB … It can take several minutes"**, because `index.ts` builds `downloadSize` from `updateInfo.files[0].size` — the size of the whole artifact — while electron-updater then fetches a block-level delta against the AppImage already on disk. **The comment above that dialog says *saying what the button is about to do is half the bug fix*, and the number it says was never measured.** The same defect as #529's README header and #525's `control` mode, in the one string whose whole purpose is to price the action honestly. The honest figure depends on the gap between versions, so the fix is to report what the updater resolves rather than the asset size. **FIXED 2026-11-20, DESKTOP PR #21 — AND FIXING IT FOUND A SECOND DEFECT OF THE SAME SHAPE, ON A PLATFORM NO MEASUREMENT HERE COULD HAVE REACHED.** `files[0]` is not necessarily the file the asking platform fetches: measured against the three published v0.3.7 feeds, **`latest-mac.yml` merges both mac runners and lists the x64 zip first, so an arm64 Mac was quoted 162MB for the 165MB file it would actually download** — and on Linux the AppImage is first only by the order electron-builder happens to write, which is why the defect survived the one platform it was measured on. `resolveUpdateArtifact` now asks the question the updater asks (the arm64 filter plus the `findFile` of `AppImageUpdater`/`NsisUpdater`/`MacUpdater`). **AND THE REAL TRANSFER CANNOT BE KNOWN BEFORE CONSENT AT ALL** — the delta is resolved inside `DifferentialDownloader` after the button is pressed — so the artifact size is a **CEILING**, and quoting the measured 2.7 MB as what to expect would have been the same defect in the other direction, true of exactly one version pair on one machine. **SO THE DIALOG NOW STATES A CEILING AND BOTH OUTCOMES WITH NO SINGLE FIGURE TO LIFT OUT, WHICH IS ROW 7.34's REMEDY APPLIED TO A STRING THE APP ITSELF SAYS** — learnt from a reader and spent on a dialog the same day: *"The whole file is 194MB, and the updater fetches only the parts of it that changed: the transfer may be a small fraction of that, or all of it if the parts cannot be compared."* The figure the dialog could not have is **logged where it first exists**: the first `download-progress` event carries the delta's grand total during a differential download and the whole file during a full one, so one line names which of the two outcomes happened and at what size. **AND IT HAS A CHECK, WHICH THIS ROW SAID TWICE IT DID NOT HAVE:** `scripts/verify-update-download.mjs` drives the module against the three **published** feeds, committed as fixtures and parsed with the updater's own `js-yaml` rather than transcribed — a restatement would agree with a wrong resolver as happily as a right one. **37 checks, and it is the first thing in the desktop repo's CI that is neither a build nor a lint.** Watched failing three ways: returning `files[0]` on darwin is five red **while the x64 section stays green**, which is the whole finding; dropping the `rpm`/`deb` exclusion is one red and invisible on the real feed; and inverting the resolved percentage leaves the full-download case **unmoved, because at 100% the inversion is the identity** — a case where the two quantities are equal cannot see which way a division goes. The `--live` leg that re-reads the release is deliberately **out** of CI, for `band-plan-check.mjs`'s reason: a rate limit there is indistinguishable from a real mismatch. **AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT ROW 6.10's DEFECT BEING COMMITTED AGAIN, HERE, AN HOUR AFTER IT WAS RECORDED.** Before running anything, five AppImages in `~/Downloads` were read — 0.1.1, 0.2.0, 0.3.0, a **byte-identical second copy of 0.3.0**, and 0.3.4 (2026-11-12), with no 0.3.5, 0.3.6 or 0.3.7 anywhere in the home — and each file's embedded `X-AppImage-Version` was checked against its filename, **on the stated premise that a successful AppImage update replaces the file in place, so a filename still naming its own version proves no update ever happened.** **THE PREMISE WAS FALSE AND THE RUN DISPROVED IT:** the updater **writes the new version beside the old under the NEW filename and deletes the old one** — after the install the directory held exactly one file, `…-0.3.7.AppImage`, and the 0.3.6 file was gone. **So "each file matches its name" is also what a working updater produces, and the inference was worth nothing.** What survives is weaker and is all that is claimed: a chain of in-place updates leaves ONE file, so five retained files plus a re-download of a version already on disk is a hand-download signature — **evidence, not proof, and the person who installs them knows which it was.** **The shape is 6.10's exactly: a negative claim resting on a mechanism nobody had run.** It did not ship only because the item said to finish the test. |

A count per agent is not: that is the Invariant 1 line the By Author tab already declines to cross. **WHAT MIGHT BE.** Anything DERIVED DETERMINISTICALLY FROM THE KEY ITSELF, so it asserts nothing the key does not already say and every client computes the same thing from the same bytes: a colour, a shape, a two-word mnemonic, an identicon. The property being bought is *same-or-different at a glance*, not *who*. **AND A SECOND, CHEAPER HALF THAT IS INDEPENDENT OF THAT DECISION:** within one panel, *same author as the critique above* is a statement about rows a reader is already looking at, needs no new vocabulary, and is the same species of marker as 6.18's *the claim's author* and 6.19's *the critique's author* — which they have now seen and reacted well to. **THE `[D]` IS WHICH, and it is not obviously ours to settle alone given who reported it twice.** **THE `[M]` IS BUILT 2026-11-13 — and the `[D]` is untouched and still the human's.** A critique whose author already objected on this claim now says *same author as an earlier critique*, beside 6.18's byline and in the same relation line as *you* and *the claim's author*. **IT SAYS "AN EARLIER ONE", NOT "THE ONE ABOVE", and that is the design rather than the wording.** The repeat is frequently not adjacent — this project's own fixture publishes stranger, author, stranger — and an adjacent-only marker would answer the question exactly when it was already easy to answer by eye. **Injected the adjacent-only version: 1 red, and it is the check that carries their sentence.** **IT COUNTS NOTHING.** *"Also wrote 3 of these"* would be a per-agent tally inside one panel, which is the Invariant #1 line the By Author tab already declines to cross; this is an identity statement about two rows the reader is looking at, and nothing is ordered, ranked or totalled by it. **Injected a version that marks every row: 2 red**, both controls. **IT IS DERIVED FROM `list`, NOT FROM 6.22's `shown`, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE — worth stating because the obvious one is false.** Today the two give identical answers, necessarily: `shown` is a LEADING slice and this search only looks BACKWARD, so for any visible row its author's first appearance is inside the slice too. The equivalence is a property of the fold being a PREFIX. | 6.24 | **A critique panel is a snapshot taken the first time it was opened, and nothing on screen says so: two readers cross-checking the same claim can be looking at different sets of critiques, and neither can tell.** *Opened 2026-11-13, found while building 6.22 — the harness could not see three freshly published critiques by collapsing the panel and re-opening it, and had to reload the page.* **THE CAUSE IS ONE LINE AND IT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT — IT WAS SEEN AND HALF-ANSWERED.** `mobile-ui/src/main.ts:2309` guards the read: `if (!critiquesByClaim.has(key)) await loadCritiques(claim)`. The comment DIRECTLY ABOVE it, fifteen lines explaining why the friction budget must be re-read on every open, says it outright: *"Re-read the budget every time a critique panel OPENS — **not inside `loadCritiques`, which the cache check below skips on a re-open**."* So the skip-on-re-open was understood, reasoned about for the number beside the list, and **the list itself was left alone.** The same handler now refreshes one thing and caches the other, for no stated reason. **WHAT A READER ACTUALLY SEES.** Your OWN critique appears, because the form's success path calls `loadCritiques` directly at `:4039` and bypasses the guard — so the one case that works is the case that proves the surface *can* update. Everybody else's is invisible until the page is reloaded, which is not an action this UI asks for anywhere. Collapsing and re-opening does nothing, which is the thing a user would try. **THIS IS §4.4's OWN FAILURE MODE, ARRIVING FROM A DIRECTION §4.4 DOES NOT DISCUSS.** That section requires *"claim text, its critiques, their `CritiqueMode`s, authorship and ordering should render identically for every viewer"*, and its argument for why is precisely this: a presentation that varies *"breaks this silently — the cross-check degrades and nobody can see that it has."* §4.4 reaches for per-user ADAPTATION as the cause. **A session cache produces the identical outcome with no adaptation anywhere:** reader A opened the panel an hour ago and sees four critiques, reader B opens it now and sees six, and when A evaluates B's critique they are not looking at the same artifact. **The irony is worth recording rather than smoothed over — 6.22's fold was measured against this rule and cleared it, while the cache sitting beside it has been violating it since the panel was written.** **AND IT IS NOT 6.15, WHICH ALREADY OWNS THE NEIGHBOURING QUESTION.** 6.15 is *how does the AUTHOR of a claim find out somebody critiqued it* — a notification problem whose `[D]` is push versus pull. (Its cost was recorded here as a permanent CapGrant per `send_peer_signal`; **that was a misread injection record, corrected 2026-11-14 in row 6.15 — one entry per agent once, zero per signal.**) **This row is about a READER WHO IS ALREADY LOOKING** and gets a stale answer to a question they asked by opening the panel. Same split as 6.16 out of 6.12: the unblocked half, invisible where it sat. **SIGNALS CANNOT FIX IT, AND THE REASON IS CHEAP TO STATE SO NOBODY SPENDS A DAY ON IT.** `SignalPayload::NewCritique(Critique)` is **declared in `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:5234` and emitted NOWHERE** — a fourth published-but-unused declaration alongside the three this file already records. Emitting it would not help either: `emit_signal` reaches the LOCAL conductor's own websocket clients, so a critique published by a remote agent emits on THEIR node and never arrives here. Live push would need `send_peer_signal`, which is 6.15's priced-and-rejected cost. **THE `[M]`, AND THERE IS A PRECEDENT IN THIS FILE FOR IT:** drop the guard so opening a panel re-reads it, exactly as `loadFrictionStatus()` already does one line above, and as `refreshTaxonomy()` does on a tab switch (`:1652`). **THE PRICE, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** `loadCritiques` fans out to `loadConductances`, `loadAssessments`, `loadUnseen` and `loadReplies`, so a claim with six critiques costs roughly seven reads per open instead of per session. That is the cost of a surface that is true. **The existing behaviour already pays it on first open; this makes it pay again on each subsequent one, and nothing about it is unbounded.** **WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT DECIDE,** because re-read-on-open does not reach it: a panel LEFT open goes stale while somebody reads it. Polling is one answer and a continuous read nobody asked for; saying nothing is another. That sub-question is genuinely open and is **not** a blocker on the line above. **THIRTEEN SIBLING CACHES HAVE THE SAME SHAPE** — `antibodiesByClaim`, `retractionsByClaim`, `groundingByClaim`, `assessmentsByClaim`, `resonanceByClaim`, `critiqueTreeByClaim` and the rest — and this row claims nothing about them. They were not audited, and whether each is a snapshot-with-consequences or a genuinely immutable record is per-map work; the `.critique-item` audit two rows up is the precedent for how that should be done, and the lesson there was that the dangerous instances were not the ones that looked alike. **THE `[M]` IS BUILT 2026-11-13 — the guard is gone, and the harness that had to work around it no longer does.** `reading-a-thread.mjs` section 7 used to RELOAD THE PAGE to see critiques published mid-run, with a comment recording why; it now **closes and re-opens the panel**, which is the gesture a reader would actually make. **THE CHANGE OF GESTURE IS ITSELF THE ASSERTION:** a reload would still pass against the caching code, and a close-and-re-open would not. **Injected the old guard back: 5 red** — 6.24's own check plus the four fold checks that cannot run without a fresh read, which is the coupling stated honestly rather than hidden. **AND THE FIX INTRODUCED A CASE THAT DID NOT EXIST BEFORE, WHICH IS WHERE MOST OF THE WORK WENT.** With a read-once panel, a failure could only happen on the FIRST open, where there is nothing on screen to mislead anybody. Re-reading on every open creates a read that fails **while a previous answer is still rendered** — and keeping that answer silently would be this row's own defect committed by its own fix: a list that reads as current because nothing says otherwise. So `loadCritiques` now distinguishes the two: on a first open the rejection is rethrown and the panel goes on saying *Loading critiques…* (unchanged, and 6.13's separate gap on this panel); on a re-open the previous list is KEPT and the panel says, above it, *"Could not re-read the critiques just now — this is what came back last time, and there may be more since."* **Placed above the list because it qualifies everything below it, and a reader who scrolls away before a footnote has been told nothing.** **THE FAILURE PATH IS VERIFIED BY INJECTION AND IS NOT GATED, which is stated because an ungated path is a liability rather than a detail.** Forcing every re-read to throw: the notice RENDERS (the control asserting its absence goes red, so the count was ≥1), the old list SURVIVES rather than blanking, and nothing throws into the page. A real gate would need the conductor to fail mid-run on command, which this harness cannot do cleanly — so the recipe is written here instead. **One thing came out better than designed:** the control added for the success case — *the panel is not claiming to be stale* — turns out to be a true detector of the stale state rather than decoration, since it reds exactly when an old list is on screen beside a notice. **A NEW THEME TOKEN, AND THE REASON IS A MISTAKE AVOIDED RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE.** The notice wanted a warning colour; `--danger-text` would have said something stronger than the screen means, and a hardcoded fallback would have been invisible in light mode and wrong in dark — the way this mistake always presents. `--warn-text` is defined in BOTH the `:root` and `prefers-color-scheme: dark` blocks. **THE PRICE IS PAID AND MEASURED:** about seven reads per open for a six-critique claim (the fan-out to conductances, assessments, unseen and replies), where the first open already paid it; the old list stays on screen while the re-read runs, so there is no flicker and no empty frame. **AND THE TWO THINGS THIS ROW SAID IT WOULD NOT DO ARE STILL NOT DONE:** a panel LEFT open still goes stale, and `critiqueTreeByClaim`'s identical guard is **deliberately untouched** — same shape, but `loadCritiqueTree` is a recursive fan-out to depth 3, so re-reading it on every open is a different cost and deserves its own decision rather than this one's momentum. | 6.25 | **The per-map audit 6.24 deferred, and the family it found is not caching: four surfaces where a read that FAILED rendered as an answer nobody got.** *Opened and BUILT 2026-11-13, as the piece of work 6.24 wrote down and declined to do — "whether each is a snapshot-with-consequences or a genuinely immutable record is per-map work".* **THE SWEEP.** `mobile-ui/src/main.ts` declares **27 module-level stores**; exactly **six** are read once behind a presence guard (`chainByClaim`, `critiqueTreeByClaim`, `resonanceByClaim`, `replacementClaimByHash`, `constitutionByAuthor`, `sampledPeriods`), plus `notesBySpace` in `notes-ui.ts`, which is a render guard and not a load one. **THREE OF THE SIX ARE NOT AMONG THE THIRTEEN `*ByClaim` SIBLINGS 6.24 NAMED**, and several of the thirteen it did name are fine — which is the `.critique-item` audit's lesson arriving a second time: *the dangerous instances were not the ones that looked alike.* **THE ONES THAT LOOK ALIKE AND ARE NOT, verified rather than assumed:** `adoptionBySpecies` (a render guard — "adoption unavailable" vs a count — and `refreshTaxonomy()` re-reads the counts on a tab switch); `antibodiesByClaim`, `retractionsByClaim`, `groundingByClaim`, `assessmentsByClaim`, `unseenByCritique`, `unseenByAssessment` (all cleared by `loadClaims` and re-read by `loadClaimEpistemicState`, so re-reading a domain refreshes them); `myCritiqueCounts` (a render guard, and its only writer is called unconditionally); `notesBySpace` (three states, loads unconditional). **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG IS ONE BLOCK, AND IT IS THE BLOCK THAT ALREADY EXPLAINS ITSELF.** `loadClaims` clears nine stores before the read and says why in a comment — *"a list that stays up while a different domain is being read is the same lie as an empty one that cannot say it was asked."* **Four more per-claim stores were simply not in it**: `chainByClaim`, `resonanceByClaim`, `critiqueTreeByClaim`, `replacementClaimByHash`. Every one of them sits on a card in the list that block is about to replace, so a reader who re-read a domain to see what had changed got one surface that updated and four that did not. **`replacementClaimByHash` IS THE WORST OF THE FOUR, BECAUSE OF WHAT IT RENDERS.** The absent branch tells the reader, in its own words, *"nobody reachable is holding it yet — not that it does not exist"* — and caching that answer for the session made the sentence false. **A read that THREW already retried** (the guard checks the result map and the error goes to `replacementReadFailed`); **a "not yet" never did.** The asymmetry was backwards: the recoverable answer was the permanent one. **EACH STORE IS CLEARED WITH ITS OPEN-SET, and that is not tidiness.** An open panel whose store has gone renders its absent state, and after a deliberate clear all four of those absent states are a lie: *"The walk returned no path"*, *"Nothing to probe against — load more claims in this domain first"*, *"Reading the critique structure…"*, *"Looking up what the author believes now…"*. `expandedClaims` is already in that block for exactly this reason. **Measured, not reasoned: clearing the chain's steps and leaving its panel open reds three checks, one more than clearing neither.** **THE CRITIQUE TREE HERE IS NOT A CONTRADICTION OF 6.24's DEFERRAL.** That one refused to re-read a depth-3 fan-out every time a panel OPENS, which costs something. Dropping it when the whole claim list is replaced costs nothing, and is the only thing stopping the structure panel from disagreeing with the critique panel one button above it — which re-reads on every open since 6.24. The per-open question is untouched and still open. **AND THEN THE FAMILY THE AUDIT ACTUALLY FOUND, WHICH IS NOT CACHING AT ALL: four reads whose failure rendered as something other than a failure.** **(1) Resonance** — the catch wrote `resonanceByClaim.set(key, [])`, which renders *"Nothing to probe against — load more claims in this domain first"*: an instruction derived from a read that never answered, pointing the reader at work that cannot help, and cached so the panel could never ask again. The empty branch's own comment — *"a legitimate answer, not a failure"* — was true of the `[]` the success path writes and false of the `[]` the catch wrote. **`loadConstitution`, forty lines away, states the rule it broke**: *"rendering a failed read as that answer would assert something nobody checked."* **(2) Constitution** — that load was RIGHT to leave a failed read ABSENT, and there was nowhere for the failure to go, so the body went on saying *"Loading…"* after the attempt had finished. 6.13's rule, in a fourth place. **(3) The critique tree** — `loadCritiqueTree` had **no catch at all** and is `await`ed from an `onclick`, so a read that threw was an unhandled rejection AND left *"Reading the critique structure…"* with nothing reading. **(4) The period sample** — the only one that already said it had failed, and the only one whose retry was blocked: the guard was `!sampledPeriods.has(i)`, so **the one state that asks to be retried was the one state that could not be**, and closing and re-opening the window — the only gesture that screen offers — silently did nothing. **BUILT: the four clears, three new failure surfaces (`resonance-failed`, `constitution-failed`, `critique-graph-failed`), and the failed-sample retry.** Each failure is a SECOND map (`resonanceFailed`, `constitutionFailed`, `critiqueTreeFailed`) rather than a widened value type, which is `replacementReadFailed`'s existing idiom beside `replacementClaimByHash` — and leaving the RESULT map absent is also what makes the presence guard retry on the next open. **AND CLEARING ALONE DOES NOT CLOSE IT, which is the one thing in this row that was reasoned rather than observed.** A read already IN FLIGHT when the reader clicks lands after the clear, writes into the store that was just emptied, and the presence guard then declines to ask again — so the panel holds a result resolved BEFORE the re-read while the badge above it holds one from after, which is the disagreement this row exists to remove arriving through the one door clearing cannot cover. A `browseGeneration` counter is bumped before the clears and captured by each of the four loaders before its first await; a result from a previous generation is dropped. **Stated as reasoning and not as a measurement: the window is a click inside a walk's duration, and no harness here can open it on demand.** **GATED: 5 checks in `evidence-retraction-ui.mjs`, 5 in `neighborhood-ui.mjs`, 2 controls in `mode-and-constitution.mjs`, 1 in `graph-ui.mjs`, every one anchored to a positive finding first** — "no panel is on screen" passes trivially on a harness that never opened one. **WATCHED FAILING SIX WAYS:** both chain clears removed → **2 red**; the chain open-set alone → **3 red**; all three resonance clears → **1 red**; the resonance open-set alone → **3 red**; the probe read forced to throw → **6 red** with the notice rendering its reason and **no uncaught page error**; the constitution read forced to throw → **9 red**, both new controls among them. **AND THREE HARNESSES WERE WAITING ONLY FOR THE GOOD OUTCOME, which is this row's own finding about the suite.** `waitForSelector('[data-testid="author-promises"]')` and two like it turn a conductor that will not answer into a harness that **stops reporting instead of reporting a failure** — the first chain injection killed the run outright at a 20-second timeout, with its reds already on screen. Each now waits for the answer **or** the failure surface, and the checks say which arrived. **Making them tolerant created a vacuous green on the spot:** a negative check (*uses no deficiency language*) scored a PASS against an empty string, so it is anchored to the text existing — the same shape this suite shipped once already. **WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE.** The failure surfaces are **injection-verified and NOT gated**, for 6.24's reason: a real gate needs a conductor that fails on command. `critiqueTreeByClaim` per-open and 6.24's sub-`[D]` on a panel left open are untouched. **And one residue is recorded rather than fixed:** `constitutionByAuthor`'s cached `null` is never re-read, so a constitution published mid-session stays invisible — it is keyed by AUTHOR and not by the domain being browsed, so no gesture on that card is obviously a request to re-read it, and inventing one is a design question rather than a clear. | ~~M for the audit~~ **M BUILT 2026-11-13** | nothing — the clears and the failure surfaces are closed; `critiqueTreeByClaim` per-open and the constitution's cached absence are recorded above as open |

**BRIEFED 2026-10-26 — `docs/four-interaction-calls-one-decision.md`. THE FOUR
ARE ONE DECISION, AND 6.1 IS MIS-FILED.** Each of the four is the same question
asked four times — *what must be on screen before a user signs?* — and the
useful split is not per-screen: **6.2/6.3/6.4 are writes that commit the
signer; 6.1 is a read that describes a third party.** Within the first family
the rule is identical and the protocol **already publishes** every precondition
the screen must show (6.2 the lens, via `describe_method`'s string already
carried into the entry; 6.3 the policy by hash, the inputs, and every competing
status; 6.4 the objections already raised). So it is one component, not three,
and the through-line is **Invariant 1 at the view layer** — a screen rendering
one agent's output without its method rebuilds the canonical measure where no
validation rule reaches. Recommended order **6.2 → 6.4 → 6.3**, with each row's
counter-argument stated in the brief.

**AND 6.1 IS NOT BLOCKED ON A DESIGN DECISION — see 6.5.** Measured
2026-10-26: **no client populates `past_cone`** (zero hits across
`mobile-ui/src` and `agent-sdk/`; the field is `#[serde(default)]`), and
`cone_recorded = !critique.past_cone.is_empty()`, so it is **false for every
critique that exists**. A 6.1 screen built today can render exactly one state —
*no cone recorded* — for every critique, which is what `critiques_unseen_by`'s
own note already says. **The feature has no true positives available to it.**

**Item 22's reopen condition is structurally gated on 6.2**, which is a useful
property rather than a coincidence. Both halves are required — *a surface for
readings exists* AND *somebody wants them faster than a person will click* —
and the first is false while 6.2 is unbuilt, so the autonomous-writer question
cannot become live by any other route.

| 7.12 | ~~**The read-layer lens does not reach the reads a sybil flood pollutes.** `SECURITY.md` names a read-layer lens as the right fix for free identity, and it exists: `AttestationPolicy` (`require_attestation_from`, `min_attestations`, hop depth) with `is_agent_attested`, verified by `trust-lenses.mjs` at 31 checks and already wired into `get_discourse_health`. Negative evidence is cheap — seed a domain from several keys, assert the unfiltered read returns all of them and a filtered read only the attested ones, so a lens that silently did nothing goes red. **Not urgent for ten invited people**: a free-identity flood is an adversary model and an invited cohort is not one, which is why 7.1 declined to wait for it. ~~ **BUILT 2026-10-25 — `get_membrane_claims`, and `scripts/live-verify/index-lens.mjs` at 15 checks.** Coordinator-only: no entry type, no link type, no validation rule, **so no DNA hash change and no fork.** `None` is `get_claims_by_domain`'s behaviour exactly, asserted against it. **Anchored to a Membrane rather than a bare domain string**, so a caller cannot ask about a domain nobody founded and the `AttestationGrant` source has the membrane its tenure bar is anchored to. **The drops are counted (§7b.1)** — `examined`, `unresolvable`, `filtered_unattested` — because a read returning three of a hundred and saying only "three" is indistinguishable from a domain holding three; `get_claims_by_domain` drops unresolvable links silently and this read counts them instead. `attestation_filtered` is §7b.2. **Injection: the policy replaced with `null` gives 4 reds**, and the over-refusal guard correctly stays green.
