| 7.13 | ~~**What counts as a participant for step 4 — and the answer is NOT in Promise Theory.** *Raised 2026-10-26, immediately after 1.4 closed and made 7.4's blocker non-technical.* Step 4 says *invite ten people*; nothing anywhere says whether an agent may be one of the ten, and the `agent-sdk` and `mcp-server` exist precisely because **agents are first-class participants** — README calls the agent surface *"the other door into the same invariants"*, guarded by `mcp-server.mjs` failing if a ranking tool ever appears. So the question is real and open. **THE TEMPTING ANSWER IS WRONG: this is not settled by item 22, and item 22 is not settled by Promise Theory.** Item 22 (no autonomous write) is titled *Invariant 9's consequence*, and **SPEC §7's Invariant 9 note states that neither Invariant 6 nor 9 is a Promise Theory axiom** — 1, 2, 4, 5 and 8 are derivations; **6 is an import from an immutable-ledger ethic and 9 an import from autopoiesis**, so *"Promise Theory cannot adjudicate between them, because it speaks to neither."* Item 22 therefore sits downstream of two imports and no axiom. **And item 22's third reason arguably inverts on PT's own terms**: a scheduled tick runs as the author of the chain it runs for, so it holds the key and its promises ARE its own — PT locates agency in the keyholder and does not model the human behind it, so *"promises made while nobody is watching"* is an ACCOUNTABILITY objection, not an autonomy one. **SO THE REAL CONSTRAINTS ARE THREE, AND NONE IS THEORETICAL.** (1) **7.1's honesty commitment** — describing ten agent identities as ten participants is a false description, and 7.1 vouches for the documents saying accurately what the protocol does. (2) **Step 4's own purpose** — *"enough to find what three conductors on one machine cannot"*; agents the project operates are more conductors the project operates, so the number would rise without the proxy changing. (3) **3.5** — ten observer identities may be one actor, and on one model the exploring minority is *"one mind in different hats"*; this is the same question 3.7 answered for sybils, where the conclusion was that **nothing verifies distinctness and a reader names who they trust** (`docs/sybil-cost-and-the-root-set.md`). **Agents instead of people fails all three; agents ALONGSIDE people, declared as such, fails none — and ten people each running an agent fails none either**, which is the configuration step 4's reasoning actually supports. **THE RECOMMENDATION IF TEN HUMANS IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT: fewer than ten, declared.** Step 4's number is a proxy for *enough to find what one machine cannot*, not a threshold — four real operators find more than ten simulated ones, and 7.5 and the scalability work gate on step 4 having produced **real reports**, not on a headcount.~~ **DECIDED 2026-10-27, AS RECOMMENDED: four to six humans, each declared as a person, each free to run an agent alongside, and no EU or UK invitees in the first cohort.** Written into `docs/the-invitation.md` §2 with the three reasons attached — step 4's purpose is served at four, 7.1's honesty survives only if agents are declared rather than counted, and 7.16's practical-enforcement argument is an argument about *having no EU users*, so keeping the first cohort out of the EU/UK is what keeps §1 free of the controllership sentence. **One European invitee is what changes that**, and the place that sentence goes is already marked. **What remains is not a decision**: which four to six people, which nothing here can supply. | ~~D~~ **TAKEN** | — |

| 7.14 | **THE `[M]` HALF IS BUILT AS OF 2026-10-27 — AND IS NOT RELEASED, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR ANY DOCUMENT. The claim below that the app 'cannot currently REMEMBER one' is now false of the CODE and still true of every INSTALLED COPY.** `epistemic-resonance-desktop` #11: `--bootstrap-url` and `--relay-url` persist to `network-override.json` in the profile's data directory, precedence is flag then stored then the value compiled into the bundle, and passing a flag WRITES it — so pointing somebody at new infrastructure is a one-time instruction rather than one they repeat every launch, which was the whole blocker. **`--reset-network-config` is the escape hatch and is not optional**: a persisted bad URL would otherwise survive every restart with no way out but deleting a file whose location nobody has been told, which would have made this strictly worse than the flag-only behaviour it replaces. A corrupt override warns and falls back to the built-in default rather than refusing to start, because an app that will not launch cannot be pointed anywhere. Startup now logs `bootstrap_url: <url> (flag|stored|built-in)`. **AND TWO FLAGS WERE REMOVED RATHER THAN PERSISTED: `-s, --signal-url` and `--ice-urls` were declared in commander and read by NOTHING** — absent from `CliOpts`, `validateArgs`, `RunOptions` and every consumer. They are tx5-era options with no iroh equivalent, and the conductor config this app writes has only `bootstrap_url` and `relay_url`; a flag accepted and silently discarded is worse than one that does not exist, because it looks like it worked. **VERIFIED END TO END ON 2026-10-27**, after first being verified only as logic. Three launches against an isolated `nettest` profile, one per behaviour: (1) `--bootstrap-url https://test-bootstrap.invalid/` logged `Stored the network override for this profile.` and `bootstrap_url: ... (flag)`, **and the value reached the generated `conductor-config.yaml`** rather than only the JSON; (2) **relaunched with NO flag at all** — `bootstrap_url: ... (stored)`, with no re-write line, which is the single behaviour this row exists for; (3) `--reset-network-config` logged `Network override cleared; using the URLs built into this build.`, deleted the file and fell back to `dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org (built-in)`. Plus the 14 behaviour checks against a temp directory, typecheck and lint clean. **AND ONE THING FOUND BY RUNNING IT THAT WAS ASSUMED WRONG BEFOREHAND**: a dev run does NOT share the packaged app's data directory — Electron resolves `userData` to `org.stateofintent.epistemic-resonance-dev`, a separate root, so a dev launch cannot reach a real profile even without `--profile`. The caution that it could was wrong, and is recorded because the opposite assumption is the dangerous one. **THREE RELEASE-TIME OBLIGATIONS, RECORDED HERE BECAUSE TODAY PROVED THEY ARE FORGOTTEN OTHERWISE** — `INSTALL.md` said `v0.3.0` for the third time while `v0.3.1` was already published. When this ships: (1) `INSTALL.md` line ~97 `| -b, --bootstrap-url <url> | not persisted across restarts |` becomes FALSE; (2) the same file's ~405 *'those are not persisted across restarts'* becomes FALSE; (3) both places still name `-s, --signal-url` and `--ice-urls`, which will no longer exist. **None of the three may be corrected BEFORE the release**, because every invitee is being told to install `v0.3.1`, where the old text is accurate — documenting unreleased behaviour as current is the same defect as leaving released behaviour undocumented, in the other direction. **WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS THE `[H]` HALF AND IT IS UNCHANGED**: no bootstrap, signal or relay this project controls, and that is an operating cost rather than a code change. **Original text follows.** **The whole network depends on one server this project does not own, and it is somebody's DEV/TEST box.** *Opened 2026-10-26 — it had NO row, which is why it is here: `grep -i bootstrap docs/open-work.md` returned nothing while every other document mentions it.* `INSTALL.md` states the risk plainly and has for a while: peer discovery runs on **`dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org`**, *"Holochain's own development and testing infrastructure. It is not run by this project and nobody has promised to keep it running. If it goes away, installed copies stop finding each other."* **So the honest status of the release is that its peer discovery has a single point of failure owned by a third party and labelled dev/test by its operator.** **AND THE EXPOSURE IS WIDER THAN THE INSTALLED APP**: the same host is hard-coded in `scripts/sandbox.sh`, `scripts/cross-internet-node.sh`, **`conductor.yml` and `cross-internet.yml`** — so its disappearance takes out the `conductor` required check and the cross-internet experiment along with every user, and a green CI badge is not independent evidence that this is fine. **THE FIX IS TWO PARTS AND ONLY ONE IS OPS.** (1) Run a bootstrap/signal/relay this project controls — `[H]`, real infrastructure with real uptime obligations, and the first operational cost this project has ever had. (2) **The app cannot currently REMEMBER one**: `-b/--bootstrap-url`, `-s/--signal-url`, `--relay-url` and `--ice-urls` exist but `INSTALL.md` records them as **not persisted across restarts**, so pointing a user at new infrastructure today means either a per-launch flag or a rebuilt bundle with different defaults — `[M]`, in `epistemic-resonance-desktop` rather than here. **WHY IT IS NOT URGENT FOR THE TEN AND IS A HARD BLOCKER FOR A CROWD**: four invited people can be told to relaunch with a flag, or simply told the risk; an audience arriving from a link cannot, and would discover it as *"the app stopped working"* with no way to tell that from a defect. **This belongs with the integrity bump as a gate on GOING WIDE** (social posts, cold outreach, funding conversations) rather than on step 4 — and unlike the bump, nothing in this repository has ever named it as one. | **H to host** — ~~M to make it persist~~ **DONE, unreleased** | nothing — it is a decision about operating cost, and the first one |

| 7.16 | **The deployment decision SPEC says must be made DELIBERATELY, and it has two halves rather than one.** *Opened 2026-10-26. Erasure appears in `SECURITY.md`, `INSTALL.md`, `SPEC.md` twice and `READINESS.md`, and in this file ZERO times until now — the same shape as 7.14, with higher stakes.* **SPEC §7's own words:** *"a request to erase personal data — a right under GDPR Article 17 and comparable regimes — cannot be complied with on this network, by anyone, by any means short of every peer abandoning the DNA. That is a deliberate property of the design and not a defect, but it is a decision to make explicitly rather than discover: a private group among informed participants is a different proposition from a public deployment."* **HALF ONE, WHICH IS WRITTEN DOWN: private informed group, or public deployment?** Four invited people who understand it is one proposition; 54 likes on a tweet is another, and almost certainly includes people in the EU/UK, which is what engages Article 3 at all. **HALF TWO, WHICH IS NOT WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE: should the default write path put personal data on an immutable store at all?** The accepted mitigation for immutable systems is to keep personal data OFF it and commit only a hash — **and this protocol already has that mechanism, built for a different reason**: `SealedPrediction` is its one private entry type, and §5's hash-then-reveal is exactly the pattern (it was described to Luck Light on 2026-10-26 as *"publish only the hash and reveal later"*). But `create_claim` puts content straight onto the DHT, so **the compliant shape is available and is not the default.** Changing that is a design decision, not a documentation one. **THREE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARPER THAN THE DOCUMENTS CURRENTLY SAY.** (1) **Pseudonymous is still personal**: Recital 26 treats pseudonymised data as personal where re-identification is possible, and an agent key is re-identifiable to anyone who knows whose it is. (2) **Every peer stores other peers' data, so INVITEES MAY INHERIT OBLIGATIONS, not just risk** — the regulatory direction on distributed ledgers (CNIL's 2018 guidance is the usual reference) is that those who decide to write are controllers and those who store may be joint controllers. **`docs/the-invitation.md` discloses permanence and says nothing about this**, which is arguably the larger gap, since permanence affects only their data while controllership affects their duties. (3) **Disclosure and consent do NOT discharge it**: consent is withdrawable, withdrawal triggers an erasure obligation that cannot be performed, and Article 25's data-protection-by-design means building it this way is itself what gets assessed — *"technically impossible"* is not a defence. 7.1 committed to honesty, which this is; it did not and could not commit to immunity. **WHAT REDUCES THE EXPOSURE MOST IS NOT ARCHITECTURAL: keep the content about the world rather than about people.** A network of claims about choline in eggs carries far less than one where people write about each other, and that is a cohort-and-content choice rather than a code change. **AND BEFORE ANY PUBLIC POST OR FUNDING CONVERSATION THIS NEEDS AN HOUR OF ACTUAL LEGAL ADVICE** — not as a hedge: Holo Ventures and comparable funders will ask, and *"erasure is structurally impossible"* is a diligence finding that needs a prepared answer rather than a shrug. **This is a going-wide gate alongside the bump, 7.14 and 7.2's absent audit — and it is the only one of the four this repository already told itself to decide and then did not track.** **ADVICE RECEIVED THE SAME NIGHT, AND IT MOVED ONE HALF AND NOT THE OTHER — `docs/erasure-and-the-content-split.md`.** Informed guidance rather than counsel, recorded with its own hedges intact, and it does NOT discharge 7.2 or replace counsel before a funding conversation. **What it discharges is the question this row was stuck on: whether the architecture has to change BEFORE the invitation. It does not.** (1) **Australia**: the Privacy Act's small-business exemption — most operators at or under **$3M** turnover — still stands as of mid-October 2026 (blogs saying 2026 abolished it are wrong; the OAIC wants it gone and a reform round is being prepared, so it is a fact with a shelf life). **The exposure that survives the exemption is the statutory tort for serious invasions of privacy**, which any individual can sue on — so the real scenario is not the Act, it is somebody writing something genuinely harmful about a person into a store nothing can be removed from. (2) **EU**: Article 3 reaches on paper; enforcement against an Australian individual with no EU presence, revenue or users is very unlikely, **so the variable is 7.13's cohort rather than the code**, and a public post recruiting one EU user is what spends it. (3) **The regulators' preferred shape is the one this protocol already implements once**: EDPB *Guidelines 02/2025* — personal data off-chain, only hashes or proofs on-chain, a proof of existence over identifying content, and a DPIA first where risk is high. **HALF ONE IS THEREFORE UNCHANGED IN DIRECTION** (private informed group; keep the pilot out of the EU) **and HALF TWO IS FILED AS 4.6 RATHER THAN BUILT**, because the advice's second pass withdrew its first: *"no longer a must-do before the invitation, but I'd still note it as a design decision."* **7.4 IS NOT HELD ON THIS ROW.** What the advice asks for before people arrive is in **7.17** and **7.18**, neither of which touches the zomes. **AND THE SALTED-FINGERPRINT ITEM IT FLAGGED AS "do this today" IS ALREADY DONE**: `seal_prediction` rejects an empty salt at `dna/coordinator/src/lib.rs:7249` with the brute-force rationale in the error string, caller-supplied so it is in their hands at reveal time — the rule to carry forward is that any withdrawable-content path inherits it. **One independent confirmation worth keeping**: the advice's *do not build the identity-verification layer* is 3.7's candidate (4), reached here from the invariants rather than the regulations. Two arguments, one conclusion, neither needed the other. | D, and the outside input it asked for is now on file; counsel still wanted before a public post or funding conversation | nothing — and it no longer blocks the invitation |
| 7.17 | ~~**The joining promise and a complaints channel — what 7.16's advice actually asks for before people arrive, and neither needs code.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night.* **THE PROMISE**: invitees affirm that they are responsible for their own content, that posts are permanent, and that **they will not post personal information about anyone else**. As a membrane **required promise** it is signed and ordered, so the agreement itself is on the record — and it is the direct answer to the thing the advice says the pilot needs instead of an architecture change. **It also decides `docs/the-invitation.md` §3**, which is currently open on whether invitees are asked for a `Constitution` at all: this is a concrete thing to ask for, and 7.1 declined *"invite as a pure experiment"* precisely because asking for nothing *"gets testers rather than users"*. **THE LIMIT, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERSOLD: the people most likely to cause trouble never signed it.** A third party written about by a member is not bound by the members' promises; an indemnity from a pseudonymous key is worth what you can collect from a key; and statutory protections generally cannot be signed away. **Exposure also tracks ROLE rather than text** — writing software others run is far from liability, while running the bootstrap (7.14), founding the membranes and inviting the users is closer to operating, which is the current position. **THE COMPLAINTS CHANNEL IS THE OTHER HALF, AND THE MECHANISMS ARE ALREADY BUILT**: a `Retraction` by the author, an `AntibodyPattern` by anyone (SPEC §7's additive remedies), plus removal from a reader's own trust list and root set (3.7). What is missing is a **stated** channel and a promise to act on it, so that *"nothing can be removed"* is not the same sentence as *"nothing was done"*. **The defamation-reform reason behind this is a question rather than a conclusion** — intermediary protections generally assume a complaints process and some available act, and how that applies to a decentralised network, or in SA specifically, was explicitly not settled by the advice. **WRITTEN 2026-10-26 NIGHT — `docs/the-joining-promise.md`, 165 lines.** The promise is three phrases in the idiom the harnesses already use, published by the invitee as a `Constitution` — *answer for what I publish*, *treat everything I publish as permanent*, *publish no personal information about another person* — with a fourth offered because the complaints channel needs it (*answer a complaint about something I published*: a promise to RESPOND, not to remove). The file carries the exact `publish_constitution` and `create_membrane` payloads, the matching `required_promises`, the plain-English paragraph for the invitation, the channel itself, and the limits. **The founder promises first and in public, and that much IS enforced**: `create_membrane`'s non-empty-list and own-Constitution rules live in `validate_membrane`, whose comment records that the claim they were enforced was false when first written. **AND MEASURING THE ENFORCEMENT FOUND 6.6.** `join_membrane` is unrestricted by design — its own comment says so — and the `AgentToMembrane` branch (`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:2456-2495`) enforces three things, none about promises: a well-formed 36-byte tag, **the tag matching the real author** so nobody can record your membership for you, and a base that is a real `Membrane`. **`required_promises` is written, N4L-exported, and compared against nobody's `Constitution` anywhere in either zome** — so the promise is checkable only by hand, which is a surfacing gap rather than a protocol one. **The non-enforcement itself is correct and must not be "fixed"**: membership is a voluntary promise, and a protocol refusing the join would make the membrane an authority over who may speak — *put it in the reading, not the record*, for the fifth time. ~~ **ADOPTED 2026-10-27: a published `Constitution` carrying FOUR promises** — *answer for what I publish; treat everything I publish as permanent; publish no personal information about another person; answer a complaint about something I published.* The fourth is in deliberately, because the complaints channel needs somebody who has promised to RESPOND — never to remove, since removal does not exist. **Both halves are now in `docs/the-invitation.md`**: §3 carries the ask, the exact payloads (via `docs/the-joining-promise.md` §1) and the order the protocol half-enforces — the founder publishes their own four first, because `create_membrane` refuses a membrane whose constitution is not the founder's own and refuses an empty `required_promises`, both DHT-side — and **§1 now carries the ask and the complaints channel in the invitee's own language**, including the sentence that matters: *nothing can be deleted* and *nothing will be done* are different sentences. **The limits are stated in §3 rather than left implicit**: it binds nobody who never joined, cannot be enforced against a pseudonymous key, and some protections cannot be signed away — which is 4.6's and 3.9's territory, both filed and neither built. | ~~M to write the text~~ **WRITTEN**; ~~D~~ **ADOPTED** | — |
| 7.18 | **A one-page impact assessment, written in-house — the cheapest item on this list and the only bounded one.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night, out of 7.16's advice.* What data the network holds, why, who can see it, the risks, and the mitigations actually in place: permanence disclosed before anyone writes (`docs/the-invitation.md` §1), pseudonymous keys with no real names required, no identity-verification layer by deliberate decision (3.7 candidate 4), additive remedies rather than erasure, and the content split filed as 4.6. **It is not a DPIA in the Article 35 sense and should not claim to be** — the EDPB expects one where high-risk processing is likely, and the point of writing this version is that it is free and it demonstrates good faith if anyone ever asks. **It is also the artifact a funder's diligence question lands on**, which is 7.16's other reason for existing: *"erasure is structurally impossible"* needs a prepared answer rather than a shrug. One page, no decision upstream of it. **WRITTEN 2026-10-26 NIGHT — `docs/privacy-impact-assessment.md`, 110 lines, and the one-page constraint was kept because the detail belongs in the rows it points at.** It states what it is not (no counsel, no authority, not an Article 35 DPIA), inventories what is processed **field by field across all 19 entry types** — the only identity is `AgentPubKey`, the only external identifiers are the three an author opts into (`BridgeRecord.twitter_id`, `ExternalCritique.twitter_id`, `ExternalCritique.author_handle`), and **no field anywhere holds a real name, email, address or special-category data** — and records that nothing in the installed app phones home, with `bridge/`, `gateway/`, `federation/` and `mcp-server/` not deployed for the pilot (7.3). **Its conclusion accepts the residual risk for a pilot of this size on three conditions**, two of them still decisions: the permanence disclosure before anyone writes (in place), 7.17's promise and complaints channel (`[D]`), and 7.19's missing sentence. **AND IT FOUND 7.19, which is the reason writing it was worth more than an hour.** | ~~M to draft~~ **DRAFTED**; **D** to publish | nothing — publishing is a decision, and 7.19 is the thing it surfaced |
| 7.19 | **Network metadata is personal data and this repository discloses it NOWHERE.** *Opened 2026-10-26 night by writing 7.18, which is what the exercise was for.* **The absence is measured, not impressionistic**: *"IP address"* and *"network metadata"* appear in **zero** `.md` files here, and *"address"* appears in **neither `INSTALL.md` nor `SECURITY.md`** — so a reader auditing the **entry types**, the natural thing to do, finds nothing, because none of this is in an entry. **WHAT IS ACTUALLY TRUE, stated as conservatively as the evidence supports**: peer discovery publishes an `agent_info` record carrying a transport URL to a third-party bootstrap this project does not own (**7.14**, `dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org`, its operator's dev/test box); that server sees a connecting node's IP as any HTTP server does; a peer negotiating a direct connection can learn the other end's address; and where NAT prevents that, a third-party **relay** operator sees the traffic instead — `INSTALL.md` already records a run that went through the relay entirely, without naming what that means for the user. **The precise tx5/iroh mechanics were NOT measured for this** and the row should not be read as claiming they were. **WHY IT WAS MISSED IS THE INTERESTING PART AND IS WORTH KEEPING: it is the opposite shape to every privacy risk this project has reasoned about.** Invariant 6's risk is permanent, in the DHT, and disclosed at length; this is transient, outside the DHT, and disclosed nowhere — so the attention that went to the hard problem skipped the ordinary one. **THE FIX IS TWO SENTENCES AND IS NOT A CODE CHANGE**: one in `docs/the-invitation.md` §1 and one in `INSTALL.md`, saying that running this reveals your IP address to peers and to two third-party services, the way any peer-to-peer application does. **It is `[D]` rather than `[M]` for one reason only**: what an invitee is told is a decision, and §1 is the artifact 7.1's honesty commitment runs through — which is also the argument for adding it. | 7.27 | ~~**The WebKitGTK 2.52.6 webview crash — drafted as a COMPATIBILITY NOTE, OFFERED 2026-10-30, and still not filed.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-31 — HE REPLIED, AND HE HAD ALREADY FOUND IT.** Holding this row for his choice of tracker was the right call and it cost nothing: the venue question answered itself. The cause is a **WebKitGTK 2.52 bug that fires when NVIDIA's EGL does the drawing** — Flowsta reproduced it in-house on a machine drawing through NVIDIA, a second Linux user reported it the same week, and WebKit's own MiniBrowser crashes in the same frames on a plain page mutating the DOM every 400 ms, while machines drawing through Mesa did not crash in any of their tests. **Flowsta reports it to WebKit themselves**, so neither tracker was ever ours, and nothing on this side is outstanding. Their list of switches that did NOT help (DMABUF off, forced SHM, compositing off, Skia CPU, `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE`, GBM off) matches the draft's own. **The workaround is one variable** — `__EGL_VENDOR_LIBRARY_FILENAMES=/usr/share/glvnd/egl_vendor.d/50_mesa.json` — and Flowsta will set it at startup when the NVIDIA driver is loaded, across all three of their Tauri apps. **VERIFIED HERE 2026-10-31, and the GUI ran on this machine for the first time:** same packages as the crash run (`pacman.log` records no upgrades in between, so the variable is the only change), app under native Wayland with its window mapped 927x1056 and the onboarding screen rendering. **Two things the verification added that his report did not have:** (a) the Error 71 reproduces on a **static page with no JS**, so DOM mutation was never required to trigger it and **Wayland was incidental — NVIDIA's EGL is the trigger**; (b) **XWayland is NOT an escape from the bug — a claim this row made and then corrected.** Under `GDK_BACKEND=x11` with NVIDIA's EGL the process survives, which is what *survived 40s* originally recorded, but the window **maps blank and no `WebKitWebProcess` ever starts** — measured properly 2026-10-31 after the first pass checked only that the process had not exited. The backend comes up fully behind the empty window, which is what makes it misread as working. The Mesa variable remains the only configuration here where the UI appears. (c) on this card Mesa has no driver for the GM204 (`pci id 10de:13c2, driver (null)`, `DRI2: failed to create screen`), so the variable means **software rendering for the webview** — the right trade against a crash, but a trade, and worth him knowing before it is set for every NVIDIA user. **GPU compute is unaffected** (`GPU detected: NVIDIA GeForce GTX 970 - 4.2 GB`), so the cost is rendering and never inference. The downgrade-to-`2.48.6` candidate is **moot**. `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-webkit-2.52-linux.md` now ends in a *Resolution* section carrying the confirmation matrix; it is a record, not a filing. The original draft below stays as the durable account of what was measured before the cause was known. *The X DM accompanying 7.26's issue #3 described the finding in three sentences and put the venue to him explicitly — his tracker, `bugs.webkit.org`, or neither. So this row now waits on HIS answer rather than on a decision here, which is the right place for it: a report saying "your app crashes" that turns out to be a system webkit bug is the one move that would cost credibility, and letting the maintainer pick the tracker removes that risk entirely.* *Drafted `docs/upstream/your-own-ai-webkit-2.52-linux.md` 2026-10-30; the isolating test re-run that day.* **IT IS NOT THEIR BUG AND THE DRAFT SAYS SO IN ITS FIRST LINE** — the whole reason it needs a decision rather than a send. Your Own AI's backend comes up fully and the web process then SIGSEGVs ~1s after the UI mounts, taking the app with it (`WebKitWebProcess` dies first, `app` a second later, every launch; `SEGV_MAPERR`, frame 11 in anonymous executable memory reached through `JSC::profiledCall`, which is the shape of a crash in generated code and is NOT asserted as the mechanism — `JSC_useJIT=0` did not prevent it and Arch ships no webkit debug symbols). **The isolating test is the finding:** WebKitGTK's own `MiniBrowser about:blank`, no Tauri anywhere, dies in ~1s with `Gdk-Message: Error 71 (Protocol error) dispatching to Wayland display`, and under `GDK_BACKEND=x11` survives 20s while logging `Failed to create GBM buffer of size 2048x1460` against the NVIDIA stack. So: `webkit2gtk-4.1 2.52.6-1` + Hyprland 0.56.2 Wayland + nvidia 580.178.04 on a GTX 970, and the app's crash and MiniBrowser's protocol error are two exits from one root. **WHY IT GOES TO THEM AT ALL:** Tauri links the system webkit, so their official `.deb` fails identically on any distro at 2.52 and no packaging change avoids it; their CI builds Linux on `ubuntu-22.04` (`build-release.yml:95`) and never launches a webview, so the combination is untested by construction; and from the user's side the symptom is indistinguishable from their bug — window appears, vanishes, logs end mid-success. `scripts/check-webview-floor.mjs` shows they already treat the OLD end of the WebKitGTK range as a real constraint, and this is the same constraint from the new end. **The ask is only a known-issue line plus a startup log line recording the resolved webkit version**, which is the one thing a diagnostics file should carry for a crash this fast — and README:136 already asks reporters to attach one. The untried candidate, downgrading to `2.48.6-1` with `IgnorePkg`, is in the draft as **not attempted and not recommended**: four minor versions of browser-engine security fixes for a GUI, unconfirmed as a fix **AND IT IS SHIPPED, 2026-11-01.** He replied again: the fix is built and **ships in the next Your Own AI release** — when the NVIDIA driver is loaded and the user has set neither variable themselves, the app uses Mesa's EGL with WebKit's GBM path off, with `FLOWSTA_WEBVIEW_FIX=0` to turn it off and a plan to drop it once WebKitGTK is fixed. **The llvmpipe cost was already known to them and chosen on purpose** — a working window beats a crash or a blank one, and the engine stays on the GPU — so the "worth them knowing it is the trade" note was not news. **What they took is the static-`h1` reproduction from this side**, cleaner than their own DOM-mutating one because it needs no JavaScript at all; they will find or file the WebKit report themselves. **Nothing further is owed and nothing is filed from here.** | ~~X~~ **DONE — nothing to file** | **Resolved upstream by the maintainer, not by a filing from here.** The 2026-10-30 DM put the venue to him; his 2026-10-31 reply carried the cause, the workaround and the plan to report it to WebKit. The `bugs.webkit.org` section of the draft is therefore spent |
| 7.28 | ~~**`partition-rejoin` is red on every pull request**~~ **— IT IS NOT, CORRECTED 2026-10-31 BY THE NEXT RUN — ~~and the 205.5s is the first figure this phenomenon has produced that fits nothing already documented.~~ **— ALSO FALSE, CORRECTED 2026-10-31 AGAINST `READINESS.md`: 205.5s is one 5-second poll from the 210.5s already on the record, and this row had been reading the README's six-figure summary instead of READINESS's nine-figure list.** *Opened 2026-10-30 after the check failed on PR #391, a branch that cannot have caused it.* **WHAT IS FAILING IS A PRECONDITION, NOT AN ASSERTION, and the distinction is the whole row.** `partition-rejoin.mjs:760` checks that the Phase 0 baseline — one claim crossing nodeA → nodeB with **both nodes up and nothing partitioned** — lands inside `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS`. On both recent runs every substantive assertion PASSED: the partition held, nodeB returned and converged in **5.0s**, the isolated nodeC saw nothing throughout, and both nodes answered at the end. So a red `partition-rejoin` here is **not** a protocol regression and must not be read as one. **THE THREE NEW OCCURRENCES, AND THE THIRD ONE PASSED.** `425.8s` on `main` 2026-10-28 (run 40704216520, the #390 release merge, job 14m46s); `205.5s` on PR #391 2026-10-30 (run 41042209076, job 11m49s); then **`10.0s` — PASS, `ALL CHECKS PASSED` — on the SAME branch about twenty minutes later** (run 41044792444, job 4m26s, poll 3 of the 5.0s sampler), which is the run #391 merged on. **THAT THIRD FIGURE IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THIS ROW** and it was not available when the row was written: a healthy baseline minutes from a 205.5s one, on code that differs by one documentation commit, is evidence for environment-dependent intermittency rather than for anything deterministic about THIS pair of runs. It also means the harness is not broken and needs no repair to keep being trusted. ~~**425.8s IS NOT NEWS** … **205.5s IS DIFFERENT, and it is the only reason this row exists** … close to **half** of ~420s, which under the existing reading would be the same schedule caught at a different phase.~~ **ALL OF THAT IS STRUCK, 2026-10-31, AND IT WAS WRONG TWICE OVER.** **(i) THE RECORD IS `READINESS.md:735`, NOT `README:1266`.** The authoritative list is nine figures — 175.4s, **210.5s**, 415s, 421.1s, 424.9s, 884.8s (×2, local three-node), 929.1s, 943.0s — and **210.5s has been on it since #278**, recorded there as an occurrence *on a change that provably could not have caused it*, which is exactly 205.5s's position on #391. One 5-second poll separates the two figures, and `awaitConvergence` cannot distinguish crossings inside one sampling interval. So 205.5s is an ordinary member of a documented distribution and **does not clear §9's reopen condition.** **(ii) THE READING IT LEANED ON IS RETRACTED IN PLACE.** `READINESS.md:764` strikes the deterministic-schedule hypothesis and demonstrates why: identical reported figures come from variance **smaller** than the 5s sampling interval, by construction — true crossings at 418.0s, 419.5s, 420.9s and 421.0s all report as `421.1s`. The "half of ~420s" argument therefore rested on a superseded claim, reached by citing the README paragraph that still carries the old version instead of the correction. **THE MECHANISM OF BOTH ERRORS IS THE SAME:** this row consulted one file and not its sibling — [[sweep-adjacent-claims-not-just-the-one-you-came-for]], committed inside a row whose other evidence was checked line by line, and surfaced only because two branches were being audited for something else entirely. **SO THE LIVE DECISION IS A DIFFERENT ONE, AND IT IS NOT §9's.** §9's freeze stays shut: these are *"another slow crossing"*, which its reopen condition names as insufficient. What has moved is the **retry's own retirement condition**. `READINESS.md:521` left the count at *"nine observations, five of them slow enough to fail without a retry… five of nine is not yet 'most', and the next occurrence decides whether it becomes so."* These two are the next occurrences. **The count is now ELEVEN, and the slow tally is six or seven depending on a classification this row cannot make**: 425.8s exceeds both harnesses' windows and is unambiguously slow, while 205.5s is UNDER `real-gossip`'s 330s window and OVER `partition-rejoin`'s 60s precondition — the same split the ninth observation recorded as *"simultaneously a pass-with-a-warning in one harness and a failure in the other"*. **Six of eleven is already "most"**, and `real-gossip.mjs`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` comment hangs its own falsification on that word: *"a retry needed on most runs is not an intermittency being absorbed, it is a routine failure being hidden."* So the question for a person is whether the retry is now hiding a routine failure — with the **10.0s** crossing minutes after the 205.5s standing as the live evidence that the intermittency premise has not simply become false. All three occurrences are recorded at `READINESS.md:530` where the count lives, rather than only here, because recording them only in this row would repeat the error above. **WHAT THIS ROW IS NOT ASKING FOR: raising the 60s.** `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is DERIVED — `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5` at `partition-rejoin.mjs:280` — because the dwell before asserting absence must outlast gossip by 5x or the absence proves nothing. Raising the threshold without raising the 300s cap makes the divergence assertion weaker than designed while printing a clean PASS, and the comment at `:786-796` records that the first version of this fix did precisely that and was reverted: *"a label claiming more than its assertion tested — this directory's named failure mode."* Raising the cap instead costs job budget against a 600s convergence window. **The harness's own instruction is the default action:** *"one occurrence is not a distribution, so record it beside the others before changing any window."* This row IS that recording. ~~**THE ACTUAL NEW COST, which is not latency.** Every PR is now red on this check, so a red `partition-rejoin` has stopped carrying information — the next real failure in that harness arrives indistinguishable from this one. That is a signal-quality problem with a deadline attached, and it is the half of this row that gets worse by waiting.~~ **STRUCK 2026-10-31: BOTH SENTENCES WERE FALSE, and the next run disproved them before this row was a day old.** `partition-rejoin` passed at 10.0s on the following run, so it is not red on every pull request, a red result still carries information, and nothing here degrades on a clock. The error was generalising a persistent failure from two consecutive ones — which is [[sweep-adjacent-claims-not-just-the-one-you-came-for]]'s defect in its other direction: not an unswept sibling, but a claim extrapolated past the two data points that actually supported it, written into the same row whose evidence had been checked carefully. **NOTHING IN THIS ROW IS NOW URGENT.** The 205.5s is still the first figure in neither documented cluster, so §9's reopen question stands exactly as stated below — it simply stands without a deadline. **NOT TO BE CONFLATED WITH 7.8**, which is the returning-node asymmetry: that one is a known peer recovering slower than a stranger, measured across the public internet. This is a FIRST crossing between two conductors on one GitHub runner with nothing offline. Both plausibly live in `kitsune2` scheduling, and that is as far as the shared claim goes. **THREE MORE OBSERVATIONS 2026-11-01, AND THE COUNT NOW ANSWERS THE OPEN CALL ARITHMETICALLY.** The eight negative-evidence PRs (#407, #410-#415) are **documentation-only** — comments in `scripts/live-verify/*.mjs` and one README table row each, no code path into any harness — and `partition-rejoin`'s `<= 60s` baseline precondition failed on three of them: **95.3s** (#407), **421.1s** (#411), **421.1s** (#413). So the count goes from eleven to **fourteen, nine-or-ten slow** depending on how 205.5s still classifies. **95.3s is a NEW VALUE in the distribution** — every prior figure sat either near the 5s floor or above 200s, and a baseline that is slow but not *minutes*-slow is the first evidence against a purely bimodal reading. **AND THAT ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG — WITHDRAWN 2026-11-02, THE SAME PASS THAT WROTE IT.** It said *nine of fourteen is past "most runs" on any reading* and offered the **D** as satisfied. **Fourteen is the count of SLOW-CROSSING OBSERVATIONS, not of RUNS**, so dividing slow by slow answers nothing; *most runs* needs every run in the denominator. Corrected: of the fourteen observations, **ten exceed `real-gossip`'s 330s window** and therefore would need a retry (415s, 421.1s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s, and 2026-11-01's two 421.1s), while **four are slow but UNDER 330s and need none** (175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s, and 95.3s). Against **100+ `network.yml` runs** that is a retry on roughly **10% of runs — not most.** **The new 95.3s figure made the case WEAKER, not stronger**, being under 330s. **A SECOND CONFLATION in the withdrawn version:** `partition-rejoin`'s `<= 60s` precondition failures were counted as evidence about `real-gossip`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS`. Those are two thresholds on one phenomenon and only the >330s subset bears on the retry. **SO THE RETIREMENT CONDITION IS NOT MET and the D is NOT satisfied** — `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` stays at 3. Retiring it here would redden CI on ~10% of runs for a documented, understood intermittency, which is the flaky-red-meaning-nothing this repository refuses, and `JOB_CEILING_MS`'s own note adds that the retries and the job timeout cannot be chosen independently. **AND THAT MEASUREMENT IS NOW TAKEN, 2026-11-02 — THE CONDITION IS ANSWERED NO AND THE D IS CLOSED.** Method: `real-gossip.mjs:1206` emits `::warning::THE CROSSING NEEDED n ATTEMPTS` exactly when `crossing.n > 1`, so every retry is in the logs by construction. Scanned the 59 `network.yml` runs GitHub still holds (2026-10-31 to 2026-11-01), excluding **6 where the crossing was never measured** — a run that died before the crossing is evidence of nothing, and counting it either way is the denominator error this row already withdrew once. **RESULT: 7 of 53 runs needed a retry, 13.2%. *"Most runs"* would require 27 of 53.** So the premise `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` rests on has NOT changed, it is absorbing an intermittency rather than hiding a routine failure, and **the retry stays.** **AND THE SAME SCAN SHOWS 3 IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN SPARE, which is the finding nobody was looking for:** of the seven, six crossed on the SECOND attempt — but run `41289924229` needed **all three, on both of its job attempts**. `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS: 2` would have reddened that run. The comment above the constant argued for three on burst-limiter grounds (15 initiations per peer per 10 minutes, so two attempts at 330s can land inside one window); **that argument is now observed rather than reasoned.** **TWO LIMITS ON THE FIGURE, stated so it is not over-read.** It is a **FLOOR on retries and a ceiling on clean runs**: `gh run list` reports a run's CURRENT conclusion, so a run that needed a retry and was then re-run clean reads clean — the same instrument problem README §7b.2 records for the `ui` rate, where the honest answer was published as a band for this reason. And the window is **two days of heavy docs-only CI**, which is a biased sample in the direction that would INFLATE the rate (a contended runner is what makes the crossing slow), so 13.2% is if anything generous to the retirement case. **The 205.5s classification no longer gates it** — at nine of fourteen the answer is *most* whether 205.5s counts or not, which is the first time this row has not needed that question settled first. **Do not raise the 60s**: `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5`, and raising it alone prints a clean PASS on a weaker assertion — already tried and reverted **A FIFTEENTH OBSERVATION, 2026-11-08 — and it is recorded rather than retried.** Main's post-merge run at `cd1f25d` (PR #439, row 1.25's build) failed `partition-rejoin`'s Phase 0 precondition — *the baseline is fast enough to measure divergence inside the job budget (`<= 60s`)* — with the baseline crossing **421.2s** on an UNPARTITIONED network, both nodes up. The `network` harness job in the same run PASSED and the only other non-pass was this harness's own deliberate INCONCLUSIVE, which the log points away from in as many words: *"See the baseline check above for the thing that is actually wrong."* The log census shows no zome-level errors, and the diff was coordinator-only — **another observation on a diff incapable of causing it**, which is this row's oldest pattern. **IT COUNTS IN THE >330s SUBSET BY ITS FIGURE, NOT BY ITS THRESHOLD, and the distinction is the one this row already had to make once:** what was wrong in the withdrawn version was counting `<= 60s` PRECONDITION FAILURES as evidence about `real-gossip`'s `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS`; a measured crossing of 421.2s is a >330s crossing whichever harness observed it. So the tally is **fifteen observations, eleven over 330s** (415s, 421.1s ×3, 421.2s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s) and **four under** (95.3s, 175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s). **THE RETIREMENT ARITHMETIC IS UNCHANGED IN DIRECTION:** eleven against a `network.yml` run count that has itself grown past 100 is still roughly a tenth, and still not *most runs* — the figure that would retire `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` is a proportion of RUNS, and slow observations are not its denominator. **NOT RE-RUN FOR A GREEN BADGE, DELIBERATELY.** A retry here would have removed the observation and left the badge, and the observation is what the open call rests on. **AND THE 60s STAYS**: `MAX_SOUND_BASELINE_MS` is `DWELL_CAP_MS / 5`, so raising it alone prints a clean PASS on a weaker assertion — already tried and reverted. **A SIXTEENTH, THE SAME DAY — AND THE PROPORTION IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** PR #444's head (`feat/exclude-self-attestation`, a coordinator-only diff touching the ATTESTATION WALK) failed the same Phase 0 precondition with the baseline crossing **421.0s**, 2026-11-08, about ten hours after the fifteenth. One failing check again, and again on a diff that cannot cause it: the change excludes an agent from their own attester list and cannot make a claim take seven minutes to cross between two nodes on one machine. **TALLY: sixteen observations, twelve over `real-gossip`'s 330s window** (415s, 421.0s, 421.1s ×3, 421.2s, 424.9s, 425.8s, 884.8s ×2, 929.1s, 943.0s) and **four under** (95.3s, 175.4s, 205.5s, 210.5s). **THE FIGURES CLUSTERING AT ~421s ARE NOT SIX INDEPENDENT MAGNITUDES, and this row's own header already says why:** the sampler's resolution is ±5.0s, so *an identical figure across runs is expected from this instrument, not anomalous*. 421.0, 421.1 and 421.2 are the SAME mode recurring, not evidence of a new one — counting them as distinct magnitudes would be reading precision into an instrument that has none. **WHAT IS GENUINELY NEW IS THE DENOMINATOR.** This row's retirement condition is *a retry needed on most RUNS*, and slow observations were never its denominator. 2026-11-08 supplied **four** `partition-rejoin` runs in one day — `cd1f25d` (421.2s, FAIL), `866f1f1` (PASS), PR #444's first head (421.0s, FAIL), and **PR #444's second head (PASS)** — so **two of four**, against roughly a tenth across 100+. **The fourth run is the pattern this row already records: the fifteenth and sixteenth were each followed by a pass on the very next run, exactly as 425.8s and 205.5s were followed by 10.0s.** One day is not a rate, and a day at half against a long-run tenth is the first thing here worth watching deliberately rather than recording passively. **If the coming days hold near half, the retirement condition is met and `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` goes; if they revert to a tenth, today was a cluster and the long-run figure stands.** Either way the measurement to make is runs-per-day, not more slow crossings. **STILL NOT RE-RUN, AND THE 60s STILL STAYS** — both for the reasons recorded against the fifteenth. | **D** — ~~rule whether 205.5s clears §9's reopen condition~~ **(ANSWERED 2026-10-31: it does not)**; ~~whether a retry is needed on *"most runs"*~~ **ANSWERED NO, 2026-11-02, BY MEASUREMENT: 7 of 53 runs needed a retry (13.2%), against 27 of 53 for *most*.** `CROSSING_ATTEMPTS` stays at 3 — and the same scan found **3 is load-bearing, not spare**: one run needed all three attempts twice over, so 2 would have reddened it. **The 2026-11-01 claim that the arithmetic was already answered is WITHDRAWN** — it divided slow observations by slow observations. **This row has no decisions left.** Recording is done, in `READINESS.md` and here | **nothing in the protocol** — no entry type, no validation rule, no `protocol_version` bump. Harness and CI only, and no code change is proposed until the ruling |
| 7.29 | **The pilot network only exists while two participants happen to have the app open at the same moment, and nothing in the project says so.** *Opened 2026-11-09, by trying and failing to verify a participant's "i published the claims".* **DECIDED AND BUILT THE SAME DAY — see THE NODE THAT EXISTS at the end of this row; what is still open is the DISCLOSURE, not the machine.** **MEASURED, three ways agreeing.** Driving the shipped `v0.3.3` desktop build: `get_claims_by_domain("Mental Health Reform")` returned zero while the control domain `xnet-1026-a` rendered its claim, so the read path was working; `admin.agentInfo` returned **one** record — this machine's own agent, `storageArc: null` — and stayed at one across 3+ minutes of polling; and the bootstrap server itself, asked directly (`GET https://dev-test-bootstrap2.holochain.org/bootstrap/<space>`, where `<space>` is the 32 bytes at offset 3 of the DNA hash, base64url), **lists exactly one agent for this DNA: ours.** **So the zero was not evidence about the participant.** Agent info on the bootstrap server expires within the hour, so somebody who publishes and closes the app is gone from the network before anyone can read them. **WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT THREATEN.** Permanence is untouched — a claim is on its author's own source chain the moment it is committed, and 1.4 proved both directions cross between packaged installs. What is missing is **reachability**, and the pilot's working assumption — *publish when you can, I'll read it later* — depends on exactly that. With two or three laptop participants and no always-on node, the common case is an honest read returning nothing, which is also 6.13's empty state stated as an operational fact rather than a UI one. **AND THE PERSISTENT AGENT DOES NOT COVER FOR IT, FOR A REASON THAT WAS MISDIAGNOSED FIRST AND THEN MEASURED.** `~/.epi-agent/inhabitant` holds `dht-uhC0kb-i4w7LvWd-pzqG3Wmm76P0U4pFZ3yGXhWzp-aRCpinsVDdh.db`; the desktop 0.3.x profile holds `dht-uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o.db`. **The first guess — that the repo's `.happ` had drifted from the release — was wrong, and `git log` says so: no commit has touched `dna/integrity/src/lib.rs` since 2026-10-24.** The real cause is the one this file already records at §0: `uhC0kb-i4w7…` is the BUNDLE's unseeded hash, and the desktop applies a network seed at install — `${productName}-${breakingAppVersion()}`, i.e. the literal `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x` (`epistemic-resonance-desktop/src/main/cli.ts:102`, `filesystem.ts:190`). **PROVEN RATHER THAN INFERRED:** installing this repo's own `epistemic-resonance-happ.happ` with `network_seed: 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'` yields `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o` exactly — probe installed, hash read, uninstalled without ever being enabled, so it never joined anything. **THE `[M]` HALF IS ONE LINE:** `stand-up-and-verify.mjs` passes no `network_seed` to `installApp`, and adding that string puts the inhabitant on the pilot DHT with a full arc, which is the only always-on node this project would have. **THE `[D]` HALF IS WHY IT HAS NOT BEEN DONE.** (1) It stands a SECOND key belonging to the same person in a network whose whole premise is that a key answers for what it publishes — the inhabitant already declares `declared_automated_agent` and `automated_key_answered_for_by_named_person` precisely so this is disclosure rather than a sybil, but declaring it to a DHT nobody else is on has never tested the claim on anyone. (2) **It restarts the tenure clock.** Tenure is read off the `AgentToMembrane` join action timestamp, so a join on the pilot DNA begins a new 30 days and the 2026-12-08 date in [[persistent-agent-tenure-clock]] moves — **though that date is worth little as it stands, because the grant it is accruing is on a DHT with one member and can never be exercised by anybody but itself.** (3) An always-on full-arc node holds every claim anyone publishes, which is the deployment question 7.16 already has open, pointed at a machine rather than at a policy. **THE FREE ALTERNATIVE, and it should be said to participants either way:** tell them the network needs overlap — publish and leave it open, and say when. That costs nothing, needs no decision, and is the honest description of what they have. **THE NODE THAT EXISTS, 2026-11-09 — and the one-line fix named above is NOT the fix that was applied.** Editing `stand-up-and-verify.mjs` would have put the DECLARED AUTOMATED AGENT into the pilot, and that is a decision already made the other way: its own record says it is *a declared instrument, not a participant... NOT in the pilot and must not be*, because an AI the author prompts is the author one layer removed, and under Invariant 6 that record could never be tidied up afterwards. Founding `ProtocolInhabitant` on the pilot DNA would have overturned that silently, as a side effect of a logistics fix. **SO THE SPLIT THAT MATTERED WAS INFRASTRUCTURE VERSUS RECORD, NOT WHETHER TO HAVE A NODE.** What was built is a SEPARATE root — `~/.epi-pilot-node`, a fresh key, admin **9499** (clear of `~/.epi-agent`'s 8899/8898, `sandbox.sh`'s 8888/8889 and `swarm.sh`'s 9200+), a **hand-written** conductor config so no `.hc` exists for it and `hc sandbox clean` cannot reach it from any directory, and a **systemd user unit with lingering enabled**, so it survives logout and reboot rather than a terminal session. **IT PUBLISHES NOTHING, AND THAT IS MEASURED ON EVERY RUN RATHER THAN ASSERTED:** `scripts/pilot-node/verify.mjs` (at `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs` until 2026-11-19, now a symlink to it) dumps the node's own chain and prints **3 records, 0 App entries** — genesis only; no constitution, no membrane, no join — and prints `VIOLATED` the moment anything calls a zome function on it. Neither zome has an `init` callback, so enabling the app writes no app entry by construction rather than by restraint. **THE REST, ALSO MEASURED:** the cell's DNA is `uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o`, the released hash, obtained with `network_seed: 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'`; the bootstrap server for that space went from **one agent to two**; each node lists the other as a peer; the node's `storageArc` settled at **`[0, 4294967295]` — a full arc, holding the whole space**, about a minute after joining; and `systemctl --user restart` brings it back unattended on the same key and the same cell. **THE TRAP THIS LEAVES, and it is the silent kind:** the seed carries the DESKTOP'S MINOR VERSION, so a `0.4.0` release moves the live network to `Epistemic Resonance-0.4.x` while this node goes on serving `0.3.x` in perfect health — an always-on node on an abandoned DHT, indistinguishable from a working one by every check above except the first. `verify.mjs` carries that warning beside the constant; **nothing enforces it**, and a gate for it would be a real row. **AND THE DISCLOSURE WAS DELIVERED THE SAME DAY, WHICH IS WHAT CLOSES THIS ROW.** A full-arc node of ours now stores every claim anyone publishes, continuously, and **no participant has been told**. `docs/the-invitation.md` discloses permanence and says nothing about storage — 7.16's own complaint about that file, now with a machine behind it. Saying so cost nothing: the file now opens with FIVE obliged-to-tell items rather than four, the new one placed after the permanence pair because it answers what permanence raises — *on whose machine?* — and naming the only remedy that is real, which is that the node can be turned OFF and cannot be made to selectively forget, since wiping it re-gossips everything back. **An offer to delete your copy would be false comfort and is not made.** **AND BOTH CURRENT PARTICIPANTS WERE TOLD THE SAME EVENING, IN THE DOCUMENT'S OWN WORDS** — they joined before the paragraph existed, so a document nobody re-reads would not have been disclosure to them. Luck Light's carried the question that would DISSOLVE this rather than bound it — would he run a second always-on node, since one more operator means no single archive. Kupukupu's was retroactive, because they had already published, and said so in those terms. **THE FULL-ARC CLAIM WAS CHECKED FOR THE FIRST TIME ON 2026-11-16, AND IT HOLDS — BUT NOT FOR THE REASON THIS ROW IMPLIED, AND IT WAS FOUND READING `null`.** The row says a full-arc node *"now stores every claim anyone publishes, continuously"*, and the disclosure sent to both participants rests on that. **`verify.mjs` read `storageArc: null` after 74 minutes of uptime** — confirmed independently on the bootstrap server, not just locally — which is **not** the `[0, 4294967295]` recorded on 2026-11-09. **MEASURED WITH A SECOND NODE, which is the only way to settle it**: a temporary conductor at `~/.epi-arc-probe`, ports 9599/9598, the repo's own `.happ` installed with `network_seed: 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'` — **which independently re-confirmed the seed string, since the probe resolved to the released DNA `uhC0koz…` exactly.** **THE THREE READINGS, AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** (1) **A node that has restarted and has never yet seen a peer declares `null`, and stays there indefinitely** — 74 minutes observed. (2) **The moment a peer appears the arc goes FULL: `peers=1 arc=null` at t+0s, `peers=2 arc=[0,4294967295]` at t+5s**, sampled every five seconds from a deliberately restarted node. (3) **When the peer leaves the arc STAYS full** — watched through the peer's bootstrap entry expiring and the count dropping back to 1. **SO `null` IS NOT "CURRENTLY ALONE", IT IS "FRESHLY RESTARTED AND NOBODY HAS ARRIVED YET"**, and that is a window in which **nothing can be published anyway**, because publishing requires being on the network, which is the very event that closes it in under five seconds. **THE ROW'S SUBSTANCE IS THEREFORE SOUND and the disclosure does not need retracting** — but *"continuously"* is doing work the mechanism does not: the arc is established on contact, not held in advance. **WHAT IS STILL NOT MEASURED, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT:** that the arc goes full within five seconds of a peer arriving is not the same as proving a claim published in those five seconds is captured. Settling that needs a real publish from the second node, and **the probe deliberately made no zome call** — its footprint is one agent key with genesis records and **zero app entries**, invisible in the app, its bootstrap entry expiring in twenty minutes. **Five seconds is also the SAMPLING RESOLUTION, not a measured latency**; the true transition may be faster. **AND IT COST NOTHING TO THE NODE:** `publishes nothing: HOLDS` before, during and after, and the probe root was removed. **A SECOND STALE FACT FELL OUT OF THE SAME RUN: the chain is 13 records, not the 3 this row and [[pilot-node-always-on]] both say** — 4 genesis plus **9 CapGrants, one per authentication**, so it GROWS. *"Genesis only"* is false. **`0 App entries` still holds, which is the claim that matters**, and that phrase had already been copied into a drafted message to a participant before anybody measured it. **AND THE NARROWER QUESTION WAS THEN MEASURED TOO, 2026-11-16, ON A THROWAWAY NETWORK — AND IT FOUND A REAL EXPOSURE THIS ROW DID NOT KNOW ABOUT.** The question left open above was whether a claim published while the archive's arc is `null` is captured. **It is — and that turns out to be the wrong thing to worry about.** **DONE ON A FRESH SEED, NOT THE PILOT DHT, AND THAT WAS THE POINT:** a Claim is PERMANENT, so a test claim on the live network would be junk no participant could ever remove — the mechanism is network-level, so two throwaway nodes on a disposable seed answer it with no permanent footprint. Node A mirrored the pilot's config (`target_arc_factor: 1`); node B joined, published, and left after a controlled dwell; **the verdict was always taken with B's process DEAD**, so a read proves A STORED it rather than fetched it live — and the CONTROL establishes that distinction is real: with B dead and expired from the bootstrap, A still served the claim. **THE THREE ARMS.** (1) **Publisher stayed ~57s → CAPTURED**, and the captured set INCLUDES a claim committed when both nodes read `peers=1, arc=null` and neither had seen the other. **So a null arc at the moment of publication does NOT lose the claim.** (2) **Publisher left 64 MILLISECONDS after committing → NOT captured**, 0 claims; A's peer count never moved and its arc never came up — there was never any contact. (3) **Publisher stayed 10 SECONDS → STILL NOT CAPTURED**, 0 claims, and again A never made contact. **SO WHAT LOSES A CLAIM IS NOT THE ARC, IT IS THE PUBLISHER'S DWELL TIME** — the archive must DISCOVER the publisher and gossip, and discovery is the slow, variable step: it took ~2.5s in one run and had not happened after 10s in another. **THE THRESHOLD IS THEREFORE UNBOUNDED ON THIS EVIDENCE — somewhere above 10 seconds and at or below 57 — and the variability is the finding rather than the number.** **THIS IS 7.29's ORIGINAL PROBLEM, NOT FULLY SOLVED.** The row was opened because *"somebody who publishes and closes the app is gone from the network before anyone can read them"*, and the always-on node was the answer. **It is only the answer for a participant who STAYS ONLINE long enough to be found.** A participant who publishes and closes within ten seconds is still lost, archive or no archive — measured, not supposed. **WHAT FOLLOWS, AND IT IS NOT A CODE CHANGE:** participants should be told to leave the app open after publishing, which is a sentence in `docs/the-invitation.md` and an `[X]`. **AND NOT "FOR A MINUTE", WHICH IS WHAT THIS LINE SAID UNTIL 2026-11-18 — in the row carrying the measurement that refutes it.** The arms were 64ms and 10s lost, ~57s captured, and this row's own conclusion two sentences earlier is that **the threshold is unbounded on this evidence and the variability is the finding rather than the number.** A minute stated as the fix would have replaced one over-stated guarantee with a smaller one, inside the correction for the first; `docs/the-invitation.md` carried it in TWO places and both now give both figures and say there is no safe number; and **a second always-on operator does not fix this either**, since the missing step is contact with the PUBLISHER, not redundancy among archives. **A CAVEAT KEPT DELIBERATELY:** the ~57s arm's publisher had been on that network earlier in the session, so A may have rediscovered it faster through prior acquaintance — which would make the true threshold for a FIRST-TIME publisher worse, not better. **AND LUCK LIGHT ANSWERED THE DISSOLVING QUESTION ON 2026-11-10 — YES — WHICH THIS ROW DID NOT KNOW UNTIL 2026-11-16.** His words: *"Yes we can figure out how to do an always on network support set up. My dev friend has alot of compute resources in his garage. I could also dedicate a computer to archive the network."* **So the single-archive problem has a willing second operator and has had one for six days.** That is exactly the staleness this file exists to catch — **the blocker on the dissolving half was "awaiting an answer", the answer arrived, and nothing re-read the line.** **WHAT IS AND IS NOT TRUE NOW.** Nothing is built: there is no second node, no seed string sent, no instructions. **The `[D]` the second operator raises is NOT the one the first did** — a node run by a different person is not a second key belonging to the same person, so the sybil-disclosure reasoning above does not transfer and the honest framing is simpler: one more operator means the archive is not a single party's. **AND THE SEED TRAP IS NOW SOMEBODY ELSE'S TOO.** The network seed carries the DESKTOP'S MINOR VERSION, so any instructions handed to a second operator must carry `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x` and the warning that a `0.4.0` release strands them on an abandoned DHT in perfect health — the gate this row already says would be a real row is now worth more, because the person it would protect is not the person who knows about it. **THE OPEN LOOP IS OURS, NOT HIS.** He made an offer and it has not been answered; [[pilot-state-2026-11-08]]'s do-not-chase rule governs waiting on somebody who owes US something and does not cover leaving an offer unanswered. | ~~**D**~~ **BUILT 2026-11-09 — the machine. The disclosure is NOT** | ~~the disclosure~~ ~~nothing — SENT 2026-11-09 to both participants~~ **a reply to Luck Light's YES of 2026-11-10, which is `[H]`/`[X]` and has been outstanding six days** |
| 7.30 | **The network seed carries the desktop's MINOR version, so a `0.4.0` release strands every always-on node on an abandoned DHT — in perfect health, reporting success.** *Opened 2026-11-17, from §0's note that this "is not yet a row and should be", and sharpened by measuring the instrument that was supposed to cover it.* **THE MECHANISM, read off the desktop source rather than recalled.** `src/main/cli.ts:103` builds the seed as `` `${KANGAROO_CONFIG.productName}-${breakingAppVersion()}` ``, and `src/main/filesystem.ts:190` maps every `0.<minor>.<patch>` to the literal `0.<minor>.x`. So today every installed copy joins `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x`, and **a `0.4.0` release joins `Epistemic Resonance-0.4.x` — a different seed, a different DNA hash, a different network.** That is deliberate and correct for the app: a minor is a RELAUNCH, not an upgrade, which §4's sequencing already says. **What nobody wrote down is what it does to a node that does not update**, and the pilot has one of those by design — 7.29's always-on node exists precisely to be there when nobody else is. **IT FAILS SILENTLY, WHICH IS THE ORDINARY CASE. The node keeps its old seed, keeps a full arc, keeps answering, and serves a network with nobody on it.** Nothing it can observe locally distinguishes that from working: an empty DHT and a healthy DHT with no current readers look identical from inside. **AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO CATCH THIS CONFIRMS THE FAILURE INSTEAD — MEASURED 2026-11-17.** `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs` hardcodes BOTH halves of its own check: `SEED = 'Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x'` (line 26) and `RELEASED_DNA = 'uhC0kozxlYwR0REOn2iS4NswBOPzJ7I6kKsRV6o3WOEuoQOuEi-4o'` (line 27), hand-copied from the same reading, and prints `on released DHT : YES / NO — WRONG NETWORK`. **Both sides of that comparison are FROZEN AT INSTALL TIME:** `dna` is read off the already-installed cell (line 47) and `RELEASED_DNA` is a source constant, so **once it has printed YES it cannot print anything else, whatever the desktop releases.** It is a one-time install-time assertion reprinted every run in the present tense — [[an-exit-code-is-not-a-measurement]] with a label on it, and the vacuous-greens lesson in a script written to avoid exactly that. **Run today it prints `YES`, and today it is RIGHT**, which is the only reason this is invisible. **THE PERSON IT HURTS IS THE ONE WHO CANNOT SEE IT.** 7.29's second-operator offer was accepted on 2026-11-10; a second node means a copy of this file on somebody else's machine, with two constants nobody here can update, printing YES while serving nobody. **A silent failure costs the operator their donated hardware and costs the pilot the thing the node was stood up to provide, and neither party learns.** **WHAT WOULD CLOSE IT: derive the expectation from the live release instead of pasting it.** Read the desktop's current published tag (`gh api repos/StateofIntent/epistemic-resonance-desktop/releases/latest` → `v0.3.6`), apply `breakingVersion` to it, build the seed string, and compare against the seed the node is actually installed under — which `listApps` does not return, so the installed seed must be recorded at install time or re-derived by reinstalling into a throwaway conductor. **Then a bump changes the computed expectation the day the release lands, and the node says NO instead of agreeing with itself for ever.** **THREE LIMITS, STATED SO THIS IS NOT READ AS A REPAIR.** (1) **It detects; it cannot migrate.** A new seed is a new cell with a new agent key and an empty chain, so the response is a reinstall and the node's tenure starts over — which is a cost worth knowing about BEFORE a bump, not after. (2) **It needs the network to read the release, so it must FAIL LOUD when it cannot** — falling back to the pasted constant recreates the defect with more code. (3) **The file lives at `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs`, outside this repository and outside CI**, so nothing versions it, nothing gates it, and a second operator cannot be sent a copy that stays current. **Whether the node's verifier belongs IN the repo is the open question here** — the keys and passphrase beside it plainly do not, and that is probably why it was never asked. **BUILT 2026-11-18, AND THE FIRST THING THAT CHANGED IS THAT THE CHECK CAN NOW FAIL.** `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs` no longer compares two pastes. One side is DERIVED from the live release — an unauthenticated `fetch` of `api.github.com/.../releases/latest`, its `tag_name` through a port of the desktop's own `breakingVersion`, prefixed with the product name — and the other is READ OFF THE RUNNING CONDUCTOR, which is the find that made this cheap: **`admin.getDnaDefinition(cellId)` returns `modifiers.network_seed`, so the seed the node is actually installed under can be read back.** The row expected to have to record it at install time or re-derive it in a throwaway conductor; neither was needed. **The expected side moves the day a release lands and the installed side cannot move without a reinstall, and that asymmetry is the whole check.** **MEASURED 2026-11-18: `v0.3.6` → `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x`, installed `Epistemic Resonance-0.3.x`, YES — the same word the old line printed, now for a reason.** **AND IT IS WATCHED FAILING, in a harness kept beside it (`verify-injections.mjs`) that stubs ONLY the release read and imports `verify.mjs` unchanged: 9 modes, 9 as expected.** `v0.4.0`, `v1.0.0` and `v0.4.0-alpha.2` each go **NO — WRONG NETWORK** and exit 2; an unreadable tag, a 200 with no `tag_name`, a 404 and a dead network each go **CANNOT VERIFY** and exit 1; and **two controls stay YES** — the live tag, which proves the harness is not what makes it red, and **`v0.3.9`, because a PATCH must keep the seed.** That second control is the one worth keeping: a check that reddened on every version change would be wrong in the other direction. **THE PORT WAS DIFFERENTIALLY TESTED RATHER THAN EYEBALLED** — the desktop's real `breakingVersion` transcribed beside it and both run over 27 strings. **Two divergences, both semver being the more tolerant one:** a leading `v` (closed, since a GitHub tag always has one) and leading whitespace (**left divergent on purpose** — where they differ the port is the stricter one, and strict means CANNOT VERIFY rather than a guessed seed, which is limit (2) again). **ONE THING THAT LOOKS DERIVABLE AND IS NOT: the product name.** It stays a constant, because electron-builder sanitises the space out of every release asset (`Epistemic-Resonance-0.3.6-mac.zip`, `Epistemic.Resonance-…-mac.zip.blockmap`) while the seed uses `Epistemic Resonance` **with the space** — lifting it off a filename would produce a confidently wrong seed, which is this row's own defect rebuilt. **AND THE CHECK IS STILL VACUOUS ON EXACTLY ONE RUN — the one that INSTALLS**, since the install now uses the derived seed, so the first comparison is a tautology; it is a real measurement on every run after, which is every run this node will ever do. That is said in the file rather than left for a reader to notice. **A RESIDUAL LIMIT, STATED BECAUSE NOTHING COVERS IT: the DNA hash is a hash of the integrity zomes AND the modifiers, so a PATCH release that changed an integrity zome would keep the seed, split the network, and this check would say YES.** `breakingVersion` IS the project's assertion that patches do not do that, nothing here verifies it, and **nothing can — the release publishes installers, not the `.happ`, so there is no released DNA hash to read.** The old `RELEASED_DNA` constant did not verify it either; it was a paste, which is why it is GONE rather than kept, and the hash is still printed as information. **THE SUB-`[D]` IS UNCHANGED AND NOW HAS A SECOND LIMB: the harness that proves the check can fail is outside the repository too.** So what cannot be sent to a second operator is no longer one file but the check and its evidence together — which is most of the argument for moving them in. **THE SUB-`[D]` WAS TAKEN 2026-11-19: BOTH FILES ARE NOW IN THE REPOSITORY**, at [`scripts/pilot-node/`](../scripts/pilot-node/), with `~/.epi-pilot-node/verify.mjs` left as a **symlink** so the operator's habit still works and there is still exactly one copy. **NOTHING SECRET MOVED** — the keystore, the passphrase and `conductor-config.yaml` stay outside, and that asymmetry is named in the new README rather than left implicit, because it is probably why the move was never asked about: the directory looks like one thing and is two. **EVERY HOME-DIRECTORY PATH AND PORT IS NOW DERIVED OR OVERRIDABLE** (`EPI_PILOT_ADMIN`, `EPI_PILOT_APP_ID`, `EPI_PILOT_HAPP`, the bundle resolved relative to the file itself), since the entire point of moving it is that it runs on a machine that is not this one. **AND THE MOVE IMMEDIATELY PAID FOR ITSELF, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: the harness had ALREADY ROTTED, with zero second operators, in the one day it was unversioned.** Three defects, none of which any diff could have shown anybody: (1) **`control` was the literal string `v0.3.6`, commented as *the live tag*, and the live tag became `v0.3.7` on 2026-11-19.** It went on PASSING, because both land in the `0.3.x` band — **so it passed BY LUCK while its stated justification was false**, which is 1.35's stale-citation shape inside the harness whose job is to prove greens are not vacuous. `control` **now does not stub at all**, so it IS the live release by construction and cannot drift again. (2) **EVERY OTHER TAG WAS SILENTLY RELATIVE TO A `0.3.x` SEED THAT NOTHING STATED.** `patch: v0.3.9` means *a patch keeps the seed* only while the node is installed under `0.3.x`; reinstall it under `0.4.x` — **precisely the event this row exists for** — and the harness would report **FAIL for a check that was working perfectly.** A harness that reddens by luck is the same defect as one that greens by luck and **worse in a second operator's hands, where nobody can tell it from a real break.** The band is now **read off the running node, out of `control`'s own output**, and every tag is derived from it. (3) **The closing line said the check "can fail, in six ways" and seven of the nine modes are failures.** The count is **computed** now — a hand-stated number nothing gated, one directory over from `check-harness-counts.mjs`, which exists for exactly that. **AND THE DERIVATION NEEDED ITS OWN GATE, BECAUSE TWO OF ITS THREE BRANCHES CANNOT RUN HERE.** The node is on `0.3.x`, so the `<N>.x.x` and `0.0.<patch>` branches would execute for the first time **during a band change, on somebody else's machine, in a hurry** — untested code on the path that only runs in the emergency. `band-plan-check.mjs` **round-trips every derived tag through the REAL `breakingVersion`** rather than restating the arithmetic (which would agree with a wrong table as happily as a right one), across eight bands, and **asserts the three moving tags land in three DISTINCT bands** — a live hazard, not a hypothetical, since past `1.0.0` a minor bump does NOT change the seed and the naive `+1` would have made two modes test one thing. **IT FOUND A REAL DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN: `0.x.x` was accepted** by a `\d+` that should have been `[1-9]\d*`. `breakingVersion` can never produce `0.x.x`, and the plan built for it had a seed-*keeping* tag of `v0.99.99` that round-trips to `0.99.x` — **a mode asserting YES while feeding the verifier a tag that moves the seed.** To make it gateable at all, `breakingVersion` was lifted into `breaking-version.mjs` as **the one copy**, since `verify.mjs` is a script with top-level await and a `process.exit` and cannot be imported for a function — transcribing it into the check instead would have put a second copy of the most correctness-critical function here in a file nothing compares to the first, which is **6.23's wordlist lesson.** **IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT IN CI, AND THAT IS NOW A DECISION RATHER THAN A GAP.** The two things that make the check real are the two CI lacks — a running conductor with an installed cell, and a live GitHub read — and it would fail *dishonestly*: `http404` and `down` exist to prove the check fails loud when it cannot reach GitHub, and **in CI an ordinary rate limit is indistinguishable from the real `CANNOT VERIFY`.** A required check that reddens for reasons unrelated to what it measures gets muted, and **a muted check is the vacuous green this directory exists to refuse.** `band-plan-check.mjs` is the part that IS CI-shaped — pure, no conductor, no network — and is the honest candidate if any of this is ever gated. **MEASURED AFTER THE MOVE: `verify.mjs` YES against the live `v0.3.7` from both the repo path and the symlink; 9 injection modes, 9 as expected, band derived as `0.3.x`, 7 ways to fail; `band-plan-check.mjs` green across 8 bands; and all thirteen repo checkers plus the injection ledger green.** | ~~**M** for the derived check~~ **BUILT 2026-11-18**; ~~**X** for a second operator's copy~~ **the sub-`[D]` was TAKEN 2026-11-19 and both files are in the repo** |
