| 7.31 | **This project asks participants to run software it does not keep running itself, and there is now more than one machine.** *Opened 2026-11-18, out of a design conversation about CFEngine-style convergence.* **WHAT IS ACTUALLY RUNNING: the always-on pilot node (`~/.epi-pilot-node`, a systemd user unit) and whatever laptop is open.** 7.30 built a check that the node is on the released DHT, and ~~its sub-`[D]` is that the check and its injection harness live OUTSIDE the repo and outside CI~~ **that sub-`[D]` was TAKEN 2026-11-19: both are now in `scripts/pilot-node/`, so the script is no longer one nobody else has.** What remains true is the rest of it — the node is still **maintained by hand**, the check is still **deliberately not in CI** (it needs a conductor and a live release read, and a CI rate limit is indistinguishable from the `CANNOT VERIFY` it exists to raise), and the node would still be **stranded in perfect health by a `0.4.0` release** until somebody runs it. **THE PROPOSAL: CFEngine, which is GPL v3 Community and genuinely a fit** — its policies are written as promises, from the same Promise Theory this protocol is built on. It would converge the node back to a described state: conductor running, right app version, bootstrap and relay URLs correct, disk not full. **THE LICENCE CONSTRAINT IS NOT OPTIONAL: this repository is dual MIT/Apache, so CFEngine runs as a SEPARATE PROCESS and is never linked in**, or the combined work goes GPL. **WHY IT IS NOT YET WORTH IT, which is the honest blocker rather than a scheduling excuse:** convergence pays off across many machines and there is currently ONE, whose failure mode is visible in a single `verify.mjs` run. **It becomes worth it at two triggers, either of which is a real event:** 7.14's decision to run our own bootstrap and relay servers (which would be infrastructure participants depend on, not just us), or Luck Light's second always-on node (offered 2026-11-10, §4 unanswered), **which is the one that makes hand-maintenance somebody else's problem rather than ours** — a second operator cannot be handed a procedure that lives in one person's home directory. **AND THE FIXED CORE IS THE PART TO GET RIGHT FIRST, not last.** Anything that repairs itself must never be able to edit the rules that stop it: the consenting-host list, the stop mechanism, and the rule that a proposal is never self-applied have to sit where the converging system cannot write them. shipped coordinator-only and nothing forked. **WRITTEN DOWN 2026-11-18 — [`docs/fixed-core.md`](fixed-core.md)**, before the system it binds exists, which is the only reason it was cheap. **It is a DOCUMENT and not a mechanism, and it says so at the top**: eight rules, of which two are enforced, three structural and three promises on paper — and the three that are paper (consent, the stop mechanism, the enumerated reflex set) are **the three this row's layer touches first.** **2026-11-21 ADDS — THE AUTHOR OF THE PROPOSAL PUBLISHED THE AGENT-ERA VERSION, AND IT CARRIES A DEFECT THIS the less we need to look"* — sampling rate decaying as trust accrues.** That is not the repair rule, it is the *detection* rule, **and it is the sharper form of this row's own objection**: a node stranded by a `0.4.0` release keeps every promise it can see itself making, so clean history earns it a longer interval and the interval grows fastest exactly when it has been wrong longest. **A blind spot that widens on success.** Written into `fixed-core.md` rule 5 2026-11-21 as a third limit — **the check interval is a constant in the core, never earned** — alongside the three measured instances | 7.32 | **The substrate leg of the autopoiesis stack was priced 2026-11-19, and the half it needs does not exist — but the reason to look at Holo anyway is RECRUITMENT, not autopoiesis.** *Opened 2026-11-19, pricing the "pays its own way" layer rows 7.31 and 5.5 assume.* **EVERY FIGURE BELOW IS PERISHABLE AND TWO HAVE A DECAY DATE, which is the reason this is a row rather than a sentence in a design doc.** **HOLOFUEL WENT TO MAINNET 2026-11-01 — eighteen days before this row** — and HOT swaps 1:1 with a window open at least six months from that date. **BUT: hosts are NOT EARNING yet.** row that assumed it would have been assuming a feature eighteen days old and half-shipped. **AND THE OPERATING BASIS IS TIME-BOXED, WHICH IS THE PART TO RE-READ RATHER THAN REMEMBER: the HoloFuel Network is operated by Net Zero FZC under a PROVISIONAL 90-DAY LICENCE from 2026-11-01, so it lapses or is renewed around 2027-01-30.** **DECAY DATE: anything this row says about HoloFuel is stale after 2027-01-30 and must be re-read, not quoted.** **And the structural point outlives the date:** for a project whose premise is avoiding a centre, making the substrate depend on **one operator under a time-boxed licence** is a structural choice and not a plumbing one — it is the same shape as the bootstrap-and-relay dependency 7.14 already records, with money attached. **THE KEY-CUSTODY QUESTION WAS THE ONE I EXPECTED TO KILL IT, AND IT DOES NOT.** Holo's browser model generates the keypair in a protected iframe (Chaperone), and when the host is about to commit on the user's behalf it **notifies the browser, the user signs, and the signature comes back** — **the host never holds the private key.** So *a key answers for what it publishes* survives Holo hosting, which is the opposite of what a custodial model would have meant. **CAVEAT ON THAT, STATED BECAUSE IT IS THE WEAKEST EVIDENCE HERE: the material describing it is older, and the repository it names is now | 7.33 | **A release tag is not reachable from `main`, and the reason this file gave for that was wrong.** *Opened 2026-11-20 to lift the trap out of §0's header, which is where its only copy lived and where it was nearly deleted in that morning's rewrite — a trap that lives in the resume pointer has the lifetime of the next rewrite.* **WHAT IS TRUE, AND IT IS THE RULE: the tag goes on desktop `release`, NEVER on `main`, because the artifacts are built from `release`.** Measured 2026-11-20 across the series rather than restated: **every release from `v0.3.4` to `v0.3.7` carries `targetCommitish: release`**, `v0.3.7` is `a7abb65`, and **`git merge-base --is-ancestor v0.3.7 origin/main` is FALSE** — the tagged commit is not on `main` at all. **WHAT WAS WRONG, AND IT IS THE SAME DAY'S LESSON A THIRD TIME:** the header said a `main`-targeted tag *"would name a commit that was NOT the build input"*, which is true of the **commit** and **false of the content.** `release` is `main` plus one merge commit per release and nothing else — **`git log origin/release..origin/main` is EMPTY** — so **the tree at `v0.3.7` and the tree at `origin/main` are the same object, `f678f5a3`.** A tag on `main` would have named a commit whose content is byte-identical to what shipped. **The rule is right and the reason given for it was not**, which is #531's shape exactly: a correct instruction resting on a justification nobody had measured. **THE REAL COST, MEASURED: SIX RELEASES ARE INVISIBLE FROM `main`.** Tags reachable from `main` are `v0.3.1 v0.3.0 v0.2.0 v0.1.1 v0.1.0`; from `release`, all eleven. **`git describe --tags origin/main` returns `v0.3.1-21-gf744e50`** — so anything deriving a version from `git describe` in a `main` checkout reports **0.3.1**, twenty-one commits and six releases stale, and reports it confidently rather than failing. **Nothing does that today, which is exactly why this is a trap and not a defect** — and the standing guard is that the version here is read from `package.json` by `const.ts`, *out of the same file `electron-updater` reads*, never from git. | ~~**M** to give it a row~~ **DONE 2026-11-20**; **H** at each release, which is the only moment the rule applies | nothing — a standing fact, not a task |

the hedge was abstract, and **specificity won.** Row 7.29 and `the-invitation.md` were both edited on 11-18 to avoid stating a minute **and the reader supplied the minute anyway** — so the defect was never in the wording of the number, it was in **leading with a number at all** when the finding is variance. **WHAT WOULD HAVE WORKED IS THE SHAPE, NOT A BETTER CAVEAT**: *the time varies between 2.5 seconds and more than 10, we have seen it fail at 10 and succeed at 57, and nothing makes it certain* — a range and two outcomes, with no single figure available to be lifted out. **A caveat is not a defence against a number.** **AND HE IS NOW REPEATING IT WHERE HOLOCHAIN PEOPLE READ** (the same thread Paul d'Aoust answered), **with our name on it** — which is what makes this a correction owed rather than an observation. **He has also stated the standard himself**, in the same week: *"We learn more from having our vulnerabilities exposed than our strengths attacked."* **§10 WAS DRAFTED AND SENT ON 2026-11-20.** **AND WHAT IT DOES IS NOW THE OPEN QUESTION, WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON THIS ROW STAYS OPEN:** this row exists because the FIRST correction did not land, so **the second one is a second data point on the same question** and it is cheap to read. **If his next mention carries a range, or no figure at all, the fix worked** — state the span and the two outcomes, leave no number standing alone. **If it carries another single number, then the defect is not in how a caveat is worded and a message cannot fix it**, and the honest conclusion would be that **a figure given to a reader becomes that reader's figure regardless of what surrounds it** — which would make the repo-side rule (never state a settlement threshold anywhere) the whole of the remedy rather than half of it. **Do not prompt him for it** — a prompted restatement measures nothing, which is 6.12's trap exactly. | ~~**X** to send §10~~ **SENT 2026-11-20**; | 7.35 | **The variable discovery time, upstream — and a Holochain-side answer says it is UNEXPECTED.** *Opened 2026-11-20 when Paul d'Aoust replied to the shared #500 result, unprompted, in the Holochain group.* **His words: *"The thing you're describing, with variable publish times, is unexpected and probably merits further attention. Who knows, maybe the core devs are already aware of it."*** **HIS MECHANISM ACCOUNT, WHICH IS NEW INFORMATION AND DOES NOT EXPLAIN OUR ARMS:** Holochain does *"fast push, slow heal"* — a source-chain write **publishes to online DHT peers immediately** with confirmation in a few seconds, then **online peers gossip at five-minute intervals** even after the author leaves, *"which is where some variability is introduced."* **OURS IS NOT THAT VARIABILITY.** #500's 64ms and 10s arms show the always-on node **never made contact at all** — peer count never moved, arc never came up. **"Immediate publish to online peers" presupposes the publisher has FOUND a peer to push to, and the measured slow, variable step is the FINDING, not the gossip.** So the gossip interval is a second-order effect here and the thing worth reporting is **peer discovery latency for a fresh publisher on a tiny network** — which is exactly what Luck Light guessed and half-got (*"probably in the design of the gossiping feature"* — discovery, not gossip). **AND ONE FORWARD-LOOKING WARNING FROM THE SAME MESSAGE THAT APPLIES TO THIS HAPP SPECIFICALLY:** confirmation time depends on *"how complex it is to validate — if it depends on a big graph of prior writes, it can get expensive."* **Every critique here validates against the claim it answers, and settlement reads a graph**, so this is the one Holochain cost that scales with exactly the structure this protocol is made of. **Nothing is measured on it; it is a named hazard, not a result.** **THE CAVEAT THAT MUST GO IN ANY REPORT** is already in 7.29: the ~57s arm's publisher had been on that network earlier in the session, so prior acquaintance may have sped discovery — **which would make a first-time publisher's case worse, not better.** **§11 WAS SENT 2026-11-21 AND HE ANSWERED THE SAME NIGHT, AND THE MEASUREMENT HELD WHILE THE FRAMING WAS REFINED BY ITS OWN SUBJECT.** *"Ahhhh that illuminates things."* **HIS CORRECTION TO HIS OWN ACCOUNT: the five-minute gossip interval delays the READERS, not the author** — *"the author gets nearly immediate feedback, that's the fast push, whereas peers trying to read the data is subject to the five-minute intervals, because when I author and publish a record, it only goes to a selection of my peers."* **SO THERE ARE THREE STAGES AND NOT TWO, AND NOBODY HAD SEPARATED THEM:** (1) **bootup, handshake and peer-table exchange** — what #500 actually measured; (2) **publish, to a SELECTION of the author's peers**, with near-immediate confirmation; (3) **gossip heal at five-minute intervals, for every other reader.** **7.35's claim that the gossip interval is second-order SURVIVES — it was true of our arms and he conceded it — but the row had framed his account as not explaining the measurement when it was describing a DIFFERENT STAGE.** Both were right about different things. **AND HE SUPPLIED THE MECHANISM FOR THE STAGE THIS ROW COULD ONLY NAME:** B may hold A's address from the bootstrap server and still not treat it as a peer — *"I'm honestly not sure if B puts A into its peer table immediately after getting A's record from the bootstrap server, or if it just says there might be a peer named A out there but I won't treat them as a peer until I chat with someone and get gossiped A's agent info"* — **which is exactly the shape of a peer count that never moved.** He adds that slow P2P bootup is *"just kinda slow … I suspect it's just an iron rule of physics and information theory"*, remembered from LimeWire and BitTorrent. **THAT PARTLY ANSWERS §11's OWN QUESTION — is this the expected cost of a tiny network — in the direction of YES**, and it sits oddly against his 11-20 *"unexpected and probably merits further attention"*; **the refinement came after the method travelled with the result, which is the argument for §11's design, and the tension is NOT to be pointed out to him.** **HE OFFERED THREE COUNTER-HYPOTHESES FOR THE FAILING ARMS — that B died before it could publish at all, through an expensive zome call, expensive self-validation, or a janky link — AND OUR DATA RULES OUT EXACTLY ONE, WHICH IS WHAT §13 CLAIMS AND NO MORE:** **the commit RETURNED and the dwell was timed from after it, so B's self-validation had completed**, and the entry was a bare one with nothing prior to resolve. **That says nothing about validation cost when there IS a graph to walk, which is his 11-20 warning, remains unmeasured, and is still the larger hazard for this happ.** | ~~**DRAFTED AS §11 AND CLEAR TO SEND**~~ **§11 SENT 2026-11-21. §13 IS THE REPLY — DRAFTED, UNSENT, AND OWED, because he asked the human a direct question by name.** X | **the human's account and the human's call; the model does not file upstream** |

| 7.37 | **AN ADJACENT HOLOCHAIN DESKTOP APP SELF-UPDATES ON macOS FROM AN AD-HOC SIGNED BUNDLE — THE EXACT CONFIGURATION ROW 6.21 MEASURED AS UNABLE TO UPDATE — AND IT DOES IT BY NOT USING SQUIRREL.MAC.** *Opened 2026-11-21 from Eric Doriean's public announcement of Flowsta Vault 1.8.0, which open-sourced four repositories and said out loud: "Devs can read or send their AIs to read the new open source repos linked above now."* **READ OUT OF THE PUBLISHED REPOSITORIES RATHER THAN THE ANNOUNCEMENT, which is the whole discipline of this row, because the announcement is a claim and the repositories are the artifact.** **WHAT IS MEASURED, TODAY.** (a) **`flowsta-update-check` DOES NOT INSTALL ANYTHING.** It is 472 lines of Rust that verify a signed release manifest — Ed25519 over `flowsta-release-manifest:v1:<app>:<channel>:<sequence>:<sha256>`, checked against signers compiled into the app, *"local math, no server"* — plus replay, downgrade, channel and rollout refusals. **Its own README puts it *beside* Tauri's updater and leaves `// install` as a comment.** So it answers *may this release be installed*, never *can this bundle be replaced*, and **our blocker is the second question**: nothing here is liftable into our Electron updater, and it is Rust besides. (b) **THE STARTER IS TAURI 2, NOT ELECTRON** — `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `@tauri-apps/cli ~2.12.4`. (c) **AND IT SHIPS AD-HOC: `bundle.macOS.signingIdentity` is `null`**, with `RELEASING.md:48` saying the workflow *"signs and notarizes when they are set, and builds an ad-hoc signed app when they are not"* — the six `APPLE_*` secrets **optional**, their absence costing only *"an unsigned `.dmg` that Gatekeeper warns about"* at first open (right-click, Open). **So a Holochain desktop app with no Apple Developer ID is claimed to keep itself updated on macOS, and 6.21 measured on the shipped artifact that ours never can.** **THE DIFFERENCE IS MEASURED AND IT IS NOT THE SIGNING.** 6.21's finding was never that the bundle is unsigned *per se* — it was that **Squirrel.Mac takes the running app's designated requirement and demands the successor satisfy it, and an ad-hoc signature's designated requirement is a bare `cdhash` no other build can ever match.** Tauri's updater does not ask Squirrel.Mac anything; it replaces the bundle itself. **His stack does not contain the component that forbids ours.** **WHAT IS NOT MEASURED, AND MUST NOT BE CLAIMED — THIS IS 6.10's DEFECT POINTED THE OTHER WAY.** Nobody here has watched a Tauri ad-hoc app take an update on macOS, **and there is still no Mac here** — the same constraint that capped 6.21. His `RELEASING.md` asserts it and a shipped Vault 1.8.0 is evidence, **but reading a README as a measurement is reading a claim as a result, which is exactly the move row 6.10 was corrected for.** What is measured is narrower and sufficient: **Squirrel.Mac is the binding constraint in OUR stack, and his stack has no Squirrel.Mac in it.** **WHAT THIS DOES TO 6.21's DECISION: IT DOES NOT FLIP IT, AND IT SHARPENS WHY.** The `[D]` taken 2026-11-18 was *accept hand-installs indefinitely*, against route (b) *build a macOS updater that does not use Squirrel.Mac*, priced then as *"a project rather than a patch"*. **That price is now confirmed from the outside rather than estimated: the working implementation exists, and it is a different application framework.** Adopting it is a migration off kangaroo-electron, not a patch, and no part of his four repositories shortens that. **BUT ONE SENTENCE THIS PROJECT HAS BEEN WRITING IS NOW TOO BROAD.** *"macOS auto-update is not coming"* is true **of this build** and **false of Holochain desktop apps in general**; the row, `INSTALL.md` and any message must say **ours** cannot, never that it is impossible — a negative claim wider than its evidence, which is the defect of the week. **AND THE SECOND FINDING IS A GAP IN THIS REPOSITORY, FOUND BY LOOKING WHERE HIS WORK POINTS.** His stated reason for all of it: *"To be a viable long running app, you need to plan for Holochain breaking change migrations at a minimum"*, and `flowsta-hc-lines` caches and runs two conductor versions side by side to carry a person's data from the old line to the new. **`docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` IS 245 LINES AND MENTIONS THE HOLOCHAIN VERSION ZERO TIMES** — measured by grep, not impression. It plans thoroughly for **our** breaking changes, and the standing decision there is that *a bump is a RELAUNCH, not an upgrade — different hash, different network, no migration, and Invariant 6 forbids even deleting the orphans.* **A HOLOCHAIN BREAKING CHANGE FORCES THAT SAME RELAUNCH ON SOMEBODY ELSE'S SCHEDULE**, and nothing in this repository says so. **This machine runs `holochain 0.7.0`; he moved Vault from 0.6.1 to 0.7.1 and is planning for 0.8.** **One design of his is worth copying conceptually whatever framework we are on: his manifest carries `holochain: { lines: [...], changes_line: bool }`, so a release DECLARES which Holochain lines it runs on and whether it moves people's data** — where our network seed encodes the desktop MINOR version implicitly in a string that `band-plan-check.mjs` has to round-trip to check. **AND THE ZERO-KNOWLEDGE POINT IS HIS, NOT A GENERIC ONE, AND IT FITS OUR MODEL:** private data cannot be migrated by the network because it is locked to whoever holds the keys, so some people sign back in late and some never return — a migration is therefore per-person and voluntary, which is what Invariant 6 would require anyway. | ~~**[M] FOR THE RECORD, WHICH IS DONE IN THIS ROW. THE HOLOCHAIN-MIGRATION GAP IS A `[D]`**~~ **THE `[D]` WAS TAKEN 2026-11-21, THE DAY IT WAS FILED: `integrity-bump-plan.md` GAINS THE SECTION, AND IT IS `§6`.** Writing it found that **the mechanism was already measured in three places nobody had joined up** — SPEC §11.1 (the hash covers the integrity zomes), **SPEC §5.1's measured three-row table in which a ~30-line COMMENT changed the DNA hash** with the revert as its control (`wasm_error!` expands `::core::line!()`, 23 uses), and `dna/integrity/Cargo.toml`'s own comment recording that **0.6 → 0.7 made `holochain_serialized_bytes` a required DIRECT dependency** because `#[hdk_entry_helper]` names it by absolute path. So *a Holochain breaking change forks the DNA hash by §1's own mechanism* is **measured, not reasoned**, and this project has already been through one. **THE ONE CHOICE IN THE SECTION, AND IT COSTS NOTHING: a release that moves the Holochain version ships as a desktop MINOR at minimum, never a patch** — the hash forks either way, so the seed is the only thing that makes the fork **legible**, and `breakingVersion` keeps the seed on a patch while moving it on a minor. **AND `verify.mjs:187-195` HAD ALREADY WRITTEN THE GAP DOWN WITH ONE CAUSE NAMED INSTEAD OF TWO** — *"a PATCH release that changed an integrity zome would keep the seed `0.3.x`, split the network anyway, and this check would say YES"* names **us** editing the zome; a Holochain bump is a second cause of the identical silent fork **and is the one nobody chooses**, which makes it worse rather than equal. **NOTHING VERIFIES THE RULE AND §6.3 SAYS SO:** the release publishes installers, not the `.happ`, so there is **no released DNA hash to read** — which also qualified §5's closing absolute, now corrected in place. **STILL NOT A BUILD — `flowsta-hc-lines` is Tauri-oriented Rust, and 7.31's rule applies: nothing here is worth building before it is needed.** | **nothing for the record.** The `[D]` is the human's. **Row 6.21 stays CLOSED BY DECISION** and this row does not reopen it; what it changes is the breadth of the sentence, not the verdict. **And §9 to Eric — the optional Granite correction — now has a non-chasing hook, since he invited developers to read these repositories; sending stays `[X]`, the human's.** |
| 7.38 | **THE MOST CAREFULLY BUILT MESSAGE THIS PROJECT HAS SENT FAILED ON ITS FORM, AND THE READER SAID SO IN THE SAME BREATH AS ANSWERING IT.** *Opened 2026-11-21 by Paul d'Aoust's reply to §11 — the second time in two days that a sent message has been measured by what the reader did with it rather than by re-reading the draft (7.34 was the first).* **HIS WORDS: *"I'm not quite following — forgive the observation but it sounds like this response is Claude-assisted and for some reason I feel incredibly dyslexic when I'm reading LLM output."*** **§11 WAS NOT A CARELESS MESSAGE; IT WAS THE OPPOSITE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE FINDING.** It was built to 7.34's remedy on purpose — **method before numbers** so the verdict could not be mistaken for a live fetch, **all three arms as a range with no single figure left standing alone**, **two caveats stated against ourselves first**, the hypothesis we got wrong admitted, **one question, no pitch, no link.** Every one of those disciplines is about *content*, and **every one of them survived contact.** What did not survive is that **he could not read it.** **SO THIS IS A DIFFERENT AXIS FROM 7.34, AND THE PAIR NOW BRACKETS THE PROBLEM:** 7.34 is *a figure given to a reader becomes that reader's figure whatever surrounds it*; **7.38 is *a correctly-disciplined message still fails if its prose is the kind a reader bounces off*.** Neither is fixable by being more careful about the argument, **and neither was detectable from inside the draft** — 7.34 needed the reader's number read back, this needed the reader to say *"I'm not quite following"* about a message whose author believed it was the clearest thing on the list. **THE MECHANISM, NAMED BY HIM AND NOT GUESSED AT: it reads as LLM output.** Long, evenly-weighted, heavily-subordinated sentences; every point pre-qualified; a uniform register with no cheap sentences in it. **That is the house style of this entire repository, and the repository is the right place for it** — a row has to carry its own caveats because nobody is coming to ask. **A MESSAGE TO A PERSON IS NOT A ROW, and until now this project has written them as though it were.** **THE REMEDY, ADOPTED, AND IT IS A STYLE RULE RATHER THAN A CONTENT RULE BECAUSE THE CONTENT WAS ALREADY RIGHT:** outreach is **short, plain, unbolded, chat register, and shorter than it wants to be** — the discipline goes into *what is claimed*, never into the sentence construction. Written into the drafts file's header so it governs every future draft rather than being remembered. **§13 is the first draft written to it.** **AND MEASURING THAT CLAIM CORRECTED IT, BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ROW COMMITTED THE DEFECT THE ROW IS ABOUT:** it said §13 was *"a third of §11's weight"* **and nobody had counted — it was 392 words against §11's 377, slightly LONGER.** Tightened to **334**, which is **11% shorter, not a third.** **AND THOSE FIGURES WERE ALL 2–6 HIGH, FOUND 2026-11-21 BY BUILDING THE COUNTER (7.39's `[M]`): THE RECORDED NUMBERS COUNTED STANDALONE PUNCTUATION AS WORDS** — the em-dashes this project uses as bullets. By the declared method (*tokens containing a letter or digit, code spans stripped — a bare em-dash is not a word*) §11 is **371**, §13 is **329**, §9 is **250**, §15 is **242**. **THE RATIO SURVIVES AND NO CONCLUSION MOVES: §13 is 11% shorter than §11 on either method**, which is the one thing those figures were cited for. The 392-word draft no longer exists so it cannot be re-measured, and the numbers above are left as the record of what was counted at the time rather than rewritten — **a measurement already taken is evidence, like a sent letter.** `scripts/check-outbound-claims.mjs` now declares the method in its output, because *count it, do not estimate it* still produced four wrong figures while the method went unstated. **AND THE CORRECTION RELOCATES THE REMEDY, WHICH MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER: §11 WAS ONLY 377 WORDS, WHICH IS NOT LONG BY ANY STANDARD, SO LENGTH WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM — REGISTER WAS.** *"I feel incredibly dyslexic reading LLM output"* is about texture, not volume. **So of the rule's five clauses the load is carried by plain sentences, chat register, no bold and some cheap sentences, while *shorter than it wants to be* is real but second-order** — and a draft that got plainer without getting much shorter is the honest demonstration of it rather than a failed one. **AND ONE THING THE REMEDY EXPLICITLY DOES NOT DO: it does not deny the assistance.** He has just demonstrated he can spot it; a draft claiming to be unassisted would be a small lie bought for nothing, and **this project's one commitment (7.1) is to be honest about what it is rather than to look good.** §13 opens by taking the hit in one line. **WHAT WOULD FALSIFY THE REMEDY, stated now so the next reply is evidence rather than relief:** if §13 draws another *"I'm not quite following"*, the problem is not sentence construction and the conclusion becomes that **a reader who has decided a correspondent is an LLM discounts the prose whatever its shape** — in which case the only remaining lever is length, and then volume. **A SHORTER REPLY THAT HE ENGAGES WITH ON THE MERITS = the shape worked.** **DO NOT PROMPT IT**, which is 6.12's trap and 7.34's rule. | **[M] — THE STYLE RULE IS WRITTEN INTO THE DRAFTS FILE HEADER AND §13 OBEYS IT.** Nothing further to build: the next data point is somebody else's reply. | **nothing. ## What this list does not contain

Anything whose blocker could not be stated. Two things were considered and left
out for that reason: a "speed and scalability" item, which `READINESS` already
says comes after real reports from step 4 and therefore has step 4 as its
blocker rather than a technical one; and a general "make it autopoietic" item,
which is §2 with the ambition removed — **the honest target is operational
closure in Varela's weaker sense, or Luhmann's autopoiesis of communications,
and not biological autopoiesis.** Varela himself declined to extend the term to
social systems. Self-production of the boundary and self-repair both stay out of
reach: a person still installs the hApp, and a system that cannot delete cannot
heal, only accrete and lose standing. **Coral reef rather than cell** — durable
accretion with living tissue on the surface — is the better description and the
better story.

## 8. Adopted from an adversarial ECM-remodelling inquiry — a sibling taxonomy, and the science carried more than the self-audit did

**Opened 2026-11-24.** Source: a 507-line external working document — the final
distillation of a long adversarial inquiry into adult extracellular-matrix
remodelling and biological size specification — **held locally by its author and
deliberately not in this repository.** Its title is omitted here on the same
grounds: this repository is public, the document is not, and nothing structural
below depends on naming it. Its
own instruction is that Parts I–IV are the worked example and **Part V is the
transferable payload**: *"If the purpose is architectural ideas for a
claim/critique system, Part V is the payload."*

> **THAT INSTRUCTION WAS FOLLOWED TOO LITERALLY ON THE FIRST PASS, AND THE
> CORRECTION IS THE REASON THIS SECTION IS SHAPED AS IT IS.** The first reading
> took its items from Part V and **described them as if all of them came from
> there, which was false** — the role grid is **§2.5, in Part II**, and §5.7
> only restates it. Asked directly whether the items came from the biology, the
> answer was no. **Re-read in full, the SCIENCE carried more transferable
> structure than the self-audit did**, and the three strongest items in this
> section (8.7, 8.8, 8.9) are from Parts II and III. **A document's own account
> of where its value sits is evidence, not an index** — which is this
> repository's own *"read the harness header"* lesson arriving from outside.

**AND IT INDEPENDENTLY DERIVES THIS REPOSITORY'S OWN CONCLUSION, THEN ADDS A
THIRD TERM.** The 2026-11-22 finding was that **source-checking can find only
one class of error**, blind to equivocation and selective sampling because in
both cases every citation is valid. Part V §5.1 reaches that from an unrelated
inquiry and names a third class this repository had not:

> **Unconsulted adjacency** — sourcing correct, inference valid, **premise set
> incomplete.** *"Cannot be closed from inside a document by any amount of
> self-audit, because the missing premise is invisible to the frame that
> omitted it. The only detector is a reader with different training."*

**The first error class on this list whose only detector is social.**

### 8.0 A measurement, because §5.2 claims its trigger vocabulary is countable

§5.2 names the most frequent error in that inquiry as **a list of alternatives
presented as complete when it was not**, and claims the triggers are few:
*exactly two, the only, must be, either/or, ruled out, no X anywhere.*
**Measured against this repository's six prose documents, 2026-11-24: 648
boundary-shaped phrases**, `the only` alone **288**, then `must be` 128,
nowhere/anywhere 110, `exactly N` 42, either/or 28, can-never 20, `ruled out`
19, there-are-N 7, exhaustive 6.

**Most are benign** — *"the only writer of that map"* is a code fact. **But the
class has a confirmed hit in this file:** line ~1811 reads *"~~It is the only
open decision on this list~~ — **FALSE, corrected 2026-11-14: TWENTY-SEVEN rows
carry a live `[D]`**."* A `the only` claim, wrong, **inherited as a premise and
quoted back as fact repeatedly inside one session** — §5.2's compounding
warning, already paid for here. One question catches it: **"over what set, and
who enumerated it?"**

### 8.1 The candidates

| # | item | who | blocked on |
|---|---|---|---|

A system that tracks claims without tracking the categories they are claims about will churn on exactly the content that changes most."* Across ~40 revisions his **categories survived and the mechanisms under them turned over.** An argument for seeding a **`CritiqueSpecies`** — runtime, hierarchical via `parent_species`, **no fork** — over a sixth `CritiqueMode` variant, which changes the integrity zome and **forks the network.** Gap and choice already recorded in the positional brief §6.2 with a worked specimen in the immune brief §7 | D | the same `[D]` — which species to seed first |
| 8.2 | **ROLE TAGS, FROM §2.5's FIVE-WAY GRID — sensor / reference / setpoint-comparator / target / budget.** §5.7's rule: *"a claim is only responsive to a question if the roles match. Most apparent progress in this inquiry was a role mismatch."* General form: ***a mechanism that reports a quantity is routinely mistaken for one that determines it*** — Weyl's law as the pure case, an exact size **sensor** carrying nothing about target size. **`Claim.semantic_tags: Vec<String>` already ships (`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs:115`), so this is a CONVENTION over a shipped field: no entry change, no bump, no fork.** This repository found a sibling independently — #555's *"the taxonomy is a taxonomy of signals, and a selector is a third kind of address"* **is a role-mismatch finding**. **AND THE GRID EARNED ITS KEEP IN THE FIELD ON 2026-11-24, WHICH IS THE FIRST REAL EVIDENCE FOR THIS ROW'S `[D]`.** The source thread asserted *"the macrophages were found to be that construction crew"* and then **retracted it one turn later in the grid's own terms**: *"They're the gate. **This is a role mix-up of exactly the kind the five-way grid exists to catch**, so worth separating cleanly — there are **three** distinct participants here, not two."* **Source** (the progenitor pool), **Crew** (the depositional lineage that lays matrix), **Gate** (macrophages, deciding build-versus-scar in a narrow window). The discriminating evidence is clean: macrophage depletion in salamanders **did not remove the builders — the builders produced scar instead** (10 of 10, thick collagen I, α-SMA myofibroblasts). *"The cells were there; the environment told them to do the wrong thing."* **So the convention caught a live misassignment, in the author's own judgement, within one turn.** That is **weak evidence FOR a convention nothing validates** — it shows the grid is load-bearing as a PROMPT even where nothing enforces it, which is exactly what this row's `[D]` has to weigh. **It is not evidence that a VALIDATED tag would have helped**, and that distinction is the `[D]` | D | whether a convention nothing validates earns its place — the declaration problem again |

buildable: the question is whether a consequence can be supplied without its ordering, and that is a design decision, not a measurement |

### 8.2 What is NOT adopted, and the best of them cannot be had cheaply

**§5.3's LOAD-BEARING CONCENTRATION IS THE ONE THIS PROTOCOL MOST WANTS AND
CANNOT AFFORD.** *"Build the dependency graph, weight each node by how much
rests on it, and flag any node whose support is thin relative to its load."* In
the source inquiry four successive conclusions each rested on the one before and
**all traced to a single secondhand, unreplicated observation with a known
confound** (§3.6) — every individual step locally sound. Its own verdict on
that: *"the model verdicts are **untested**, not ruled out."*

**MEASURED: THIS PROTOCOL HAS NO CLAIM-TO-CLAIM DEPENDENCY EDGE.**
`Stance::Supports` is one agent's position on a target, not a structural premise
link; `past_cone` is retention, not dependency; no `LinkTypes` variant expresses
*this claim's argument uses that claim*. A new link type **changes the integrity
zome and forks the network.**

> **DELIBERATELY NOT ADDED TO §4's BUMP CANDIDATE SET, on that section's own
> rule.** `docs/integrity-bump-plan.md` §2 is the authority on the set, and an
> index listing a candidate the authority does not **is the index being wrong.**
> **A `[D]` nobody has taken is not a bump candidate** — 4.7 was withdrawn by
> decision, so the set is for what is wanted, not what is imagined. **If the
> premise edge is ever decided FOR, it joins §4 and the plan's §2 in the same
> commit, never one without the other.**

**The read-layer half IS available and is a different thing.** This file's rows
cite each other and `check-stale-blockers.mjs` already does cross-reference
work, so dependency-weighting over `docs/open-work.md` needs no protocol at all.
Not opened; named so it is not confused with the forked version.

**THE THREE OF §5.9's FIVE DURABLE CATEGORIES NOT IMPORTED, ASSESSED RATHER
THAN SKIPPED.** §5.9 names five that survived ~40 revisions — the A/B/C split,
the role grid, capacity/occupancy, recorder/governor, the budget category — and
the first pass took one.

- **Capacity/occupancy** — *"occupancy deficits present identically to capacity
  deficits, because both are measured by the same observable"* — is a family
  this repository holds under other names: row **6.25**'s four surfaces *where
  a read that FAILED rendered as an answer nobody got*, and the standing rule
  that **an exit code is not a measurement**. Two states, one observable.
  **Corroborates; nothing to import.**
- **The A/B/C split** — three problems routinely conflated, where *"most
  apparent progress on B turns out to be A or C wearing B's clothes"* — has
  **no identified analogue here**, and inventing one would be the role mismatch
  it warns about. **Named as not-transferred rather than left out.**
- **The budget category** is 8.7 and 8.8, and it was **already built twice**
  before the category arrived.

**§5.5's cross-product of accepted claims is declined.** Its own text calls it
*"cheapest class to detect, easiest to miss"* — true of a document, false of a
DHT: O(n²) over claims, local to no agent.

**§5.8's revealed clustering is an analysis method, not a feature.** *"When
every successful intervention in a field clusters on one side of a distinction
the field does not draw, the distinction is probably real."* Worth knowing;
nothing to build.

**AND ONE PHYSICS RESULT THAT CORROBORATES A CHOICE ALREADY MADE.** §2.1: *"a
local rule is scale-free unless a scale enters it"*, and every formalism
examined **concedes the scale rather than deriving it** — in noncommutative
geometry the absolute scale enters through the operator's normalisation,
*"inserted as data, not derived"*; in modular theory the flow has **no unit**
and the scale is fixed from outside. *"The scale is physics, not mathematics,
and the search object therefore has units."* **This protocol already obeys
that**: `SettlementPolicy`'s parameters *"are all integers and all comparisons
are exact"*, published and named by whoever aims them, and friction limits are
integers in a window. **The scale is inserted as data and says so.** Nothing to
change — recorded because a future proposal to *derive* a threshold rather than
publish one is now answerable with a structural reason instead of a preference.

### 8.3 The caveat this section owes, on the model of §5's own

**THE RECORD HAS NOW BEEN READ, AND ONE OF PART V's TWO CENTRAL CLAIMS DOES
NOT SURVIVE IT.** The first pass said Part V's authority rested on its own
statement that *"every entry below was derived by auditing this inquiry's own
errors, each traceable to a specific correction in the record"*, that **the
record had not been read here**, and that the most exposed claim was §5.2's
*"single most frequent error"*. **The 1,921-line source synthesis was supplied
on 2026-11-24 and its §12 is that record.** Both claims were checked.

**§5.1's THIRD CLASS IS SUPPORTED.** §12.5 is the table Part V §5.1 condenses,
and it carries the empirical basis the distillation omits: **two spectral
errors, both formally valid arguments over accurate citations** — one drawing
an over-strong conclusion from true isospectral counterexamples, the other
omitting **self-adjointness**, a premise *"not stated in any source because
sources do not restate their own standing assumptions."* §12.1b records that
**both *"were caught by an interlocutor, not by verification"***. The class has
instances, and they were detected the way §5.1 says they must be.

> **AND §12.5 STATES THE CONSEQUENCE MORE SHARPLY THAN THE DISTILLATION DOES,
> naming its own sections: *"A system that verifies sources and not inferences
> will certify this document's original §8.2, §10, §11.2 and §12.3 as fully
> sourced."*** That is the 2026-11-22 finding with a worked list attached.

**§5.2's FREQUENCY CLAIM IS NOT SUPPORTED BY ITS OWN APPENDIX. COUNTED
2026-11-24:** §12.1 tabulates **13** sourcing/quantifier corrections, §12.1b
**13** structural ones, and §12.1c **5** non-exhaustive enumerations — some of
the 5 also appearing among the 13. §12.1c nonetheless asserts *"the single most
frequent error is **not** a wrong fact or an invalid inference"*, and on its own
tabulation a wrong fact is **13 against at most 5.**
> **THE CLAIM IS AN INSTANCE OF THE CLASS IT INTRODUCES.** *"The single most
> frequent"* is a superlative over a set, asserted without the set being
> counted — §5.2's own *"over what set, and who enumerated it?"* applied to
> §5.2. **This is not a reason to discard the class**, which is real, has five
> documented instances and three of them sitting in the same document; it is a
> reason the ROW ORDER in §8.1 does not rest on it. **8.3 was already scoped
> to a report over our own prose rather than to the claim's ranking**, which is
> the form that survives.

**WHAT THE RECORD ALSO SHOWS, WHICH NEITHER DOCUMENT CLAIMS: WHO CAUGHT THEM.**
Of the 26 rows in §12.1 and §12.1b, **7 name the user/interlocutor** as the
trigger, 4 a literature or directed search, and **2 a self-audit** — the rest
unattributed. **The largest named detector is another person**, which is the
same conclusion §5.1 reaches from a different direction, and it is the part of
this import least amenable to anything this repository can build.

**THE SUBSTANTIVE BIOLOGY REMAINS UNCHECKED and nothing above depends on it.**

**THE SUBSTANTIVE BIOLOGY IS UNCHECKED AND NOTHING ABOVE DEPENDS ON IT BEING
TRUE.** Parts I–IV carry figures this repository has not verified — a ~17-fold
dog body-mass range, six loci at ~46–52.5% of breed size variance, *Drosophila*
critical size ~1.10 mg at 17 °C vs ~0.87 mg at 25 °C, a photoperiod null at
F = 0.0001, macrophage depletion abolishing regeneration in 10 of 10 animals,
18 cm of new bone by distraction at 1 mm/day. **Every row in §8.1 borrows a
STRUCTURE — budget-not-target, recorder-versus-governor, count-versus-timer,
name-the-carrier — and none of them needs its biological instance to hold.**
That is deliberate: a structure that depends on an unverified figure would be
§5.3's load-bearing concentration, built here on purpose.

**And the document is honest about its own weakest point in a way worth
recording:** §3.6 names a single secondhand, unreplicated, *uninterpretable*
observation as the single point of failure for most of §3.4, and states the
consequence rather than burying it. **That disclosure is why Part V's taxonomy
is worth borrowing from at all** — it is the behaviour row 7.40 and READINESS §3
ask of this repository, arriving from someone else's inquiry.

### 8.4 Row 8.6's audit, run 2026-11-24 — and determinism is what decides which fields get a governor

**THE `[M]` HALF OF ROW 8.6 IS DONE.** The audit it generates was *"for every
field that reports state, what governs it, and is there a check comparing the
two?"* It was run over the integrity zome's **19 entry types** — 23 structs in
`dna/integrity/src/lib.rs`, four of which (`DnaProperties`, `Promise`,
`Condition`, `PeriodBoundary`) are not entries but were read too, since
`PeriodBoundary` turned out to carry one of the reported fields — by
enumerating every field and reading every `validate_*` callback. **Counted, not
estimated.** **The `[D]`
half — whether this becomes a standing rule in `docs/fixed-core.md` — is
untouched and is H's.**

**THE AUDIT FOUND AN ORGANISING PRINCIPLE, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE LIST,
AND THE CODE STATES IT ALREADY.** `SettlementPolicy`'s doc comment
(`lib.rs:992`) explains why its parameters are all integers: *"validation
recomputes the status from these numbers, so every peer must reach the identical
answer. A float, a ratio or a clock would each break that in a different way."*

> **THAT IS THE WHOLE RULE: A REPORTED FIELD CAN BE GOVERNED IN VALIDATION
> EXACTLY WHEN ITS GOVERNOR IS DETERMINISTIC ACROSS PEERS.** Recomputation,
> hashing and `must_get_valid_record` qualify — `validate_membrane`'s own
> comment says the last of these is chosen because *"every validating peer
> resolves the same constitution or defers, rather than disagreeing based on
> what each happens to have gossiped."* **Wall-clock `now` does not qualify.**
> So the governed/ungoverned split is not carelessness; it mostly tracks a real
> constraint. **The findings below are the cases where a DETERMINISTIC governor
> exists and is not used anyway.**

**FOUR TIERS, AND EVERY REPORTER FIELD IN THE ZOME IS IN ONE OF THEM.**

| tier | fields | state |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Governed in validation — recomputed and compared by every peer** | `SettlementStatus.state` (recomputed via `settle()` from `inputs` + `policy`, `lib.rs:1601-1602`, with inputs classified and padding refused); `Revelation.prediction`/`salt` (re-hashed against `SealedPrediction`, `:1433`); `Critique.past_cone` and `Assessment.past_cone` (every member resolves, target-matched, de-duplicated, **transitively closed**); every `author`/`agent`/`creator`/`proposer`/`importer`/`aggregator`/`assessor` binding | **the pattern working.** `SettlementStatus`'s error message is the model: *"Settlement is a pure function and every peer computes it."* |
| **2. Governor EXISTS but is opt-in at the read layer** | `WorldlineTrace.checksum` — `verify_trace_checksum` (`coordinator:3721`) recomputes the Merkle root and compares; `PeriodBoundary.entry_count` is coordinator-**computed** (`:3422`) and folded into that root (`:5300`), so a tampered count breaks it, and a test asserts so (`:6539`) | **validation checks only `checksum.len() != 32`.** A hand-written trace with a bogus 32-byte checksum **validates fine**; only a reader who chooses to call the verifier finds out. **An odometer with a calibration tool in the glovebox.** |
| **3. A DETERMINISTIC GOVERNOR IS AVAILABLE AND UNUSED — the findings** | `BridgeRecord.original_length`/`excerpt_length`; the **18** self-declared time fields | see below |
| **4. No governor possible — and only sometimes said so** | `ExternalCritique.author_handle` (**documented**: *"this DHT cannot verify it and does not pretend to"*); `BridgeRecord.carried_fields`/`dropped_fields` (**documented**: *"the lists' actual accuracy to what was really dropped can't be independently verified; only their internal consistency can"*); `Critique.replication_attempted` (**NOT documented anywhere**) | the documented ones are the right outcome. The undocumented one is the gap. |

**FINDING 1 — `BridgeRecord.original_length` IS CALLER-SUPPLIED IN A STRUCT
WHOSE DOC COMMENT ARGUES, AT LENGTH, FOR TAKING A FIELD OUT OF THE CALLER'S
HANDS.** `author` is filled from `agent_info()` *"so the field states who called
rather than who claims to have"* (`lib.rs:460`). Two fields later,
`original_length` is taken straight from `payload.original_length`
(`coordinator:4236`). **And the governor is already loaded in BOTH places:**
`record_twitter_mirror` fetches the mew's record to pick the link type
(`:4241`), and `validate_bridge_record` calls
`must_get_entry(record.mew_hash)` and **discards the result** (`lib.rs:2149`).
A deterministic comparison against the mirrored entry's own content length is
one line from code that already ran.

> **THE STRUCT'S COMMENT SAYS THESE ARE THE ONLY TWO CHECKS POSSIBLE** —
> *"independent of any DHT lookup"* — **and that qualifier is doing unnoticed
> work, because the DHT lookup happens anyway on the line above.** The claim is
> true of `carried_fields`/`dropped_fields`, which depend on what the bridge
> chose to send and are genuinely tier 4. It is **false of `original_length`**,
> which is a function of an entry validation already fetches.

**FINDING 2 — THE ZOME CARRIES 18 SELF-DECLARED TIME FIELDS AND BINDS NONE OF
THEM TO ITS OWN ACTION'S TIMESTAMP, WHILE WRITING OUT THE REASON NOT TO TRUST
THEM.** `validate_pre_registration`'s comment (`lib.rs:1374`) is explicit: the
ordering is *"checked against the question's own ACTION timestamp rather than
any self-reported field, because a self-reported timestamp is exactly what an
author gaming this would set freely."* **That is the correct pattern, stated,
and used for exactly one thing.**

**MEASURED: zero of the 18 are ever compared to `action.timestamp()`.** The
only two comparisons involving them are **self-declared against
self-declared** — `expires_at <= published_at` on `Constitution` (`:2058`) and
`expires_at <= created_at` on `WorldlineTrace` (`:2114`).

> **AND THAT ONE IS LOAD-BEARING, WHICH IS WHAT LIFTS IT ABOVE A TIDINESS
> POINT.** The read layer treats expiry as fact:
> `constitution.expires_at.map_or(true, |exp| exp > now)` decides whether an
> agent's promises are live (`coordinator:5589`), and the same shape gates
> traces (`:769`, `:3505`). **So `published_at: 0` with `expires_at: u64::MAX`
> passes validation — `MAX > 0` — and is read as a live constitution forever.**
> `validate_membrane` resolves a founder's constitution and checks the agent
> matches, but **never consults its expiry**, so nothing downstream catches it
> either. **A check that looks like it governs an expiry governs only the
> ordering of two numbers the author chose.**
>
> **THE DETERMINISTIC FIX EXISTS AND IS ALREADY IN THE FILE.** `action.timestamp()`
> is in the record every peer holds, so binding a declared time to it — exactly
> or within a stated tolerance — breaks no peer agreement. **Comparing it to
> `now` would**, which is precisely why `SettlementPolicy` refused a clock.

**FINDING 3 — `Critique.replication_attempted` IS A TIER-4 FIELD WITH NO NOTE
SAYING SO, AND IT IS EXPORTED AS A PROPERTY.** It appears in **no** validation
callback, has no governor even in principle (it reports the author's own offline
conduct), and is rendered into the N4L export as `"replication attempted"`
(`coordinator:127`). Every harness in the repository sets it `false`; one
coordinator test sets it `true`. **The gap is not the field — tier 4 is a
legitimate place to be — it is that `author_handle` and `dropped_fields` both
carry a comment admitting the limit and this one does not.** A reader of the
N4L export cannot tell a verified property from an unverifiable one.

**WHAT THE AUDIT DID NOT FIND, STATED SO THE NEXT RUN DOES NOT RE-DERIVE IT.**
No ungoverned derived counter of `adoption_count`'s kind survives anywhere in
the zome — that class is genuinely closed, and `PeriodBoundary.entry_count`,
the one field shaped like it, is computed rather than declared and is covered by
a checksum. **The four instances row 8.6 lists were a closed set, and the audit's
new material is the three findings above plus the determinism principle.**

> **ONE MORE CORROBORATION FOR 8.8, FOUND ON THE WAY.** `SettlementPolicy`
> **rejected a clock explicitly** — *"a float, a ratio or a clock would each
> break that in a different way"* — and chose integer counts. §8.8's
> count-versus-timer distinction is therefore **already decided one way in
> settlement and the other way in conductance**, which strengthens 8.8's `[D]`
> rather than changing it: the protocol has made both choices, in different
> places, for stated reasons in one of them.

**WHAT IS LEFT, AND IT IS ALL `[D]`.** Whether to bind declared times to
`action.timestamp()` (a validation change, so **a fork** — it belongs in §4's
bump set, not in a patch); whether `original_length` should be recomputed (also
validation, also a fork, and it is **one line**); whether tier 4 fields must
carry a comment admitting their limit (**free, no fork, and the cheapest of the
three**); and 8.6's original `[D]`, whether the recorder-versus-governor
distinction joins `docs/fixed-core.md` as a standing rule. **Blocked on: H.**
